# Contracts (locked in GRO-1961)

Source of truth for types: [`shared/types.ts`](../shared/types.ts). This file is the prose companion.

## Repo layout / scripts

```
package.json          npm workspaces: client, desktop. Root scripts:
                        dev            electron-vite dev (cwd desktop/): builds main + preload, serves the renderer with HMR, launches Electron
                        build          electron-vite build → desktop/out/{main,preload,renderer}
                        desktop:build  build, then electron-builder --mac → desktop/dist-app (.app + dmg, F1 — see "Packaging"); the root version is injected via extraMetadata
                        typecheck      tsc -p client && tsc -p shared && tsc -p desktop
                        test           vitest run  (FOUR projects: client=jsdom, desktop=node, tools=node, perf=jsdom — client/vitest.perf.config.ts, the budget tripwires kept off the main run's contended pool)
                        e2e            build, then playwright test --config desktop/e2e/playwright.config.ts (G1 — Playwright-Electron smoke suite)
                        e2e:only       the same Playwright suite on the existing desktop/out, no rebuild
                        perf           tools/perf/run.mjs: the local CDP perf harness on the packaged app (A/B with --vs, ABBA order; see tools/perf/README.md). Never in CI
                        perf:budget    tools/perf/measureBudget.mjs: the size and integrity gate on desktop/out + the packaged .app and DMG (run after desktop:build; see "Packaging")
                        perf:budget:ci the same gate on desktop/out alone (--out-only; after build) — what CI runs
client/               The renderer: React 19 + TS, @milkdown/crepe 7.22.x, plus ONE lazily-loaded dependency: `@excalidraw/excalidraw` PINNED to 0.18.1 (YAZ-878 — the module is imported only by `drawings/renderScene.ts`, and only through a dynamic `import()` on the first drawing preview or modal open, so it never enters the main chunk; its stylesheet is a second dynamic import, made by the modal's surface alone, YAZ-879). No scripts of its own — electron-vite (desktop/electron.vite.config.ts) builds it with ./index.html as the entry, and its renderer config `dedupe`s `react`/`react-dom` — 🔒 load-bearing since YAZ-879: npm hoists an OLDER react beside the Excalidraw tree at the repo root, and without the dedupe `<Excalidraw>` mounts against a second React and dies on a null dispatcher. Every entry below lives under client/src/:
  App.tsx                 composes this window's vaults (`roots`, YAZ-2602 D1 — `root` is the first, and a window may show up to `MAX_WINDOW_ROOTS`) + `useWorkspace` identity, window-local `sidebarCollapsed` (YAZ-1280), Sidebar, retained main editor layers, and the full-editor RightPanel; global settings/width still follow `storage.subscribe`, while the lens (window identity since YAZ-1628) and both panel visibility states persist independently per window. Owns ONE SCOPE PER VAULT (`vault/useVaultScope.ts`, held in slots — `vault/slots.ts`): each vault's watch/index/properties/sync/review/IDs feeds, every one loaded at once, and `scopeOf(path)`, the scope of the vault that holds a page, which every editor, rename, notice and row menu asks ("Multi-window": Several vaults in one window); `addVault` / `removeVault` / `dropVault` (a vault joins, leaves, or its folder is gone), `reorderVaults` (a drag of a vault row gives the vaults a new order, YAZ-2631 D5) and `closedVaults`, the vault rows the user closed (session-only, R9). Also owns menu/link/file lifecycle listeners, renderer ⌘B, the renderer ⌘X/⌘C/⌘V listener (asks the Sidebar's `clipboardRef`, YAZ-1674), the ⌘K uncollapse/focus handshake (`openSearch`: it shows the Search tab first, and `changeLens` never stores that tab — YAZ-2638 🔒 D2, the `sidebar/` entry), rename/delete carry-before-remap ordering, external-rename notice, the one passive toast (`notify(text, kind)` — bottom-left, iconed, D10), title/hash, theme, and responsive split-vs-overlay derivation; a null root (no vault) renders Welcome with no auto-dialog.
  vault/                  what a window holds once PER VAULT (YAZ-2602 D1): useVaultScope (`useVaultScope(root, notify)` → `VaultScope`: the vault's `useWatch` source, its three link sources — `wikilinks`, `wikilinkCandidates`, `viewOnlyLinks`, fed by its own `WikilinkIndexBridge` — its property declarations, its GitHub sync and attention banner, its display name, its review settings and session, its external-rename detector, and its answer on IDs with the box that asks; a `null` root is an idle scope — no bridge call, no timer) and slots (`VaultSlots`, `EMPTY_SLOTS`, `assignSlots`, `renameSlots`, `movedRoot`: `MAX_WINDOW_ROOTS` slots, a FIXED number of hook calls per render; a vault KEEPS its slot while it is in the window, so nothing of a vault that stays loads again when another joins, leaves or is renamed) (+ slots.test.ts; the scopes are proved through `App.test.tsx`)
  Welcome.tsx             the Welcome screen (C2, GRO-2164): app name, recents as one-click rows (dead folder → "Folder not found" + MRU drop), Open folder… (+ test)
  api.ts                  `api` = window.yaseenDocs in `CONTRACT`'s shape, looked up at call time: invokes reject with BridgeRequestError, pushes and the specials pass through; the renderer's one door to main (+ test)
  bridge.d.ts             `window.yaseenDocs: YaseenDocsApi` (derived from `CONTRACT` in shared/ipc.ts; installed by desktop/src/preload)
  editor/                 Editor dispatches BEFORE mounting an owner: a path the Files tree shows as a FOLDER gets `views/FolderView` (YAZ-2290 D3, see "Folders are the pages"); otherwise by the shared file kind — Markdown gets the Crepe host + conflict bar (the scroller holds the page title, properties, Crepe, the comment stream — YAZ-1472, see "Comments" — "Linked mentions" and "Reviews"), while view-only kinds delegate to `viewers/` and never mount Crepe, autosave, frontmatter or backlinks owners. createCrepe (locked factory, see "Editor rules"), featureConfig (Crepe feature allowlist + guard test), drawingMenu (the Drawing slash item on Crepe's own BlockEdit menu, YAZ-877), drawing/drawingPreview + drawingPreview.css (decoration-only `![[x.excalidraw]]` previews, see rule 27; the editing MODAL a preview click opens is React beside the editor, `src/drawings/`, rule 28), image/ (YAZ-1656, rule 30: imageSrc — the `app://vault` URL builder + Obsidian's `alt|width` parse/format; imageView + .css — THE ONE node view, on the commonmark `image` node; insertImage — paste/drop bytes → `assets/images/`; imageOptions — the ctx slice that carries the host's options to the paste handler; ImageModal + .css — the lightbox), listItemRoundTrip (empty-item round trip; `mapOutsideFences`, the one walk every text rule reads the text through, rule 8), SaveIndicator, PageSettings (YAZ-2643, rule 34: the per-page cog left of the zoom pill and its menu — the "Line numbers" switch and the word and character count), lineNumbers/ (YAZ-2643, rule 34: blockLines — the start line, the end line and the kind of each block of a text, from micromark events, the worker's one job; lineNumbers.worker + workerDocument — the web worker and the `document` stand-in it imports first; fileLines — the text the editor parses and the map from its lines to the file's; lineNumbers — the view-state `$prose` plugin, `showLineNumbers`, and `requestBlockLines`, the window's one worker; codeLines — the one CodeMirror compartment every code block shares and `syncCodeLines`, which make a code block's own gutter count in lines of the file, D8) — this list used to name a `frontmatter` module too; there has never been one here, only `frontmatter.test.ts`, which covers `shared/frontmatter.ts` (rule 1's split/re-prepend) from the surface that consumes it — TOMBSTONE: baseEmbed/ and baseCodeBlock/ (the `![[X.base]]` decoration plugin and the ```base node view) were deleted with the format in YAZ-844, see rules 20/21
  editor/marks/           underline (mark: Mod-u ↔ `<u>…</u>` inline HTML, $remark + $markSchema + $shortcut) (+ test), highlight (mark with an optional `color`, see rule 31) (+ test), htmlPairs (shared inline-HTML pair wrapper for underline + coloured highlight), markTestKit (the shared mark test harness)
  editor/outline/         outlineFolding ($prose plugin: every foldable bullet folds — parents and image bullets, YAZ-1709) + outlineFoldKeys + outlineFolding.css, listCommands (outliner keymap), hotkeys (Obsidian hotkeys), zoom + zoom.css (zoom into a bullet), guideLines + guideLines.css (click-to-fold guide lines), listNodes (shared helpers), bullets.css (depth glyphs) (+ tests)
  drawings/               createDrawing (YAZ-877): the empty-scene constant + the clock name + the never-overwrite create loop over `api.writeAsset`, the host half of the editor's Drawing slash item; drawingScene (YAZ-878: `isDrawingTarget` + the base64 → UTF-8 → JSON read over `api.readAsset`, throwing for everything that is not a scene — plus `openDrawing`, YAZ-879's load, which keeps the resolved path and mtime a save needs), renderScene (the ONE lazy `@excalidraw/excalidraw` module import + `exportToSvg`, and where `window.EXCALIDRAW_ASSET_PATH` / `EXCALIDRAW_EXPORT_SOURCE` are pinned before it), drawingFeed (the subscribe/poke refresh source), and the modal (YAZ-879, rule 28): DrawingModal + drawingModal.css (the chrome — overlay, Save, dirty, discard strip, error line), ExcalidrawSurface (🔒 THE ENGINE SEAM: the only `<Excalidraw>` mount and the only serializer), saveDrawing (the write + the canvas-wins CONFLICT retry) (+ tests)
  editor/wikilink/        wikilinkPlugin + wikilink.css (decoration-only [[wikilink]] rendering, see rule 23), wikilinkClick + createFromLink (Links C click navigation + create-on-click, rule 23), wikilinkPicker + wikilinkPicker.css (the `[[` completion popup + its keymap, see rule 24), wikilinkMenu (the right-click menu on a rendered id link — the note's title or `<folder> (folder)`, its id, Copy ID; YAZ-2293, see rule 23), WikilinkIndexBridge (the one Markdown index feed plus the separate tree-catalog navigation feed; root changes retire old view-only rows before either new feed can compose) (+ tests)
  hooks/                  useFile (Markdown + strict view-only text load; migration/frontmatter ownership remains Markdown-only), useViewOnlyCatalog (structural-watch Files-tree catalog, on the window's one tree feed PER ROOT `lib/treeFeed.ts`, YAZ-2191 — one catalog per vault of the window, YAZ-2602), useAutosave (debounce/flush/conflict; frontmatter-only external changes absorb silently, GRO-2186, and a frontmatter-only CONFLICT retries once, YAZ-2175; `diskParts()` is the frontmatter block and the body as they are on disk, what a line number counts in, rule 34), useWatch (one bridge watch() per root, fan-out), usePickFolder (native dialog), useLinkEvents (the window's two `link.*` push listeners, then `link.ready()` — main holds a link push until the page listens, YAZ-2589 A2; see the bridge table)
  viewers/                TextViewer (selectable monospaced read-only text with two-axis overflow), PdfViewer (revoked Blob URL around Chromium's passive native PDF frame), and ImageViewer (exact binary bytes decoded once and painted as one static canvas frame) (+ tests)
  lib/                    pure logic with unit tests: autosave state machine, storage (bridge-backed in-memory cache of the main-owned app state + window identity; init() before the first render, subscribe() for live changes; since YAZ-2555 also `getWindows` — every window's entry — and a vault's number: `vaultKey` / `keyedVaults` / `setVaultKey`; since YAZ-2556 `listVaults()` — the shared `listVaults` over the cache, the vault switcher's ONE read of its rows — and `keyedVaults()` is the shared function of that name over the cache's `folders`; since YAZ-2602 the window's vaults — `getRoots`, `setRoots` (add, remove or reorder the vaults: unlike `setRoot` it keeps the tabs, the lens and the focus list, and drops the focus items of a vault that left in the same write), `mirrorRoots` (a renamed vault folder, cache only) — and `getVaultSets`, the saved workspaces, read-only; since YAZ-2631 D1 the Favorites order across vaults — `getFavoritesOrder`, the cache's own list, the same one until the order changes, and `setFavoritesOrder(roots, paths)`, which merges in the cache as main merges (`mergeFavoritesOrder`) and wakes `subscribe` at once), treeState (incl. `favoriteRoots` and, YAZ-2631 D1, `favoriteOrder(roots, favorites, order)` — the Favorites tab's paths as ONE flat list across the vaults of the window: the stored order says which vault has each place, and each vault fills its places in its own file's order), paths, windowTitle (the OS window title, `<vault> — <page>` — the vault FIRST since YAZ-2555 D6; "Multi-window": Window title), theme (resolve the Appearance setting to light/dark, K), urlHash (the #/abs/path.md mirror, rule 17), renameContinuity (Links E1 GRO-2194 / E1b GRO-2241: the per-path autosave handle map + dirty-buffer carry across a rename — per affected open path for a folder rename — see "Links": Rename; ALSO `retireDeletedPath`/`retireDeletedDir` for GRO-2272, which retire ONLY — a delete has no destination, so capturing or stashing a buffer would be a resurrection vector), dragSlot (YAZ-1471: `insertionSlot` / `dropIndex`, the insertion-slot arithmetic BOTH horizontal tab strips reorder by — the window's tab bar and a folder's view tabs — one spelling so the two cannot drift apart), pageLabel (YAZ-2290: `isFolderPath` / `useFolderPaths(roots)` — whether a tab path is a FOLDER, by the newest Files tree of the vault that holds it (YAZ-2602 S31; a path in no vault of the window asks the first) — and, since YAZ-2420 D14, `pathTitles` / `usePathTitles` — every note's and folder's title by path, off ONE vault's index snapshot, the Map kept per vault so a save in one vault hands no new Map to another's name holders — `useAllPathTitles(sources)`, the same over every vault of the window (YAZ-2602 D1: the tab strip, the right panel, the sidebar and the sheets name a page of any vault; one vault → that vault's own Map) — and `pageLabel`, a path as a name: its title, else its file name — a file hides Markdown's extension, a folder keeps its whole name), selection (the sidebar multi-select reducer + `orderedSelection`, YAZ-1336/1338), windowChord (the ONE ownership boundary for renderer window chords), sidebarHotkey / copyPathHotkey / fileClipboardHotkey (⌘B YAZ-1280, ⌘⇧C YAZ-1338, ⌘X/⌘C/⌘V YAZ-1674 — each a key on that boundary), notice (`LINK_NOTICE_MS`, `NoticeKind`, `Notice` — the toast's shared type, YAZ-1674), leadingTrailing (the watcher-burst gate: the first event after a quiet spell reads at once, a burst coalesces into ONE trailing read, YAZ-2240), treeFeed (the window's ONE `fs:tree` feed per root, YAZ-2191), windowFlush (the close/quit flush registry for the writers below App, YAZ-2174)
  components/             ContextMenuSurface.tsx (action-free context-menu mechanics for menus whose commands stay domain-owned), NoticeIcon.tsx (the toast's per-kind stroke glyphs, D10)
  links/                  completion.ts — the ONE [[…]] completion matcher (Links B, GRO-2191): trailingLinkFragment / matchLinkCandidates (ends-trimmed, case-insensitive over the candidate name, ranked exact → prefix → substring, cap 8 applied after ranking — GRO-2197; TOMBSTONE, YAZ-2293: matchLinkNames, the same match over plain names, went when the cell editors began completing over candidates) / linkCandidates (LinkCandidate { name, insert, label } rows from index records: each note under its TITLE, YAZ-2420 D17, the shortest unambiguous spelling of it, + one alias row per frontmatter alias, Links E2 GRO-2214 — every row of a note that has an `id` INSERTS that id, a note without one its title, its path or the piped alias form, YAZ-2293) / basenameCandidates (YAZ-2293: the index titles as candidates, each inserting the note's `id` when it has one — what the cell editors complete over), shared by EditableCell's link editors and the editor's [[ picker (rule 24); renameLinks.ts — the automatic link updates after an in-app rename/move (Links E1 GRO-2194, folders + cross-dir moves E1b GRO-2241: maskCode / rewriteBodyLinks / rewriteNoteLinks / updateLinksAfterRename + countLinkReferences, the E1c dry-run referencing-set count — Markdown sources/targets use index records, while explicit view-only targets use the separately pinned catalog described under "Links": Rename); renameDetector.ts — the E1c external-rename hypothesis detector (GRO-2242: detectRenames with the locked guard rails, diffRecords for the while-running snapshot feed, preRenameRecords to synthesise the engine's pre-rename view); useExternalRenames.ts — the E1c queue/banner hook (both feeds → confirm-first banner state, session dismissed set, in-app-rename suppression, the Update repair+rewrite flow — see "Links": Rename → External renames); backlinks.ts — the Links D referencing set + context snippets (GRO-2193: `backlinksFor` over an index snapshot through the shared resolver, memoized per (records, path); `mentionSnippets` over one note's raw content, code-masked, for the expanded rows — one snippet per LINE, rendered as display text through `linkDisplayText` with every mention on it highlighted, GRO-2197), BacklinksSection.tsx + backlinks.css — the collapsible "Linked mentions (N)" block at the bottom of a note or a folder's page (see Editor rule 25); folderLinks.ts — links to FOLDERS (YAZ-2290 D10: `vaultDirs`, `folderResolver`, `linkResolver` — the path-level resolver every wikilink surface shares, a note first and a folder only when none answers — `folderLinkCandidates` for the `[[` picker, `pageResolver` for the views and the properties panel (a link to a folder reads as the folder's name) and `belongsToBasenames` for a link column narrowed to a folder; see "Links": Links to folders); shortcuts.ts — the one door to `also_in` and what a folder shows (YAZ-2290 D2/D4, see "Note shortcuts"); reservedKeys.ts — `reservedKeys(ids)`, the frontmatter keys the app owns, by the vault's kind ("Two kinds of vault") (+ tests)
  comments/               the comment stream (YAZ-1472, see "Comments"): CommentsSection.tsx + comments.css (the block under a note's body and under a folder's views — the properties-panel header, one card per thread, bullet-style folds, the one composer in its three seats; a VIEW over CrepeHost's `disk`, no watcher and no read of its own), markdown.ts (`commentHtml` — the ONE Markdown→HTML door in the app, `marked` gfm+breaks → DOMPurify — and `commentSummary`, the folded row's first line); the model is `shared/comments.ts`, whose contract test sits here as comments.test.ts so vitest's client project collects it (+ tests)
  sidebar/                Sidebar, VaultSwitcher (the header's vault panel, YAZ-1767 — one-line rows with a hover ⓘ full path and vault display names since YAZ-1974; since YAZ-2555 two groups, "Open" and "Not open", over the recents plus every open and every numbered vault, and a `⌘<n>` badge on a vault that has a number; since YAZ-2556 its rows are `storage.listVaults()`, the list `yaseendocs vaults` prints too — and its right-click vault menu, YAZ-1798 — items as data in `vaultMenuSections.ts`, "Set shortcut ▸" among them since YAZ-2555 D2; since YAZ-2602 it takes `roots`, names every vault of the window on its trigger, lists the saved workspaces FIRST under "Workspaces" with their own menu (`buildVaultSetMenuSections`), offers "Add to this window" on a vault that is not in the window and, on the trigger of a window with two or more vaults, "Save as workspace…"; see "Multi-window": Open beside and Workspaces), Tree (the ONE tree every lens draws; with two or more vaults in the window its top rows on Files are VAULT ROWS — `vaultRows`, `.tree__row--vault`, a folder row that is a whole vault and no page — and a top row of the Focus tab and of the Favorites tab wears its vault's name, `.tree__vault` (YAZ-2602 D3, A4; YAZ-2631 D1, D4 — "The sidebar lenses": Several vaults); since YAZ-2290 a folder row opens the folder's tab and carries its shortcut rows — "Folders are the pages", "Note shortcuts" — and it shows no number (YAZ-2631 D2); a row is LABELLED with its title, the index's, and until the index has loaded with its file name, while the ORDER stays by file name, YAZ-2420 D15), ShortcutPicker ("Add note shortcut") and folderShortcuts (`folderShortcuts` → each folder's shortcut rows by its path, one pass per index snapshot). The lens row is "The sidebar lenses". The sidebar chrome is TWO rows since YAZ-847: ROW 1 the tablist (Files · Search · Focus · Favorites, in `SIDEBAR_TABS` order — the three lenses are YAZ-2619's, and Search is a tab of its own and no lens since YAZ-2638 🔒 D2; `DEFAULT_SIDEBAR_LENS` = Files since YAZ-1846 — a new window and a switch to a different vault land on it; the per-window `WindowEntry.sidebarLens` since YAZ-1628, which holds a lens and never Search) — carrying, right-aligned at its end, the ⚡ YAZ-862 expand/collapse-all button: ONE double-chevron acting on the dirs the ACTIVE lens has ON SCREEN (`bodyDirs`), in a single `setAll` dispatch on the one `expanded` set every lens shares (a SESSION bucket since YAZ-1642: shared by every window on the vault, never restored by a launch); it collapses those dirs whenever ANY of them is open and expands them only when none is — on Files with two or more vaults the vault rows fold with them (YAZ-2631 D6 — "The sidebar lenses": Several vaults) — and it is GONE rather than disabled on the Search tab (YAZ-2638 S17: the folds there are the search's own) and whenever the active lens has nothing to unfold, no folder and no vault row — and ROW 2 the search bar (YAZ-801), on the SEARCH TAB ONLY (YAZ-2638 🔒 D2, cases S1–S25 on the issue). THE SEARCH TAB: Search is the second tab, a magnifier (`SearchIcon`, its word in `title` + `aria-label`), so the bar has no magnifier of its own. Files, Focus and Favorites have no bar (S6). This overturns four locked rules on purpose. YAZ-797: search was "never a tab", and the bar was ALWAYS visible, on every lens, loading/error/empty vault included. YAZ-847 🔒 D5: a typed query replaced the body of the ACTIVE tab. YAZ-801: Esc emptied a typed query first, and only the second press gave up focus. YAZ-2630 🔒 D8: the `esc` keycap showed only while a query was typed, and a click cleared the query. WHY: Yasin wants to go back to a search after he leaves it, and he does not search from Focus or from Favorites. THE CARET HAS ONE PATH: ⌘K and a click on the tab both show the tab and put the caret in the bar, with the text that is there selected (S2, S5 — ⌘K's `openSearch` and the tab's click both reach App's `changeLens('search')`, which raises `pendingSearchFocus`: the App handshake above), ALSO when the Search tab already shows (S4). With the sidebar hidden, ⌘K shows the sidebar first (S3). A plain show of the sidebar while it is on the Search tab — hidden, then shown, with no ⌘K — does NOT take the caret: the flag is false at that mount. ESC IN A PAGE (`focusSidebar`, `lib/focusHandoff.ts`): while the Search tab shows it puts the caret in the search bar, empty or not — the bar gets it whenever the bar is on the page; on Files, Focus and Favorites the tree's active row gets it, as before. ESC IN THE BAR goes back to the lens the window last showed, and the text STAYS (S12 — `leaveSearch`); it writes nothing when the lens did not change (`changeLens` does not write the lens that is stored already). The `esc` keycap at the bar's right is ALWAYS in the bar, and a click does what Esc does (`.sidebar__search-back`, `aria-label` "Leave search", `title` "Back (Esc)", no Tab stop; S13). There is no clear button: to clear, select the text and delete it, and ⌘K selects it (S14). Refused: Esc clears the text (one habit key press would lose the search); a `↩ <query>` keycap in an empty bar that brings the last search back (the tab keeps the search). THE SEARCH STAYS (S10): the text, the folds and the highlight live in `useSidebarSearch`, which stays mounted with the panel, so they stay while a different tab shows. While a different tab shows, the search does no work and scrolls nothing (`useSearchResults(vaults, active ? query : '')`: a search that is not on screen ranks nothing); on return to the Search tab the text, the results, the folds and the highlight are back. New typed text gives a new ranking and the search's own folds again (S11). NEVER STORED (S15): `changeLens` writes each tab but Search to the window identity, so `storage.setSidebarLens` and `window.setIdentity` never get `'search'`, the main process did not change, and a window that starts again opens on its last lens. A switch to a different vault lands on Files, and the text is gone (S16, YAZ-1846). Known and accepted: a hidden sidebar unmounts the panel, so the text is lost, as before. THE BODY: with no text it is one line, "Type to search every note and folder.", and no tree (S7); with text that matches nothing it is "No matches" (S9); with text it is the search TREE (YAZ-2620, overturning the flat-list ruling on YAZ-739 for the sidebar; the shortcut picker keeps the flat `SearchResults` list): the Files tree of the WHOLE vault — with two or more vaults the FOREST, so each match stands under its vault's row, a parent row that is never a match (YAZ-2602 A9) — cut down by `searchTree` to the ranked matches and the folders that lead to them and drawn by the same `Tree` — a matched folder with no match inside keeps all it holds, closed (🔒 D2); the folders that lead to a match are open, and a fold clicked there lasts as long as its query and never reaches the Files tree's stored folds; no shortcut rows, and nothing there creates, renames or drags; a match shows the typed text bold on the highlight wash (`--mark-bg`) and every other row is dim (`Tree`'s optional `marks`, 🔒 D5); the ranking keeps the best `SEARCH_CAP` (50) candidate rows, and a line below the tree says so once it is reached (🔒 D6) — the candidates carry the loaded tree's FOLDERS (🔒 D1–D2, YAZ-1491 — fed from the Sidebar's own `dirs` memo, `folderCandidates` spliced ahead of `searchCandidates` so a folder ranks first within a rank bucket) and its files that are no notes, each matched by its file name (YAZ-2620 🔒 D3 — `fileCandidates` off the `otherFiles` memo, spliced last; a note still never matches on its folder name, the folder itself is one row; rows match and read by TITLE, YAZ-2420 D14, and text that holds a note's or a folder's id shows the pages whose ids it holds FIRST, then the title matches for the same text, no row twice — `searchRows`, D32 and YAZ-2677 🔒 D9. What "holds" means is `holdsId(query)` in `shared/noteId.ts`, which reads the query ONE time and answers null for a text that can hold no id, so a search by title costs nothing more (S78; `searchCandidates.perf.test.ts` measures 10,000 notes): an old ID, or a number ID typed as `YAZ-12`, `yaz-12`, `yaz12` or `yaz 12`, anywhere in the text — a pasted id, a `[[<id>]]` link, a file name, a whole path — with letters that end a longer word not an ID's; letters the note's vault had before (`SearchCandidate.was`, S77); and a BARE number, which is the whole text and finds that number in each vault of the window (S69, S70). An id is held whole or not at all (`12` is not `YAZ-1` or `YAZ-120`). The row of a number ID match is labelled `YAZ-12 — Title`, and the search tree shows that: `useSidebarSearch` hands the tree `TreeMarks.ids` (path → full ID) for each result whose label starts with its ID, and `Tree.tsx` draws `YAZ-12 — ` in front of that row's title (S70 — in a window with two vaults each row has its own letters; a note found by its title, or by an old ID, reads as its title alone). The sidebar draws its results as a tree in tree order (YAZ-2620), and the ID match is the ranking's first row, so it is the one the highlight starts on), Enter on a folder row OPENS its page as a tab, exactly as on a note row it opens the note (`activate`; ⌘⏎ opens a background tab, and a file with no viewer in the app opens in its default app), while a CLICK is the tree row's own — it opens a note and folds a folder, a double-click opening the folder's page (YAZ-2620 🔒 D1), and the Search tab stays after each of them (YAZ-2638 S24) — the search tree is the body of the Search tab ALONE (YAZ-2638 🔒 D2; before, 🔒 D5 of YAZ-847, it replaced the ACTIVE lens tab's body), while the tabs row stays live and a tab switch keeps the query — a conditional render, never a teardown, so every bit of a lens's tree state (data, expansion, pending create/rename, drag) is waiting untouched when that lens shows again — fed by `client/src/search/` (ranking engine YAZ-802, `useSearchResults(vaults, query)` YAZ-803 — since YAZ-2602 R2 over every vault of the window: one index snapshot per vault, each kept current by its own watcher and kept while its vault stands, the candidates of all of them ranked as ONE list, so the `SEARCH_CAP` counts the matches of every vault together — `searchTree` YAZ-2620; the hook's index feeds are LAZY since YAZ-808: no `index(root)` read and no watch subscription until the first non-empty query, latched on for good after it, because the always-on index feed of each vault is its WikilinkIndexBridge's) and driven entirely from the input, which never loses focus: the highlight starts on the BEST-ranked match, wherever the tree draws it, and is held by its PATH (🔒 D4, YAZ-2620); ↑/↓ walk the matches ON SCREEN in tree order, never a parent row, stop at both ends and never wrap (the `[[` picker's rule), and a highlight whose match left the screen moves to the next match still drawn; on a NOTE row ⏎ opens it in place, ⌘⏎ in a background tab, a row click opens and ⌘-click background-opens (I3) — the results stay up either way; the blank-space context menu is the TREE's, so right-clicking the Search tab's blank space offers nothing, with text or with none ("New note" there would have no target; YAZ-2638 S19) — not even Electron's text menu, which the body's handler default-prevents (YAZ-2050) — while a result ROW opens that row's own tree menu through the same `openMenu` (YAZ-2050, the tree row's `onNodeContextMenu`; the right-click moves the highlight onto a match, and any row wears the selected style while its menu is open): 🔒 D1 it follows the FILES rules, a search row being a disk row — `MenuTargets.lens` pins `'files'` for a row of the Search tab (the active lens otherwise) and every lens rule in the menu path reads it (`startCreate`'s hop to Files) — and since YAZ-2638 🔒 D1 it is the menu of its row in Files PLUS one item, "Show in sidebar", FIRST ("The sidebar lenses": "Show in sidebar" on a row — before, YAZ-2050 🔒 D1 made the two menus the same); 🔒 D2 the items that draw INTO the hidden tree (Rename, New note / dated note / folder / dated folder) first leave the search through `onRevealInFiles` (App flips the lens to Files and issues the same reveal request the tab menu uses; the Files reveal accepts a dir, which opens ITSELF too via the create menu's synthetic-child `expandTo`) — `MenuTargets.leaveSearchTo` names the row, `viaTree` calls `onRevealInFiles` with it, the reveal expands and flashes the row, and the query STAYS in the Search tab (YAZ-2638 S22; before, the reveal cleared it) — and every other item acts in place, on the Search tab (the focus toggle and the favorite toggle among them: an add from a search row changes the list and the Search tab stays, YAZ-2638 S20, S21 — this overturns YAZ-2619 🔒 D3 for the Search tab, where an add showed the Focus tab); "Show in sidebar" on a document tab shows Files, and the text stays too (YAZ-2638 S23); multi-select never reaches this menu (the selection ends when the Search tab shows, YAZ-2638 S18); ⌘-click on a file row opens it in a BACKGROUND TAB of this window (the locked I3 ruling, GRO-2235); the file- and folder-row context menus carry New note / dated note / folder / dated folder ("New note" is born from the target folder's `.template.md`, YAZ-2290 E3; what is typed inline is the note's or the folder's TITLE, free text, and the name on disk is built from it, YAZ-2420 D6, D20 — where the vault does not use IDs it IS the name, YAZ-2523; `EntryKind` is `file` | `dir`; TOMBSTONE: "New base" and its `base` `EntryKind` were deleted with the format in YAZ-844), on a FOLDER row Open (YAZ-2290 D3), "Review this folder" (YAZ-2322) and "Add note shortcut" (YAZ-2290 D2), on a note row the review toggle (YAZ-2322), Rename (files Links E1 GRO-2194, folders E1b GRO-2241 — inline input replacing the row label, prefilled with the TITLE of a note or a folder, whose commit is a title edit (YAZ-2420 D16; where the vault does not use IDs, a rename of its name, YAZ-2523), and with the full name of any other file, Enter commits / Esc-blur cancels; no hotkey v1 — F2 is a polish note), Copy path, Open in ▸ New window (D2, GRO-2168 — context menu only since I3; a flyout leaf since YAZ-1674) — TOMBSTONE: the type-conditional "New ▸ <type>" submenu and "New type…" (`NewTypeDialog`) were deleted with the type system in YAZ-836; Delete (GRO-2272 — opens ConfirmDelete, never deletes directly) and Open in ▸ Reveal in Finder (GRO-2274 — a flyout leaf since YAZ-1674; file rows, folder rows AND blank space, where it targets the vault ROOT — of a window with one vault; with two or more, blank space is no one vault's and each vault's own row carries these, YAZ-2602 S10, S13); the menu is DATA → sections → render since YAZ-1674 (🔒 D8): `menuSections.ts` holds every gating rule as one small function returning a `MenuItem` or null and the pure `buildMenuSections(targets, handlers)`; `ContextMenu` takes `{ x, y, sections, onClose }` and only draws — a `role="group"` per NON-EMPTY section with a CSS rule between adjacent groups, one button per item whose only text child is the bare label (the ⌘X / ⌘C / ⌘V / ⌘⇧C hints are drawn from `data-hint`, so `textContent` and the accessible name stay what four test files pin); menu ORDER is SEVEN groups (🔒 D7 as amended; YAZ-2249 splits Create in two): [Show in sidebar (one row of Search, Focus or Favorites — YAZ-2638 🔒 D1, D3) · Open · Open N in new tabs · Add to focus / Remove from focus] · [Cut · Copy · Paste · Copy N paths · Copy path] · [New note · New folder] · [New dated note · New dated folder] · [Review this folder · Turn review on/off · Rename] · [Add to favorites · Add note shortcut · Open in ▸ · Add vault to this window ▸ (blank space only, YAZ-2602 D2)] · [Delete — or, on a shortcut row, Remove shortcut; on a vault row, Remove from this window (YAZ-2602 D7) — the group where a row LEAVES, one item of it at most] — empty groups are skipped (on a single file row of the Files tab the first group holds the focus toggle alone, YAZ-2619 R6 — before, that row started with the clipboard group, as blank space still does; on a row of Search, Focus or Favorites "Show in sidebar" leads that group and the menu, YAZ-2638 S27, S34), "Open N in new tabs" still leads the whole menu while "Copy N paths" moved down beside "Copy path" (a deliberate loosening of YAZ-1337 🔒 D5), "Copy path" is the one copy item a row has and copies the plain path (YAZ-2420 D7, D31 — a note's id is in its file name), and Delete is LAST and alone (GRO-2272 `C1a-`, destructive-last, pinned by a test in Sidebar.test.tsx); "Open in ▸" is the one PARENT item (`MenuParent.children`, one level deep): its flyout holds [New window · VS Code · Default app] · [Reveal in Finder] with every child's own gate intact, opens on hover, click, ArrowRight or Enter, one flyout at a time, closes when the pointer enters a different top-level item or on ArrowLeft / a first Escape, flips to the LEFT of the parent when it would spill off the window's right edge, and wears its chevron as a masked SVG in CSS (never text); blank space also carries Copy path targeting the vault root (GRO-2273) — with one vault; with two or more its menu is "Add vault to this window ▸" alone (YAZ-2602 S10: `MenuTargets.targetDir` and the root fallbacks are `null` there, and `addVaults` / `removeVault` are the two targets the vault items read); each menu item resolves its OWN target through `MenuTargets` — no item derives visibility from another's value (GRO-2296); ConfirmDelete is the in-app delete sheet (never a native dialog; focus starts on Cancel, `deleteConfirmMessage` owns the copy, counts come from the loaded tree + vault index so the delete path makes no extra fetch); menu and submenu clamp to the viewport edges (GRO-2204); RenameInline is the CreateInline-idiom input for Rename; dragging a FILE row onto a folder row (or the root header) MOVES it there (E1b — the TabBar HTML5-drag idiom, dropDir highlight, same rename pipeline; no multi-select, no folder dragged to a new place on disk — Future; a TOP row of a tab that reorders drags along that tab's list instead and moves nothing on disk, YAZ-2631 D3 — "The sidebar lenses": Drag in the tree; Cut ⌘X / Paste ⌘V, YAZ-1674, moves a multi-selection and folders through the same rename pipeline; a drag moves inside ONE vault — a drop on a folder or a vault row of a different vault is refused with "To move between vaults, use cut and paste", and with two or more vaults the header is no drop target, each vault's own row being its root's, YAZ-2602 R11, S14); hooks/ holds what YAZ-2202 moved out of Sidebar.tsx as-is (YAZ-2131 🔒 D10): useVaultTree (`useVaultTree(vaults, closedVaults, …)` — since YAZ-2602 ONE tree per vault, each with its own feed, first read and watcher refresh for as long as the vault is in the window; `forest`, what Files draws — one vault → its tree, two or more → one folder row per vault; `vaultRows`, `rootOf(path)`, `openTo(file)`; the one `expanded` list split per vault on its way to the store; the focus list and `reorderFocus`; `favoritesByRoot`, each vault's favorites by its root, `favoritePaths`, every favorite of the window in the tab's order, and `saveFavoriteOrder`, the writer of a drag on that tab (YAZ-2631 D1); the missing-file checks, asked of the tree of the vault that holds the file — and `useSameList`), rowGestures (multi-select — a selection may hold rows of two vaults, and a vault that leaves the window ends it — the two row drags, `useTreeDrag`: a file onto a folder, and ONE reorder state for the tab that is shown (YAZ-2631 D3) — the file clipboard, inline create and rename, each judged by the vault that holds the row: `vaultOf`) and useSidebarSearch (`useSidebarSearch(vaults, tree, …)`: the Search tab's query, results over every vault, folds and highlighted row — kept while a different tab shows, when it does no work — the caret in the bar on ⌘K and on a click on the tab, and Esc in the bar, which leaves the tab; YAZ-2638 D2)

Named basesFixture/makeBasesFixture until YAZ-861; both fixtures lost their `.base` files with the format (YAZ-844)

For how far the folder specs can be trusted, see the note under the legend of `docs/REGRESSION.md`

Import from shared: `import type { TreeResponse } from '@shared/types'`.

## Supported file capabilities (YAZ-1289, locked)

`shared/fileKind.ts` is the one case-insensitive classifier. The final filename suffix decides the kind; extensionless names, leading-dot-only names such as `.json`, compound names ending in an unknown suffix such as `data.json.gz`, and every unlisted extension are unsupported. Existing dot-entry and `node_modules` exclusions apply before classification.

| Kind | Extensions | Files view / open | Read | Write / create | Semantic index | Markdown link |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `markdown` | `.md`, `.markdown` | Visible; editable Markdown editor | `readFile`, 10 MiB maximum; preserves the existing Node UTF-8 replacement-character and leading-BOM behavior | Yes: create, edit, autosave, frontmatter and Markdown-only feature writes | Yes: the only `IndexRecord` kind and therefore the only content-search, folder-row, properties, views, aliases and backlinks source | Semantic wikilinks; existing missing-note create-on-click behavior applies |
| `text` | `.txt`, `.log`, `.csv`, `.tsv`, `.json`, `.jsonc`, `.jsonl`, `.ndjson`, `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.toml`, `.ini`, `.cfg`, `.conf`, `.xml`, `.env`, `.properties`, `.lock`, `.js`, `.jsx`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.py`, `.rb`, `.go`, `.rs`, `.java`, `.kt`, `.kts`, `.c`, `.h`, `.cc`, `.cpp`, `.hpp`, `.cs`, `.swift`, `.php`, `.sh`, `.bash`, `.zsh`, `.fish`, `.ps1`, `.sql`, `.html`, `.htm`, `.css`, `.scss`, `.sass`, `.less`, `.vue`, `.svelte`, `.graphql`, `.gql`, `.mdx`, `.rst`, `.tex` | Visible; selectable, copyable, read-only monospaced viewer with exact whitespace and two-axis overflow | `readFile`, 10 MiB maximum; fatal UTF-8 decode, UTF-8 BOM preserved, any malformed UTF-8 or NUL rejected passively | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; no embeds, headings/blocks, aliases, create-on-click or semantic record |
| `pdf` | `.pdf` | Visible; Chromium's native PDF viewer in a read-only frame | Dedicated `readPdf` returns `Uint8Array`, 50 MiB maximum; never text, base64 or the asset pipe | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; the same no-semantic/no-create rules as text |
| `image` | `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif`, `.webp`, `.avif`, `.bmp` | Visible with its full filename; centered, read-only static canvas viewer | Dedicated exact-path `readImage` returns `Uint8Array` + allowlisted MIME, 50 MiB maximum; never base64, fuzzy lookup or the asset pipe | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; the same no-semantic/no-create rules as text/PDF |
| unsupported | every other suffix, including `.svg` | Hidden from Files and refused by supported-file deep links | Never through `readFile`, `readPdf` or `readImage` | Never through the note mutation boundaries | Never | None |

Every supported text/PDF/image target can use the normal tab, background-tab, restore, reveal, delete, open-in-new-window and open-in-VS-Code paths. Rename/move and external-rename repair must preserve what the existing bytes actually encode: Markdown and text retain their same-`FileKind` behavior, PDF stays `.pdf`, images keep the same extension case-insensitively except that `.jpg` and `.jpeg` are equivalent spellings. A rename never transcodes, so `.png` → `.jpg` and every other cross-encoding transition fail before disk mutation. Markdown remains the only creatable or writable kind.

Text, PDF and image transport uses one bounded regular-file snapshot: pre-read `fstat`, reject an oversized or non-regular file before allocation, allocate/read at most the initial size plus one byte (never more than the applicable cap plus one), then post-read `fstat`. A size/mtime drift or a byte count inconsistent with the snapshot fails `IO_ERROR` rather than returning mismatched bytes and metadata. Text above 10 MiB and PDF/image above 50 MiB fail `TOO_LARGE`.

PDF rendering uses an `application/pdf` Blob URL over the dedicated bytes. The viewer revokes the owned URL exactly once on replacement, path change and unmount. Electron enables Chromium PDF plugins while retaining `contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, and `nodeIntegration: false`; there is no PDF.js, annotation/editor layer, custom pagination, base64 conversion or asset-pipe reuse.

Raster rendering gives the dedicated bytes and allowlisted MIME to `createImageBitmap` once, sizes a canvas to the decoded dimensions, paints only the default frame, and immediately closes the bitmap exactly once. Animated GIF/WebP therefore display their static default/first frame; no timer or animation loop exists. Same-path external refresh keeps the last good canvas until replacement paints, while a path change hides old pixels immediately and stale decode completions can never paint. Decode/bridge failures remain passive. SVG is deliberately deferred: it is structured XML that can carry script and external references, so support needs an explicit sanitization and no-network policy rather than entry through this raster boundary.

View-only wikilinks are backed by a lightweight catalog flattened from the Files tree, never by `IndexRecord[]`. The catalog refreshes only for supported-file/directory structural watch events, and reads the tree itself only when no other consumer of the window's one tree feed (`lib/treeFeed.ts`: same-turn calls share a request, later ones share one trailing request, every answer reaches every listener — YAZ-2191) has read it since; content changes reload an already-open viewer without rebuilding semantic state. Resolution uses exact root-relative paths or case-insensitive basenames, with the shallowest path and then deterministic path order winning duplicates; autocomplete emits the shortest unambiguous spelling with the real extension. Recognized explicit-extension targets always take this navigation-only branch—even before the catalog is ready or when the target is missing—so they can never fall through to Markdown creation. Missing/removed targets and view-only heading/block suffixes remain unresolved and produce passive notices. `|display text` changes presentation only. View-only files produce no backlinks, properties, aliases, searchable content, folder-view rows, semantic records or writes; rename-reference repair reads/writes only Markdown source notes using the pre-rename view-only catalog.

A root switch synchronously clears the old navigation resolver, catalog targets and merged picker rows while the new tree is pending. The semantic source object keeps its identity, but an old vault's catalog can never compose with the new vault's semantic snapshot.

## Bridge API (`window.yaseenDocs`, locked in GRO-2153 — Desktop A1)

**The table** (YAZ-2131 🔒 D9, YAZ-2200): every door below is one line of `CONTRACT` in `shared/ipc.ts` — channel, argument and answer types, arity. The preload builds `window.yaseenDocs` from it, main registers `handle(CONTRACT.x.y, fn)` (typed from the table), and `client/src/api.ts` wraps it, so a new door is one table line plus its handler, and a missing or mistyped one fails to compile. The specials stay hand-written in the preload (`SPECIAL`): `watch` (multiplexed by subscription id), `window.onFlush` (the `app:flush` / `app:flushed` handshake) and `menu.onCopyAs` / `onPasteAs` (with their `menu:copy-text` / `menu:paste-text-fallback` fallback invokes); `state.setFolds` / `setBaseGroups` send a copy of their key list and `properties.onChange` hands its listener the payload's `properties`, as they always did. `desktop/src/preload/surface.test.ts` pins the whole surface byte for byte; `desktop/src/contract.test.ts` pins that every door is wired exactly once. The renderer reaches main only through `api`, except `clipboardPaste.ts` / `clipboardCopyOut.ts`, which subscribe with optional chaining so they also run outside Electron.

The desktop app has no HTTP server (GRO-2095 D2): the renderer is sandboxed and calls the typed bridge the preload installs; `ipcMain.handle` answers from the main process (GRO-2157 deleted the server and every HTTP path). All paths are absolute POSIX paths; no jail — any absolute path is allowed. Every method rejects with a plain `BridgeError { code, message, path?, mtime? }` (`BridgeErrorCode` in `shared/types.ts`, plus `CONFLICT` which carries the disk mtime); the client's `api.ts` wraps it in `BridgeRequestError` (same fields) so the renderer can `instanceof` it.

| Bridge | Notes |
|---|---|
| `tree(root)` | `TreeResponse` — recursive; every regular file appears (YAZ-1577 D1) carrying its preview `kind` — exact `FileKind` for Markdown/text/PDF/image, `null` for a file with no in-app viewer, which the renderer hands to the OS default app; every dir shows, files or not (GRO-2022); dot-entries, `node_modules` and crash-left atomic-write tmps (`isAtomicTmp`: `<name>.tmp-<12 hex>`, never deleted, YAZ-2179) are skipped; dirs before files, each sorted case-insensitive. Missing / not a dir → `NOT_FOUND` / `NOT_A_DIRECTORY`. ONE walk per root at a time in main (YAZ-2191): a call during a walk joins the single trailing walk queued behind it, so every answer post-dates its call; the sidebar and search read at once on the first structural event after a quiet spell, then coalesce a burst into ONE trailing read 100 ms after its last event (`lib/leadingTrailing.ts`, YAZ-2240); `ready` reads at once |
| `readFile(path)` | `FileResponse` — Markdown or approved view-only text, 10 MiB maximum. Markdown preserves the legacy Node UTF-8 decode behavior; view-only text uses fatal UTF-8 decoding, preserves a UTF-8 BOM, and rejects NUL. PDF, image and unsupported extensions → `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION` |
| `readHeads(paths)` | `(FileHead \| null)[]`, in the order asked (YAZ-2648 D6, `desktop/src/main/fs/heads.ts`) — `FileHead { path, mtime, text }`: the top of a note for a page of the tab board. `.md`/`.markdown` regular files only; main reads `HEAD_READ_BYTES` (8192) off the start of the file at most, whatever its size — never the whole file — decodes it leniently (a read that stops inside a character drops that character), takes the frontmatter block off (`splitFrontmatter`; a block still open where the read stops gives `text: ''`) and answers the first `HEAD_CHARS` (600) of the body, never half of a surrogate pair. A path that fails — a deleted file, a folder (one may be named `Notes.md`), a file that is no note, a relative path — answers `null` and never fails the rest; more than 256 paths: the rest are `null`. A value that is not a string array → `BAD_REQUEST`. The renderer makes ONE call for every open note when the board opens (`client/src/tabs/useTabHeads.ts`) |
| `readPdf(path)` | `PdfResponse { path, data: Uint8Array, mtime, size }` — `.pdf` only, 50 MiB maximum, dedicated binary IPC for the native viewer; never base64 and never routed through `readFile`/`readAsset` |
| `readImage(path)` | `ImageResponse { path, data: Uint8Array, mime, mtime, size }` — exact absolute path only; approved raster extensions only; MIME comes from the single extension allowlist; 50 MiB maximum; never fuzzy, base64 or routed through `readFile`/`readAsset` |
| `writeFile({ path, content, expectedMtime? })` | `FileWriteResponse { path, mtime, size }` — `.md`/`.markdown` only; atomic write (`<name>.tmp-<rand>`, fsynced, + `rename`, YAZ-2177); parent dir must exist; if `expectedMtime` is given and the disk mtime differs → rejects `CONFLICT` with the disk `mtime` and nothing is written |
| `createDir(path \| { path, title? })` / `createFile(path \| { path, content?, id? })` | `CreateDirResponse { path }` / `CreateFileResponse { path, mtime, size, id? }` — parent must exist (else `NOT_FOUND`); `.md`/`.markdown` only (else `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`; TOMBSTONE: the `.base` branch, which seeded a minimal table view, went with the format in YAZ-844); the object form's `content` (Bible B, GRO-2202) is the file's initial content and lands in the same write — content-at-create for a note born in a folder from its template and seed (YAZ-2290 E3), no create-then-write race. 🔒 In a vault that uses IDs EVERY note is BORN with its id (YAZ-2293 D3 — see "Note ids"): this is the one door all creation goes through, so the frontmatter `id` is set here, in that same write — the request's `id` (for a caller that had to know it before the file existed: it took it from `mintNoteId` below; anything that is not a number ID with the vault's CURRENT letters, an old 12-character ID included → `BAD_REQUEST`, nothing created — YAZ-2677 R3) or the vault's next number, taken from the door and saved BEFORE the note is written (R18: a create that then fails leaves a gap, S31) — appended after the seed's own keys, and an `id` already in the seed is REPLACED (a template's is never copied). A create with no `content` therefore holds only its id block (`---`, `id: <id>`, `---`) — never zero bytes — and `size` is the created file's real byte length; the response's `id` is the one written. A seed whose frontmatter does not parse is created exactly as given, with no id in the file and none in the response: the id never blocks a creation, and the index gives the note one once the YAML is fixed ("Note ids": The sweep). Durable create (fsynced `<name>.tmp-<rand>` + `link` to claim the name, then the same bytes renamed in over it — YAZ-2380: macOS reports nothing when a file left on a hard-linked inode is later deleted, so the note would stay in the tree and the index; or a fsynced `wx` write where the volume has no hard links, YAZ-2177), never overwrites: exists → `ALREADY_EXISTS`. 🔒 A FOLDER is born with its id too (D13): `createDir` — the one door "New folder", "New dated folder" and `ensureFolder` go through — makes the directory, then durably creates `<folder>/.folder.md` holding the vault's next number as its `id` (YAZ-2677 S33) and, when the object form carries one, the folder's `title` (YAZ-2420 D6 — a `title` that is not a string → `BAD_REQUEST`, nothing created); the bare path gives a file holding only its id (`---`, `id: <id>`, `---`); a folder whose file could not be written stands without one, and the sweep gives it one. Neither door builds a NAME: the caller hands in the path, which for a note or a folder made in the app is the one built from its title (D3, D6 — `views/scaffold.ts` `createNote` / `folderPath`, "Folders are the pages": Births). 🔒 All of that by the vault's kind (YAZ-2523 V3 — "Two kinds of vault"): each handler asks `doorFor` once — a vault of the calling window holds the path, the most specific one, and THAT vault said yes (YAZ-2602 S78): it then holds that vault's door, and else null — and where it did not, `createFile` writes the content as given (none is zero bytes) and answers no `id`, `createDir` makes the directory alone, and a request carrying an `id` or a `title` is refused `BAD_REQUEST` (`this vault does not use IDs`), nothing made. A create is never an answer about IDs: it turns nothing on |
| `mintNoteId(path)` | `string \| null` (`fs:mint-note-id`, YAZ-2677 🔒 D4, R17) — THE DOOR for the front end: the next ID of the vault of the calling window that holds `path` ("Note ids": The door), for a caller that must know it before its note exists — `createNote` (the file name holds it), the `[[` picker's Create row (the link holds it) and `folderId` (a folder's first shortcut or value). The number is saved in this Mac's count file BEFORE it is answered (R18), so one that is then not used is a gap and no ID is ever given two times; one request at a time for a vault (S32). `null` — and nothing written — where no vault of the window holds the path, or that vault does not use IDs. A path that is not absolute → `NOT_ABSOLUTE` / `BAD_REQUEST`. The ID goes back in `createFile`'s `id`. |
| `ids.set(root, enabled, letters?)` / `ids.check(root)` | `void` / `string` (`ids:set`, `ids:check`, YAZ-2677 🔒 D2, D7 — "Note ids": Two notes with one ID, Which Mac gives a number). Both act on a vault OF THE CALLING WINDOW: any other folder → `BAD_REQUEST`, nothing done. **`ids.set`** is the switch in Settings: main saves the vault's answer in its `ids.json` and, with a yes, its ID letters in capitals (`letters` that are not 2 to 5 letters A to Z, or an `enabled` that is no boolean → `BAD_REQUEST`). The read and the write are ONE step in main (`live.ts` `saveIdsAnswer`, one save at a time for a vault), so every key the save does not change stays (R10, R12 — `letters`, `was`, anything else) and two windows that save at one moment each keep the other's change; the renderer never reads-then-writes `ids.json` itself (TOMBSTONE: `saveIds` in `useVaultScope.ts` did, through `vaultConfig.read` + `write`). A file that is not valid JSON is not written over → `INVALID_CONFIG`. The write goes through `writeConfig`, so every window hears `vaultConfig:changed` and refetches its index. A YES is the user asking on THIS Mac (R32, S64): every note and folder is given its number now (`giveIdsNow`), whichever Mac is the vault's first — not awaited, so a vault of many notes does not hold the switch. A Mac that only SEES the yes arrive by the sync waits (S65). **`ids.check`** is "Check for duplicates" (S55 to S57): the notes that share an ID are settled now, on this Mac (`checkDuplicates`), and the answer is ONE line for the row to show — `No duplicates.`; the notice of a fixed clash (`YAZ-101 was used on two Macs. "Bar" is now YAZ-102. 1 link updated.`); `Fixed 1: "Seed" is now YAZ-101.` for a copy; or, where ANOTHER Mac must fix it, `YAZ-101 is on two notes. The Mac that made "Bar" fixes it when its app runs.` — and then nothing was written. It gives no number to a note that has none. A vault that does not use IDs → `BAD_REQUEST` |
| `ids.state(root)` / `ids.reletter(root, letters)` / `ids.backfill(root)` | `IdsState` each (`ids:state`, `ids:reletter`, `ids:backfill`, YAZ-2677 🔒 D5, D6 — "Note ids": Change letters, Give old IDs numbers). Each acts on a vault OF THE CALLING WINDOW that uses IDs: any other folder, and a vault that does not → `BAD_REQUEST`, nothing done. `IdsState` is `{ letters, notes, stale, old }`, counted by main from its live index, a folder's settings file like a note: the vault's letters now (the default of R11 while `ids.json` holds none), the notes with a number ID, the notes that still have letters the vault had before (in their own ID, in an ID they name, or in their built file name), and the notes with an old 12-character ID. **`ids.state`** reads it for the rows "ID letters" and "Old IDs". **`ids.reletter`** is "Change letters" (`live.ts` `changeLetters`): `letters` that are not 2 to 5 letters A to Z → `BAD_REQUEST`; FIRST `ids.json` takes `letters` in capitals and the letters of before go to the front of `was` (the same one-save-at-a-time step as `ids.set`, every other key kept), THEN each file follows (`reletter.ts`). The letters the vault already has write nothing into `ids.json` and only finish a change that stopped ("Finish"). **`ids.backfill`** is "Give old IDs numbers" (`giveOldIdsNumbers`). Both resolve when the change is DONE, to the state after it (main puts what it wrote into its index first, so the counts are not those of before), and each file that takes a new name takes the rename handler's downstream AT ONCE (`afterRename`: `store.renamePath`, the favorites repair, `file:renamed` to every window) — its open tab and its favorite follow (S86), also when the change stops later. Image events refresh Files/view-only navigation and an open viewer but never trigger an index scan. A bad root yields one `error` event and no subscription; an unsubscribe that lands before the root check finishes cancels the subscribe (YAZ-2178). Client: `useIndex(root, watch)` fetches once per root and refetches (300 ms debounce) on markdown `add`/`change`/`unlink`, `unlinkDir` and `ready` events, and at once when the vault's `ids.json` changes |

Null before the first `index(root)` build for the root and again after idle eviction — the E1c detector reads it AFTER the first index snapshot. Honest miss semantics: trust the three lists only when `cacheStatus === 'hit'` — on `miss`/`corrupt`/`version-mismatch` they are empty and the status says why |

Otherwise `file.ts`'s semantics exactly: atomic write (`<name>.tmp-<rand>` + `rename`); `expectedMtime` given and the disk mtime differs → `CONFLICT` with the disk `mtime` and nothing written; `create: true` uses `createFile`'s durable create and **never overwrites** — an existing target is `ALREADY_EXISTS`. Content above 10 MiB → `TOO_LARGE`. a vault named twice folds to one, compared with the trailing slash off (`normalizeRoots`); THE LIST LEADS — with `roots` in the patch `root` becomes `roots[0]` (`[]` → `null`), whatever `root` the patch names; a patch with `root` ALONE (the path of before YAZ-2602 — `storage.setRoot`) keeps the list when it names the vault the window is already on, and otherwise makes the list that one vault (`[root]`, or `[]` for `null`). `storage.setRoots` sends `roots` and, when a vault left, the `focusList` without that vault's items, in the same patch; a long list is NOT cut here: the renderer cuts on an add and the store on load) — each absent = untouched; `tabs` and `file` may name a DIRECTORY, a folder tab (YAZ-2290 D3); `open({ root, file })` = an independent new window (GRO-2160 — the sidebar context menu's "Open in new window", D2 GRO-2168, on the vault that holds the file, alone, YAZ-2602 S33; | `window.openSet/saveSet/renameSet/removeSet` | Saved sets of vaults (YAZ-2602 🔒 D8 — the user reads "workspace"; the code says `vaultSets` / `VaultSet`, because `workspace` already names the tabs code; "Multi-window": Workspaces, "App state": `vaultSets`). An `id` or a `name` that is not a string → `BAD_REQUEST`; what it names is the store's to decide. `openSet(id)` → `OpenSetResult { opened, missing }` (S65, S66 — the manager's `openVaultSet`; any window may ask): each folder of the set is probed; the vaults that are LEFT, in the saved order, are what opens, and the folders that are gone come back in `missing` as absolute paths — the saved set keeps them, a folder can come back (a drive that is not connected); every live window whose vaults are EXACTLY the ones left (`sameVaults`: the order does not count) is raised, as `openRecent` raises, and with none ONE new window opens on them, on the first vault's `folders[root].lastFile`; either way the set becomes the last used (`touchVaultSet`); no folder left, or an unknown `id`, opens nothing and changes nothing → `opened: false`. It answers an object, not D8's boolean: the window that asked needs the names to say which folder is gone (the case, S66, wins over the diff). `saveSet(name)` → `boolean` (S63): saves the CALLING window's own vaults — the renderer never sends a list — under the cleaned name; a set of that name is REPLACED, keeping its `id` (S69); `false` = the store refused: fewer than two vaults, an empty name, or a NEW name while `MAX_VAULT_SETS` (20) sets exist (R12, S68). `renameSet(id, name)` → `boolean` (S67; not in D8's diff): `false` = an unknown id, an empty name, or a name a DIFFERENT set has. `removeSet(id)` → `void` (S67): forgets the entry; the folders are never touched; an unknown `id` is a no-op. Each change reaches every window through `state:changed`, like any state write |
| `menu.onOpenFolder/onOpenRoot/onSwitchVault` | pushes from the application menu to the focused window's renderer (B3, GRO-2161): File › Open Folder… (⌘⇧O) → run the pick-folder flow; File › Open Recent chose `path` → App's `openVault` (YAZ-1914: Welcome switches in place, a vault window hands it to `window.openRecent`); File › Switch Vault… (⌘O, YAZ-1767) → toggle the sidebar header's vault switcher, un-collapsing the sidebar first (see "Menus & shortcuts") |
| `menu.onSettings` | push from Yaseen Docs › Settings… (⌘,) to the focused window's renderer (`menu:settings`, YAZ-1679): open the settings dialog — the same one the sidebar cog opens (see "App state": `settings`) |
| `link.onOpenFile/onNotice/ready` | pushes from deep-link routing (E1, GRO-2171): `onOpenFile(path)` = a link resolved to this window (the path is guaranteed inside one of its vaults — YAZ-2602 D6); `onNotice(message)` = a link could not be opened — show it unobtrusively, never a dialog (see "Links"). `ready()` (invoke `link:ready`, YAZ-2589 A2) = this window's two listeners are on. WHY: a page that is still loading hears no push, and at a launch EVERY window is loading when the waiting links are handled — so main HOLDS each link push for a window (`heldLinks` in `windows.ts`, by `webContents.id`, from the moment the window is created: one a launch brought back, and one a link itself opened) until that window's renderer calls `ready()` — `useLinkEvents`, right after it puts the two listeners on, in every window (the Welcome window too) — then sends what it held, in order; from then on a push to that window goes out at once, and a second `ready()`, or one from a sender the manager does not know, does nothing. The window is raised at once; only the words wait. A window that closes first takes its held pushes with it. `app:flush` is never held |
| `file.rename({ oldPath, newPath })` / `file.onRenamed` | In-app file/folder rename or move (Links E1/E1b): validates the target, never overwrites, and for files requires a transition that cannot misrepresent unchanged bytes (same kind for Markdown/text/PDF; same image extension case-insensitively, with `.jpg`↔`.jpeg` allowed). It repairs every stored main/right path plus roots — each vault of each window's `roots`, YAZ-2602 S48 — recents, folder state and the saved sets of vaults (S71) in one commit, then broadcasts `{ oldPath, newPath, kind }`. A vault root of the calling window — ANY of the vaults it shows — is refused `BAD_REQUEST` (S47), while another window whose vault is a subfolder of this one follows the rename. Each renderer carries dirty Markdown editor buffers and retires old controllers before `useWorkspace` remaps current paths, retained mounts and histories. The normal watcher unlink+add echo heals the appropriate index/catalog feed; no extra push or watcher exists. |
| `file.retitle({ path, title })` | A title edit (YAZ-2420 D16; `fs/retitle.ts`) — the ONE operation behind the page title, the sidebar's Rename and a table's Name cell, for a Markdown note or a folder. The `title:` line is written — a note's in its own frontmatter, a folder's in its `.folder.md`, created when it is missing — THEN the entry is renamed, through `renameFile`, to the name built from that title (`shared/noteName.ts`: a note's `<kebab-title>-<id>.md`, a folder's the kebab-case alone). The title goes FIRST, so a rename that fails leaves the title right and the name stale, and the next title edit fixes the name. A note with no id is given one first (D29, the sweep's `giveId`); one that could take none has its title written and keeps its name. Answers `RenameFileResponse`: a built name that is the one on disk moves nothing — `newPath` is `oldPath`, no store repair and no push, the index reads the new title off the watcher — and a path that changed takes `file.rename`'s downstream verbatim (`store.renamePath`, the favorites repair, `file:renamed`). Refused with nothing written: an empty title, a vault root of the calling window (any vault it shows), a path that no vault of the calling window holds (`no vault of this window holds that path` — the title rules are those of the vault that holds the note, YAZ-2602 S78), and properties that do not parse (D24) → `BAD_REQUEST`; a folder title with no letter or digit → `BAD_REQUEST`, and one whose name a neighbour holds → `ALREADY_EXISTS` (D25 — a folder's name has no id to fall back on); a file that is not Markdown → `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`; and every call for a path whose vault does not use IDs → `BAD_REQUEST` `this vault does not use IDs` (YAZ-2523 V3 — a name changes there by `file.rename`, "Two kinds of vault") |
| `file.repairRename({ oldPath, newPath })` | Store/tab repair for a rename that ALREADY happened on disk (Links E1c, GRO-2242 — the confirmed-banner path, see "Links": Rename → External renames). There is no fs work: the handler validates the claim — `newPath` must EXIST (its stat derives `kind`), `oldPath` must NOT (`BAD_REQUEST` — a live old path means the hypothesis was wrong; refuse, never guess), the same encoding-preserving and dot-dir rules mirror `rename` — then runs the SAME `store.renamePath` repair and pushes the SAME `file:renamed { oldPath, newPath, kind }` to every window, so tabs, viewer continuity and title/hash reuse the E1 downstream unchanged. No vault-root guard (nothing moves; a window rooted at an externally renamed folder is exactly what the repair heals) |
| `file.delete({ path })` / `file.onDeleted` | Sends the file/folder to system Trash only; no permanent fallback. Validation rejects hidden infrastructure and a vault root of the calling window (any vault it shows, YAZ-2602 S47). One store repair removes affected main/right paths and related recents/folder state — and the folder from each saved set of vaults, a set with fewer than two vaults left going with it (S71) — while leaving `window.root` and `window.roots` for the existing root-missing probe (S49), then broadcasts `{ path, kind }`. Each renderer retires matching autosave controllers before `useWorkspace` removes ownership, preventing a dirty unmount from recreating the trashed file. Referencing notes remain byte-identical. |
| `file.clip({ paths, op })` / `file.clipState()` / `file.paste({ targetDir })` / `file.onClipChanged` | Cut / Copy / Paste of files and folders (YAZ-1674). ONE app-wide clipboard lives in main (`fileClip.ts`, 🔒 D1): session-only, never persisted, so a paste in ANY window takes what ANY window clipped — within a vault or across two. `clip` replaces it with the ORDERED selection (absolute, de-duplicated) and the verb (`copy` \| `cut`, 🔒 D2); nothing on disk is touched or stat'ed, a stale entry is reported per entry BY the paste. Every change is pushed to every window as `clip:changed` (`FileClipState`: `{ count, op, paths }` or null — the count and verb label the menu's Paste, the paths, as stored, let a window say what pasting a Cut would move, D21), and `clipState` is the catch-up read for a window that mounted after the clip. `paste` lands the clipboard INTO `targetDir` (must exist, never created — `NOT_FOUND` / `NOT_A_DIRECTORY`; empty clipboard → `BAD_REQUEST`), per entry in clipboard order, one bad entry never stopping the rest (`PasteResponse { pasted[], failed[] }`, 🔒 D3): a COPY is `copyEntry` (`fs/copy.ts`, never over anything, folders whole, hidden dirs inside included, 🔒 D4), and where the target's vault uses IDs a page's copy is its own page at once (YAZ-2420 D21): a NOTE's holds a fresh `id` and `title: <title> copy` under the name built from both, so two copies of one note differ by their ids; a FOLDER's is titled `<title> copy` under the kebab-case of that — counting on, `<title> copy 2`…, while a neighbour holds the name, a folder's name carrying no id — its `.folder.md` holding a fresh `id` (made when the folder had none), every note in it born again under its own title with a fresh id and a rebuilt name, every folder inside copied the same way under the name and title it had, and last its notes carry their values for the original to the copy's id (`carryFolderValues` — "Note ids": The sweep), so the copy's table is not empty. Each file lands holding its fresh id, so the sweep meets no second holder of an original's. Any other file — a note whose properties do not parse, and EVERY entry pasted into a vault that does not use IDs (YAZ-2523 V3: `doorFor` on `targetDir` answers null; a folder keeps its whole name, `v1.2` → `v1.2 copy`) — is `fs.cp` (`errorOnExist`, timestamps kept) under Finder's next free name (`Photo.png` → `Photo copy.png` → `Photo copy 2.png`, counting on from an existing ` copy N`, last-extension split). Either way NO store repair and NO push (nothing moved — the watcher's add echo fills the tree); a CUT is the rename pipeline above, verbatim, per entry (`renameFile` → `store.renamePath` → `file:renamed`), so a clash is `ALREADY_EXISTS`, an entry already in `targetDir` is skipped silently, and `EXDEV` (another volume) fails `IO_ERROR` "cannot move across disks; copy it instead" rather than a copy-then-delete nobody asked for. A cut pastes ONCE (the clipboard clears when ≥1 entry landed; an all-failed cut keeps it); a copy pastes again and again. Guards borrowed from rename/remove where they transfer: a hidden source (`isSkipped`) and a folder into itself are `BAD_REQUEST`. No vault-root guard: Cut/Copy are only ever offered on rows. NOT in v1: the OS/Finder clipboard (Future YAZ-1707) and carrying a note's assets across vaults (Future YAZ-1708) |
| `shell.reveal({ path })` | Reveal in Finder (GRO-2274 — **VS Code parity is the locked reference for this issue**). `shell.showItemInFolder`: the entry is selected **in its parent**, for files, folders and the vault root alike — deliberately NOT `shell.openPath`, which would open a folder's contents; the verb is "Reveal", and VS Code and Obsidian both behave this way. The path is stat'd first so a stale row (deleted or moved externally) rejects `NOT_FOUND` — `showItemInFolder` returns `void` and silently does nothing for a missing path, which reads to the user as a broken menu item. Read-only, so unlike `file.delete` / `file.rename` there is deliberately **no dot-entry guard and no extension guard**: any absolute path the app can already show is fair game, matching the "no jail" posture above. No store repair and no broadcast — nothing changes — but still enveloped so the `NOT_FOUND` reaches the renderer as a passive notice |
| `shell.openVsCode({ path })` | Open in VS Code (YAZ-963). `shell.openExternal('vscode://file/<path>')`, every segment percent-encoded — the OS deep link, **never a spawned process** (no install-name or PATH coupling; the OS picks which VS Code answers). Same read-only posture and the same stat: a stale path rejects `NOT_FOUND` for the passive notice. Offered on every sidebar row, blank space (= the vault root) and the tab's menu. |
| `shell.openLink({ href, sourcePath? })` | Standard Markdown link routing (YAZ-1309). Main is the only trust boundary: `http`/`https`/`mailto`/`tel`/`ftp` are canonicalized and sent to `shell.openExternal`; absolute and note-relative `file:` targets resolve from `sourcePath`, lose URL query/fragment, and go to `shell.openPath`, so the OS default app decides. Fragment-only links stay renderer-local. Empty/malformed requests, non-local file URLs, and every other protocol reject before an OS side effect. Every `window.open` is denied by `setWindowOpenHandler`; allowlisted external popup URLs are rerouted through this same function, so Electron never creates a child browser. |
| `vaultConfig.read/write/onChange` | Vault-local config in `<root>/.yaseendocs/` (Desktop J, GRO-2188) — the `.obsidian/` analogue: travels with the vault. `read(root, name)` → parsed JSON, or `null` for a missing folder/file; malformed JSON rejects `INVALID_CONFIG`, so a caller can tell the two apart and never writes over a file it could not read; reading NEVER creates the folder. `write(root, name, value)` `mkdir -p`s the dotfolder lazily and writes pretty-printed JSON atomically (tmp + rename); `name` must be a plain `<stem>.json` basename (separators / `..` → `BAD_REQUEST`, other extensions → `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`). The shared vault watcher never sees dot-entries, so main runs one tiny watch per open-vault root scoped to the dotfolder (`desktop/src/main/vaultConfig.ts`, subscribed per vault of each window — `rootsOf(state)`, every entry of every `windows[].roots`, YAZ-2602 S77 — dropped with the last window that shows the vault); own writes notify synchronously and their watcher echo is deduped by mtime, external edits (sync tools) are debounced — either way every window gets `vaultConfig:changed { root, name }` and filters by its own root. `.yaseendocs` never appears in the tree/sidebar, the vault index, or a folder's views |
| `favorites.get/set/onChanged` | The Favorites tab's list in `<root>/.yaseendocs/favorites.json` (YAZ-1766 6A, 🔒 D11–D15) — `{ version: 1, favorites: string[] }` of VAULT-RELATIVE POSIX paths in the user's order (append on add, rewritten by drag-to-reorder), so the list travels with the vault and GitHub sync carries it between machines. The file holds ONE vault's order; the order ACROSS the vaults of a window is not in it — that is `AppState.favoritesOrder`, per machine (YAZ-2631 D1; "App state"). The API speaks ABSOLUTE paths: `get(root)` → `string[]` (absent or malformed → `[]`, never creates anything; unsafe entries — empty, leading `/`, a `..` segment, NUL — and duplicates are dropped on read, capped at `MAX_FAVORITES`); `set(root, paths)` replaces the list — every path must be `root` or under it (→ `BAD_REQUEST`), the dotfolder is created on the first write, and DEAD ENTRIES are dropped on the way (D14: a path that no longer exists on disk leaves the list; the renderer never prunes). Corrupt policy (D12, properties' R2.5): unparsable JSON or a wrong shape/version reads as `[]` and every `set` rejects `INVALID_CONFIG` — the file is never overwritten or moved aside. Repair (D13): the fs handlers in `desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.ts` (rename, delete, repair-rename, paste-as-cut) call `favorites.renamePath` / `removePath` right after `store.renamePath` / `removePath`, on the LONGEST open root (`rootsOf(state)`: every vault of every window, YAZ-2602 S77) that owns the path — a renamed folder carries its descendants, a path moved OUT of the vault is dropped, nothing is written when nothing changed, and a repair failure is `console.warn`ed without failing the file op or skipping its broadcast. Writes and repairs are serialised per root (properties' chain). Main subscribes one name-filtered `subscribeConfig` per open root and broadcasts `favorites:changed { root }` to every window on an own write or an external edit (sync); renderers filter by root and re-read. Module `desktop/src/main/favorites.ts` (Electron-free), IPC `desktop/src/main/ipc/favorites.ts` |
| `properties.get/setProperty/removeProperty/onChange` | Vault-wide property declarations in `<root>/.yaseendocs/properties.json` (YAZ-835, succeeding the Bible A registry of GRO-2201) — the ONE surviving registry concept: what editor a frontmatter key gets and what a link column points at, for the whole vault. Read/written through vaultConfig (`desktop/src/main/properties/`, Electron-free like the index). `get(root)` → `PropertiesResponse { root, version, properties, error? }` — see "Properties" under "View modules" for the full rules (version semantics, lazy creation, validation grammar, the `types.json` clean break). Any change — in-app mutation or external edit (the vaultConfig dotfolder watcher) — broadcasts `properties:changed { root, properties }` to every window; client: `useProperties(root)` (`client/src/views/`) → `{ status, properties, error }`, live-replaced on each broadcast (a corrupt properties.json is `status: 'ready'` with `properties.error` set, not a fetch error) |
| `github.status/syncNow/setEnabled/onStatus` | Per-vault GitHub sync (YAZ-1081) — plain `git` against the vault's own repo, no GitHub API and no token of ours. **OFF BY DEFAULT and per vault**: the switch is `<root>/.yaseendocs/github.json`, read as `{ enabled: true }` EXACTLY (absent, malformed or `"true"` all mean off), so it travels with the folder and a corrupt file fails closed rather than starting background git work nobody asked for; a disabled root is completely silent — no watcher, no timers, no passes. The repo is AUTO-DETECTED, never configured here: a root with no `.git` at its own level, or no `origin`, is `off` (not an error), and `status` on an off root still reports read-only `repo { remoteUrl, branch }` for a settings panel. Every method answers the same `GithubSyncStatus { root, state, attention?, message?, repo? }` the push carries — `off` / `synced` / `pending` / `syncing` / `attention` (`no-git` / `no-identity` / `auth` / `conflict` / `error`) — so a mutation never needs a follow-up read; `status` answers from the last broadcast without touching git. Passes are EVENT-DRIVEN (`desktop/src/main/git/manager.ts`): opening or enabling a vault pulls, edits mark it `pending` at once then commit after a 30s quiet period, an offline pass arms ONE 2-min retry, and window focus — a focus asks EVERY vault the window shows to pull (YAZ-2602 S43) — / OS wake (`powerMonitor` `resume` + `unlock-screen`, wired in `main/index.ts`) pull behind a 10s cooldown. One pass at a time per root, a burst collapsing into exactly one follow-up.
