A pass is commit → fetch → rebase → push (the commit never stages an atomic-write tmp, YAZ-2179), and the **lossless rule** is absolute: nothing is ever stashed, and a rebase that conflicts is `--abort`ed — the working tree comes back byte-for-byte and the vault reports `attention/conflict` for a human. On quit the renderers flush FIRST, then `flushForQuit` runs the final pass, so the last edit is in the last commit. `github:status-changed` goes to EVERY window on every transition; renderers filter by `status.root` |

Bases (GRO-2097) adds its methods to `CONTRACT` additively (`index(root)` and `readAsset(root, ref)` above), never routes; the drawing embed (YAZ-852) extends the same pipe the same way with `writeAsset(req)`.

Notes
- Folder picking (client, `usePickFolder`): the vault switcher's last row "Open folder…" (the sidebar header, YAZ-1767 D4) and the Welcome screen's Open folder… call `pickFolder()`; `{ path }` → set root + push to recents, `{ cancelled }` or a rejection → nothing. No dialog ever opens by itself — a window with no folder shows the Welcome screen (C2, GRO-2164). One dialog in flight at a time; the trigger buttons are disabled meanwhile. There is no in-app folder browser.
- Main-process writes trigger `change` events on the watcher; the client must ignore events for a path whose mtime equals the mtime it just received from its own `writeFile` (echo suppression).
- Auto-save: client debounces 500 ms after last `markdownUpdated`, also flushes on file switch / the close flush handshake (`window.onFlush` — GRO-2158/2160 — is what guarantees the last save; `beforeunload` is best effort). Only content that differs from the last loaded/saved markdown is saved (Crepe's first serialisation is a normalised rewrite and is never written on its own). Frontmatter-only external changes absorb silently (GRO-2186): an incoming `change` whose BODY matches the last loaded/saved body just replaces the autosave baseline — no reload, no conflict bar — so unsaved body edits and the caret survive a cell edit landing on that note's frontmatter from a folder's view (see "Cell editing"). The same rule covers a save that loses that race (YAZ-2175): a `CONFLICT` whose re-read shows the body unchanged adopts the new frontmatter and retries ONCE, so the app's own property/comment write never raises the bar; a body that changed still does.
- Desktop tests write right after `ready`: on macOS libuv starts the FSEvents stream asynchronously, so the watcher engine announces `ready` only once a probe watch has heard the stream (YAZ-2192), and walks the tree again then, so nothing that landed in between is missed.

## App state (`yaseendocs.json`, main-owned — GRO-2159, D9)

One user-global JSON file owned by the main process: `app.getPath('userData')/yaseendocs.json` = `~/Library/Application Support/Yaseen Docs/yaseendocs.json` (`app.setName(APP_NAME)` runs before anything reads userData; `--user-data-dir=<dir>` relocates it, which is how the Playwright checks seed state, and so does the `YASEEN_DOCS_USER_DATA_DIR` env var, applied right after the name — `applyUserDataOverride`: trimmed, absent or blank = no override; the two names are `APP_NAME` and `STATE_FILE` in `desktop/src/main/userData.ts`). Renderer web storage is not used at all. The renderer never touches the file: it reads a cache (`storage.ts` in client lib, filled by `storage.init()` before the first render) and writes through the targeted `state.*` mutators; `desktop/src/main/store.ts` owns the file. ONE other process reads it, and only reads: the command line's `yaseendocs vaults` (YAZ-2556 🔒 D2 — "Multi-window": The vault list). It has no Electron to ask, so it finds the folder with `userDataDir(env, platform, home)` (`userData.ts`) — `YASEEN_DOCS_USER_DATA_DIR` when set, by `applyUserDataOverride`'s own rule, else where Electron puts an app of this name: `~/Library/Application Support/Yaseen Docs` on macOS, `%APPDATA%\Yaseen Docs` on Windows — and it does NOT follow a `--user-data-dir` given to the app. Nothing here is ever written into the vault or the markdown.

| Key | Shape | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| `version` | `1` | anything else → the file is treated as corrupt (see below) |
| `settings` | `SettingsState` `{ lineSpacing, blockGap, bulletThreading, threadWidth, threadColor, theme, contentWidth, newNoteLocation, newNoteFolder, confirmDelete, confirmRename, commentsOrder, startupWindows }` | **Edited in the settings dialog** (YAZ-1679, `client/src/settings/SettingsDialog.tsx`): an Obsidian-style centred modal — ONE scrolling page of every settings section (Appearance · Editor · Files & Links · Sync) with grouped rows on the right, the section titles down the left as nav anchors (click = scroll to the section; the active one follows the scroll) under a search box, and below a nav divider the `standalone` sections that are their own page inside the dialog (Hotkeys — a reference, not settings) — opened by the sidebar-footer cog, `⌘,` or Yaseen Docs › Settings… (`menu:settings`), all three the ONE dialog App mounts. Every page, row and search hit reads from the one registry, `client/src/settings/registry.tsx` (`SETTINGS_SECTIONS`): a setting is declared once — id, label, hint, keywords, render — inside a section's GROUP (a titled sub-heading, optionally hinted, or plain rows), and each row carries `data-setting="<id>"` (the `SettingsState` field name for the twelve fields that have a row here — `newNoteFolder` shares `newNoteLocation`'s —, `githubSync` for the per-vault switch, `ids` for the vault's IDs switch — "Two kinds of vault": The switch — and `duplicates`, `idLetters` and `oldIds` for the three rows under it, "Note ids"), which is how tests and e2e specs address a row. Search (`searchSettings.ts`) ranks with the `[[` picker's `matchLinkCandidates` over label + hint + keywords + group title + section title, and shows hits under a "Section › Group" breadcrumb. "Settings cog" below means this dialog. THE PAGES FOR ONE VAULT (YAZ-2602 R10, S39) — the Review section, the Sync section and Files & Links › "This vault" (the IDs switch), each marked `vault: true` in the registry — act on the ACTIVE vault, the one that holds the tab in front (with no tab, the window's first), and in a window with two or more vaults each says which: a line "Vault: <name>" under its title (`SettingsCtx.vaultName`, which App hands over only then); with one vault the pages read as they always did. Global: one value for every folder and window; validated field by field on load (`threadWidth` ∈ 1/2/3, `threadColor` `#rrggbb` or null, `theme` ∈ system/light/dark — default `system`, so a pre-K file just gains it; `contentWidth` ∈ narrow/medium/full — default `narrow`, so older files and junk safely preserve the existing 1040px behavior; `newNoteLocation` ∈ root/current/folder — default `current` (YAZ-1643) — and `newNoteFolder` a root-relative path per `isValidNewNoteFolder` (`''` = the vault root; no leading/trailing `/`, and every segment must be one create-on-click would accept — so no empty segment, nothing starting with `.` (which subsumes `.`/`..`), no NUL: GRO-2197, because the panel used to accept `.archive` and then EVERY unresolved-link click failed on the per-segment `validateEntryName`; `shared/types.ts` holds the rule and `sidebar/createEntry.ts` is the sibling that must stay in step) — defaults, so a pre-C2 file just gains them; `confirmDelete` a boolean defaulting **true**; `commentsOrder` ∈ oldest/newest — default `oldest`, so a pre-1515 file just gains it; it is the settings-cog **Comments** control, `Oldest first` / `Newest first`, above the Files & Links section, and the comment block's own header toggle writes the same field — YAZ-1515; `startupWindows` ∈ all/last/none (`STARTUP_WINDOWS`) — default `last`, so an older file just gains it and a value that is not one of the three reads `last`, YAZ-2589 🔒 D2), an invalid field falls back to `DEFAULT_SETTINGS`; `setSettings` takes a complete valid object (else `BAD_REQUEST`). `contentWidth` (YAZ-1176) is the settings-cog Narrow/Medium/Full control: App stamps it as `data-content-width` on `.app`, where one `--content-max-width` maps it to 1040px/1440px/fluid; page header, editor, a folder's views, comments, and backlinks consume that one token and another window's change lands live through the existing `storage.subscribe` path. `newNoteLocation`/`newNoteFolder` (Files & Links, Links C2- — GRO-2240) are the settings-cog "Default location for new notes" control (Obsidian's three options; the folder input shows only for `folder` and validates on commit — invalid keeps the stored value) driving where a bare unresolved `[[link]]` creates its page (rule 23); the setting is GLOBAL but `newNoteFolder` is interpreted per-vault, against the vault that holds the page the link is in (`scopeOf(sourcePath)`, YAZ-2602 S37). `confirmDelete` (GRO-2272 — VS Code's `explorer.confirmDelete`) gates the delete confirm sheet: it defaults ON and should stay that way, because the sheet is the ONLY guard delete has — `shell.trashItem` offers no programmatic un-trash, so there is no in-app restore behind it. Cleared by the sheet's own "Don't ask me again" and re-enabled from the settings cog (both halves are required: a one-way switch would leave hand-editing `yaseendocs.json` as the only way back). `theme` (Appearance, Desktop K — GRO-2218) is the settings-cog System/Light/Dark control: main mirrors it into `nativeTheme.themeSource` before the first window exists and on every change (`desktop/src/main/theme.ts`; new windows also get a matching `backgroundColor`, so no white flash on dark launches), and each renderer resolves it (`system` follows `prefers-color-scheme` live) to `data-theme="light"\|"dark"` on `<html>` — app.css's `[data-theme='dark']` token block — while swapping Crepe's frame/frame-dark var blocks in one managed `<style>` (`client/src/editor/crepeTheme.ts`, CSS-only: the open editor never remounts). `startupWindows` (YAZ-2589 🔒 D2, A3) is Files & Links › **When the app starts** › **Reopen** — a group of its own for the one row, no settings section for it — a three-way control `All vaults` / `Last vault` / `None` (`STARTUP_WINDOWS_OPTIONS`, `client/src/settings/options.ts`), with the hint "A vault that you open from a link or a launcher always opens alone.": WHICH saved windows a PLAIN launch brings back — every one, those of the vault used last, or none (the Welcome window with the list of vaults). The renderer only writes the field; MAIN reads it ONCE, when the app starts (`main/index.ts` → `manager.restore`, "Multi-window": Restore), so a change is saved and shown in every open window at once, like any setting, and takes effect at the next launch; a launch that asked for something that can open does not read it |
| `sidebarCollapsed` (legacy top-level) | `boolean` | retired by YAZ-1280. On load it seeds only window entries that lack their own boolean; a per-window boolean wins; missing/junk → `false`. The sanitized snapshot and next persisted write omit this top-level key. |
| `sidebarWidth` | `number` | global; clamped to [180, 520] on load and on write; non-finite → 260 (YAZ-738) |
| `recents` | `RecentRoots` `[{ path, lastOpened }]` | most-recent first, de-duplicated by path, max 10 (`pushRecent(path)`, absolute path); wrong shape → `[]`. "Most recent" is last USED, not only last opened (YAZ-2555 🔒 D5): besides the open-recent door and the renderer's in-place open, MAIN bumps a vault when a window on it takes focus and when `openWindow` opens one on it — so the list changes on focus, and `lastOpened` is the time of the last use ("Multi-window": Last used). The QUIT writes it too (YAZ-2589 A1): it records the vault of the window in front, so `recents[0]` at the next launch is the vault you were in — the vault `startupWindows: 'last'` brings back |
| `windows` | `WindowEntry[]` `{ id, root, roots, file, tabs, sidebarCollapsed, sidebarLens, focusList, bounds: { x, y, width, height } }` | **`roots`** (YAZ-2602 🔒 D1) is every vault the window shows, absolute paths in the window's order — the order they were added, or the order a drag of a vault row gave them (YAZ-2631 D5; "Multi-window": Reorder the vaults); `root` stays and is the FIRST. The invariants, held by ONE function, `normalizeRoots(roots, root)` (`shared/types.ts` — the loader, `store.upsertWindow` behind every `setIdentity`, and the client cache all call it): `roots[0] === root`; `roots: []` ⇔ `root: null` (a Welcome window shows no vault); a vault appears once, compared with the trailing slash off (`stripSlash`), and every entry after the first is stored with it off; `MAX_WINDOW_ROOTS` (8) at most (R7 — each vault holds a watcher and an index). ON LOAD (S73, S74) it reads with `tabs`' rule: a missing list repairs from `root` (`[root]`, or `[]`), a junk ELEMENT — a non-string, a relative path — drops alone and the rest stay, duplicates drop, entries after the 8th drop, and when `root` is not the first entry the loader puts it first. The key is ADDITIVE within `version` 1, like `tabs`: an old state file has none and is repaired, and an OLD BUILD that reads a new file drops the key and opens `root`, the first vault, alone — no crash, and its next write loses the other vaults of that window (S75: the downgrade is lossy, on purpose). `renamePath` moves every entry of `roots` with its folder, as it moves `root` (S48: a vault that is a subfolder renamed in another window); `removePath` touches neither `root` nor `roots` — the window sees the missing folder and drops the vault itself (S49, R6). The window's `focusList` holds items of every vault in `roots`, and a vault that leaves takes its items in the same write (A5). One entry per open window, saved for the next launch — which brings back the ones it is asked for, or the ones `settings.startupWindows` names, with bounds clamped to a live display, and FORGETS the rest (YAZ-2589 🔒 D1–D3, see "Multi-window": Restore); malformed or duplicate-id entries dropped on load; `upsertWindow` replaces by id or appends; an entry is removed when its window closes — unless the app is quitting (⌘Q, or the last window closing, which quits), so every window open at the quit is saved — and when a launch does not bring it back (`removeWindow(...ids)`, all of them in one commit). `tabs` (GRO-2232) = absolute paths left→right, with `file` doubling as the ACTIVE tab; a path may be a file or — since YAZ-2290 D3 — a DIRECTORY, a folder tab ("Folders are the pages"), and the store does not tell them apart; `normalizeTabs` repairs both on load and every `setIdentity`. `sidebarCollapsed` (YAZ-1280) is an independent boolean per window: new/first/deep-link windows start open, a duplicate inherits then diverges, and legacy entries migrate from the retired top-level value as described above. `sidebarLens` (YAZ-847, per window since YAZ-1628) is `'files' \| 'focus' \| 'favorites'` (Favorites since YAZ-1766, Focus since YAZ-2619) on the same rule: new/first/deep-link windows start on `DEFAULT_SIDEBAR_LENS` (Files since YAZ-1846), a duplicate inherits then diverges, a root CHANGE lands it on Files in `setRoot`'s one write (🔒 D2, YAZ-1846 — reversing the old "root change keeps it"; re-setting the same root still keeps it), `window.setIdentity` refuses anything but the three lenses (`BAD_REQUEST`) — the Search tab of YAZ-2638 🔒 D2 is no lens and is never sent: it lives in App's state alone, so a window that starts again opens on its last lens — and legacy entries migrate exactly as `sidebarCollapsed` does — a v1 file's retired global `sidebarLens` seeds every window without a valid lens of its own, junk or missing (a PRE-847 file) reading as the default (`files`), and the next write drops the old key. `focusList` (YAZ-2619 D1; it replaces `focusDirs` / `focusFavorites`, Focus Mode's two lists of YAZ-1605 / YAZ-1628 / YAZ-1766) is the window's ONE focus list, what its Focus tab shows — absolute paths, files AND folders, of every vault of the window (YAZ-2602 A1), in the order added or the order a drag on the tab gave (YAZ-2631 D3), empty meaning nothing in focus, `MAX_FOCUS` (500) at most — independent per window exactly as `sidebarCollapsed` is: new/first/deep-link windows start with an empty list, a duplicate inherits the list by value then diverges, a root change clears it (`setRoot`), a vault that leaves the window takes its own items and no others (`setRoots`, A5), and `renamePath` / `removePath` repair it as they repair `tabs`; on load it reads with `tabs`' rule (relative or junk elements drop, a missing or junk list is empty) and a long one is cut to `MAX_FOCUS` (R2); the old keys `focusDirs` / `focusFavorites` are not read (R1, S32: no migration — a focus that is active at the update is lost one time, and the next write drops both keys), and a pre-1628 file's per-vault `folders[root].focusDirs` is not read either (the bucket could not say which window was focused; the next write drops it). |
| `folders[root]` | `FolderState` `{ expanded: string[], lastFile: string \| null, folds: { [file]: string[] }, baseGroups: { [pagePath::viewName]: string[] }, name: string \| null, key: number \| null }` | per folder; `name` (YAZ-1974 D3) is the vault's DISPLAY NAME — `null` = its folder name; trimmed and capped at `MAX_VAULT_NAME` (80) code points by `cleanVaultName` on load and on every patch; a name equal to the folder's basename is stored as `null` (`storage.setVaultName`); persisted, per machine; it survives Remove from recent vaults (recents never prune `folders`) and follows the vault through `renamePath` (a Finder rename outside the app drops it, like recents); `key` (YAZ-2555 🔒 D2) is the vault's NUMBER, 1–9 — `null` = none, the default: `⌘<key>` goes to that vault from any window (the Window menu's row — "Menus & shortcuts") and the vault switcher shows it as a badge; it is set by the vault menu's "Set shortcut" (`storage.setVaultKey` → `setFolder(root, { key })` — "Multi-window": The vault menu); `cleanVaultKey` keeps a whole number 1–9 and reads anything else as `null`, on load and on every patch; ONE vault per number — a `setFolder` that gives a number takes it from the vault that had it in the SAME commit (`freeVaultKey`, `shared/types.ts`, which the client cache applies at once too), with no confirm, and a damaged file that gives one number to two vaults keeps the first and clears the others on load (`sanitizeFolders`); persisted, per machine; like `name` it follows the vault through `renamePath` and goes with the bucket when the vault's folder is deleted in the app (`removePath`); UNLIKE `name` it does not survive Remove from recent vaults — the vault switcher clears it there, in a write of its own (A2: with no row left it would be a key nobody can see or change; `state.removeRecent` itself never touches `folders`) — while a folder that is GONE keeps it (A3: the door and the in-place open prune the MRU entry only — a drive that is not connected comes back); `setFolder(root, { expanded?, lastFile?, name?, key? })` merges and creates the entry with defaults when missing (other keys ignored); `expanded` is a SESSION list since YAZ-1642 — held in the in-memory store and shared by every window on the vault, stripped from the file on every write (`toDisk`) and read as `[]` on load, so every launch starts the tree collapsed (the Sidebar likewise leaves the ancestors of the file it MOUNTS with closed; a file opened later still reveals) — while `lastFile`, `folds` and `baseGroups` persist (the Favorites list is NOT here since YAZ-1766 6A/D15 — it lives in the vault as `.yaseendocs/favorites.json`, see the `favorites.*` bridge row); `setFolds(root, file, keys)` replaces one file's collapsed outline fold keys (max 500; an empty list removes the file entry, the folder entry stays); `setBaseGroups(root, key, collapsed)` does the same for one view's collapsed group keys (max 200, 4C — GRO-2137). The `baseGroups` KEY is `<pagePath>::<viewName>` and since YAZ-2290 that path is a FOLDER's directory — the folder tab's own identity — and it stays session chrome that is never written into the folder's `.folder.md` (the name kept its `base` spelling because the store's rename/delete repair, `remapBaseGroupKey` / `baseGroupGone`, is pinned to it by tests); invalid lists dropped on load |
| `vaultSets` | `VaultSet[]` `{ id, name, roots, lastUsed }` | the saved sets of vaults (YAZ-2602 🔒 D8) — what the user reads as **workspaces**; the code says `vaultSets` / `VaultSet` because `workspace` already names the tabs code (`client/src/workspace`). LAST USED FIRST, `MAX_VAULT_SETS` (20) at most (R12), per machine: never in a vault, so it does not sync, and no file type of its own. A set is saved by hand from a window that shows two or more vaults (`window.saveSet`, the bridge table) and is a COPY: a vault that joins or leaves that window later does not change it (S69). A drag of a vault row in a window whose vaults are exactly the set saves it again under its name, so it takes the new order, keeps its `id` and moves to the front (YAZ-2631 D5, S48). `id` a string of its own (a UUID the store mints), `name` cleaned like a vault's display name (`cleanVaultName`: trimmed, `MAX_VAULT_NAME` code points) and ONE set per name, `roots` its vaults in the saved order — absolute, trailing slash off, each once, TWO at least and `MAX_WINDOW_ROOTS` at most (`cleanSetRoots`) — `lastUsed` the time of its last save or open in ms, `0` = not known. THE MUTATORS (`store.ts`; each one commit, a refusal none): `saveVaultSet(name, roots)` answers the saved set — a set with the same cleaned name is REPLACED, keeping its `id`, taking the new vaults and moving to the front (S63, S69) — or `null`: fewer than two vaults after cleaning, an empty name, or a NEW name while 20 sets exist (S68; a save under a name that exists is never the one refused); `renameVaultSet(id, name)` → `false` for an unknown `id`, an empty name or a name a DIFFERENT set has, and the same name again commits nothing; `removeVaultSet(id)` forgets the entry and touches no folder; `touchVaultSet(id)` stamps `lastUsed` and moves the set to the front — what an open does (S65). THE LOADER (`sanitizeVaultSets`): the windows' rule, a bad entry drops alone — no string `id`, an `id` seen before, a name that cleans to nothing, fewer than two vaults left after cleaning — a junk `lastUsed` reads `0`, the list is cut at 20, and a file without the key loads as `[]` (additive within `version` 1, like `windows[].roots`: an old build drops it). THE REPAIR (S71): `renamePath` moves a renamed vault folder in every set, and `removePath` takes a deleted folder out of every set; either way a set stays a set — each vault once, and one with fewer than two vaults left is REMOVED. A folder that is merely GONE from disk changes no set (S66, by the rule YAZ-2555 A3 gave a vault's number: a drive that is not connected comes back) — `window.openSet` leaves it out of the window and names it. Not included: `⌘` numbers for a workspace, workspaces in `yaseendocs vaults`, in Alfred and on the Welcome screen (S72) |
| `favoritesOrder` | `string[]` | the Favorites tab's order ACROSS vaults (YAZ-2631 🔒 D1, rules R1–R7, R13, R14, R23, R25) — absolute paths in the order of the last drag, `MAX_FAVORITES_ORDER` (4,000: `MAX_FAVORITES` for each of `MAX_WINDOW_ROOTS` vaults) at most, per machine: never in a vault, so it does not sync (R1). Each vault keeps its own `.yaseendocs/favorites.json` (the `favorites.*` bridge row), and this list only says which VAULT has each place of the flat list: inside one vault the order of that vault's file wins, so a synced file still wins there (R2 — `favoriteOrder`, `client/src/lib/treeState.ts`; "The sidebar lenses": Several vaults). WHY in the app state: the order holds when a new window opens the same vaults, and every window follows it. Rejected: the order in the window identity, like the focus list (a new window with the same vaults loses it); a flat list with no stored order (a drag works inside one vault only). THE MUTATOR: `setFavoritesOrder(roots, paths)` — what a window hands in for the vaults `roots`: after a drag, that window's favorites in the new order; after "Remove from favorites", the stored order without the removed paths — merges with ONE function, `mergeFavoritesOrder(stored, roots, paths)` (`shared/types.ts`; the client cache calls it too, so both hold one answer, R14): the window's entries take the new order in the places they held, a path with no place goes last, an entry of a vault the window does not show keeps its place (R5 — a window on A and B does not move the places of C's favorites, S13), and the list is cut at the limit; it returns the stored list ITSELF when nothing changes, which is main's test for no commit. TWO WRITERS (R4, amended by R23). A drag on the Favorites tab writes it where the window has two or more vaults (`saveFavoriteOrder`), and hands in only the vaults whose `favorites.json` this window has read: the places of a vault whose list is still on its way do not change (R25 — `loadFavorites` keeps a first answer that is empty, so a vault with no favorite counts as read and its stale entries leave on a drag). "Remove from favorites" takes the removed paths out of it (`toggleFavorite`), with any number of vaults and only when the list holds one of them: every other entry keeps its place, and the same favorite, added again, goes last with no other row moved (R23, S6, S7). "Add to favorites" does not write it, and a favorite with no place stands after the placed ones (R13). The favorite toggle writes nothing for a vault whose `favorites.json` this window has not read: it has no list to add to, so a write would replace the list on disk; the notice says "Favorites are still loading. Try again." (R26). Known and accepted: a sync from a different machine that removes a favorite leaves its path in this list; if that favorite comes back, its vault has one more place at the old position, the list stays valid, and the next drag corrects it. THE LOADER: a missing list, or one that is not all strings, is `[]`; a relative element drops alone; a long list is cut to the limit (R7; additive within `version` 1: an old state file has none, and an old build drops the key). THE REPAIR (R6): `renamePath` moves a renamed favorite in its place, a folder with what is under it, and `removePath` drops a deleted one, as they repair `focusList` — each one commit, also when this list alone holds the path. An entry of a vault that is not in the window draws no row and keeps its place, so its favorites come back in place with the vault (S14) |

Write semantics: every mutation updates memory, notifies `onChange` listeners synchronously and schedules one debounced (150 ms) atomic write (`<file>.tmp-<rand>` + rename, pretty-printed JSON, parent dir created on demand), skipped when its text equals the last write's — a folder expand / collapse is session-only and changes no byte (YAZ-2198); `flush()` writes at once and `before-quit` holds the quit until it lands (`event.preventDefault()` + `app.exit(0)`), so nothing is lost on ⌘Q. A file that is unparsable, not an object or not `version: 1` is moved to `yaseendocs.json.corrupt-<epoch>` and the defaults are used — the bad file is never overwritten silently. Unknown top-level keys are dropped on load. The read itself is `parseState(text)` (`store.ts`, YAZ-2556 D2): the text as a state sanitized field by field, or `null` for those three cases. The store's `load` is the caller that moves a `null` aside; `yaseendocs vaults`, the other caller, sees exactly the state the app would load, refuses a `null` with `<file> is not a valid app state` (exit 1) and leaves the file where it is for the app to move — and with no file at all it lists no vaults and creates none.

Live broadcast: after any change — from any window, or from main itself (window upserts, and the "last used" bump of `recents` when a vault's window takes focus — YAZ-2555 D5) — every live `BrowserWindow` gets `state:changed` with the whole new state; the preload surfaces it as `state.onChange`, the client cache replaces itself and `storage.subscribe` listeners fire (`App` re-reads global settings and sidebar width). The cache keeps its own `favoritesOrder` list while a broadcast holds the same paths, so what reads the order draws again only when it changed; and `storage.setFavoritesOrder` — a drag on the Favorites tab, or a "Remove from favorites" that takes a path out of the order (R23) — is the ONE write of this window that wakes `storage.subscribe` itself: the cache holds the new order at once and does not wait for main's broadcast (YAZ-2631 R15). Per-window `sidebarCollapsed` — and `sidebarLens` since YAZ-1628 — is read from the boot identity and written optimistically through `window.setIdentity`, so another window's state echo cannot change it.

Per-window identity: main loads each window as `<renderer>?win=<id>` and keeps a `webContents.id → window id` lookup (`desktop/src/main/windows.ts`). `window.identity()` answers `{ id, root, roots, file, tabs, sidebarCollapsed }` from `windows[]` for the calling window (`BAD_REQUEST` for an unregistered sender, `NOT_FOUND` once the entry is gone); `window.setIdentity({ root?, roots?, file?, tabs?, sidebarCollapsed? })` validates and merges into that entry (`id` and `bounds` cannot be changed this way; how `root` and `roots` meet is the bridge table's `setIdentity`). On the client, `storage.getRoot()`, `storage.getRoots()` (YAZ-2602 D1) and `storage.getSidebarCollapsed()` are identity reads; root/file/tab helpers and the sidebar toggle write back through that same targeted identity path. `storage.setRoot(root)` makes the window a window on that ONE vault and resets the tabs, the lens and the focus list (an in-place switch, S61); `storage.setRoots(roots)` adds or removes a vault and keeps all three (D2, D7); `storage.mirrorRoots(roots)` follows a vault folder that main's store already renamed, with no identity write. The active file is the `lastFile` of the vault that HOLDS it (`rootOfPath`), not of the window's first vault.

## Menus & shortcuts (B3 — GRO-2161)

The menu bar is a pure template (`desktop/src/main/menu.ts`, `buildMenuTemplate(inputs, handlers)`) so structure, accelerators and roles unit-test without Electron; `main/index.ts` applies it (`Menu.buildFromTemplate` + `setApplicationMenu`) and rebuilds it ONLY when `AppState.recents` or the Window menu's vault rows actually changed (`subscribeMenuRebuild`: `recents` compares by reference — store snapshots reuse untouched sub-objects; the rows — `menuKeyedVaults(state)`, every vault that has a number, in number order, named by its display name else its folder name (the shared `keyedVaults(state.folders)` of `shared/types.ts` since YAZ-2556 — "Multi-window": The vault list) — compare by VALUE, because `state.folders` is a new object on every folder write (a fold, a last file), and that one pass over the folders runs only when `state.folders` IS a new object; YAZ-2555 D3). So a new, moved or cleared number and a display name set or reset on a numbered vault each rebuild once; a fold, a last-file write, a name on a vault with no number and the same number given again never do; and since `recents` follows focus (YAZ-2555 D5 — "Multi-window": Last used), a switch to another vault's window is a rebuild too. A label that shows a name or a path writes `&` as `&&` (`menuLabel`, YAZ-2555 — the Open Recent rows and the vault rows): Electron reads a single `&` as a mnemonic mark and drops it, so "R&D" would read "RD"; the handler still gets the raw text. Item ids are stable (`menu.file.new-window`, `menu.file.open-recent.<i>`, `menu.window.vault.<n>`, …) so a live check (Playwright) can drive them via `Menu.getApplicationMenu().getMenuItemById(...)`. `buildContextMenuTemplate(params, actions)` is the same idiom for the right-click menu (spell suggestions → Add to Dictionary → cut/copy/paste per `editFlags`; on an `<img>` — `params.mediaType === 'image'`, which Chromium sets only for elements laid out as images, never inline `<svg>`, so sidebar icons keep the text rows — exactly two rows instead: Copy Image through the native `webContents.copyImageAt`, and Reveal in Finder through `revealVaultImage` → `revealItem`, a silent no-op for a non-vault src such as a card cover, YAZ-1666); every window's factory wires it in `main/index.ts` (YAZ-672).

"The focused window" in the table below means `pickMenuTargetWindow` (`menu.ts`, GRO-2197), not a bare `BrowserWindow.getFocusedWindow()`: the focused window when there IS one, else the window focused most recently, else any live window. macOS reports NO focused window whenever the app is not the frontmost application — a detached DevTools window, a native panel, or a programmatic `menuItem.click()` from the e2e harness — and every one of these items used to become a silent no-op in that state (measured: `tabs.spec.ts`'s ⌃Tab step failed 4 runs in 5 under CPU load because the `menu:next-tab` message was never sent at all). A menu item that quietly does nothing is the worst available answer, so the fallback IS the contract. `main/index.ts` tracks the last-focused id from `app.on('browser-window-focus')`; the picker itself is Electron-free and unit-tested.

| Item | Keys | Does |
|---|---|---|
| Yaseen Docs › Settings… | `⌘,` | sends `menu.onSettings` to the focused window's renderer, which opens the settings dialog (YAZ-1679; the renderer owns the dialog, so the gesture never touches it from main); no focused renderer → no-op |
| File › New Tab | `⌘T` | sends `menu.onNewTab` to the focused window's renderer (YAZ-2655 D10, D11; id `menu.file.new-tab`, the FIRST item of the File menu): it shows the ONE blank tab — "New tab", last in the strip and active, over the empty page — and puts the caret in the sidebar's search bar by ⌘K's own door, un-collapsing the sidebar first; while the blank tab shows it only takes the caret back to the bar. The strip's "+" is the same gesture. A no-op during a review and on the Welcome window (see "Multi-window": Tabs — the blank tab); no focused renderer → no-op |
| File › New Window | `⌘⇧N` | duplicates the FOCUSED window — same folder, same file, cascaded bounds (`window.duplicate` semantics, GRO-2167); no focused window → no-op |
| File › Open Folder… | `⌘⇧O` | sends `menu.onOpenFolder` to the focused window's renderer, which runs its pick-folder flow; the picked folder goes through App's `openVault` (YAZ-1914 🔒 D1): on Welcome the window becomes that vault in place, in a vault window it goes to main's `openRecentBeside` door — the vault's windows raised or a NEW window on its last file. A window that already shows a vault never changes vault |
| File › Switch Vault… | `⌘O` | sends `menu.onSwitchVault` to the focused window's renderer, which TOGGLES the sidebar header's vault switcher — un-collapsing the sidebar first when hidden; a no-op on the Welcome screen (YAZ-1767 D8; see "Multi-window": Open beside) |
| File › Open Recent › `<path>` | — | every click (⌥ no longer differs — YAZ-1914 🔒 D2) sends `menu.onOpenRoot(path)` to the focused renderer, which applies App's `openVault`: on Welcome it switches in place (MRU bumped by the renderer); in a vault window it goes to main's `openRecentBeside` door (YAZ-1767 D1): the directory is probed first (a dead folder is pruned from the MRU and opens nothing, GRO-2211), the MRU is bumped, and the vault's open windows are raised or a new window opens on its last file. Empty MRU → one disabled "No Recent Folders" item |
| File › Search Vault | `⌘K` | sends `menu.onSearch` to the focused window's renderer, which shows the sidebar's Search tab and puts the caret in its bar, with the text that is there selected — un-collapsing the sidebar first when it is hidden (YAZ-804; YAZ-2638 🔒 D2; the renderer owns the bar, so the gesture never touches it from main, and main did not change). The bar is on the Search tab ONLY, so ⌘K SHOWS that tab from whichever tab is showing, and on the Search tab it puts the caret back in the bar. The Search tab is never stored, and Esc in the bar goes back to the lens the window last showed, with the text kept. This overturns the YAZ-847 rule that the bar lived on EVERY lens, that ⌘K never switched the tab and that the results replaced the ACTIVE tab's body (🔒 D5 — see "The sidebar lenses": The Search tab) |
| File › Close Tab | `⌘W` | sends `menu.onCloseTab` to the focused window's renderer — the ⌘W ladder (see "Multi-window": Tabs): close the active tab; with zero tabs open (Welcome included) close the WINDOW through the real close path (`window.closeSelf`) |
| File › Close Window | `⌘⇧W` | role `close` — goes through the flush handshake like any close (see "Multi-window") |
| Window › Next / Previous Tab | `⌃Tab` / `⌃⇧Tab` | sends `menu.onNextTab` / `menu.onPrevTab` to the focused window's renderer — wraparound cycle over the strip's left→right order; hidden menu duplicates carry the `⌘⇧]` / `⌘⇧[` equivalents (`acceleratorWorksWhenHidden`) |
| Window › Tab Overview | `⌘⇧M` | sends `menu.onTabOverview` to the focused window's renderer (YAZ-2648 D5; id `menu.window.tab-overview`, after the tab-switching items; the key was ⌘⇧A until R39 replaced it — ⌘M with no Shift is macOS's minimize): it opens the tab board, and closes it again, as the strip's grid button does. A no-op during a review and on the Welcome window (see "Multi-window": The tab board); no focused renderer → no-op |
| Window › `<vault name>` | `⌘1` … `⌘9` | one row per vault that HAS a number (`folders[root].key` — YAZ-2555 🔒 D3; `MenuInputs.keyedVaults` = `menuKeyedVaults(state)`), in number order, labelled with the vault's display name else its folder name, id `menu.window.vault.<n>`, accelerator `CmdOrCtrl+<n>`. The rows stand above `front` with ONE separator after them; with no numbered vault there is no row and no extra separator, so ⌘<n> on a number no vault has does nothing. The MENU owns the key, so it answers from any window, with the caret anywhere, and with every window minimized — not while another app is in front (YAZ-2556). 🔒 Trade accepted: tabs can never take ⌘1–⌘9. A click or the key is `goToVault(path, name)`: STRAIGHT to main's `openRecentBeside` door, never through the focused renderer (A5 — unlike Open Recent above, so from a Welcome window the vault opens in a NEW window and Welcome stays): the vault's windows are raised, a minimized one restored, or with none open a new window opens on its last file; on the vault of the window in front that window stays in front. A folder that is gone → the door prunes it from the MRU and opens nothing, and `linkNotice` says `Can't open <name>: folder not found` in the window that had focus last ("Links": Failure surface); the number stays (A3) |
| View › Toggle Sidebar | — | sends `menu.onToggleSidebar` to the focused window's renderer, which runs the same local toggle as every other sidebar gesture; no focused renderer → no-op |
| App chrome | `⌘B` | renderer-owned sidebar toggle (YAZ-1280): only plain Cmd+B outside input/textarea/select/contenteditable and while no aria-modal tool is open. Milkdown keeps Cmd+B for bold; Yaseendraw/modal tools keep their own gesture. There is deliberately no Electron accelerator or global shortcut. |
| App chrome | `⌘X` / `⌘C` / `⌘V` | renderer-owned file clipboard (YAZ-1674, D6): the same `ownsWindowChord` boundary as ⌘B / ⌘⇧C (a field, the editor or a modal keeps the key — text copy/paste is untouched); App asks the mounted Sidebar's `clipboardRef` handle and swallows the key ONLY when it acted (Cut/Copy need a selection ≥1, Paste a non-empty clipboard; an open context menu owns the verbs). Hand-confirmed on macOS in the demo that the renderer wins over the Edit-menu role. |
| View › Actual Size / Zoom In / Zoom Out | `⌘0` / `⌘+` (also `⌘=`) / `⌘−` | NOT the stock roles (YAZ-1710): each sends `menu.onZoom(step)` (−1 / 0 / +1) to the focused window's renderer, which routes it — `requestZoom` (`editor/zoomRequest.ts`) bubbles a cancelable `yaseendocs:zoom` event from the focused element; the note whose `section.editor` contains the focus claims it and steps its own `documentZoom` (`stepZoomByKey`: the pill ladder to 200, then 25 at a time to 400; ⌘0 → 100%; a wall is a no-op); unclaimed (blank sidebar, a dialog, the search box, Welcome) → `window.zoom(step)`, and main sets the sender's zoom level to `0` or `± 0.5`, exactly what the roles did, persistence included |
| View › Reload, Edit roles, Window roles | — | stock Electron roles; dev builds add View › Toggle Developer Tools; the Window submenu has `role: 'window'` so macOS appends the window list |
| Help › Yaseen Docs on GitHub | — | `shell.openExternal(HELP_URL)` |

## Multi-window (D3/D6 — GRO-2160/2167/2168/2169)

The window manager (`desktop/src/main/windows.ts`) is Electron-free — `main/index.ts` injects window construction and display geometry as a `WindowHost`, so everything below runs under vitest with fakes.

- **Restore** (YAZ-2589 🔒 D1–D3, with A1, A2 and A6 — ⚡ OVERTURNS 🔒 D3 of GRO-2160, "on launch, one window per `AppState.windows` entry": that rule had no switch, so a launch that asked for ONE vault brought every old window back with it): `manager.restore(which)` runs once, at launch — `main/index.ts`, after every IPC door is registered and before the link queue is flushed — and says which SAVED windows come back. **A launch that asked for something** (🔒 D1) — a `yaseendocs://` link, Finder's Open With, `open -a` (a launcher such as Alfred), waiting in the link queue because macOS hands it over before `ready` — brings back ONLY the saved windows of the vaults it asked for: `index.ts` reads `links.pending()`, finds each request's vault with `manager.rootFor(path, rootOverride)` — a folder is its own vault, trailing slash off; a file belongs to the root `resolveLinkTarget` picks ("Links": Routing — the saved window one of whose VAULTS contains it, the most specific vault whichever place it has in its window (YAZ-2602 A8), else the recent root that contains it, else its parent folder; a `?root=` that contains it names the vault) — and calls `restore(roots)`. **A window is on each vault it shows** (YAZ-2602 🔒 D6, A8): a saved window comes back when ONE of its `roots` is a vault the launch asked for — or, for `last`, is the vault used last — and it comes back whole, with ALL its vaults; so a launch that asks for B brings back the window that shows A and B beside B's own windows, and forgets a window on A alone. A vault with two saved windows gets both; two requests bring back the windows of both vaults; a vault with NO saved window brings back nothing, and no Welcome window opens for it, because the request itself, handled when the queue is flushed right after, opens the vault in a new window on its `folders[root].lastFile` (a folder), or on the file ("Links": Routing). **A request that cannot open is not a request** (A6): `rootFor` answers `null` for a path that is no folder and is no supported, existing file — the ONE check, `cannotOpen`, that `routeToFile` answers with a notice ("Links": Failure surface) — and a link that does not parse names no path at all; `index.ts` leaves both out of what was asked. One request that can open and one that cannot: only the vault of the good one comes back, and the notice shows there. NO waiting request can open: the launch is a plain launch, and the notice shows in a window that came back — so a dead link in a launcher never costs you your saved windows. **A plain launch** (🔒 D2) follows `settings.startupWindows` ("App state"; Settings › Files & Links › When the app starts › Reopen): `all` — every saved window, a saved Welcome window too (the old rule, one switch away); `last`, the DEFAULT — the saved windows of the vault used last, `recents[0]`, which the quit recorded (A1 — Last used, below), so it is the vault you were in, and a saved Welcome window does not come back; `none` — no saved window. **A saved window that does not come back is forgotten** (🔒 D3), as a window you close is: its `windows[]` entry is removed — all of them in ONE store commit, `store.removeWindow(...ids)` — so its tabs, size and place are gone, and its vault later opens in a new window, default size and place, on its last page. `recents` and `folders` are not touched: the vault keeps its last file, its display name and its number, and stays in ⌘O under "Not open". Rejected for this issue: "each vault remembers its windows" (a new saved list, with rules of its own for a renamed or deleted folder). **The Welcome window**: when a plain launch brings back nothing — `none`; `last` with no saved window on that vault, or with an empty `recents`; any value with no saved window at all, a first launch — `restore` opens ONE Welcome window (`openWindow({ root: null, file: null })`), with the list of vaults. A launch that asked for a vault opens none: the request opens the vault. So the app never runs with no window (A2; `linkNotice` holds the last case — "Links": Failure surface). Vault roots compare with the trailing slash off (`stripSlash`), so a window saved on `/v/` belongs to the vault `/v`. Restored bounds are clamped fully inside the display work area they overlap most (fully off-screen → the nearest one; oversized → shrunk to fit) — so a window from an unplugged display always lands somewhere visible — and a clamp that moved a window is saved. Bounds changes (`move`/`resize`) are committed to the state debounced (250 ms per burst — one `state:changed` per drag). Two known limits: macOS can hand a request over AFTER the app decided (S9) — the launch is then a plain launch, and the vault that was asked for opens after it; and on Windows a link at a cold start is not read at all (`argv` is read only in `second-instance`), so the plain rule applies there. Proof: `desktop/src/main/windows.test.ts` ("what a launch brings back", "a request that cannot open is not a request"), `desktop/src/main/index.startup.test.ts` (what `index.ts` asks the manager for), `desktop/src/main/store.test.ts` (`removeWindow`, the setting's guard), `desktop/src/main/linkQueue.test.ts` (`pending`); scenario 8 of `desktop/e2e/scenarios.spec.ts` ("Last vault") is written and has not been run, and a request at a cold start on the real app is by hand (`docs/REGRESSION.md` W5).
- **Duplicate** (GRO-2167): `⌘⇧N` / `window.duplicate()` — same folder — EVERY vault the window shows, copied by value (YAZ-2602 S34) — same file, same TABS (the copy carries the whole set), fresh id, bounds cascaded +24 px and clamped at the display edge. A Welcome window duplicates to another Welcome window.
- **Open beside** (D2, GRO-2168): a file row's context-menu "Open in new window" → `window.open({ root, file })` — an independent window on the same folder with that file open; from a window that shows several vaults, on the vault that holds the file, ALONE (YAZ-2602 S33). Deep-link routing uses the same `openWindow` path (default bounds, clamped). **The vault switcher** (YAZ-1767, `sidebar/VaultSwitcher.tsx`): the sidebar header's top-left button — bold vault name + the Octicon triangle chevron (D6) — drops, on click or ⌘O, a `ContextMenuSurface` panel hung off the header (`ctx-menu--panel`, D5) — at least the header's width (`minWidth`) and growing to fit its longest name up to 480px, past which a name wraps, so no name is ever cut (YAZ-1974 D7): the filter input first and autofocused (D7), every vault as ONE line — display name, its key badge when it has a number, relative time (YAZ-1974 D2) — the current vault `aria-current` (D3) — "current" being EVERY vault of this window since YAZ-2602 — "Open folder…" last (the folder picker, D4 — it opens beside since YAZ-1914). With two or more vaults in the window the trigger names them (YAZ-2602 S15: `vaultsLabel` — two names joined with " + ", more as the first two then "+ N more"; its `title` lists every path), or, while the window's vaults are EXACTLY a saved workspace (`sameVaults`, the first that matches), shows that workspace's name; a click drops the same panel. **Two groups** (YAZ-2555 🔒 D1; since YAZ-2602 D8 the saved workspaces stand above them under "Workspaces" — Workspaces, below): the vaults that have a window stand FIRST, under a small muted label "Open"; the others follow under "Not open", with a hairline above that label when the first group stands over it. The labels are not rows — never focused, never highlighted, outside the highlight's index space — and a group with no row shows no label. A minimized window's vault is "Open" like any other (no "minimized" mark — the door restores the window), a vault open in two windows is ONE row, each vault of a window that shows several is "Open" (S59), and a Welcome window (root `null`) is no vault. **The rows** (A1) are `storage.listVaults()` — the shared `listVaults` over the state cache, no IPC; the SAME list `yaseendocs vaults` prints (YAZ-2556 🔒 D2 — The vault list, below): the recents PLUS every open vault (`openVaultRoots` — every vault of every window, `windows[].roots`, once, trailing slash off; YAZ-2602 D6) PLUS every vault that has a number (`keyedVaults`), so a numbered vault does not leave the list when it falls out of the 10 recents. The panel then puts them in its two groups: "Open" is the open recents in last-used order, then the open vaults that are not in the recents; "Not open" the other recents in last-used order, then the numbered vaults that are not in the recents, in number order. A row that is not in the recents has an empty time slot (`lastUsed` is `null`; a `VaultRow` is `listVaults`'s `VaultEntry` plus `folder`, the folder's name the filter also matches). **The badge** (🔒 D4): a numbered vault's row wears `⌘<n>` (`<kbd class="vault-switcher__key">` — small, outlined, muted, monospace) after its name, in both groups, ALWAYS — ⌘<n> works with the panel up or down, so nothing is held to see it; the row's grid is name · key · ⓘ · time, the last three each in a named column, so a row with no key keeps its ⓘ and its time where a row with one has them. The rows are read from `storage` on every open, never cached across opens. The path is one hover away (YAZ-1974 D2): an ⓘ before the time shows while its row is hovered (visibility, so nothing shifts; a dead row too) and hovering it draws the WHOLE path — wrapped at each `/`, never truncated — in a `role="tooltip"` portalled to `<body>` with `position: fixed` at the row (below it, or above when there is more room there), so the panel's scroll box never clips it; mouse-only by design; clicking the ⓘ is the row's click. **Display names** (YAZ-1974 D3–D6): `storage.vaultName(root)` (`folders[root].name ?? basename(root)`, live through `useVaultName`) is what the trigger, the OS window title (`windowTitle(vault, file)` takes the NAME), Welcome's recents and "Copy vault name" show, and the SAME rule names the switcher's rows (`listVaults`) and the Window menu's numbered-vault rows (`menuKeyedVaults` — since YAZ-2556 the shared `keyedVaults`, where main had a spelling of its own); File › Open Recent keeps full paths and the Reveal / VS Code "no longer there" notices keep the FOLDER name on purpose. Ranking is `matchLinkCandidates` over each row's display name AND folder name (one candidate per name, the `[[` picker's alias idea — each row once, at its best rank), and the ranked rows are THEN put in the two groups by a stable split (YAZ-2555 A6: the filter keeps the groups and the badges; with no query each group is in last-used order); the highlight starts on the first NON-current row (`defaultHighlight(matches, query, ...roots)` — the first row that is no vault of this window; 🔒 with an EMPTY query it never starts on a workspace row, though those stand above: `⌘O ⏎` is still "the vault you used last", YAZ-2555 wins over YAZ-2602 here) so ⌘O ⏎ jumps to the last-used other vault, an OPEN one first — with two or more vaults open, the open vault used before this one, and "last used" follows focus since D5, so a mouse click or ⌘\` counts (Last used, below); with only this window's vault open, the first "Not open" row; with a typed query, the first row shown, which is the best match among the open vaults when one matches; ↑/↓ clamp, hover moves it, ⏎ activates, **⇧⏎ / ⇧-click open the vault IN this window** — the menu's "Open in this window" (YAZ-1974 D8; a plain ⏎ on a vault of this window; on any other vault it REPLACES all the window's vaults with that one and resets the tabs, YAZ-2602 S61 — to keep them, "Add to this window") — and while ⇧ is held the highlighted row's time slot reads "Open here" in the accent colour (D9; never on the current vault, a dead row or Open folder…); Esc closes on the first press, a second ⌘O closes, and the panel closes when its WINDOW loses focus (`blur`, YAZ-2555 A4: ⌘<n> can take you to another window, and a panel left up behind would show an old list; a row's menu and rename field go with it, the header's stay — so "Reveal in Finder" and "Open in VS Code", which hand focus to another app, close it too). Every activation goes through `window.openRecent(path)` — raise the vault's open windows (🔒 D9) or open a new one on its last file (D2); `false` (dead folder, MRU pruned) disables the row with "Folder not found" and keeps the panel open. App's `openVault` is the same door for a vault window's Open Folder… and File › Open Recent (YAZ-1914), the Window menu's ⌘1–⌘9 rows reach it in main (YAZ-2555 D3 — "Menus & shortcuts"), and so does a FOLDER that comes from outside the app (`routeToFile`, YAZ-2556 🔒 D1 — "Links": Routing). **The vault menu** (YAZ-1798): right-clicking the trigger (= the current vault, in a window with ONE vault; with two or more the trigger is several vaults and its right-click is the one item "Save as workspace…" — Workspaces, below) or any live row opens the sidebar's own `ContextMenu` with `buildVaultMenuSections({ path, name, isCurrent, renamed, keyed })` (`sidebar/vaultMenuSections.ts`, pure; `keyed` = `storage.keyedVaults()`, who has which number; `isCurrent` = the vault is one of this window's, YAZ-2602 S8) — another vault gets [Add to this window · Open in this window `⇧⏎`] · [Set display name · Reset to folder name · Set shortcut ▸] · [Copy vault name · Copy path] · [Reveal in Finder · Open in VS Code] · [Remove from recent vaults]; a vault of this window the middle three groups; **"Add to this window"** (YAZ-2602 D2, S8) is App's `addVault` — the blank-space item's own door, Several vaults in one window, below: added → the panel closes; refused → App's notice has said why and the panel stays, the row as it was; "Reset to folder name" only while a display name is set (YAZ-1974 D5 — never labelled "Rename", which renames on disk in the file menu). **"Set shortcut"** (YAZ-2555 🔒 D2) is a parent, last in its group: its flyout lists `⌘1`–`⌘9`, each with a hint — the name of the vault that has that number, `✓ <name>` on this vault's own, "free" on one nobody has — and, in a group of its own and only while this vault has a number, "No shortcut". A number another vault has is offered like a free one: picking it moves it here, no confirm. Picking is `storage.setVaultKey(path, n)` — ONE `state.setFolder(root, { key })`, the cache and the store each taking the number from the vault that had it ("App state": `folders[root]`) — and "No shortcut" is `setVaultKey(path, null)`; the open panel's badges follow at once, the one on the vault that lost the number too, the Window menu gains, renames, moves or drops its row ("Menus & shortcuts"), and the number is there after a relaunch. A row that is listed only for its number and loses it stays until the panel closes; the next open does not list it. "Set display name" turns the name into an inline `TextField` WHERE it was right-clicked — the header or the row, a `<div>` in place of the `<button>` meanwhile — the name selected, the folder name as placeholder: ⏎ or blur saves, Esc cancels, an empty field is the folder name again; the field is the top layer: Esc and click-away end only it (the panel stays), and a mousedown on another row ends it (the blur saves) and swallows that click, so a vault never opens by accident. "Open in this window" is App's `openRoot` (in place — besides Welcome, the ONE deliberate in-place switch since YAZ-1914 made every other open go beside, D8/D11; it lands on Files like any vault switch, YAZ-1846; `false` greys the row like a click's); Reveal / VS Code are the Sidebar's own verbs (any absolute path, `NOT_FOUND` notice); the copies confirm through the sidebar notice; Remove is `storage.removeRecentRoot` with no confirm (the folder is untouched): it drops the row at once and CLEARS the vault's number (YAZ-2555 A2 — `setVaultKey(path, null)`, only when it has one); a vault that still has a window is listed again on the next open, under "Open", with no time and no number (A1). The menu is the top layer while it stands: Esc and click-away close only it, and the filter ignores ↑/↓/⏎/Esc meanwhile. Every right-click swallows Electron's native text menu; a "Folder not found" row gets no menu — the door has pruned its MRU entry; one that has a number keeps it (A3) and is listed again on the next open (A1), where its menu can clear the number. ⌘-click on a sidebar file row is NOT this gesture any more: since I3 (GRO-2235, locked) it opens a background tab in the SAME window — see Tabs below.
- **Several vaults in one window** (YAZ-2602 🔒 D1–D7, rules R1–R12, cases S1–S62 and S73–S80, amended by A1–A9, all on the issue): a window shows one vault or up to `MAX_WINDOW_ROOTS` (8, R7) — `WindowEntry.roots`, `root` the first ("App state": `windows`). With ONE vault everything is as it was before this work: no vault row, the same bridge calls, the same paint (S11, S79 — pinned by `App.test.tsx`, "App with one vault makes the bridge calls it made before"). **One scope per vault** (🔒 D1): each vault of the window stays a FULL vault. App holds one `VaultScope` per vault (`vault/useVaultScope.ts`) and every one stays LOADED. WHY: a note must behave the same in every window — its `[[links]]` and `[[` picker (R1: they stay inside the note's own vault; no link crosses two vaults), its properties, its images, its sync chip, its IDs and its review settings come from the vault that holds it, whichever tab is in front (S30) — and a loaded scope makes a tab switch BETWEEN two vaults cost what a switch inside one costs (S29, S80). Rejected: one loaded vault that follows the active tab (the full index over IPC on each switch, and the hidden tabs on the wrong vault); extra folders in the tree only (links, images and properties off the wrong vault); opening the parent folder (one merged vault). The scopes live in SLOTS (`vault/slots.ts`): `MAX_WINDOW_ROOTS` `useVaultScope` calls on every render, because a scope must exist in the FIRST render — an editor that waited a frame for its vault would change the launch paint — and hooks cannot be called a varying number of times; `assignSlots` gives a new vault the first free slot and a vault KEEPS its slot while it is in the window, through a rename too (`renameSlots`), so nothing of a vault that stays loads again; a slot with no vault is idle — no bridge call, and `useReview` starts no midnight timer for it. `scopeOf(path)` is the scope of the vault that HOLDS the path — the most specific one, `rootOfPath` — and a path in no vault of the window (a pasted `#/abs/path` URL) gets the first vault's (S35); it is ref-backed, so the callbacks under the mounted editors keep their identity. `editorPropsFor(path)` hands each editor — a main tab, a right-panel page, a review's note — the scope's own stable values, so a change in one vault renders no editor of another (`RetainedEditor` is memo), and there is one `WikilinkIndexBridge` per vault, drawn in SLOT order, so a new order of the vaults moves none and no vault reads its index again (YAZ-2631 R22). **Per vault** (the scope): the watcher, the three link sources, the property declarations, GitHub sync, the review settings, its count and its session, the external-rename detector, the answer on IDs and the box that asks, the display name; in the sidebar the tree and its feed, the favorites, the stored open folders (`folders[root].expanded`) and the shortcut rows; in main the one watcher and the one index per root that every window shares, and the per-vault subscriptions (vault config, favorites, properties, sync), which follow `rootsOf` — every vault of every window (S77). **Per window**: `roots` and their order, the tabs and the right panel (absolute paths, of any vault), the lens, the focus list, the selection, the vault rows the user closed, the one notice — and ONE of each thing that asks for attention: the sync banner of the first vault that has one, the external-rename banner of the first vault that has one (S44), the IDs box of the first vault that must answer (S40), and one review session (S41, S42: a start in one vault closes the session of every other, and it runs with ITS vault's settings and notes whichever tab is in front). **Per machine, for every window**: the Favorites order across vaults (`AppState.favoritesOrder`, YAZ-2631 D1). **The active vault** is the vault that holds the active tab, else the window's first: the window title (R3), the settings pages for one vault (R10, "App state": `settings`) and "last used" (S60). A new note is made in the vault of the row that was clicked, and one made from an unresolved `[[link]]` in the vault that holds the page the link is in, at that vault's "default location for new notes" (R4, S37 — `newNoteFolderFor(sourcePath)`), which for the tab in front is the active vault. **Add a vault** (🔒 D2): two gestures, one door. A right-click on the sidebar's blank space → "Add vault to this window ▸", whose flyout lists the KNOWN vaults that are not in this window, in `storage.listVaults()` order, then "Open folder…" in a section of its own (the system folder picker; a cancel changes nothing, S3); and "Add to this window" in the menu of a `⌘O` row (S8). Both are App's `addVault(folder)`: the folder becomes the window's LAST vault with its row open and goes to the top of the recents (S2), and the tabs, the lens and the focus list STAY — the list of vaults is the only identity write (`storage.setRoots`), where every other way to change a window's vault resets them. A folder the window cannot take is refused with a notice and nothing changes: one that is in the window → `<name> is already in this window` (S4); one inside a vault of the window → `Can't add <name>: it is inside <vault>`, one that contains a vault of the window → `Can't add <name>: it contains <vault>` (R8, S5 — by path segment, so the vaults of a window never nest and every path has ONE vault); one that is gone on disk → `Can't add <name>: folder not found`, and it leaves the recents (S6); the ninth → `A window holds 8 vaults at most` (R7, S7). The probe is the tree feed's own read (`fetchTree`), so the sidebar's first tree of the new vault is that walk and not a second one; the refusal is asked again after the probe, which waits; any other probe failure still adds, and the sidebar shows the error. A vault that another window shows can be added: both windows then show it (S9). **Remove a vault** (🔒 D7): "Remove from this window" on a vault row is App's `removeVault(root)` — the tabs and the right-panel pages of that vault close as their ✕ closes them (the editor unmounts and its autosave saves the buffer on the way out; NEVER the `retire…` helpers of a delete, which drop the buffer), its focus items leave in the same identity write (A5), the selection clears, and the vaults that stay keep their slots, so nothing of theirs loads again. When the FIRST vault leaves the next one is `root` (S52); the Sidebar mounts again only when the vault it is keyed on left (`sidebarKey` — Reorder the vaults, below). An active tab that was in the vault hands over to its neighbour, and with no tab left the window shows the empty state (S54). Nothing on disk changes — the vault's `favorites.json` neither (S50) — so the item is not `danger` and asks nothing. Rejected: the tabs stay open (a tab with no vault behind it has no links and no properties). The only vault of a window never leaves this way: a window with one vault has no vault row (S51). **A folder that is gone** (R6, S53): a tree read that answers `NOT_FOUND` / `NOT_A_DIRECTORY` for a vault, at launch or during use, is App's `dropVault(root)` — the ONLY vault of the window: the Welcome screen, as before, with no notice; one of several: it leaves as a removed one does, the notice says `<name> is gone: its folder was not found`, and the folder leaves the recents. `store.removePath` never touches `roots` (S49): this probe owns the repair. **A window is on each vault it shows** (🔒 D6 — the rule of YAZ-1767 D9, raise the window and open no copy, kept): the open door `openRecentBeside` — `⌘O`, `⌘1`–`⌘9`, a folder from outside the app — raises every live window that shows the vault, alone or beside others, and opens nothing (S55, S56, S58; rejected: an exact match only, where `⌘O` on B would open a second window on B alone); `resolveLinkTarget` routes a file from outside to the window ONE of whose vaults holds it — the most specific vault wins across windows, a tie keeps the first in `windows[]`, and a `?root=` finds the window that shows that vault (S57) — and answers that vault as the `root`, which is what `rootFor` hands a launch (Restore, above — A8); the `⌘O` panel's "Open" group holds every vault any window shows (S59); "last used" is the window's active vault (Last used, below). **A vault folder renamed elsewhere** (S47, S48): main refuses a rename and a delete of ANY vault root of the calling window; a vault that is a subfolder renamed in ANOTHER window follows — `store.renamePath` moves the entry in `roots`, and on `file:renamed` App mirrors it (`storage.mirrorRoots`, `renameSlots`: the vault keeps its slot and its scope) with no identity write that could clobber the repaired file and tabs. **Files between two vaults** (R11, S45, S46): a drag to a folder or a vault row of a different vault is refused, with the notice `To move between vaults, use cut and paste`; Cut / Copy / Paste between two vaults of one window works as it does between two windows, judged by the vault it lands in. **The other opens are unchanged**: "Open in this window" (`⇧⏎`) replaces ALL the window's vaults with one and resets the tabs (S61); Open Folder… and Open Recent from a window with vaults open beside (S62, YAZ-1914). **Reorder the vaults** (YAZ-2631 🔒 D5, rules R12 and R19–R22, cases S45–S55): a drag of a vault row on the Files tab ("The sidebar lenses": Drag in the tree) is App's `reorderVaults(next)` — ONE identity write, `storage.setRoots`, the vaults in the new order, and `root` follows the first. Each vault keeps its slot and its scope, so no tree, no index and no favorites file is read again, and the tabs, the open folders and the selection stay (S47). The order survives a restart, being the window identity's `roots` (S46); the header's label and a `⌘⇧N` copy follow it (S53, S54); with no stored Favorites order the Favorites tab follows it, and the Focus tab keeps its own order (S55). Where the window's vaults are EXACTLY a saved workspace (`sameVaults` — the order does not count), App then asks `window.saveSet(<its name>)` once, after the identity write, so the workspace takes the new order, keeps its name and its id and moves to the front of the `⌘O` list (S48) — a save that fails is logged (`[reorder-vaults] saveSet failed:`), and the window keeps its order; else only the window changes (S49). THE SIDEBAR'S KEY (R12, R19): `sidebarKey`, the vault that was first when the Sidebar mounted, for as long as that vault is in the window — so a vault that joins, leaves or moves mounts nothing again, the first vault is not always the key, and the Sidebar mounts again only when the key's vault leaves, or on "Open in this window". Rejected: the key on the first vault (a drop at the top mounts the Sidebar again — one tree walk per vault, and a lost selection); a saved workspace that keeps its old order (the next open shows the old order). The `⌘O` request is pinned to that key and not to the first vault (R21): a reorder changes the first vault with no new mount, and the request must not fire again. Known and accepted: with no tab open, the window title and the empty page follow the new first vault. Not included: a keyboard gesture to reorder the vault rows, links between two vaults. The sidebar's half — the vault rows, their menu, Focus, Favorites, search and the Inbox across vaults — is "The sidebar lenses": Several vaults. Proof: `client/src/App.test.tsx` ("App with one vault makes the bridge calls it made before", "App with two vaults keeps one scope per vault", "App adds and removes a vault" — the YAZ-2631 cases of a reorder stand there, and one under "App ⌘O vault switcher"), `client/src/vault/slots.test.ts`, `client/src/lib/storage.test.ts`, `client/src/lib/pageLabel.test.ts` (`useAllPathTitles`, `useFolderPaths`), `client/src/sidebar/Sidebar.test.tsx` ("several vaults in one window"), `desktop/src/main/store.test.ts` ("a window's vault list"), `desktop/src/main/ipc/window.test.ts` ("the vault list"), `desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.test.ts` ("a window that shows several vaults"), `desktop/src/main/windows.test.ts` (the S55–S60 and A8 cases); end to end `desktop/e2e/multiVault.spec.ts` and, for the order of the vault rows, `desktop/e2e/sidebarOrder.spec.ts`, each WRITTEN AND NOT RUN, and the perf scenario `multi-vault` (`tools/perf/README.md`), written and not run — S79 and S80 are measured by hand on an idle machine (`docs/REGRESSION.md` S17).
- **Workspaces** (YAZ-2602 🔒 D8, R12, cases S63–S72): a workspace is a saved set of vaults — `AppState.vaultSets` ("App state"), `VaultSet` in the code, because `workspace` already names the tabs code — saved BY HAND and per machine. WHY by hand: the list holds only what the user chose. Rejected: every window with two or more vaults saves itself (half-built sets fill the list); a workspace file on disk (a new file type and a picker). **Save** (S63, S70): with two or more vaults the header trigger's right-click is the ONE item "Save as workspace…" (`SAVE_SET_TEXT`; a window with one vault has no such item — its trigger's right-click is the vault menu). It turns the header's name into the display name's inline `TextField`, holding EVERY vault name joined with " + " (never the trigger's "+ N more"): `⏎` saves the window's vaults under the name — as it stands, or edited (`TextField`'s `commitOnEnter`) — through `window.saveSet`; a blur saves an edit only; `Esc` or an empty field saves nothing. A name that exists REPLACES that workspace (S69 — the one way to update one by hand: a vault that joins or leaves a window never changes a saved workspace; a drag of a vault row in a window that shows exactly the workspace's vaults saves it again through this same door, so the next open has the new order — YAZ-2631 D5, S48); a NEW name while `MAX_VAULT_SETS` (20) are saved is refused with the notice `Remove a workspace first` (R12, S68 — said by the renderer in its own words, because main's `false` does not say which of its reasons it was); a save says `Saved workspace "<name>"`. **The header** (S15): while the window's vaults are exactly a saved workspace, the order aside, the trigger shows that workspace's name. **The list** (S64): the `⌘O` panel shows the workspaces FIRST, last used first, under the label "Workspaces" (`GROUP_WORKSPACES_TEXT` — a label, not a row, like "Open" and "Not open", which follow as before); with none saved the panel is as it was. A row shows the name and, where a vault shows its time, "N vaults"; the filter matches the name with the vaults' own ranking; the workspace this window shows is `aria-current`. The rows are in the highlight's index space, BEFORE the vault rows — `↑` reaches them, and a typed query starts on the top row, a workspace's when one matches — 🔒 but with an EMPTY query the highlight starts where it did before workspaces, on a vault row: `⌘O ⏎` stays "the vault you used last" (YAZ-2555), and a saved workspace never takes that key over. **Open** (S65, S66): activating a row asks main's `window.openSet(id)` → the manager's `openVaultSet`: a live window whose vaults are EXACTLY the set (`sameVaults` — the order does not count) comes to the front; else ONE new window opens on the vaults, in the saved order, on the first vault's last file; the workspace becomes the last used. A folder of the set that is gone is left out and the notice names it (`Not found: <name>, …`), and the window that matches is the one that shows the vaults that are LEFT — so a window opened earlier without the missing folder is raised, not doubled; the saved workspace keeps the folder. With NO folder left nothing opens, the row is disabled with "Folders not found" (`SETS_MISSING_TEXT`) and the panel stays, as a vault's dead row does. `⇧` means nothing on a workspace: no "Open here" cue, and `⇧⏎` is `⏎`. **The row's menu** (S67 — `buildVaultSetMenuSections`): [Rename · Remove from workspaces], on a dead row too (the workspace is still saved). "Rename" is the word here — a workspace has one name and no folder behind it — the same inline field, saved through `window.renameSet`; an empty field changes nothing, and a name another workspace has is refused with `Can't rename: a workspace has that name`. "Remove from workspaces" is `window.removeSet`, no confirm: the entry is forgotten and the row leaves at once; the folders are not touched. **Repair** (S71): a vault folder renamed or deleted in the app moves in, or leaves, every workspace, and one with fewer than two vaults left is removed ("App state": `vaultSets`). Not included: a `⌘` number for a workspace; workspaces in `yaseendocs vaults`, in a launcher and on the Welcome screen (S72). Proof: `desktop/src/main/store.test.ts` ("vaultSets"), `desktop/src/main/windows.test.ts` ("openVaultSet"), `desktop/src/main/ipc/window.test.ts` ("saved sets of vaults"), `client/src/sidebar/VaultSwitcher.test.tsx` ("workspaces", "several vaults in one window"), `client/src/sidebar/vaultMenuSections.test.ts`, `client/src/lib/storage.test.ts` (`getVaultSets`), `client/src/views/view/TextField.test.tsx` (`commitOnEnter`); end to end steps 6, 8 and 9 of `desktop/e2e/multiVault.spec.ts`, written and not run.
- **Last used** (YAZ-2555 🔒 D5): `AppState.recents` is the order the vaults were last USED in, not only opened in. A `focus` on a vault's window bumps that vault to the top of the MRU, stamped with the time of the use (`noteFocused` → `noteUsed` → `store.pushRecent`) — of a window that shows several vaults, its ACTIVE vault (YAZ-2602 S60, A8 — `activeVault(entry)`: the vault that holds its active file, `rootOfPath`, else its first), and the same vault is what the quit and the last window's close record, below — — so a mouse click on a window, ⌘\` and the macOS window list count exactly like ⌘O, and the switcher's highlight starts on the vault used before this one — an open one first, "Open beside" above — however you got here. Three guards: (1) NOT on a window's FIRST focus — a relaunch focuses every restored window in turn, and that must not reorder the list, so `recents` after a relaunch is what the quit left; (2) NOT during a quit (once `flushAllForQuit` has started) — each window destroyed hands focus to the next; (3) NO write when the vault is already `recents[0]` — focus moving between two windows of ONE vault commits nothing, and neither does a focus on a Welcome window (root `null`, not a vault). Roots compare with the trailing slash off (`stripSlash`, `shared/types.ts` — the rule `resolveLinkTarget`, the door and `openVaultRoots` share), so a window on `/v/` and the MRU's `/v` are one row. `openWindow` bumps the vault it opens on through the same `noteUsed`, and that window's first focus then adds nothing — so "Open in new window" and a deep link's new window are a use of their root; `duplicateWindow` does not bump (the copy is on the vault of the window in front), and the open-recent door bumps through the same `noteUsed`, so going to the vault that is already on top (`⌘<n>` for this window's vault, S25) writes nothing. One bump is ONE store commit: one debounced atomic write, one `state:changed` to every window, one menu rebuild ("Menus & shortcuts"). **The quit records the vault in front** (YAZ-2589 A1): guard (1) means `recents[0]` can be BEHIND the window in front — a window that came back at launch and was never left and re-entered has not bumped its vault — so `flushAllForQuit` bumps the vault of the window that had focus last (`focusOrder[0]`) through the same `noteUsed`, as its first step, before guard (2) starts and before any window goes; `runQuitSequence` flushes the store after the windows, so it is on disk. A Welcome window in front records nothing, and a vault already on top writes nothing. When the LAST window closes — which quits (`window-all-closed`) — `closed` records that window's vault the same way; a close while other windows are open records nothing. So at the next launch `recents[0]` is the vault you were in, which is what `startupWindows: 'last'` brings back (Restore, above). The one launch this cannot cover: the first after updating from a version that did not record it — `recents[0]` is then what focus and ⌘O last left. File › Open Recent, Welcome's list, the vault switcher's order and its row times all read this one list. Proof: `desktop/src/main/windows.test.ts` ("last used", and "close" for the last window).
- **The vault list** (YAZ-2556 🔒 D2): `listVaults(state)` (`shared/types.ts`) is every vault the app knows, as `VaultEntry` rows `{ path, name, key, open, lastUsed }` — `name` the display name else the folder's name (`basename`), `key` the number or `null`, `open` whether a window is on it (`openVaultRoots`: a window is on EVERY vault it shows, YAZ-2602 D6; a Welcome window is no vault, `/v/` and `/v` are one; a window read straight off an old state file has no `roots`, and its `root` answers), `lastUsed` its time in the recents (epoch ms) or `null`. ORDER: the recents, last used first; then the open vaults that are not among them, in `windows[]` order; then the numbered vaults that are in neither, in number order. A `folders` entry that is not recent, not open and has no number is no vault and is not listed. ONE list with two readers, so they cannot disagree: the ⌘O panel (`storage.listVaults()`, which then stands the open rows first — Open beside, above) and the command line's `yaseendocs vaults`, which prints the list in THIS order, not the panel's "open first". **`keyedVaults(folders)`** (`shared/types.ts`; `KeyedVault` rows `{ key, path, name }`) is the numbered part on its own — the folders that have a number, in number order, each by its display name else its folder name: the Window menu's rows (`menuKeyedVaults`), the "Set shortcut" flyout (`storage.keyedVaults()`) and `listVaults` all call it, where each had a copy of its own, so the three name and order a numbered vault the same way. **`yaseendocs vaults [--json]`** (`desktop/src/cli/cli.ts` — the door of "Comments"; the one verb that takes no page): without `--json` one line per vault — `<name>  <path>`, then `  ⌘<n>` when it has number n, then `  (open)` when a window is on it — or `no vaults`; with it the rows as a 2-space-indented JSON array, each with its keys in the order above, `[]` for none.
