It only READS, and only the app's state file — never writes it, never creates it, never moves it aside, and never talks to the app: the file is `yaseendocs.json` in `userDataDir(env, platform, home)` (`desktop/src/main/userData.ts` — the `YASEEN_DOCS_USER_DATA_DIR` override when set, else the folder Electron gives an app of this name; "App state"), read through the store's own `parseState`, so the command sees the state the app would load, field for field. No file (the app never ran) → no vaults, exit 0; a file `parseState` refuses → `<file> is not a valid app state` on stderr, exit 1. So the answer is the same with the app running or not: with the app closed, `open` is a window the app opens again at its start, and with it running a change is in the answer once the store's debounced write has landed (150 ms — atomic, so the command reads the old file or the new one, never half of one). `HELP` says the order, each field and that the command never writes the file. The list is a door for anything outside the app — a script, an agent, a launcher: with the folder door of "Links": Routing, `open -a "Yaseen Docs" <path>` on a row's `path` goes to that vault. A row is listed whether or not its folder is still there (`listVaults` never looks at the disk), and the app holds no launcher's code. Proof: `client/src/lib/storage.test.ts` (`listVaults`; `storage.keyedVaults`), `desktop/src/main/menu.test.ts` (the menu's rows), `client/src/sidebar/VaultSwitcher.test.tsx` (the panel over `storage.listVaults()`), `desktop/src/cli/cli.test.ts` ("vaults": the rows and their order, the plain lines, no file, a damaged file, the override, and that not a byte or the mtime of the file changes — against a state file the real store wrote in a temp folder) and `desktop/src/main/userData.test.ts` (`userDataDir`).
- **Window title** (C3, GRO-2165; the order is YAZ-2555 🔒 D6): `windowTitle(vault, file, titles, folder)` (`client/src/lib/windowTitle.ts`; App sets it on `document.title` and Electron follows) is `<vault> — <page>`, the VAULT FIRST; the vault alone while no page is open; `Yaseen Docs` (`APP_NAME`) on the Welcome screen. In a window that shows several vaults `<vault>` is the ACTIVE vault — the one that holds the tab in front, else the first (YAZ-2602 R3, S36) — and the title follows that vault's name, index and tree. ⚡ D6 OVERTURNS C3's Obsidian-style order, which put the page first: macOS cuts a long title at the END, and with several vaults open the vault is the part you look for in ⌘\`, the Window menu's window list and the Dock's. `<vault>` is the vault's NAME — its display name, else its folder name (YAZ-1974 D4), never its path — and `<page>` is `pageLabel` (YAZ-2420 D14: the note's or folder's title, else its file name, Markdown's extension hidden). Proof: `client/src/lib/windowTitle.test.ts`, `client/src/App.test.tsx` ("App window title"), and e2e the `titleOf` helpers of `vaultSwitcher.spec.ts` and `scenarios.spec.ts` and the two title checks of `smoke.spec.ts`.
- **Tabs** (GRO-2232/2234/2235): `WindowEntry.tabs` is the ordered main-pane set and `file` is its active path. A tab is a FILE or — since YAZ-2290 D3, overturning 🔒 D3 of YAZ-1578 — a FOLDER, whose path is the directory's ("Folders are the pages"); its label, like a note's, is its title (`lib/pageLabel.ts`, YAZ-2420 D14), and its menu offers Copy path like a note's. `normalizeTabs` repairs legacy/malformed identity; `tabs: []` iff `file: null`, the active path belongs to the set, and paths are unique. The renderer model now lives inside `client/src/workspace/useWorkspace.ts` with the right panel; it mirrors one complete workspace identity per logical change — N tabs that close together are ONE change (`close-many`, a fold over `close`, as `delete-dir` is; `closeMany`), so ONE write — while `folders[root].lastFile` — of the vault that HOLDS the file, YAZ-2602 — changes only when the active main file changes. The strip holds tabs of every vault of the window, and a switch between two vaults costs what a switch inside one costs, because every vault's scope stays loaded (YAZ-2602 D1, S29, S80). ⌘-click appends in background, activation lazy-mounts one retained editor layer, drag reorders, middle-click/✕ closes, and ⌘W follows right-neighbour then left-neighbour then empty-window then close-window. Per-tab Back/Forward history remains renderer-session-only. **The preview tab** (⚡ YAZ-2648 D1–D4, R1–R8, cases S1–S30 on the issue — OVERTURNING "Sidebar/deep-link opens replace the current tab", rule 4 of GRO-2234): an open from the sidebar — a click on a row of Files, Focus or Favorites, a click or Enter on a search result, a note made in the sidebar (R2) — and an open from a deep link go to the ONE preview tab (`TabsState.preview`; `open-current` in `tabsReducer`). With no preview tab the page gets a NEW tab at the END of the strip (R7); with one, the page takes that tab's slot wherever it stands — its stack gains the page — and the tab becomes the active one; a path that is open already is activated and nothing is replaced. Every other tab is a KEPT tab, and a kept tab is never replaced. What KEEPS the preview tab (D2): a double click on its tab or on its file row (`Tree` `onKeepFile` → the workspace's `openKept`, the ONE door for a kept tab: `open-new` takes the mark off first when the page is the preview tab's; with ⇧ or ⌘ the double click keeps nothing, R6); the first text the user types in its body (R3, R4: `Editor`'s `onUserEdit`, said when the autosave turns `unsaved` — a reload from disk makes the reloaded text the baseline first, so an edit made outside the app keeps nothing, and neither does a property, a comment or the title); and a drag of the tab to a new place (`move`). Opened KEPT from the start: ⌘-click and "Open N in new tabs" (`open-background`), a folder row's double click and its menu's Open (`open-new`, R1 — Enter on a folder row goes by the preview rule), a page that comes from the right panel (R8), and every tab a launch or ⌘⇧N brings back. A link INSIDE a page — a wiki link, a "Linked mentions" row, a row link of a folder's view — is `navigate` (D3), the rule every open had before: the page takes the ACTIVE tab's slot with history, kept tab or preview tab alike, and Back returns; Back and Forward in the preview tab leave it the preview tab (R5). The mark follows a rename and a folder rename, and goes with a close, a delete and a move to the right panel. **The blank tab** (YAZ-2655 D10, D11, R19–R22, cases S70–S82): File › New Tab (⌘T) and the strip's "+" show ONE tab named "New tab", last and active, over the empty page a window with no tabs has — every visited tab's layer stays mounted, hidden, under it — and put the caret in the sidebar's search bar by ⌘K's own door (`openSearch`). `TabsState.blank` is all there is of it: it is NOT in `tabs`, and while it shows, `active` — the stored `file` — is still the last real active tab, so `tabs: []` iff `file: null` holds in every mirror. The next page opened (`open-current`, `open-new`) FILLS it: a KEPT tab at the end, where the blank tab stood; a page that is open already is activated and the blank tab goes; a trip to another tab drops it unused; ⌘W, its ✕ and a middle click close it alone, and the tab that was active is active again; ⌘-click under it appends a kept background tab and it stays active; a second ⌘T only takes the caret back to the bar; opening the board drops it; under it the window title is the vault's alone and the sidebar highlights no row; ⌘T during a review does nothing. NOTHING of the two is stored (D4): `preview` and `blank` are renderer- and session-only, like the histories; `keep`, `new-tab` and `close-blank` mirror nothing; `WindowEntry` and main did not change; after a restart, and in a duplicated window, every tab is a kept tab and there is no blank tab. Proof: `client/src/workspace/useWorkspace.test.tsx`, `tabs/TabBar.test.tsx`, `App.test.tsx`, `sidebar/Sidebar.test.tsx`, `editor/Editor.test.tsx` (the edit signal); `desktop/e2e/tabs.spec.ts` was rewritten for these rules with YAZ-2658 and is not evidence until it has been run once. While the sidebar is collapsed the strip's nav row carries one button more, FIRST, **Show sidebar** (`aria-label="Show sidebar"`, the same `.tabbar-nav__btn` dress as ◀ ▶ and the grid button), rendered by TabBar from App's `onShowSidebar` (absent while the sidebar is open) — it replaced the floating `.sidebar-reopen` button that used to sit over Back (YAZ-1759), so nothing overlaps ◀. **The strip** (`client/src/tabs/`, `.tabbar`; the look is YAZ-2656 D12 as R40–R43 changed it — Linear's bar, in place of the underline-active tablist): the row (`.tabbar-row`, which the review bar wears too) is 44 px high, and every tab is a rounded PILL centred in it — 32 px high, 200 px wide, radius 8, gap 4, label 14 px — in three fills, inactive / hover / active, set per theme at the top of `tabs.css` (`--tab-row`, `--tab-fill`, `--tab-hover`, `--tab-active`, `--tab-edge`, `--tab-text`: each a theme token or a `color-mix()` of two, so the strip follows the theme with no colour of its own; in the light theme the row takes the sidebar's tint and the active pill wears `--accent` — 16% in `--bg`, with an edge at 45% — and in the dark theme the pills step up from `--bg`). There is no underline and no line between tabs; the preview tab's label is italic (`.tabbar__tab--preview`); a long label FADES OUT at its end (`mask-image`), with no ellipsis; the ✕ shows on hover and on the active tab, 18 px round, by `visibility`, so the label never moves when it appears. Tabs shrink to a 140 px floor first and then the strip SCROLLS, with the ACTIVE tab `scrollIntoView`n on every activation. The tablist is as wide as its tabs, so the "+" (`.tabbar__new`, `aria-label="New tab"`, `title="New tab (⌘T)"`) stands right after the last tab and OUTSIDE the scroller — on show however far the strip scrolled — and `.tabbar-tail` takes the rest of the row and is the end slot of a drop. With the right panel closed the row keeps 44 px free at its end (`reserveEnd` → `.tabbar-row--end`) for the floating "Show right panel" button (`.right-panel-reopen`, `top: 9px; right: 9px`, centred in the row). The nav buttons and the "+" are 28 px with 16 px icons; right of ▶ stands the grid button (`aria-label="Show all open tabs"`, `title="Show all open tabs (⌘⇧M)"`, `aria-pressed` while the board shows — YAZ-2648 D8). **The slide:** a tab that JOINS the strip slides open in about 150 ms (`.tabbar__tab--in` — only when the count of tabs grew: a page that takes a tab's place, or fills the blank tab, does not slide), and one that leaves slides shut as a GHOST (`.tabbar__tab--out`: a label with no role and no button, dropped after `SLIDE_MS` — the ONE source of that time: the row hands it to the stylesheet as `--tab-slide-ms`); with `prefers-reduced-motion`, neither. The ghosts and the tabs are ONE keyed list, so a tab that closes or moves builds no other tab again: a later tab keeps its element, and its slide does not play twice. **The gap of a drag:** the tabs MOVE APART at the drop slot (`.tabbar__tab--gap-before` / `--gap-after`, the tab's own transparent border, so the pointer stays over the tab it parted and the slot does not flicker; no gap on either side of the grabbed tab) — in place of the accent insertion edge, and the ORDER a drop gives is what it always was. **The lit tab** (R45): the tab of the page the tab board highlights wears `.tabbar__tab--lit` (the hover fill and an edge in `--accent`) and is scrolled into view; the path lives in `tabs/boardHighlight.ts`, a store of its own, so a move of the highlight renders the strip and nothing else. The slide, the gap and the lit tab are TabBar's own state: no editor renders for them. Since YAZ-1471 neither the scrolling nor the reordering arithmetic is spelled here alone. The scroll is the shared `.scroll-strip` utility in `app.css`, worn beside `.tabbar` and by a folder's view tabs too ("View chrome": Toolbar): `overflow-x: auto` with NO scrollbar (`scrollbar-width: none` is the whole hiding rule on Chromium ≥ 121, so the old `::-webkit-scrollbar` rules are gone), plus a `@keyframes strip-fade` mask driven by `animation-timeline: scroll(self inline)` — a scroll-driven fade marking whichever edge still hides tabs, and a strip that does not overflow has no timeline and so no mask at all. The insertion-slot idiom this strip invented is now `client/src/lib/dragSlot.ts` — `insertionSlot(e, i)` (the target's own horizontal midpoint) and `dropIndex(from, slot)` (the grabbed item leaves the list before it re-enters, so a slot past the grab point shifts one left) — ONE spelling for both horizontal strips, so the window's tabs and a folder's view tabs cannot drift apart; the validated right→main insertion slots above run on it, and so does the view tabs' own reorder.
- **The tab board** (YAZ-2648 D5–D9 and D13–D15, R9–R18, R23–R39, R44, R45, cases S31–S69 on the issue; `client/src/tabs/TabOverview.tsx`, `board.ts`, `useTabHeads.ts`, `TabMenu.tsx`, the board's half of `tabs.css`): every open MAIN tab of the window as a small page, in the tab stack's place while it shows; the strip stays above it. Whether it shows is App's renderer state for the session (`overview.phase`: `closed` / `open` / `leaving`) and is never stored. **Open and close:** the strip's grid button and Window › Tab Overview (⌘⇧M) toggle it; it does not open during a review, a review that starts closes it (R14), and the Welcome window has none. Under it App hides the active tab's layer the way every other layer is hidden (`.tabstack__layer--hidden`) and unmounts nothing, so each page keeps its scroll, caret, undo history and unsaved text. It leaves WITH a zoom on a click on a page, Enter, Esc, the grid button, ⌘⇧M and a click on a tab of the strip; it leaves AT ONCE on every other trip to a page — a sidebar click, ⌃Tab, a deep link (R12, R31: the effect on the active file) — and a trip to the page ALREADY open is a trip too (S46): the effect knows one only when the active file moves, so the doors of those trips close the board themselves (App's `openPage`, `openKeptPage`, `toNextTab`, `toPrevTab`), and a row of the sidebar asks for its page also when that page is the open one, which the workspace answers with no change and no write. ⌘W closes the active tab, and with it the board (R13). A ✕ on a page, on an island's label row or on a tab of the strip closes those tabs by the ordinary close path — the autosave flush is the same — in ONE change of the workspace (`close-many`), and leaves the board open, the active tab's too (the board marks a change of the active tab it made itself, `ownChange`); when its LAST tab closes the board closes with it, and the window shows the empty state (R18). "Move to right panel" leaves it open from the menu of a tab of the strip as from a page's (R30, one door: `movePageToRight`), and so does a rename or a move of the open page, or of a folder above it. **The zoom** (D9, R35): opening, the active tab's layer shrinks onto its own page of the board (`.tabstack__layer--zoom-out`, a class on the hidden layer) while the board settles in around that page (`data-zoom="in"`); leaving, both run back onto the chosen page (`.tabstack__layer--zoom-in`, `data-zoom="out"`), and `ZOOM_MS` (250) later the board says it left. Transform and opacity only; the point, the scale and the time (`--tab-zoom-ms`, from `ZOOM_MS`) are custom properties the board sets on `.tabstack`, where its own animation and the page layer's both read them; the point is read by layout offsets; no editor renders for it. With `prefers-reduced-motion` — and where there is no `matchMedia` — there is no animation and the board closes at once. **Pages and islands** (D9, R9, R26, R27, R38): a page is 200 × 266 px and shows the title — italic for the preview tab, an accent line along the foot of the active tab's page (R32) — and, for a note, its first lines. Pages stand on ISLANDS, one per folder that DIRECTLY holds them (`buildBoard`: `Clients/Acme` and `Clients/Globex` are two), labelled with the whole folder path in its vault joined with " / ", then the count; the vault's top level is named by the vault; a long label loses its start first; with two or more vaults each vault's name stands above its islands, and with one there is no vault label; a page in no vault of the window is last, on an island named by its folder. Islands stand side by side and wrap; an island sets three pages in a row at most, four as two and two. The grouping is read off the paths: nothing is stored. **The first lines** (D6): ONE `readHeads` call ("Bridge API") for every note tab when the board opens; `useTabHeads` holds the answers for the session by path and mtime, never more than the open tabs, shows what it holds at once the next time, and asks alone for a tab that joins; a call that fails takes no text away; `plainLines` takes Markdown's marks off in the renderer — a heading's, a bullet's, emphasis of one mark or two around a word, a link's, a tag's, a table row's outer bars, and a code fence's own lines — and no text with them (`a < b` stays); a first line that only repeats the title is not said twice (R15, `withoutTitle`); a folder, a PDF, an image and view-only text show a title alone (R16). **The stack rule** (D9, R23, R33, R34, R44 — `stackedIslands`): an island shows as a STACK — its pages as one, fanned — only when BOTH hold: the board does not fit its area, AND the island has `BOARD.stackMin` (4) or more pages. Islands stack largest first, the active tab's island last, until the board fits; then it scrolls. A click spreads a stack until the board closes, and text in the filter spreads every one. The rule measures no page: it computes the board's height from the sizes of `board.ts` — the same numbers `TabOverview` hands the stylesheet as custom properties — and its one input from the screen is the scroller's own box (a `ResizeObserver`), which the window sizes and the pages do not, so the answer cannot change the question and the rule cannot loop; a size that differs between `board.ts` and `tabs.css` makes the board stack a little early or scroll a little. **Keys** (R10, R11, R24): the focus stays in the filter box; typing narrows the pages by title and by path in the vault, and with nothing left the board says one line; the four arrows move the highlight to the nearest page that way (`nearest`) and never the caret, and onto a stack they spread it; Enter opens the highlight; the pointer moves the same highlight, which starts on the active tab's page, and on a stack it highlights the page the stack shows and spreads nothing (a click spreads). For a screen reader the filter box is a `combobox` over the `listbox` of pages (`aria-controls`), and `aria-activedescendant` names the highlighted stop — a page or a stack, each an `option` with an id. An island's ✕ shows under the pointer and on keyboard focus (by `opacity`, so the Tab key gets to it). **Peek** (D13, R36): Space, held, shows the highlighted page large, and lets go of it on release; it activates nothing, closes nothing and reads nothing new. Space is a peek while the filter is empty, a space while a query is being typed, and a peek again once an arrow or the pointer moved the highlight. **Drag to the side** (D14, R30): a page drags with the strip's own payload (`pageDrag`, owner `main`); while it is dragged a drop area along the board's right edge reads "Drop here to open it beside your page", and a drop there — or on the right panel itself — moves the tab to the right panel (`transferMainToRight`), and the board stays. **Pick** (D15, R37): ⇧-click picks a page and never opens it; while pages are picked a bar at the bottom left reads "N picked", "Close them", "Close the others", "Clear"; both closes drop the pick and leave the board open; Esc drops the pick first, and leaves the board on the next press; a plain click still opens. The pick is the board's own and goes with it. **Menus** (D9, R28, R29): a right-click on a page opens the tab's own menu — `TabMenu`, the ONE spelling the strip's tabs open too (Move to right panel, Show in sidebar, Copy path, the review toggle, Reveal in Finder, Open in VS Code) — and on one of two or more picked pages "Close N tabs" and "Close the others" instead; on an island's label row "Show in sidebar" (the folder's row; for a vault's top level the vault's row, which exists only with two or more vaults), "Copy path" and "Close N tabs". A reveal in the sidebar leaves the board open. The pages of the right panel are not on the board (R17), and neither is the blank tab (R19). Proof: `tabs/board.test.ts` (islands, the stack rule, the arrows, the title line), `tabs/TabOverview.test.tsx`, `tabs/TabBar.test.tsx` (the shared menu, the lit tab), `App.test.tsx`, `desktop/src/main/fs/heads.test.ts`; step 3b of `desktop/e2e/tabs.spec.ts` was written with YAZ-2658 and is not evidence until it has been run once.
- **Right panel** (YAZ-966): `client/src/workspace/useWorkspace.ts` is the renderer's ONE owner for main tabs, right headers, both session histories, retained mounts, and lifecycle repair. The durable per-window identity is `{ open, width, items, expanded }` beside `{ tabs, file }`; legacy entries gain the default, width is clamped to 320–720 px (440 default), and normalization enforces absolute unique paths plus global-in-window exclusive ownership. Every logical mutation mirrors ONE complete `{ tabs, file, rightPanel }` identity. The panel reuses the full `Editor` and the existing watch/index/properties/sync feeds — of the vault that holds each page, so a page of any vault of the window opens there and uses its own vault (YAZ-2602 S32, `editorPropsFor`); one right header is expanded at most, visited expanded headers retain hidden editor layers, and plain/⌘ link navigation plus Back/Forward stay right-local. Ownership transfers call `carryEditorAcrossPane` before the source unmounts or the identity mirrors, so dirty text crosses without a second editor/cache. Entry gestures are the shared Table/Board `PageContextMenu` (`Open in right panel` LAST since YAZ-1556 — the pane-specific open sits below the page actions every surface shares), ⌥-click or ⌥⏎ on a board card or a table name (YAZ-1557 🔒 D1 — ONE rule in `client/src/lib/openGesture.ts` for both views, mouse and keyboard: plain = current tab (the tab the view stands in: a link inside a page, so `navigate` since YAZ-2648 D3, never the preview tab), ⌘ = background tab (I3), ⌥ = right panel, ⇧/⌃ = nothing, because ⇧ is the selection gesture everywhere; a plain click on a board card no longer opens anything — it SELECTS the card, 🔒 D2 below under "Views" — and neither does one on a table's Name cell, plain text since YAZ-2420 D26, where the rule is its ⌘/⌥-click and Enter; secondary-click, group drag, and preview remain separate), tab/header move commands, and native drag under private MIME `application/x-yaseen-workspace-page`; only validated absolute `{ path, owner: 'main' | 'right' }` payloads enter midpoint/end insertion slots, stale/wrong/external data no-op. The divider previews locally and persists only on commit; below 192 px hides without losing width, while narrow available space derives an overlay without rewriting identity. Rename/delete/root repair covers current paths, retained mounts, expanded choice, and histories; delete retires autosave before workspace removal, rename/transfer carries before remap, and duplicate windows deep-copy identity so later changes are independent. Automated ownership/drag/shell/lifecycle proof lives in `client/src/workspace/*.test.*`, `client/src/right-panel/RightPanel.test.tsx`, `client/src/tabs/TabBar.test.tsx`, `client/src/App.test.tsx`, and the desktop store/window/IPC tests. Since YAZ-2171 one pointed Playwright spec, `desktop/e2e/rightPanel.spec.ts`, pins move-in, resize, hide/show and the relaunch restore; final acceptance still uses the isolated Electron profile and focused demo vault before merge.
- **Single instance** (GRO-2160): `requestSingleInstanceLock` — a second launch quits itself; the first instance gets `second-instance`, routes any `yaseendocs://` URLs in its argv (E1), else focuses a window.
- **Close/quit flush handshake** (GRO-2160): `close` is ALWAYS intercepted — main commits the bounds, sends `app:flush`, the renderer flushes its autosaves (`window.onFlush` listeners: the note editor's own, and App's `flushWindow`, which flushes the outline editor from its live document and then awaits every in-flight `transformFile` frontmatter write, YAZ-2174) and acks `app:flushed`, then main `destroy()`s the window; a hung save can never block close (hard 5 s cap). A user close removes the window's state entry; the LAST window closing quits (`window-all-closed`), and quits — like ⌘Q's flush of every window, all at once under one 5 s cap (YAZ-2198) — keep the entries, so the next launch can bring them back; WHICH of them it brings back is Restore, above (YAZ-2589), and what it does not is forgotten then, at the launch — never at the quit, so nothing unsaved is at risk. A quit also records the vault of the window in front, and the last window's close records that window's vault (YAZ-2589 A1 — Last used, above). ⌘Q is `runQuitSequence` (`main/quitSequence.ts`, YAZ-2174): the windows first, then `allSettled` of the state file, the index cache and the last sync pass, then `app.exit(0)`; a rejected step skips none of the others.
- **Same file in N windows** (GRO-2169 — D6/D9/A4): no dedicated sync channel — the disk-change path IS the sync. A window's save lands on disk → the shared watcher emits `change` to every window → each other window reloads silently when clean, or shows the conflict bar (Reload / Keep mine) when dirty. Echo suppression is per window: each window's own `Autosave` controller holds the mtime from ITS last `writeFile` response and only drops events matching that mtime (client `editor/Editor.tsx` — the one host left since YAZ-844 retired `BaseHost`), so window A's save is never mistaken for window B's echo. Folds are shared per file through the app state (`folders[root].folds`) — last writer wins, applied the next time the file is opened; a live window never adopts ANOTHER window's keys. ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-1342: a window's OWN silent reload does re-fold, from its LIVE session keys — both fold plugins take `seedCollapsedKeys`, a getter read on every state init rather than a set frozen at mount — so folds on unchanged lines survive an external (AI) edit to the open file and the rebuilt plugin can no longer report an empty set and wipe the persisted keys. ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-1347: that silent reload is no longer a rebuild — `applyExternalMarkdown` (`editor/external/`) parses the new body, structurally diffs it against the live doc (`diffDocs.ts`) and dispatches ONE `addToHistory: false` transaction stamped `external-edit`, so folds, caret, zoom and scroll ride ProseMirror's position mapping through ANY reword of a collapsed line, and the persisted keys re-derive from the live labels afterwards. The content-hash keys (rule 5) now serve cold start and the fallback only: a whole-document rewrite (one range ≥ 80% of the doc), an unparsable body, or a diff that fails to reproduce the parsed doc exactly falls back to the `setMarkdown` rebuild and its key-based reseed, so no edit ever behaves worse than v0.9.1. Enumeration prefixes (`12) `, `3. `, `X) `) are stripped from key labels (YAZ-1353), so a renumber done while the app is closed keeps cold-start folds too. Zoom is per window and in-memory only, never persisted. One watcher per folder in main, shared by every window and subscription (`desktop/src/main/fs/watchers.ts`); it closes when the last subscriber leaves.

## Links (E1/E2/E3 — GRO-2171/2172/2173)

- **Format** (E1, locked D7): a note's shareable URL is its `yaseendocs://` file link — path-only, absolute, percent-encoded (`yaseendocs:///Users/me/vault/My%20note.md`), no vault id; an optional `?root=` (also a percent-encoded absolute path) overrides which folder it opens under. `shared/links.ts` (`fileLink`/`parseFileLink`) is the ONE encoding, shared by main's parser and its own `open-file` re-encode (E2); `encodeURI` plus `#` and `?` encoded on top, so either never truncates the path. The path may also name a FOLDER (YAZ-2556 🔒 D1 — Routing, below): the same encoding, a trailing slash allowed and taken off, and a `?root=` on it ignored. No gesture in the app writes a folder link; main's `open-file` re-encode is the one maker.
- **Copy link** — TOMBSTONE (YAZ-1554): the sidebar row menu's "Copy link" (E3 GRO-2173, a `yaseendocs://` URL; a `[[wikilink]]` since YAZ-957) was deleted. It sat directly under "Copy path" and the two were easy to confuse, and `[[` already links from inside a note. Everything that existed only for it went with it: `MenuTargets.copyLinkText`, the sidebar's `viewOnlyLinks` prop, and `linkName` on the view-only source and catalog. Main's `yaseendocs://` handler and `shared/links.ts` are untouched.
- **Routing** (E1): a link — clicked anywhere (`open-url`), handed over by Finder's Open With (`open-file`, E2 — the plain path is re-encoded through `fileLink` and rides the same pipeline), or in a second launch's argv — routes identically (`resolveLinkTarget`): (1) the open window ONE of whose vaults contains the file (YAZ-2602 🔒 D6: a window is on each vault it shows — "Multi-window": Several vaults in one window) — the most specific vault wins across every window, a tie keeps the first window in `windows[]`, Welcome windows never match — is focused and opens it as a tab (`link.onOpenFile`); (2) else a new window opens on the most recent recents folder containing it (`?root=` pins the effective root instead when it contains the path); (3) else on the file's parent directory. Either answer says the vault's `root` (`{ kind: 'existing', id, root }` / `{ kind: 'new', root, file }`) — what `rootFor` hands a launch (YAZ-2589 🔒 D1), and where a new window opens when the window it picked is already gone (a mid-close race). **A FOLDER** (YAZ-2556 🔒 D1) is not a file to route: `routeToFile(path, rootOverride)` asks `host.dirExists(path)` FIRST, and a directory that comes from outside the app — a `yaseendocs://` link to it, or `open -a "Yaseen Docs" <folder>`, which arrives as `open-file` and rides the same re-encode — is "Open Folder…" on it. It goes to the ONE open-recent door, `openRecentBeside(stripSlash(path))` ("Multi-window": Open beside): the vault is bumped in the MRU and its windows are raised, a minimized one restored, nothing sent to them; with none, a new window opens on its `folders[root].lastFile`; a folder the app never opened opens as a vault, on no file, and enters the recents. A folder INSIDE an open vault opens as its OWN vault window — the vault that contains it is not raised and is sent nothing (what Open Folder… does on it, YAZ-1914; a path from outside never means the folder's page inside its vault). `resolveLinkTarget` is not asked, so a `?root=` on a folder link is IGNORED. The trailing slash a link keeps (`parseFileLink` does not trim) is taken off before the door, because the door keys the vault's `folders` bucket and its MRU entry by the path it is given: `/v/wiki/` and `/v/wiki` are one vault — one bucket, one recents row, the same last file. The probe is what is on disk, not the name: a directory called `folder.pdf` is a folder. NOT claimed: a folder dropped on the app's Dock icon — the bundle declares no folder document type ("Packaging"), and that case (YAZ-2556 S8) is open. Links queue until the launch has brought its windows back on cold start (`linkQueue.ts` — macOS fires `open-url` before `ready`), and what waits decides WHICH windows those are (YAZ-2589 🔒 D1: only the saved windows of the vaults that were asked for — "Multi-window": Restore). The request then routes exactly as it does hot — a folder too: the door finds that vault's restored window, or opens one — with ONE difference a running app does not have: the windows are still loading, so the push (`link.onOpenFile`, or the notice) is held until the window's page says `link.ready` (A2 — the bridge table), and so is a push to a window that an earlier request in the same launch just opened. With the app running nothing changes: the vault is raised or opened, and no other window is touched. Proof of the folder door: `desktop/src/main/windows.test.ts` ("routeToFile": S1, S2 with and without the slash, S4, S5 with a `?root=`, a folder named like a file); what macOS hands over, and the window coming in front of another app, are by hand (`docs/REGRESSION.md` P6).
- **Failure surface** (E1): an unsupported, missing or non-regular-file target — or an unparsable link — focuses a live window and shows the passive in-window notice (`link.onNotice`), never a dialog: `Can't open <path>: unsupported file type` for a path with no supported kind (asked first), `Can't open <path>: file not found` for a supported name that is no live regular file, `Can't open link: <url>` for a link that does not parse. The first two reasons come from ONE check (`cannotOpen` in `windows.ts`, YAZ-2589 A6), which `rootFor` asks too: at a launch a target that cannot open, like a link that does not parse, asks for no vault, so the launch is a plain launch and the notice shows in a window that came back ("Multi-window": Restore). With NO live window `linkNotice` opens the Welcome window and says it there (A2: the app never runs with no window; not during a quit, which has none on purpose) — since A6 the safety net for one case, a folder that goes away between the launch's check and the open. The notice is a link push, so it waits for the window's page like `link.onOpenFile` (`link.ready`, the bridge table). A FOLDER is no failure since YAZ-2556 D1 — it opens as a vault (Routing, above) — and a folder that is GONE is not a directory any more, so it gets the notice its name earns, in practice "unsupported file type" (S6: unchanged on purpose). WHICH window (`linkNotice`, YAZ-2555 S27): the one that had focus last, else the first live one — restored when minimized. The Window menu's ⌘<n> on a vault whose folder is gone speaks through the same surface (`Can't open <name>: folder not found`), so it says so in the window you are in and raises no other vault's window — that focus would make the other vault "last used" (D5). Every supported Markdown/text/PDF/image file may route through the existing window/new-window pipeline.
- **In-window wiki-link navigation** (Links C, GRO-2192) is RENDERER-LOCAL: clicking a `[[wikilink]]` in the editor drives this window's own tabs API (`navigate` / `openBackground`, Editor rule 23 — `navigate` since YAZ-2648 D3: the sidebar's own open, `openCurrent`, goes to the preview tab now, and a link inside a page keeps the rule it had) — no IPC, no main-process involvement; the manager's `routeToFile` (the Routing bullet above, `windows.ts`) stays the ONLY path that focuses another window or creates one.
- **Aliases** (Links E2, GRO-2214): a note's frontmatter `aliases` are extra NAMES it answers to across the whole app — the index carries them (`IndexRecord.aliases`, table below), the ONE resolver consults them LAST (see "Link resolution"), so `[[CAC]]` renders resolved (Editor rule 23) and clicking it opens the aliased page, and the `[[` picker offers the page under each alias, inserting the piped `[[Name|Alias]]` — or, for a note that has an `id`, the plain `[[<id>]]` (rule 24, YAZ-2293). Two consequences pinned elsewhere: alias values are never outgoing `links`, and a rename never rewrites an alias-form link (Rename, below).
- **Note ids** (YAZ-2293, YAZ-2677 — see "Note ids"): a link whose target is a note's frontmatter `id` — `[[YAZ-12]]`, or an old `[[k3m9x2pq7abc]]` — names the NOTE, not its name or its place. The ONE resolver tries the id FIRST (see "Link resolution"), in any case (`[[yaz-12]]`, S17) and by letters the vault had before (`[[OLD-12]]`, `ResolverOptions.letters`, S82), and a target that is the id of NO note falls through to the names, so `[[YAZ-7]]` still opens a note that is only named so (S26); the app WRITES a link that way whenever the note has an id (the `[[` picker, rule 24; a view's link editors, "Cell editing"); the editor, the backlinks snippets and every view show the note's current title in the id's place (rules 23 and 25; "Note ids": What a link reads as); and no rename ever rewrites one (Rename, below). A link written by name keeps working exactly as it did, and a note with no id is still linked by name.
- **Rename** (E1, GRO-2194 — decision E of GRO-2096, LOCKED): an in-app rename updates links AUTOMATICALLY, no prompt (Obsidian's default). ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-888 for NAME changes only: a changed NAME now asks first, once, in one sheet — the links still ALWAYS update, and a MOVE asks only when it would clear a folder's values (D21 — "Folder settings": Asking first), so everything below is unchanged and runs on confirm ("The page title + rename confirm"). Scope since E1b (GRO-2241): FILE rename and cross-directory move, and FOLDER rename — the extension kind stays unchanged for files; E1b lifted E1's same-parent and files-only guards. Gestures: the sidebar file- OR folder-row context menu's "Rename" → inline input over the row (a NOTE or a FOLDER: prefilled with its TITLE, free text, and the commit is a title edit — YAZ-2420 D16, "The page title + rename confirm" — or, where the vault does not use IDs, with its name, held to `validateEntryName`, and the commit is a rename of that name, YAZ-2523 V3, "Two kinds of vault"; any other file: prefilled with its full name, `validateEntryName` + the extension re-append via `renamedPath`; the same text = no-op; no hotkey v1); and drag a FILE row onto a folder row or the root header → move there (same pipeline; no multi-select, no folder dragging — Future; Cut ⌘X / Paste ⌘V, YAZ-1674, moves a multi-selection and folders through the same rename pipeline). Flow, in order: (1) the originating window FLUSHES its own autosave for the file, so the buffer travels with it; (2) it snapshots the vault index BEFORE renaming (afterwards the old name no longer resolves); (3) `file.rename` renames on disk, repairs the app state and broadcasts `file:renamed` (bridge table above) — for a title edit `file.retitle` stands in its place, writing `title:` first and answering with the path it built (YAZ-2420 D16); (4) every window's renderer — BEFORE remapping its tab — captures a DIRTY old-path buffer, RETIRES the old autosave controller (its unmount/close flushes become no-ops: the old path can never be resurrected) and stashes the buffer under the new path, where the freshly mounted editor applies it as an UNSAVED change (`lib/renameContinuity.ts`; title/hash follow the active tab's existing effects); (5) the originating window drops, from each note that moved, the values of the folders it left (D20 — "Folder settings": Leaving a folder), then rewrites every referencing note (`links/renameLinks.ts`): the referencing set is the index records whose `links`/`embeds` resolve — through THE shared resolver (`resolverFor`) — to the old path; per file read → rewrite → write with `expectedMtime`, one CONFLICT re-read-retry, a second conflict skips the file. Rewrites preserve `|alias` and `#heading`/`#^block`, preserve link FORM (bare→new bare name, pathed→new root-relative, explicit extension stays), cover `![[embeds]]`, skip fenced/inline code (a length-preserving mirror of the index's `stripCode`), and touch frontmatter only as whole-value exact `[[…]]` strings via `setFrontmatterProperty` — at the top level, and by the same rule in each folder's block of `in` (D19), each rewritten where it stands (`setFrontmatterIn`). ONE key is walked DEEPER than whole-value (YAZ-864): `folder_settings`, the single app-owned key with app-defined link semantics (🔒 Q1 — "Folder settings: `.folder.md`"; since YAZ-2290 it lives in the folders' `.folder.md` files, which ride the rename as referencing files beside the notes — `UpdateLinksOptions.folders`, rewritten like notes, never resolved to), whose `views[].order` entries, outline LINES that are exactly a wikilink (YAZ-900, `mapOutlineLinks` — a link inside prose is not a leaf) and `columns.<name>.target` strings are links the index never extracted and a rename therefore used to leave dangling; they rewrite exactly as a top-level entry does (same `resolves`/`newTarget`, so the bare-vs-pathed and alias rules cannot drift), the whole key goes back through `setFrontmatterProperty` as the one door's own write shape, and the referencing-set probe learned the SAME leaves — a page whose only reference lives in there has no `links` entry to be found by, so probe and rewrite stay one construction. The key's own module owns both the key name and the list of link-bearing leaves (`folderSettings.ts` `mapFolderSettingsLinks` / `folderSettingsLinks`), read from the RAW value rather than the tolerant read, so a hand-written unusable shape is never normalised away by a rename; a stale entry that never resolved to the renamed page stays byte-identical, as it always did. Outcomes are PASSIVE (never a dialog): one summary notice "Updated links in N notes" (+ "; M skipped (unsaved changes)"), `ALREADY_EXISTS` and other failures land in the same link-notice; another window's dirty referencing editor falls back to the normal conflict bar — EXCEPT for a referencing note that the rename itself remaps (a folder rename moving an open dirty note), whose editor remounts, re-reads from disk and adopts the post-rewrite baseline, so the conflict never surfaces (GRO-2197 audit, deferred). Accepted residual race: a save already in flight over IPC when the rename lands can still recreate the old path — the systematic paths (debounce, unmount flush, close flush) are all covered by the retire step. ALIAS-form links are never rewritten (E2, GRO-2214): `[[CAC]]` reaching the moved file through its frontmatter `aliases` keeps resolving afterwards — the alias travels with the file — so the referencing-set probe resolves BY NAME ONLY (`resolverFor(records, root, { aliases: false, ids: false })`); a note referencing the moved file only by alias is never even read. ID-form links are never rewritten either (YAZ-2293 D5): `[[k3m9x2pq7abc]]` reaches the moved file through the `id` in that file's own frontmatter, which travels with it too, so the same probe resolves no ids (`ids: false`) — an id link is never counted, stays BYTE-IDENTICAL beside a name link that is rewritten, and a note referencing the moved file only by id is never even read. TITLE-form links travel the same way (YAZ-2420 D17): `[[UP-001 - Abdul]]` reaches a note through the `title:` in its own frontmatter, or a folder through the one in its `.folder.md`, so a rename or move never counts or rewrites it; a page with no `title:` is titled by its name, and a link spelling that is a name link, rewritten as ever — as is every link where the vault does not use IDs (`UpdateLinksOptions.ids` false, also given to `countLinkReferences` and `preRenameRecords`; YAZ-2523 V12): a `title:` line is an ordinary property there, every title is the file's name, and no link travels by one. The ONE rename a title does not travel through is a TITLE EDIT (D16 — `UpdateLinksOptions.title`, also given to `countLinkReferences`): a link that spelled the old title is counted and rewritten to the new one — or to the page's `id` when a link cannot spell the new title or another page answers to it (YAZ-2478), and by name, as below, only when the page has no id — for a note and for a folder alike, while a link that reaches ANOTHER folder by its title is left alone; links by name or by path follow the new file as in any rename, and id links stay as written. KNOWN GAP (GRO-2197 audit, deferred): the banner's N and the number of notes actually written can still disagree on a pure MOVE — N counts every note whose links RESOLVE to the old path, while the rewrite writes nothing for a bare link that stays bare, and in a pure move every bare link stays bare, so "update N links?" can be followed by "Updated links in 0 notes". Closing it needs N to become link-FORM aware, not just resolution aware. E1b bare-vs-pathed rules, LOCKED: a FOLDER rename rewrites only PATHED links/embeds to the notes inside it (to the new root-relative path) — bare-name links keep resolving (names unchanged) and stay BYTE-IDENTICAL; links to the FOLDER ITSELF (🔒 D10, YAZ-2290 — "Links to folders" below) follow the FILE rule — a bare `[[Projects]]` becomes the new name, a pathed one the new path, and a bare one stays untouched while the name still reaches the folder (a move), resolved over the PRE-rename folders (`UpdateLinksOptions.dirs`); a FILE move keeps a bare link bare only when the bare form still resolves to the moved file AFTERWARDS (checked through the resolver against a POST-move record set — the shallowest rule can hand the name to a duplicate), else it escalates to the pathed form (a kept bare form is never spliced, padding included). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-844): the base-embed half of this rule is gone — `resolveBasePath`, the PRE-rename TREE snapshot the rewrite used to take beside the index one, and the `tree` argument of `updateLinksAfterRename` / `countLinkReferences` all died with `![[X.base]]`. The retired engine `tree` argument stays gone: Markdown targets resolve through index records, while explicit text/PDF/image targets use the separate catalog; App may fetch `api.tree` only before the rename gate to pin that catalog. Refusals (all passive notices): existing target → `ALREADY_EXISTS`; missing target parent → `NOT_FOUND` (never a mkdir); dot-dirs and a vault ROOT of the calling window — any vault it shows, YAZ-2602 S47 — → `BAD_REQUEST` (root identity is a recents/vault question, out of E1b scope). A window with a vault AT (or under) the renamed folder follows it live, for every such vault it shows (S48): its tabs prefix-remap and its local vault list remaps without an identity write (main's store repair already moved `WindowEntry.root` and `roots`; a vault that moved keeps its slot and its scope). The sheet itself is one setting since YAZ-2420 D35: `confirmRename` ("Ask before renaming", on by default). Off, a rename or a title edit runs at once and the links still update; the sheets that warn a folder's values will be cleared are not governed by it.
- **External renames** (E1c, GRO-2242 — LOCKED ruling): a rename/move done OUTSIDE the app (Finder, terminal, sync tools) is detected and repaired too — CONFIRM-FIRST, never automatically, never a dialog (ahead of Obsidian, which does not repair external renames at all — verified). ONE hypothesis detector (`client/src/links/renameDetector.ts` `detectRenames`), TWO feeds: (a) COLD-START — after the first index snapshot the client reads `coldDiff(root)` (bridge table above) and joins `removed` × `added`; trusted only on `cacheStatus === 'hit'`; (b) WHILE-RUNNING — consecutive `useIndex` snapshots (`WikilinkIndexBridge.onSnapshot` — no new watchers, no polling), diffed into the same removed/added shape, with UNMATCHED removals and additions carried forward exactly ONE snapshot generation. That carry is load-bearing, not belt-and-braces (GRO-2197): the watcher settles each path on its own (100 ms; chokidar's `awaitWriteFinish` ~200 ms on the polling fallback), so an `add` can trail its `unlink`, and the index applies `unlink` synchronously but `add` behind an async `scanFile`, so the index genuinely passes through a ~100 ms window showing the removal WITHOUT the addition. The 300 ms refetch debounce usually straddles both — but when it does not, the pair splits across two generations, `detectRenames` never sees it, and the hypothesis was previously lost forever (measured: `externalRename.spec.ts` steps 1 and 3 failed ~50-100% of runs under CPU load, 0/9 idle). Carried entries are re-validated against the new snapshot first (a removal whose path came back was a save, not a rename; an addition that vanished again is dropped) and only the CURRENT generation's leftovers are carried on, so the memory is bounded to one generation. Guard rails, firm: markdown files only; join key = EXACT (size, mtime) equality (macOS rename/move preserves both); zero-byte files skip; only UNAMBIGUOUS 1:1 pairs — a signature carried by several removed or several added files skips entirely (no guessing); equal basenames (a pure move) still hypothesize — pathed links may need rewriting. Either feed lands in ONE passive app-level banner (`useExternalRenames` — the link-notice idiom with two actions, one hypothesis at a time, oldest first): "Looks like `X` became `Y` — update N links?" [Update] [Dismiss]. N is the rewrite engine's own referencing-set count (`countLinkReferences`, the dry-run twin of `updateLinksAfterRename`, over a PRE-rename snapshot synthesised by re-pathing the moved record back — `preRenameRecords`); N === 0 → no banner, nothing happens at all. Update → (1) `file.repairRename` (bridge table above — store/tabs/editors follow via the existing `file:renamed` downstream; a refused claim, e.g. the old path came back, lands as a passive notice), (2) the E1/E1b rewrite engine old→new over fresh index/tree snapshots re-pathed pre-rename, (3) the usual summary notice — always shown here (the user explicitly asked). Dismiss → the pair joins a SESSION-level dismissed set and is never re-offered (the next refetch re-detects the same diff; the set is what keeps it quiet); a handled (Updated) pair joins the same set. In-app renames echo through the watcher as identical unlink+add pairs, so every `file:renamed` push also feeds the suppression set (exact pair for files; prefix mapping for dirs, since a folder rename echoes as one pair per moved note) — own renames never banner. The DIR prefix mappings are cleared on a root switch (GRO-2197): they exist only to swallow the current vault's echoes, and left standing they sat in the per-hypothesis linear scan for the rest of the session, across vaults. (The dismissed PAIR set is session-level by ruling and deliberately survives a root switch — its keys are absolute paths.)
- **Backlinks** (Links D, GRO-2193 — decision D of GRO-2096, LOCKED): the reverse direction is RENDERER-LOCAL too. "Linked mentions (N)" is a collapsible block in the note's own scroll — and in a folder's page (YAZ-2290 D10) — computed per render from the index snapshot the window already has through the ONE resolver (so alias-form and id-form links count) — no reverse map in main, no new IPC, no index field, no new watcher. Context snippets are read on demand for the rows actually shown. Full rules: Editor rule 25.
- **Links to folders** (🔒 D10, YAZ-2290 — `client/src/links/folderLinks.ts`): a folder is a page, so it is linkable. `linkResolver(records, root, dirs, folders)` is THE path-level resolver every wikilink surface shares (`ResolveLink`: target → absolute path or null), built by `WikilinkIndexBridge` per snapshot: a NOTE first — id, path, title, name, a path of titles, alias, through `resolverFor` ("Link resolution") — and a FOLDER only when none answers, so no link that resolved before means anything new and a note or alias of the same name always wins. `folderResolver` tries the id of the folder's `.folder.md`, then its root-relative path, then its title (YAZ-2420 D17), then its bare name — duplicates of either to the SHALLOWEST, equal depth to the first in tree order, case-insensitive, `[[…]]` / `|alias` / `#heading` stripped. Which folders exist is the Files tree's to say (`vaultDirs`, off the one tree feed of the note's own vault — links never leave it, YAZ-2602 R1), so empty folders count and the vault ROOT is not linkable. A folder has no index record: what its link resolves to is the DIRECTORY path — a folder tab's identity — so a click opens the folder tab, ⌘-click a background one, and the backlinks pass compares it like any path ("Linked mentions" on a folder's page lists the notes and the folders that link to it; a folder's settings are a mention source, Editor rule 25). `[[` completion offers folders (`folderLinkCandidates`) after the note rows, each reading `<title> (folder)` — display text only, never typed or written. A folder whose `.folder.md` holds an id is inserted by that ID, under its title (YAZ-2420 D17) — its root-relative path when another folder takes the title — so a note of the same name is simply a second row. A folder with no id is inserted by that TITLE — or by root-relative path when the title belongs to something else — and gets no row when neither spelling reaches it. The picker never writes a `.folder.md`. A link to a folder READS as the folder's title in view cells and in the properties panel (`pageResolver`, over `FolderHost.resolveLink` in a view), and sorts and groups under that name. Renaming a folder rewrites the links to it (Rename, above). A link column's `target: "[[Folder]]"` narrows its picker to what that folder shows — the notes that live in it and its shortcuts (`belongsToBasenames`, 🔒 Q2 of YAZ-815): resolved like a click, so a note of that name wins and narrows nothing; when the target is no folder, or the folder holds none, the picker offers ALL notes (report-don't-block: it narrows when it can and never goes empty). Candidates are offered by title (YAZ-2420 D17) and written by `id` (YAZ-2293). Tests: `links/folderLinks.test.ts`, `links/renameLinks.test.ts`, `views/FolderView.page.test.tsx`.
- The in-app `#/abs/path.md` hash (Editor rule 17) is separate and unchanged.

**YAZ-1289 rename amendment (supersedes the tombstone's final `api.tree` sentence):** the removed base-embed tree argument stays dead, and semantic Markdown targets still resolve only through index records. For a text/PDF/image file rename, App reuses a ready catalog only when it proves that exact target; a directory rename always obtains one fresh lightweight `api.tree(root)` snapshot so newly arrived descendants cannot be missed. A required snapshot failure stops before confirmation or mutation, the accepted root/generation is checked after the await, and the same pre-rename catalog is pinned through confirmation into count/rewrite. Switching roots cancels an old request silently and clears any open rename sheet. Explicit-extension links resolve through that catalog, while the referencing and written files remain Markdown records only. Post-move catalog resolution decides whether a bare duplicate spelling can remain bare or must escalate to a root-relative path. View-only target files are never read or written by link repair.

## Two kinds of vault (YAZ-2523 — decisions V1-V13, LOCKED)

What the app may write into a folder it opens, and what it offers there. Read this before changing either: every rule in the other sections about ids, titles, built names, a folder's `.folder.md` at birth, shortcuts and per-folder values holds in a vault that uses IDs, and only there.

**The answer** (🔒 V1, V5, V10): a vault is one of two kinds by its OWN recorded answer, `<root>/.yaseendocs/ids.json` — `{ "enabled": true | false, "letters": "YAZ", "was": ["OLD"] }` (`IDS_FILE`, `idsAnswer`, `idLettersOf`, `shared/noteId.ts`; `letters` and `was` are the vault's ID letters, YAZ-2677 R9 — "Note ids": The letters — and every write of the file keeps each key it does not change, R10). Yes is an **ID vault**: everything this document describes. No, or NOT ANSWERED — the file missing, unreadable, not JSON, or its `enabled` not a boolean — is a **plain vault**: the app is a viewer and editor of the files as they are, and writes nothing the user did not ask for. There is no third kind. The file is ordinary vault config (bridge table, `vaultConfig`), so it syncs with the vault. 🔒 That `.yaseendocs/` EXISTS means nothing: a favorite, a review setting or the sync switch never turns IDs on, and neither does a note or a folder made in the app. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-2293's gate): the dotfolder's existence, or the first create from a window rooted there, used to start the id sweep; neither does now.

**ONE question, ONE source per process** (🔒 V5): "does this vault use IDs?" No feature works the answer out for itself — not from `.yaseendocs/`, not from a note that holds an `id`, not from a file's name. MAIN: `idsOf(root)` (`vaultIndex/idSweep.ts`) reads the file off the disk each time it is asked — no cache and no watcher, so the command line shares it — and `givesIds(root)` is that answer `=== true`; the fs handlers ask through `doorFor(e, req)` (`ipc/fs.ts`): a vault of the calling window holds the path (`rootsOfSender` → `rootOfPath`, the most specific one — YAZ-2602 S78: a window may show several vaults, of both kinds), and THAT vault said yes — the answer is then that vault's door (`mint.ts` `doorOf(root)`, "Note ids": The door), and else null. Both read the file through `mint.ts` `vaultIds(root)`, which also reads the vault's letters. THE WINDOW: `IndexResponse.ids`, which `useIndex` → `WikilinkIndexBridge` put on the vault's own `WikilinkResolveSource` as `source.ids` (ONE source per vault of the window, in its scope — "Multi-window": Several vaults in one window) — false until the first index lands, and forgotten (`source.update(null)`) the moment the vault leaves its slot — and hand to the vault's scope through `onSnapshot`. Every surface reads `source.ids`, or an `ids` argument its caller read there (`reservedKeys(ids)`, `folderSettings(record, ids)`, `createNote(dir, title, ids, …)`, `UpdateLinksOptions.ids`). A gesture whose outcome depends on the kind — a create, a rename — refuses while no index has landed (`source.resolve === null`).

**What the index hands out** (🔒 V12 — `vaultIndex/live.ts`): the live record map holds what is in the files, whatever the answer; the kind is applied in `getIndex`, as each response is built. An ID vault: the records as scanned — each `id` read as an ID of THIS vault ("Note ids": Where a note gets it) — with `ids: true` and `letters`, the vault's ID letters (the current ones first, then each of `was`). A plain vault: each record through `plain` — NO `id`, and its `title` its file name (`scan.ts` `fileTitle`; a `.folder.md`'s is its folder's name), `id` and `title` still among its `properties` — and `ids: false`. While the answer is NOT YES — no answer, or a no — the response also carries `ask: { notes, folders, foreign }` (YAZ-2677 🔒 D2, S12: the box in Settings shows the counts for a vault that said no too; before, only a vault with no answer carried it), what a yes would write (`wouldWrite`): the notes with no id that can take one, `foreign` of them holding another tool's `id`, and the folders with no `.folder.md` or no id in the one they have. So a change of answer needs no rescan and no `CACHE_VERSION` bump: `useIndex` refetches at once on `vaultConfig:changed` for `ids.json`, and the same notes go out as the other kind.

**The per-write gate** (🔒 V1, V4): the sweep asks `givesIds(root)` before EVERY write, not once per pass ("Note ids": The sweep), and `restoreFolderId` asks too — so nothing is written in a plain vault, and a no that lands while a pass is running stops it at the next file. A yes that `getIndex` meets on an index built before it starts the pass over what is indexed (`sweepIndexed`). A no removes nothing: ids, `title:` lines, built names and `.folder.md` files stay as they are (V13).

**The quiet yes** (🔒 V11): a vault that already uses IDs carries over with no question. At the index build, a vault with no answer is saved as yes — `ids.json` is the only file written, every key it already holds kept (YAZ-2677 R10) — exactly when some note holds an id AND a yes would write nothing at all (`ask.notes === 0 && ask.folders === 0`): one folder without its `.folder.md` is enough to leave the vault plain instead (S3). A save that fails leaves the vault unanswered.

**No box when a vault opens** (YAZ-2677 🔒 D1, S1 to S4): a vault with no answer, or with a no, opens as plain files. The app asks nothing and writes nothing, in a window with one vault or with several; IDs are a setting. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-2523 V2): `components/ConfirmIds.tsx`, a sheet App rendered whenever a vault's snapshot carried `ask` — **Give IDs** / **Not for this vault**, Esc closing it for that window only (`idsAsk`, `closeIdsAsk`, `idsAskClosed` in `useVaultScope.ts`). All of it is gone; its words moved to the box below, and `ask` now only feeds that box.

**The box** (YAZ-2677 🔒 D2, S5 to S13 — `settings/registry.tsx` `GiveIdsSheet`, on the one typed-confirm piece `TypedConfirmSheet` of `settings/IdLettersControl.tsx`, which "Change letters" and "Give them numbers" use too — "Note ids"): a click on **On** in Settings opens it, and NOTHING is written before it is confirmed. Its text is `idsAskMessage(ask, vault)` (`settings/idsAskMessage.ts`): what an ID is (`like BUS-12`), what a yes would write, counted from the snapshot's `ask` — `write an ID into N notes`, `add a hidden settings file to N folders`, always `name the file of each note you make or retitle`, and how many notes hold an ID from another tool, which the app replaces — and how to undo it (`To undo, turn it off. The IDs stay in the files and the app stops using them.`). With two or more vaults in the window the question NAMES its vault (S13, YAZ-2602 S40: "Give the notes in <vault> IDs?"; with one, "this vault"). A yes that would write nothing still opens it: it sets the letters (S10). Under the text is ONE text field, which holds the focus, and the line above it (`idsLettersAsk`): a vault with no saved letters CHOOSES them — `Type the ID letters for this vault: 2 to 5 letters, like BUS.` (S5) — and a vault that has them types exactly those, in any case — `Type this vault's ID letters, YAZ, to confirm.` (S9). The saved letters are read at the click on On, from the vault's `ids.json` (`useVaultScope.ts` `readLetters`; letters that are not valid count as none; a file that cannot be read or is not valid JSON is said in the notice and no box opens). **Give IDs** and Enter do NOTHING until what is typed is valid (`ConfirmSheet`'s `confirmDisabled`; `isIdLetters`, `shared/noteId.ts`: 2 to 5 letters A to Z in any case, nothing trimmed — an empty field, one letter, six, a digit or a space is not valid, S6). Confirmed, the typed letters go out in capitals: `ids.set(root, true, letters)` (bridge table) saves `enabled: true` and `letters`, and this Mac gives the numbers at once (S7, R32). Cancel, Esc or a click outside closes the box with nothing written, and the switch stays Off (S8); the keys stay inside the sheet, so the dialog under it does not close. A device that opens the vault before the answer has synced is a plain vault until `ids.json` arrives, and then waits before it sweeps ("Note ids": Which Mac gives a number, S65).

**The switch** (🔒 V4, V13, YAZ-2677 D2 — `settings/registry.tsx` `IdsControl`): Settings › Files & Links › This vault › "Give this vault's notes IDs", present only with a vault open whose index has loaded (`SettingsCtx.ids`). It shows the snapshot's `ids`: a vault that said no and one that has not answered both read Off (the renderer cannot tell them apart, and need not). Only a CHANGE acts: a click on the side the row shows is nothing — so a click on Off in a vault with no answer writes nothing (before YAZ-2677 it saved the no, so that the box at the opening did not come back). On: the box above. Off where a note holds an id (`held`) asks first, on a `ConfirmSheet` whose keys stay inside it — `This vault's notes will show their file names, and links stored as IDs will not open, until you turn this back on. Nothing is removed.`, Cancel / **Turn off** (S11) — and anywhere else saves at once. The save is `ids.set` in MAIN (`useVaultScope.ts` `saveIds` only calls it; a refusal is said in the notice): the read and the write of `ids.json` are one step there, so Off keeps `letters` and `was` (R12) and an `ids.json` that is not valid JSON is never written over. While the vault uses IDs three more rows stand under the switch — "Duplicate IDs" (`duplicates`), "ID letters" (`idLetters`) and, while the snapshot holds an old ID, "Old IDs" (`oldIds`): "Note ids".

**Create, rename and copy in a plain vault** (🔒 V3) are what Finder would do. MAIN: `createFile(req, ids)` writes the content it was given and nothing else — none is zero bytes — and answers no `id`; `createDir(req, ids)` makes the directory and no `.folder.md`; `copyEntry(from, toDir, ids)` is `fs.cp` under Finder's next free name for EVERY entry, a folder whole (`freeName(dir, name, kind)`: a folder's name is not split at a dot). A request shaped for an ID vault — a `createFile` `id`, a `createDir` `title` — and every `fs:retitle` is refused `BAD_REQUEST` `this vault does not use IDs`, nothing made or written: it comes from a window one answer behind. CLIENT: what is typed is the NAME on disk (`sidebar/createEntry.ts` `plainEntryName`: as typed, held to `validateEntryName`, a note given `.md` unless a Markdown suffix was typed). `createNote` writes the folder's template as it is — or, with a seed, the seed's keys as the note's own properties — and no `title` or `id`; "New" in a folder's view is `Untitled`, then `Untitled 2`, …; a note made from a link is `<typed>.md`, each segment of a pathed target a name on disk, and the `[[` picker's Create row leaves `[[typed text]]`. Every title surface — the page title, the sidebar's Rename, a table's Name cell — still reaches App's `requestRetitle`, which there builds the path and goes through the one door as a rename of the NAME: `file.rename`, the same confirm, and links rewritten by name (`UpdateLinksOptions.ids` false: a `title:` line names nothing, so no link travels by one).

**Not offered in a plain vault** (🔒 V5, V7, V12). Gated on `ids`: "Add note shortcut" (`MenuTargets.shortcutDir`), the id link's right-click menu and its Copy ID (`wikilinkMenu.ts` `idLinkAt`), per-folder properties (the panel's "Properties from" and its folder rows) and "Delete column…". The rest falls out of the index handing out no ids, with no gate of its own: no shortcut rows, nothing found by id in ⌘K, the `[[` picker and the link cells insert names, and no block of `in` is ever stale, so a move asks nothing and drops nothing. A click on an unresolved link shaped like an id says `This vault does not use IDs, so this link cannot be opened` and creates nothing. 🔒 The keys that stay the app's are `reservedKeys(false)`: `comments` and `reviews`. `id`, `title`, `also_in` and `in` are ordinary properties there — rows with editors in the panel (`in` a map, so the YAML chip), columns in a table. Favorites, review, GitHub sync, comments, backlinks and Copy path work in both kinds.

**A folder's page in a plain vault** (🔒 V6, V11): the same `FolderView`, asking once (`ids = source.ids`). Its rows carry the notes' OWN properties — no block of `in` is read — so a view with no saved order shows Name and every key the notes hold (`propertyKeys`), and a value written — a cell, a board drop, a "New" seed — is that one top-level key of that note (`writeProperties`); no folder id is asked for or made. The defaults (`folderSettings(undefined, false)`) are a Table alone and no column: no Board, no `status`. Opening writes nothing; the first SETTINGS change — a view, a column declared or hidden — creates `.folder.md` holding `folder_settings` and nothing else, no `id` and no `title`. The host has no `deleteColumn` (`FolderHost.deleteColumn?`), so neither menu offers "Delete column…": a column is hidden instead.

**The command line** (🔒 V10 — `cli.ts` `idVault`): "no" means no for agents too. For `id` and `links` the vault is the nearest folder at or above the page that has ANSWERED — a folder that only holds app settings is passed over — and only a yes counts. A no, or no answer anywhere above, refuses with exit 1 and nothing written: `id`, even for a page that holds one, with `<page>'s vault does not give its notes IDs (turn on "Give this vault's notes IDs" in the app's Settings)`; `links` with `<page>'s vault does not use IDs, so there are no ID links to list`. The comment verbs and `due` work in both kinds — `HELP` says so — and `vaults` (YAZ-2556) asks no vault anything: it reads the app's state file alone ("Multi-window": The vault list).

**NOT built** (🔒 V7, V8): REMOVING IDs from a vault that has them — Off stops future writes and removes nothing; a vault that ever needs it is done once, by an agent from written steps. And per-folder properties keyed by FOLDER NAME: in a plain vault a property is the note's own frontmatter, and a folder's own values are what an ID vault gives.

**Testing**: `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/liveIds.test.ts` (the gate, the quiet yes, `ask`, the two hand-outs), `idSweep.test.ts`, `desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.test.ts` (`doorFor`, the refusals, `ids:set`), `desktop/src/main/fs/create.test.ts` / `copy.test.ts`, `desktop/src/cli/cli.test.ts`; in the client `App.test.tsx` (no box when a vault opens, the switch, a typed name), `components/ConfirmSheet.test.tsx` (`confirmDisabled`), `settings/SettingsDialog.test.tsx` (the box and its typed letters) / `idsAskMessage.test.ts` (its words), `views/useIndex.test.tsx`, `editor/wikilink/WikilinkIndexBridge.test.tsx`, `sidebar/Sidebar.test.tsx`, `sidebar/createEntry.test.ts`, `views/scaffold.test.ts`, `views/FolderView.test.tsx`, `views/folderSettings.test.ts`, `editor/FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`, `links/reservedKeys.test.ts` / `renameLinks.test.ts` / `shortcuts.test.ts`. Hand steps: `docs/REGRESSION.md`, "Two kinds of vault"; the flow end to end is `desktop/e2e/ids.spec.ts`.

Files: `shared/noteId.ts` (`IDS_FILE`, `idsAnswer`, `isIdLetters`), `shared/types.ts` (`IndexResponse.ids`, `letters`, `ask`), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/mint.ts` (`vaultIds`, `doorOf`) + `idSweep.ts` (`idsOf`, `givesIds`) + `live.ts` (`getIndex`, `plain`, `wouldWrite`, `sweepIndexed`, `saveIdsAnswer`) + `scan.ts` (`fileTitle`), `desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.ts` (`rootsOfSender`, `doorFor`), `desktop/src/main/fs/create.ts` + `copy.ts`, `desktop/src/cli/cli.ts` (`idVault`, `HELP`), `client/src/views/useIndex.ts`, `client/src/editor/wikilink/wikilinkPlugin.ts` + `WikilinkIndexBridge.tsx`, `client/src/vault/useVaultScope.ts` (`ids`: `saveIds`, `readLetters` — per vault since YAZ-2602), `client/src/App.tsx` (`requestRetitle`), `client/src/components/ConfirmSheet.tsx`, `client/src/settings/registry.tsx` (`IdsControl`, `GiveIdsSheet`, `SettingsCtx.ids`) + `IdLettersControl.tsx` (`TypedConfirmSheet`) + `idsAskMessage.ts`, `client/src/sidebar/createEntry.ts` (`plainEntryName`), `client/src/views/scaffold.ts`, `client/src/links/reservedKeys.ts` + `renameLinks.ts`, `client/src/views/FolderView.tsx` + `folderSettings.ts` + `engine.ts` (`propertyKeys`).

## Note ids (YAZ-2293 — decisions D1-D8)

This whole section describes a vault that uses IDs ("Two kinds of vault"): in any other the app writes no id, and the index hands none out.

- **The id** (🔒 YAZ-2677 D3, `shared/noteId.ts`): every Markdown note carries a permanent identity in its frontmatter — `id: YAZ-12`, the key spelled `NOTE_ID_KEY`. It is given once and never derived from the file's name or place, so a rename or a move by ANYTHING — the app, Finder, a sync tool, an agent — keeps every `[[<id>]]` link pointing at the note. **Two shapes** (`isNoteId`). The NUMBER ID (R1, `isNumberId`): the vault's ID letters, a hyphen and a number — `^[A-Z]{2,5}-[1-9]\d*$`, no leading zero (`YAZ-012` and `YAZ-0` are no ID, S24), 15 digits at most (every such number is one JavaScript counts exactly). The NUMBER is the real ID: the next one of the vault, given by the door below. It is READ in any case and WRITTEN and indexed with capital letters (R2, `canonicalNoteId`): `[[yaz-12]]` opens `YAZ-12` (S17), and `id: yaz-12` written by hand is held as `YAZ-12` with the file NOT rewritten for the case alone (S18). The OLD ID (YAZ-2293 D2): exactly 12 characters of lowercase Crockford base32 (`0123456789abcdefghjkmnpqrstvwxyz` — no `i l o u`) with ALWAYS at least one digit. 🔒 An old ID is still read everywhere — links, `also_in`, a folder's values, search — and is NEVER written again and never written over (R3, the seat belt: `liveIds.test.ts`, "a vault that has only old IDs behaves as it did before number IDs"). It has no letters and no number, so it never counts for the next number (S20). **An ID of THIS vault** (R5, `vaultNoteId(value, letters)`): an old ID, or a number ID whose letters are the vault's — or letters the vault had BEFORE (`was`), which read as the same number with the current letters, because a number is used one time in a vault whatever the letters (R4). Any other `LETTERS-NUMBER` text is not an ID here: as a link it is a NAME (`[[GPT-4]]`, `[[BUS-12]]` — R6, S21, S22), and as a frontmatter `id` it is another tool's, as an `id` of any other shape is — never an id to the index, the resolver or any menu, and the app writes its own over it (R7, S23; YAZ-2420 D30, The sweep below): every note in a vault holds an id of that vault. The frontmatter `id` is the truth; the id at the end of a file name the app built (`<kebab-title>-<id>.md`, in LOWERCASE as the whole name is — `my-note-yaz-12.md`, R8, `shared/noteName.ts`) is a copy of it, written when the name is built, so every other tool sees it too (YAZ-2420 D1, D4). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-2677): `mintNoteId()`, a random 12-character id, and `noteIdFrom(draw)`, one built from any byte source, stood in `shared/noteId.ts`; nothing in `shared/` or the renderer makes an id now.
- **The letters** (R9 to R12, `idLettersOf`, `defaultIdLetters`; read off the disk by `mint.ts` `vaultIds(root)`): a vault's ID letters are `letters` in its `ids.json` (2 to 5 letters A to Z, read in any case), and `was` lists the letters it had before. A vault that said yes and holds no valid `letters` uses a DEFAULT — the first three letters A to Z of its folder's name in capitals, `DOC` when the name has fewer than two (R11) — and the door saves that default in the file when it gives the first number, every other key kept (R10). The letters reach the renderer as `IndexResponse.letters` (the current ones first, then each of `was`; only when `ids`), and from there the window's link source (`WikilinkResolveSource.letters`), the link resolver (`ResolverOptions.letters`) and the search candidates (`SearchCandidate.was`).
- **The door** (🔒 YAZ-2677 D4, R13 to R21 — `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/mint.ts` `mintIds(root, count, floor?)`): THE ONE place that makes an id; plain Node, no Electron and no watcher, because the `yaseendocs` command uses it too. The next number is 1 plus the highest of (a) each number ID of this vault among its notes and folders and (b) `last` of each Mac's count file (R16) — so a number a deleted note had is never given again (S28, S29), a number written by hand is the new floor (S37), and a new vault starts at 1 (S15). **One count file for each Mac** (R13 to R15): `<root>/.yaseendocs/ids/<mac id>.json` — `{ "name", "since", "last", "made" }`: `name` the host name (for people; nothing reads it), `since` when the file was made (ISO time), `last` the highest number this Mac gave, `made` each number it gave in the last 30 days as RUNS `{ "from", "to", "at" }`, oldest first, one run on a line (a new note is a run of one; a sweep of many notes is ONE run, so the file stays small after a large sweep). Only the Mac that owns a file writes it, atomically (`atomicWrite`), so two Macs that sync a vault never change one file. `made` drops a run older than `MADE_DAYS` (30, R21) and keeps `MADE_MAX` (5,000) runs at most. The MAC ID is `{ "id": <12 random characters> }` in `mac.json` in the app's user-data folder — made one time with an exclusive create, never the host name (`macId`; main hands the folder in with `initMint(app.getPath('userData'))`, the command finds the same one by `userDataDir`, so both use ONE count file: R20). **One request at a time for a vault** (R17): a queue per root. Each request lists the count folder and `stat`s each file (64 at most), reading again only one whose size or time changed; asks for the vault's highest number — from the live index's records in memory (`countNotesWith`, registered by `live.ts`; the sweep hands in its own records as `floor`; with no index, the command, the vault is walked and scanned as `links` does); checks that its own count file did not change under it (the app and the command are two processes of one Mac) and starts again if it did; writes its count file; and only THEN answers (R18) — a crash, or a create that then fails, leaves a gap and never a number given two times (S30, S31). All the ids of one call are saved with ONE write (R19). A count file that is missing or not valid JSON reads as empty, and this Mac's is written good again (S38, S39); another Mac's is never written. `null`, and nothing written, where the vault does not use IDs. Cost, measured by `mint.test.ts`: about 6 ms a number, almost all of it the one fsynced write. The count files are hidden (`.yaseendocs/`), so the index and the tree never hold them, and the config watcher reports only files DIRECTLY in `.yaseendocs/`, so a new number wakes no window (`vaultConfig.test.ts`). Every id the app writes comes from here: `createFile` / `createDir` and a copy (`fs/copy.ts` — one request for a whole copied folder, S34, S53) take an `IdDoor` (`doorOf(root)`) from `ipc/fs.ts`, the sweep and a title edit call `mintIds` / `nextId`, and the renderer asks through `mintNoteId` (bridge table). Two Macs CAN give one number before they sync: **Two notes with one ID** below settles that. The same module answers what that rule and the next one ask of the count files — `countsOf(root)` (each Mac's count by its Mac ID and which is this Mac's, in the door's turn and from what the door already holds: a `stat` for each count file, a read only for one that changed), `firstMac(files)` (R33) and `makersOf(files, number, now)` (R22, R23). A count file holding `last: 0` is read as a Mac that gave no number yet; the door never writes one. The app and the `yaseendocs` command are two processes of one Mac with no lock between them: two numbers taken in the same millisecond can be one number, and the two notes are then a COPY by R22 (one Mac holds the number in `made`) — the keeper rule gives one of them its next number, on one Mac, with no link changed.
- **Where a note gets it**: BORN with it — every creation goes through `createFile`, which sets it in the creating write (bridge table) — or, for a note that ARRIVED without one, given it by the sweep below; either way it is the door's next number. The index carries it as `IndexRecord.id` ("Property index"), and that field is what the whole renderer reads: no surface parses a file to learn an id. 🔒 `scan.ts` puts the id in the record as the FILE holds it, in capitals (R2); WHOSE id it is is said where the index is handed out (`live.ts` `getIndex`, which reads `ids.json` once a call, as it always did for the vault's kind — YAZ-2523 V5) and where it is swept: a number ID with letters of before goes out with the current letters, one with any other letters goes out as none (`owned`). The letters can change after a record was scanned and cached, so a record never holds a reading of them (`CACHE_VERSION` 8).
- **The sweep** (D3, D4, D13 — `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/idSweep.ts` `sweepIds(root, records, among, knew, dirs, opts)`): a note the app did not create arrives with no id, or with another tool's, and a note copied OUTSIDE the app arrives carrying its original's (a copy made in the app is born with its own — `fs/copy.ts`, YAZ-2420 D21). The sweep runs in main on the live index (`live.ts`): after EACH watch-event scan (`add` / `change`), over that one record, and once after the cold-start reconcile, over every record — NOT awaited, so a vault of id-less notes never holds up its first index.
