Each note in its batch that has NO id is given one, and an `id` that is not an ID of this vault is none — another tool's, a number with a leading zero, a number ID with another vault's letters (YAZ-2677 R7, S23, S24): the app's is written over it and the old value is not kept (YAZ-2420 D30). An OLD id is an id like any other here: kept, never written over (R3). 🔒 **Every folder holds its id** (D13): each of `dirs` whose `.folder.md` the index does not hold is given one holding only its `id` — at the cold start every folder the index walk went into (`scan.ts` `walk`'s `dirs`: what the tree shows, so no dot-folder, no `node_modules`, nothing inside one, and never the vault root), on a watch `addDir` that one folder, and for a vault that says yes after its index was built, a fresh walk (`sweepIndexed`). An empty folder and one holding only images get it too. A `.folder.md` deleted while the app runs, from a folder that is still there, is created again holding the id it had (`restoreFolderId`, on the watch `unlink`). Of the indexed notes SHARING an id — a COPY; two Macs that each gave one number are a clash, settled by **Two notes with one ID** below — only the KEEPER keeps it — the note the index already knew to hold it (at cold start, per the persisted cache; on a live event, every other indexed note is known to hold what it holds and the scanned one what it held before the event), else the first in path order, which is the same answer on every device — and any other note in the batch is given a fresh one. So a copy gets its own id, and every link to the original stays the original's. A `.folder.md` given a fresh id in place of a shared one is a COPIED folder's, whose notes still hold their values (D19) under the original's id: `carryFolderValues(dir, from, to)` walks that folder itself (the index's `walk`, not the index) and, in every note under it at any depth that holds a block of `in` for `from` and none for `to`, names that block `to` (`moveFolderValues`). Each re-IDed folder carries its own; the original, its notes and a MOVED folder (same id) are never written; a note that will not parse is left, and one that changed under the write is read again, once; a second run writes nothing. The folder takes its new id LAST, after its notes hold their values under it (`giveId`'s `first`): a carry cut short by a quit leaves the folder still holding the original's id, so the next sweep finds it again, gives it the next number and carries what still holds the original's (the number of the pass that was cut is a gap). The copy is remembered while the app runs (`carried`): a long copy is still arriving when its folder is given its id, so the sweep carries each copied note that reaches that folder afterwards. The app's own folder copy calls `carryFolderValues` too, once the folder it made holds its fresh id (YAZ-2420 D21): its notes were written holding their values under the original's. The sweep's writes run one at a time (`inTurn`), so a copied note's own id and the values carried to it never lose a compare-and-set to each other. The sweep never removes a block. 🔒 **Only in a vault that said YES** — its `.yaseendocs/ids.json` holds `{ "enabled": true }` (`givesIds`, YAZ-2523 V1 — "Two kinds of vault"): the app never writes behind the user's back into a folder it has merely been pointed at (the standing rule of YAZ-797), and that `.yaseendocs/` exists means nothing. The sweep is started by every index event all the same and asks `givesIds(root)` before EACH write, so a no stops a pass that is running; a yes that arrives after the index was built is met by the next `getIndex`, whose `sweepIndexed(root)` (`live.ts`) gives ids to the notes already indexed and to the folders there. In any other vault the sweep touches no file, a note that arrived without an id stays without one, and it is linked by name; a folder there has no `.folder.md` until something changes it, and reads as the defaults. **The id is the vault's next NUMBER, from the door** (YAZ-2677 D4): one pass takes ALL its numbers with one write of the count file (R19) before it writes any note (R18), and its floor is the highest number among the records it was handed. Only what can take an id is counted — a note the index could read (no number for one whose YAML is invalid or that is over the size limit, pass after pass), and a `.folder.md` the index has not seen is read first, so a folder that arrives WITH its file costs none. A note that changes between the count and the write loses its number: a gap. The number is higher than every number in the vault, so it is never an id some indexed note holds (YAZ-2378). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-2293): the id was DERIVED — `noteIdFrom` over the sha256 of the note's place and bytes — so that two devices meeting one note before syncing made the same edit; with numbers they would not, and which Mac gives the number is YAZ-2677 D8's rule (**Which Mac gives a number**, below). **Compare-and-set**: `giveId(file, held, letters, fresh, first?)` re-reads the file, goes on only while its frontmatter `id` — read as an ID of this vault (`letters`, R5) — still IS what the index saw (none — another tool's reads as none, D30, R7 — or the shared one), asks `fresh` for the number only then, sets that one key through `setFrontmatterProperty` — every other byte kept — and writes with `expectedMtime`, so a note that changed under the sweep is left for its next index event. Left ALONE, silently: a properties block that does not parse and an oversize file. It never throws and is safe to run any number of times: its own write comes back as a watch `change`, rescans the note, and the second look needs nothing. A folder's settings file (`.folder.md`) sits in the same record map and is given an id like any note. The ONE file it creates is a missing `.folder.md` (D13): `giveId` reads it as empty and creates it exclusively (`createDurable`), holding only its id, so one that appears before the write stays as it is; a folder gone by then is passed over. A folder renamed or moved keeps its file and its id.
- **One sweep at a time, and one number for a file** (YAZ-2677, a finding on YAZ-2681): every index event starts a sweep, and a file that just arrived is often in two of them — the first index and its own `add`, an `add` and a `change`, a folder's `addDir`. `sweepIds` therefore runs ONE pass at a time for a vault (each waits for the one before it and then reads what the index holds for its paths NOW), an index event that arrives while a pass of the index waits its turn JOINS that pass — so a sync that brings many files is few passes, each with one write of the count file, and what a pass did is reported one time, to the event that started it; a pass the user asked for is never joined — and a pass reads the BYTES of each file it means to number before it asks the door (`needs`, 16 files at a time): a file that already holds an ID is not counted. All the numbers of a pass are still taken with one write (R19). A file a pass could not write is remembered with its mtime while the app runs (`refused`, 1,000 entries), so the next pass takes no number for it until it changes. Still a gap by design (S30): a file another writer changes between the count and the write. `sweepIds(root, records, among, knew, dirs, opts)` resolves to a `SweepResult` — `fixes`, `theirs`, `copies`, `later`, `kept` — and never throws.
- **Two notes with one ID** (🔒 YAZ-2677 D7, R22 to R30a — `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/clash.ts`, called by the sweep for each ID that two or more notes ON DISK hold; two paths of one file are one note, by the inode). **Clash or copy** (R22): a CLASH when the count files of two or more Macs hold the number in `made`, in a run not older than 30 days (`makersOf`); any other pair is a COPY — a Finder duplicate, two notes with one old ID, a `made` that expired (S49). The count files are read only then, never for each index event. **A clash has ONE writer** (R23, R24): the note of the Mac that gave the number first keeps the ID (equal times: the Mac ID that is first in order, S50); each OTHER Mac that gave it fixes ITS OWN note, and a Mac that did not give the number writes nothing. **Its own note** (R25, `myNote`) is the one this Mac's index held with the ID before the other arrived (`knew`); it is said ONCE, at the first sight of the pair, and saved in the diary, because the index holds both notes from then on. A Mac that cannot tell — its index cache is lost — falls to R29: the first note in path order keeps the ID, the Mac of the newer time gives each other note the next number, no link is changed, and the notice says so (S48). **The fix** (R26, R28, `settleClash`), in this order: (1) the note is read — nothing is taken for one that cannot take its number now; (2) the next number from the door, SAVED IN THE DIARY before the vault changes; (3) each file the diary first saw naming the ID more than `LINK_GRACE_MS` (5 s) before the other note arrived has that ID changed to the new one (`rewriteIds`, a compare-and-set, tried once more on a conflict); (4) the note takes its ID — LAST, and a folder's notes carry their values to it first (`carryFolderValues`, R30a, S54); (5) a note whose file name is the one the app builds from its title and the old ID is renamed to the name of the new ID (`followName`) — the bridge runs the rename handler's downstream for it, so its tab, favorite and stored paths follow; a link written BY FILE NAME or by path to that note (`[[bar-yaz-101]]`) is NOT changed, because no window started this rename and the record names only links by ID for a clash (the app writes links by ID in such a vault, and a link by title still opens the note); (6) the diary marks the fix done, and ONE notice goes to each window of the vault (`windows.ts` `vaultNotice`, the `link:notice` push): `YAZ-101 was used on two Macs. "Bar" is now YAZ-102. 1 link updated. Not changed: "Inbox", "Log".` — the list is each indexed file that still names the old ID (5 names, then `and N more`). 🔒 SAFE TO RUN AGAIN AND TO STOP ANYWHERE: a fix that stops before step 4 leaves the note holding the old ID, so the next sweep finds the pair, finds the saved number and continues — it never takes a second number, and a link already changed names the old ID no more. 🔒 The number STAYS in the fixing Mac's `made`: taken out, the other Mac could read the pair as a copy for a moment (a count file can arrive before a note) and renumber the same note. So while both count files hold the number only that Mac writes for it, and a note that holds the ID later (a Finder copy of the keeper) is a copy that Mac renumbers at once. **A copy** (R30) keeps the keeper rule of YAZ-2293 D4, with one change: the note the index knew FIRST is saved in the diary at the first sight of the pair too, because a number that waits for the vault's first Mac (below) is given ten minutes later, when the index holds both. A copy is silent, as it always was. **While a clash stands** (S46 — the fixing Mac is off, or a write lost): the sweep reports which note keeps the ID (`SweepResult.kept`), and `getIndex` hands that ID out for the keeper only — the other note goes out as a note with no id yet — so `[[YAZ-101]]` opens the keeper on every Mac. A clash is settled only after the index has read every file its watcher already reported (`SweepOptions.indexed`), so the links that arrived in one sync with the second note are in the diary and in the notice's list.
- **The diary** (R21, R25, R27 — `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/diary.ts`): one JSON file for each vault in THIS Mac's user-data folder, `id-diary/<sha256 of the vault path, 16 hex>.json`, never in the vault. `links`: for each ID this Mac made (in its `made`), each file where its index first saw a link, an embed, an `also_in` entry or a link leaf of a folder's settings name it, with the time. `clashes`: for each ID two notes held, the path of the note this Mac knew first, when the other arrived, the new number once taken, the links changed so far, and `done`. Every index event feeds it IN MEMORY (`see`; a note that names no number ID costs nothing); the file is written 2 s after the first change and once at quit (`flushDiaries`, in the quit sequence beside the index cache), atomically — never one write for each event. Limits: only IDs in this Mac's `made` (so 30 days), 20,000 link entries, a pair seen more than 30 days ago is dropped. A diary that is lost only means that no link is changed and each is listed.
- **Which Mac gives a number** (🔒 YAZ-2677 D8, R31 to R34): a number the SWEEP gives — to a note with no id, to a copy, to a folder's settings file — comes at once only on the vault's FIRST Mac: the one whose count file has the lowest `since`, and of two equal the Mac ID that is first in order (`firstMac`). Every other Mac leaves the file, notes when (`SweepOptions.waits`, held by the live index for each open vault), and gives the number `ID_WAIT_MS` (10 minutes) later if the file still needs one — two Macs must never write two numbers into one file (S60 to S62). A vault with NO count file has no first Mac: every Mac waits there, because a Mac that took "no count file" for "I am first" would number every note the moment a yes arrives by the sync ahead of the first Mac's count file (S65); the Mac that then gives the first number is the first. ONE timer for each open vault (`live.ts` `armWait`, R34), set for the file that waited longest and set again for the next; it sweeps only the files whose wait is over, is cleared when the index entry of the vault is evicted, and an entry with a file waiting is not evicted for idleness. A restart starts the wait again (S66). A number the USER asks for comes at once on that Mac (R32): a new note, a new folder, a copy made in the app, a title edit, "Give IDs" (`ids.set`), "Check for duplicates" (`ids.check`) and `yaseendocs id` — none passes `waits`. A clash fix never waits: the rule already names its one Mac. Known and accepted (S67): the first Mac is on with its sync stuck for more than 10 minutes.
- **The ID rewrite** (🔒 D5, D6, D7 — `shared/linkRewrite.ts` `rewriteIds(content, change, { own? })` → `{ content, changed }`): ONE function changes an ID to a new ID in a file's text, for a clash fix, a change of the vault's letters and the backfill of old IDs. `change` is a map or a function from an ID as the app writes it to its new ID. It changes `[[id]]`, `[[id|label]]`, `[[id#heading]]`, `![[id]]`, a link that is a property's whole value (in a list, in a folder's values, in the link leaves of a folder's settings), an `also_in` entry, a folder-value key (`in.<id>` moves with its values, never over a block the new ID has) and, with `own`, the file's own `id:` line. An ID is matched WHOLE (`YAZ-1` is not in `YAZ-12`) and in any case; code blocks and code spans are left; a file that names no changed ID keeps every byte; a second run changes nothing. The file holds the per-file half of the rename engine too (`maskCode`, `rewriteInner`, `rewriteBodyLinks`, `rewriteNoteLinks`, `WIKILINK_RE`), moved from `client/src/links/renameLinks.ts`, and `shared/folderSettingsLinks.ts` holds the link leaves of a folder's settings (`mapFolderSettingsLinks`, `mapOutlineLinks`), moved from `client/src/views/`: one link parser, never two.
- **Change letters** (🔒 YAZ-2677 D5, S79 to S87 — `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/live.ts` `changeLetters`, `reletter.ts` `reletter`; Settings › This vault › "ID letters", `client/src/settings/IdLettersControl.tsx`, shown only while the vault uses IDs). The row shows the vault's letters and "Change letters"; the box counts the notes that change and takes the new letters in a text field — bad input, and the letters the vault already has, do nothing (S80, S85). TWO STEPS, in this order (S81): (1) `ids.json` takes `letters: NEW`, and the letters of before go to the front of `was` (`configWithLetters`; NEW leaves `was` when it was there — letters of before may come back, S85); (2) ONE PASS over every Markdown file of the vault as it is on disk, in path order: `rewriteIds(content, (id) => vaultNoteId(id, letters), { own: true })` — each `id:` line, link, `also_in` entry and folder-value key with letters of before takes the letters of now — and then the file's NAME, only where it is the name the app builds from the title and the ID with letters of before (`noteFileName`, the rule of R26). A number never changes; an old 12-character ID has no letters and is not touched (S87); `[[GPT-4]]` stays a name. 🔒 After step 1 every note opens whatever happens next, because the index reads an ID with letters from `was` as an ID of the vault (S82, S83): a change that stopped, or a file that arrived later by the sync, leaves notes that `IdsState.stale` counts, the row then says "N notes still have the old letters", and "Finish" runs the same change again with the letters of now. 🔒 Safe to run any number of times and to stop anywhere: each write is a compare-and-set against the bytes and mtime read (tried one more time when the file changed under it), a file that names no changed ID keeps each byte, and the content is written BEFORE the name — a run that stopped between the two is finished by the next, which finds the new ID under the old name. 🔒 The same change on two Macs writes the same bytes (S84): the pass takes no number and reads no clock. A vault with no `letters` in its `ids.json` shows its default (R11); a change there saves the chosen letters and keeps the default in `was`, so a note that carries the default follows.
- **Give old IDs numbers** (🔒 YAZ-2677 D6, S88 to S91 — `live.ts` `giveOldIdsNumbers`, `reletter.ts` `backfill`; Settings › This vault › "Old IDs", shown only while the vault uses IDs AND its index snapshot holds a note or folder with an old 12-character ID, which the renderer counts itself — `useVaultScope.ts` `ids.old`). The box says the count, tells the user to sync the vault and close the app on the other Macs first (S91), and takes the vault's own letters as the typed confirm. Each note and each folder's settings file with an old ID takes the vault's next number: oldest file first (`IndexRecord.ctime`, the creation time), then the path, starting after the highest number in use, ALL with one write of this Mac's count file (R19). 🔒 THE PLAN: the map from each old ID to its number ID is saved in the vault, `.yaseendocs/ids-backfill.json` (`BACKFILL_FILE`), BEFORE any note is written. A run that stopped is finished by the next with the SAME numbers — a note in the plan takes the plan's number, only a note that is not in it is given a new one (after each number of the plan) — on this Mac or another. The plan is deleted when a run ends with no file left. 🔒 THE ORDER: part A writes each old note's own `id:` line, oldest first, and its built file name; part B is the same pass as "Change letters" over every Markdown file with the plan as the map — each link, `also_in` entry and folder-value key. A link changes only after its note holds the new ID, so an old ID that remains after a stop still works: its note holds it and no link to it was changed (S90). Compare-and-set writes and the rename downstream as above. Known limit: a file of part B that cannot be written (two tries) keeps its old links and the plan stays, but the row is gone once no note holds an old ID. Never run by the app on its own, and the code that reads old IDs stays: a file with an old ID can still arrive by the sync.
- **Links by id**: resolution tries the id first ("Link resolution"); the app writes `[[<id>]]` from every `[[` picker row and its Create row (Editor rule 24) and from the views' link editors ("Cell editing"); a rename never counts or rewrites one ("Links": Rename). Still written BY NAME: a link to a note that has no id, a hand-typed link, and a link to a FOLDER that has no id — the `[[` picker inserts a folder's id when it has one, else its title or path (YAZ-2290 D10, "Links": Links to folders). A hand-typed `[[Name]]` resolves by title before file name (YAZ-2420 D17, "Link resolution").
- **Search by number** (🔒 YAZ-2677 D9, S69 to S78 — `shared/noteId.ts` `holdsId`, `client/src/search/searchCandidates.ts` `searchRows`): the search box finds a note by its number. `12`, `YAZ-12`, `yaz-12`, `yaz12` and `yaz 12` all find `YAZ-12`, as the FIRST row, with the title matches for the same text after it; a bare number is the whole text of the box (`meeting 2024` is a title search) and finds that number in each vault of the window, each row showing its full ID; an ID matches whole (`12` is never `YAZ-1` or `YAZ-120`); letters the vault had before find the note too (`OLD-12`), and so does an old 12-character ID, a pasted `[[YAZ-12]]`, a file name or a whole path. The hyphen is optional in the search box ONLY: on disk an ID is always `YAZ-12`. The rule in full, its cost and the row are in "Repo layout", at `sidebar/` (the search).
- **What a link reads as** (D8 — the file stores the id, the app shows the title): in the editor, Editor rule 23; in the backlinks snippets, rule 25. In views the ONE answer is `linkText(link, resolve?)` (`client/src/views/expr/values.ts`): the hand-typed label, else the current title — `IndexRecord.title`, YAZ-2420 D14 — of the note the id names (a `#heading` rides along), else the target as written. `render(v, resolve?)`, and `chip` / `cellContent` / `GroupHeader` in `client/src/views/view/GroupHeader.tsx`, take that optional resolver, and WITH it the title is what is shown and what is ordered by: table, list, cards and board values and chips, group headers and board column headers, the summary values, the properties panel's link rows, a link column's sort, the order and label of groups, and the views' search. WITHOUT a resolver `render` is exactly the stored `[[…]]`, and that is what every write-back and persisted key passes — a cell commit, the group-drag swap, a group "+" seed, and the collapse key `groupKeyOf` (`v:[[<id>]]`) — so renaming a note never rewrites a property and never reopens a collapsed group. A formula's `list.join()` renders without one too. The hover preview (`client/src/views/view/PreviewCard.tsx` `usePreview(enabled, wikilinks?)`) receives the window's link source, because an id link has no readable text of its own.
- **Copy ID** (`client/src/lib/copyNoteId.ts` `copyNoteId(id, onNotice?)`): exactly the note's id on the clipboard, said through the passive notice either way — `Copied ID`, or `Can't copy ID: <reason>` when the clipboard refuses. ONE surface (YAZ-2420 D31): right-click on a rendered id link in the editor (Editor rule 23), where the id is the LINK's — the note it points at — and a link has no path to copy. A file's own menus — the sidebar row's, the tab's, a view row's — carry one copy item, **Copy path**, which copies the plain path and nothing else (D7): the id of a note the app named is in that path, and the id of a folder, or of a file the app did not name, is read from its `.folder.md` or its frontmatter.
- **Reserved**: `id` is one of `reservedKeys(true)` (`client/src/links/reservedKeys.ts`: the keys the app owns where the vault uses IDs) — a **Reserved** row with no editor in the properties panel ("The properties panel") whatever its value (a hand-written `id: 42` is the app's key too, which writes its own id over it — YAZ-2420 D30), and a column a view can hide but never delete ("Folder settings: `.folder.md`": Delete column). It is no column of a folder's table, because the rows hold the folder's values and not the note's own keys ("Folder settings": Values); `propertyKeys` (`client/src/views/engine.ts`) itself leaves no key out (YAZ-2523). In a vault that does not use IDs `id` is an ordinary property — an editor in the panel, a column in the table ("Two kinds of vault").
- **The command line** (`desktop/src/cli/cli.ts` — the `yaseendocs` door of "Comments", whose `--help` is the contract an agent reads): two verbs, so an agent can work in names while the files hold ids. 🔒 Both work only in a vault that gives its notes IDs (YAZ-2523 V10 — "Two kinds of vault": The command line): the vault is the nearest folder at or above the page that has ANSWERED in its `.yaseendocs/ids.json` (`idVault`), and only a yes counts. `yaseendocs id <page.md>` prints the page's id. A page that has one is only read — not a byte and not its mtime changes. A page with none — or with an `id` some other tool wrote, another vault's letters included (YAZ-2420 D30, YAZ-2677 R7) — is given one first, through the sweep's own `giveId` and the app's own door (`mint.ts`): the vault's next number, counted in this Mac's ONE count file, so the command gives the number the app would (R20, S40). The command holds no index, so the door walks and scans the vault for its highest number, as `links` below does; it finds this Mac's ID in the app's user-data folder (`userDataDir`). A number ID is printed as the app writes it, whatever its case in the file. Refused with exit 1, the reason on stderr and the bytes untouched: `<page>'s vault does not give its notes IDs (turn on "Give this vault's notes IDs" in the app's Settings)` — the vault said no, or nothing above the page has answered, and whether or not the page holds an `id`; `the properties block does not parse (invalid)`; and `<page> changed while it was being given an id — run this again`. `id <folder>/.folder.md` on a folder with no settings file yet creates it, as the sweep would, and prints its id; a folder that is not there is not found. `yaseendocs links <page.md> [--json]` lists every id ON a page once, number IDs and old IDs alike (S92; each read as an ID of this vault and printed as the app writes it, so `[[yaz-12]]` and a link with letters of before are `YAZ-12`, and `[[GPT-4]]` is a name) — the note ids among its links (the index's own `links` extraction, a folder's values included) first, then the note ids in its frontmatter `also_in` list — each with what that id names NOW, found by walking and scanning the vault exactly as the index does (`walk` + `scanAll`), in path order, so of two pages still sharing an id the one named is the sweep's first choice too. One line per id, `<id>  <title>  <where>` (the title since YAZ-2420 D14 — the path stays the real one): a note → its title and its vault-relative path; a folder (its id is carried by its `.folder.md`) → its title and the folder's path with a trailing `/`, followed by `  (also in)` when the page reaches it only through `also_in`; no page or folder → `<id>  (missing)`. A link written by name is not an id and is not listed; a page with none prints `no ids on <page>`. After them, apart — a blank line, then `in:` — the folders the page holds values for (the blocks of its `in`), one line each: `<id>  <title>  <folder path>/`, or the id alone when no folder has it. `--json` prints `{ links, in }`, 2-space indented: the same rows as `{ id, kind: 'note' | 'folder' | 'missing', title?, path? }`, and the folders as `{ id, title?, path? }`. `--help` says that a vault gives its notes IDs only when the Settings switch is on and that `id` and `links` are refused in one that does not, where a folder's values live, what a folder's id is, and that a page's title is its `title:` line, from which, with the id, the app builds the file name (`<kebab-title>-<id>.md`) — so a page is found from an id by searching the vault's file names for it, and a file the app did not name by its `id: <id>` line. A page whose vault does not use IDs is refused: `<page>'s vault does not use IDs, so there are no ID links to list`.
- **Testing**: `desktop/src/main/noteId.test.ts` (the two shapes, an ID of this vault, the letters, `holdsId`), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/mint.test.ts` (the door: scenario record S27 to S40, and the measured cost of one number), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/twoMacs.test.ts` (two vault folders as two Macs, each with its own Mac ID, count file, index cache and diary, and a sync that fails the test when both Macs wrote one file: S41 to S68, the fix run two times, the fix stopped halfway, the 10-minute wait with fake timers), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/diary.test.ts`, `desktop/src/main/linkRewrite.test.ts` (`rewriteIds`), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/reletter.test.ts` (Change letters: S81 to S87, two copies of one vault end at the same bytes, a change that stopped) and `backfill.test.ts` (Give old IDs numbers: S88 to S90 on a temp vault, a run that stopped continues with the same numbers), `client/src/settings/SettingsDialog.test.tsx` (the two rows and their boxes), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/liveIds.test.ts` (the seat belt for old IDs, the letters at hand-out, the door on the live index), `desktop/src/main/fs/create.test.ts` (born with it), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/idSweep.test.ts` (the sweep, alone and wired into the live index), `scan.test.ts` / `cache.test.ts` (the field and the version pin), `desktop/src/cli/cli.test.ts` (the two verbs); in the client `views/engine.test.ts` (resolution, sort and group labels), `links/completion.test.ts`, `links/renameLinks.test.ts`, `links/backlinks.test.ts`, `editor/wikilink/wikilinkPlugin.test.ts` / `wikilinkClick.test.ts` / `wikilinkPicker.test.ts` / `wikilinkMenu.test.ts`, `views/expr/values.test.ts`, `views/view/EditableCell.test.tsx`, `sidebar/menuSections.test.ts`, `tabs/TabBar.test.tsx`, `views/view/PageContextMenu.test.tsx` and `editor/FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`. Hand steps: `docs/REGRESSION.md`, "Note ids".
- Files: `shared/noteId.ts`, `shared/noteName.ts` (`noteFileName`), `shared/alsoIn.ts`, `shared/linkRewrite.ts` (`rewriteIds`), `shared/folderSettingsLinks.ts`, `shared/ipc.ts` (`mintNoteId`, `ids.set`, `ids.check`, `ids.state`, `ids.reletter`, `ids.backfill`), `shared/types.ts` (`IndexRecord.id`, `IndexResponse.letters`, `IdsState`, `CreateFileRequest.id`, `CreateFileResponse.id`), `desktop/src/main/fs/create.ts` + `copy.ts` + `retitle.ts`, `desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.ts` (`doorFor`), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/mint.ts` (the door) + `idSweep.ts` + `clash.ts` + `diary.ts` + `reletter.ts` (`reletter`, `backfill`, `idsStateOf`) + `scan.ts` (`walk`, `scanFile`) + `live.ts` (the two hooks) + `cache.ts` (`CACHE_VERSION`), `desktop/src/cli/cli.ts`, `client/src/views/engine.ts` (`makeResolver`, `ResolverOptions.ids`), `client/src/links/completion.ts` + `renameLinks.ts` + `backlinks.ts` + `reservedKeys.ts`, `client/src/editor/wikilink/wikilinkPlugin.ts` (`idLinkTitle`, `linkDisplayText`) + `wikilinkClick.ts` + `wikilinkPicker.ts` + `createFromLink.ts` + `wikilinkMenu.ts`, `client/src/editor/blockHandleMenu.ts` (`renderMenu`), `client/src/views/expr/values.ts` (`linkText`, `render`), `client/src/views/view/GroupHeader.tsx` + `EditableCell.tsx` + `PreviewCard.tsx`, `client/src/lib/copyNoteId.ts`.

## Folders are the pages (YAZ-2290 — decisions D1-D11 and E1-E6, decided 2026-10-04, LOCKED)

A real FOLDER is the page: the subject is a DIRECTORY, never a note. Its config is "Folder settings: `.folder.md`", the notes it shows from elsewhere are "Note shortcuts", a link to it is "Links": Links to folders; this is the surface.

**A folder is a tab** (🔒 D3 — OVERTURNING 🔒 D3 of YAZ-1578, "a folder cannot be a tab"): the tab's identity IS the directory path, held in `WindowEntry.tabs` beside file paths. Three doors, all on the folder's row in the Files tree (`sidebar/Tree.tsx`): a DOUBLE-CLICK, **Open** in its menu (`menuSections.ts` `open`, `MenuTargets.openPath` — a folder row outside a 2+ selection), and ENTER on the focused row. Since YAZ-2648 (D2, R1) the double click and Open make it a KEPT tab at the end of the strip, and Enter opens it by the preview rule ("Multi-window": Tabs — the preview tab); before, each of the three took the current tab's place. A single click still selects and folds, Space still folds, shift never opens; "Open N in new tabs" counts folders with files. Only the Files tree can say which path is a folder — one may be NAMED `Notes.md` — so `Editor` asks the newest tree of its own vault — the `root` it is handed, the vault that holds the page (YAZ-2602 S31) — (`lib/pageLabel.ts` `useTreeKind` — folder, file or neither; `isFolderPath` for labels) BEFORE the file-kind dispatch and mounts `FolderView` for it; until a tree for an in-root path is known it renders nothing rather than guess, and a tree that lists the path as neither is not believed until one asked for NOW answers (it may only be behind a rename). The tab strip, the right panel and the window title label a folder by its TITLE (`pageLabel`, YAZ-2420 D14 — the `title` in its `.folder.md`, else its directory's whole name), and its tree row is the active row while its tab is. An in-app rename or delete of the folder carries or closes its own tab like a file's (`useWorkspace`). A folder deleted OUTSIDE the app while its tab is showing keeps the tab, on the missing-file message (`useTreeKind` answering neither); the sidebar's two stale-tab checks — the first tree after launch, and a tab activated after its path left the tree — close a folder tab exactly as they close a file's (`treeHasPath`). The vault root has no row to open and no page (D11).

**What a folder shows** (🔒 D4): `links/shortcuts.ts` `folderRows(records, folders, folder)` — every markdown note UNDER this folder, at any depth, plus the notes that are shortcuts into it or into a folder under it, each once, in the index's own order. A subfolder is no row, and no other file kind is. The same rule from the note's side is `foldersShowing(folder, properties, byId)` — the folder the note lives in and each folder above it, nearest first and short of the root, then each folder its `also_in` names with the folders above that one, each once; a note directly in the vault root is shown by the root alone — and the rows are BUILT from it (`rowsByFolder`), so the two can not disagree. No set-level filter is stored or offered (🔒 Q3 of YAZ-815 — the set IS what the folder holds); a view's own `views[i].filters` narrow within it (YAZ-1218). The engine RUNS over those rows and RESOLVES over the whole vault (`RunOptions.resolve`, 🔒 D2 of YAZ-818), so a link cell pointing outside the folder still resolves, and the link pickers get the whole snapshot too (`FolderHost.vaultRecords` / `vaultFolders`). Feed: the ONE `WikilinkResolveSource` of the vault that holds the folder (`records`, `folders`, `resolve`; one per vault of the window since YAZ-2602, handed down by `editorPropsFor`) — no fetch, watcher or IPC of its own. Before the first snapshot (`resolve === null`) nothing renders: only the index can say a folder has no settings file.

**The page** (🔒 D9), top to bottom in the note editor's own scroller (`.editor-host`): `PageTitle kind="dir"` — the folder's title, a commit reaching App's one rename door as a title edit of kind `'dir'`: `title:` is written into its `.folder.md` and the directory is renamed to that title in kebab-case, nothing inside it renamed and no id changed (YAZ-2420 D6, D16 — "The page title"; where the vault does not use IDs the directory is renamed to the name typed and no `title:` is written, "Two kinds of vault") — then the folder's OWN `FrontmatterPanel`, the views (`.folder-view` → `ViewsPane`), the folder's OWN `CommentsSection`, and `BacklinksSection` for the directory path (D10). Properties and comments are the components a note uses, mounted on the bytes of `<folder>/.folder.md` and re-read whenever that file's index record moves; a folder with no settings file reads as `''`, and its first property, comment or raw-YAML Save creates the file. There is no body: no Crepe mount, no autosave, no Reviews section.

**The adapter** (🔒 D3 of YAZ-818): `ViewsPane` stays ONE component with ONE mount, `FolderView`. The host builds the def IN MEMORY from the settings — `folderViewSet`: `parseViews(stringify({ formulas, properties, views, defaultView }))`, a real parse because `ParsedViews` carries the yaml `Document` every edit is written into; YAML that will not parse falls back to the default views of the vault's kind (`folderSettings(undefined, ids).views`) — and turns every `onChange` into exactly ONE `folder_settings` write. Which view is ACTIVE is session state and switching writes nothing; the tabs themselves edit (🔒 D0, YAZ-1471), and the saved START rides in the def as `defaultView` (YAZ-1104; 🔒 D4, YAZ-1471). Values ARE the host's (**Values** below): it maps the rows it hands over and owns the one writer. **The echo guard**: one `stampOf` over views / formulas / labels / `defaultView` decides whether the def is rebuilt from an index snapshot; own writes queue their stamp in `pending`, so the echo of an older own write never rebuilds the def under a menu mid-edit (YAZ-1234/1241), while a real external edit clears the queue — disk wins. Column declarations run AHEAD of the index the same way (`ahead`, YAZ-1549) and are what `FolderHost.settings.columns` exposes. A refused settings write shows `Could not update the folder: <message>` above the views, never a dialog.

**Collapsed groups** persist in app state as `baseGroups[<folder path>::<viewName>]` (4C), never in `.folder.md`; `ViewsPane.moveCollapsed` carries the entry on a view rename and prunes it on a delete (YAZ-1493). The keys inside are a persisted contract: `groupKeyOf` — `∅` for "No value", else `v:<render(key)>` with NO resolver, so an id link is keyed as stored (YAZ-2293 D8) — and for an inner group `nestedGroupKeyOf`, outer and inner joined by U+001F (YAZ-745).

**The outline is a document** (🔒 D5/D5a; 🔒 D2 of YAZ-900 stands): ONE free-form markdown bullet list, held whole as the string `views[i].outline`. A link in it is just a link — it decorates, completes and navigates like any wikilink and says NOTHING about what the folder holds. `views/outlineDoc.ts` owns the string's grammar (relative-indent depth, one canonical spelling, `escapeBlockStart`); `OutlineEditor` (YAZ-901) is a second, small Milkdown instance — `createCrepe()` locked to one bullet list by `editor/outline/bulletsOnly.ts` — seeded at mount, guarded against a lossy seed (YAZ-974: read-only, reported, never written back), fed later disk changes as a diff in which typing wins (YAZ-1356), saving on Crepe's debounce plus 500 ms and flushing on unmount and on the close/quit handshake. One per folder: `ViewsPane` reads and writes the FIRST outline view's document, and the "+" menu offers Outline only while there is none. While it shows, the toolbar offers no Properties, Filter, Preview or Search.

**Births** (🔒 D4, E1, E3; the title and the name YAZ-2420 D3, D6, D20): "New" and a group "+" in the folder view (`FolderView`'s `FolderHost.create`), "New note" in the Files tree (`sidebar/hooks/rowGestures.ts`) and a note created from an unresolved `[[link]]` (`editor/wikilink/createFromLink.ts`) are born alike — `views/scaffold.ts` `createNote(dir, title, ids, seed?, id?)`, the ONE birth function: the `.template.md` of the folder it lands in, frontmatter and body, then what the caller's seed makes of its properties — a folder view's "New" sets its values in that folder's block ("Folder settings": Values) — then its `title`, which replaces any the template or the seed holds, and nothing else, in one atomic `createFile` at `<dir>/<kebab-title>-<id>.md` (`noteFileName` — the id made here, or the caller's when a link to the note is already written); it resolves the path it built, which is what every caller opens. From the view and the tree the note lands IN the folder. The TITLE is free text, never a name: what was typed in the tree's inline box, on the board's inline add, or as a link's text — the last segment of a pathed link, whose earlier segments are the titles of its folders, each one a folder that is already there before it is one to make (`ensureFolder`, YAZ-2478). From the view's "New" and a group "+" it is `Untitled`, every time: the ids keep the files apart, so nothing is numbered and no create is retried. A FOLDER is born the same way (D6): "New folder" and "New dated folder" call `createDir({ path, title })` with what was typed as the title and `folderPath(parent, title, ids)` as the path — the title in kebab-case, no id; a title with no letter or digit has no such name and is refused, as is one whose name a folder beside it holds (D25, `ALREADY_EXISTS`). 🔒 All of this where the vault uses IDs. Where it does not (YAZ-2523 V3 — "Two kinds of vault": Create, rename and copy) the same doors make plain files: what is typed is the NAME on disk (`plainEntryName`), no `title` and no `id` is written, a folder is its directory alone, and the view's "New" is `Untitled`, then `Untitled 2`, …; until the window's index has landed the kind is not known, and the tree's inline box creates nothing and says so.

Files: `client/src/views/FolderView.tsx` + `folderView.css` (+ `FolderView.test.tsx`, `FolderView.page.test.tsx`), `client/src/views/ViewsPane.tsx` (`FolderHost`), `client/src/views/view/OutlineView.tsx` + `OutlineEditor.tsx` (+ tests), `client/src/views/outlineDoc.ts`, `client/src/views/scaffold.ts`, `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (the dispatch), `client/src/lib/pageLabel.ts`, `client/src/sidebar/Tree.tsx` + `menuSections.ts`, `client/src/tabs/TabBar.tsx`, `client/src/right-panel/RightPanel.tsx`.

## Folder settings: `.folder.md` (YAZ-2290 — D1, D8, E1-E4; the key's rules are Q1-Q8 of YAZ-815, LOCKED)

**Storage** (🔒 D1): a folder's settings live in a hidden `<folder>/.folder.md` (`FOLDER_SETTINGS_FILE`, `shared/types.ts`), FRONTMATTER ONLY. It holds the view settings under the ONE key `folder_settings`, and beside it the folder's own properties (ordinary top-level keys), its `comments`, its `id` and its `title` (YAZ-2420 D6 — the folder's real name; the directory's is that title in kebab-case, with no id). The file travels with the folder, so a move of the folder updates nothing in it, and a title edit only its `title:` line.

**Born with the folder** (🔒 D13): in a vault that uses IDs ("Two kinds of vault") every folder the tree shows holds a `.folder.md` with its `id` from the moment it exists — written by `createDir` for a folder made in the app (a fresh id, and the `title` it was given — YAZ-2420 D6), and by the id sweep for a folder made anywhere else (the vault's next number and no `title`, so the folder shows its directory's name as its title, D5; ONE Mac writes it — the vault's first Mac at once, each other Mac only after 10 minutes, YAZ-2677 D8 — "Note ids": The sweep, Which Mac gives a number. TOMBSTONE: the id was derived from the folder's path, so that two devices wrote the same bytes). At birth it holds ONLY that: no `folder_settings`, no body, so it still reads as the defaults, and a first settings write is an ordinary edit of it.

**Else created on the FIRST change** (🔒 D1) — a folder in a vault that does not use IDs, or one the sweep has not reached yet: no file means the defaults, and opening a folder writes NOTHING. `views/writeProperty.ts` `readForWrite` reads a missing `.folder.md` (`isFolderSettingsPath`) as EMPTY — `{ content: '', mtime: 0 }`, nothing created — and the WRITE that follows is what creates it (`writeFile` with `expectedMtime: 0`; another writer creating it first is a `CONFLICT`), so a first save that changes nothing, or is refused, leaves no file on disk. Every `transformFile` writer and the properties panel's raw Save get it from there. It is born by a plain write, not through the `fs:create-file` door: it holds no `id` until the sweep gives it one ("Note ids": The sweep) — a folder's first shortcut writes the id itself ("Note shortcuts": Add) — and where the vault does not use IDs it never holds one: its birth is no answer about IDs ("Two kinds of vault"). The command line's first `comment` on it creates it too (`transformOnDisk(…, create)`), holding no `id` until the app's sweep gives it one.

**The index and the watcher see it** (🔒 D8): `.folder.md` is the ONE dot-entry the index walk (`vaultIndex/scan.ts` `walk`) and the shared watcher (`fs/watchers.ts` `ignored`) let through, unless a folder above it is skipped. One record map holds notes and settings files; `getIndex` splits them by file name into `IndexResponse.folders` (sorted by path) and never `records`, so a settings file is no view row, no search result, no `[[` completion row, no backlink source, no review candidate and no external-rename hypothesis. `tree(root)` still skips it like every dot-entry, so the Files tree never lists it. Its record's `folder` (root-relative) names the folder it configures. The renderer holds the list as `WikilinkResolveSource.folders` (`useIndex` → `WikilinkIndexBridge`); the id sweep gives it an `id` like any note ("Note ids").

**The one key, the one module** (🔒 Q1): `client/src/views/folderSettings.ts` is the only reader and the only writer of `folder_settings` — `folderSettings(record | undefined, ids)` reads, `writeFolderSettings` and `writeFolderColumn` write, and the rename engine comes in through `mapFolderSettingsLinks` ("Links": Rename). Shape: `columns` (name → `{ kind, target?, required?, options?, optionSort? }`, `kind` one of `PROPERTY_KINDS`' nine; an unknown kind means the column is absent) · optional `defaultView` (a view NAME; absent or stale → the first view) · optional `formulas` (name → expression, `ViewSet.formulas` verbatim, YAZ-745) · optional `properties` (the column LABELS, below) · `views` (the `ViewDef` objects verbatim — unknown view types and extra keys ride along).

**Tolerant parsing** (🔒 Q1): the read never throws and never blocks. Every bad shape becomes one human line in `problems` plus a safe default — a non-map `columns` declares none, a bad column costs only that column, a bad view entry is dropped — and `problems` is shown beside the views, never thrown, never written back.

**The defaults** (🔒 D5a, E2; Q7 as amended): a folder with no file, or a file without the key, has `DEFAULT_VIEWS` — **Table, then Board** — and `DEFAULT_COLUMNS`, one `status` Select (`1-Backlog` / `2-Todo` / `3-In-Progress` / `4-Done`, `client/src/views/folderSettings.ts`): every folder has a Status column by default, and nothing is written for it until a value is set. Once the key EXISTS it states the columns exactly: the first settings write states the default columns with it, a file that saved no columns has none, and Status is never added back. Cards, List and Outline are the "+" menu's to add. A `views` list that is unusable or empty reads as the defaults; a usable one reads back exactly as written, nothing injected (🔒 D3, YAZ-1471 — a read that adds a view back is a Delete the user cannot make stick). 🔒 Where the vault does not use IDs (`folderSettings(record, false)`, YAZ-2523 V11 — "Two kinds of vault": A folder's page) the defaults are a Table ALONE and NO column: a column there is the notes' own property, and none is made up.

**No empty keys in notes** (🔒 E1): a declared column is NEVER stamped into a note — not on open, not when a column is added or retyped, not at birth. A missing value is an empty cell: `RunOptions.declared` hands the folder's column names to `propertyKeys`, so a column shows before any note carries it, and a key reaches a note only when a value is set. A note's own properties panel lists the columns of the folder in force that it has no value for as empty rows ("The properties panel"). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-2290): YAZ-999's presence invariant, which stamped every declared key into every member, is gone.

**Typing is per folder** (🔒 Q8): `settings.columns[key]` — read by `views/editorType.ts` `columnTyping` — is ONE folder's own declaration, the typing ladder's top rung ("Cell editing"). Two folders may declare the same key differently and neither wins globally — no conflict resolver exists and none may be built. Retyping moves the declaration only; note values are never migrated or rewritten (C1 of YAZ-896/897, LOCKED).

**Labels** (🔒 D4, YAZ-1513): a column's KEY never changes; `properties` (`key → { displayName }`) says what the header says. Reading is `engine.ts` `propertyLabel` (the entry, looked up as written, bare and `note.`-prefixed, else `defaultLabel`); writing is the one function `view/columnLabel.ts` `setDisplayName`. A blank label is written as absent, and an emptied map deletes itself.

**Values** (🔒 D19 — where the vault uses IDs; where it does not, the rows are the notes' own properties and a value is written at the top level of its note, "Two kinds of vault": A folder's page): a folder's values for a note are saved in the NOTE under `in`, in a block named by the folder's id — `in: { <folder id>: { <column>: <value> } }` (`shared/folderValues.ts`) — and a folder's views read and write only that block, so two folders showing one note can hold different values under a column of the same name. The note's OWN fields — `id`, `aliases`, `tags`, `also_in`, comments, the review log, anything typed at the top level — are the same wherever it shows and are not `note.*` columns: a top-level field named like a column is the note's own, and no table shows it. ONE door each way, both in `FolderView`: the rows it hands `ViewsPane` carry `folderValues(properties, <folder id>)` as their `properties` — the engine, filters, sorts, groups, formulas, summaries and skins know nothing of folders or `in`; `vaultRecords`, which only resolves links, stays unmapped — and every value a view writes (a cell, a board or section drop) goes through `FolderHost.writeValues(path, writes)` → `writeFolderValues`, one guarded transform of that block (`setFolderValue`) that also drops the note's stale blocks (D20, below). `EditableCell` has no writer of its own. A missing value is an empty cell and nothing is written until one is set; an emptied block is removed, and `in` with its last block. An `in`, or a block, that is not a map reads as empty and is never written over (`FrontmatterWriteError`). A folder with no id shows empty values, and the first value written gives it one first — `folderId`, the path its first shortcut uses. "New" writes its seeded values into the OPENED folder's block of the new note, beside whatever the template holds there (`withFolderValues`) — also when a group-by-Folder "+" births it in a subfolder — while a `file.hasTag` seed is the note's own `tags`; a template is otherwise copied as written. `in` is one of `reservedKeys(true)`. There is no fallback to top-level values and no migration.

**Leaving a folder** (🔒 D20): a folder's values leave the note when the note leaves the folder. A block of `in` is removed only when its id is KNOWN — some record of the index's `folders` has it — and that folder does not show the note (`foldersShowing`, over the note's current folder and the `also_in` in the bytes being written): `withoutStaleFolderValues(properties, showing, known)` (`shared/folderValues.ts`), applied to a note's bytes by `dropStaleFolderValues(root, path, folders)` (`links/shortcuts.ts`). A block for an id no folder has (deleted, or not synced yet) is kept, always; so is every block of a note outside the vault. The removal only ever rides a write the user caused, in that same write, so a refused write removes nothing: (1) an in-app move or rename of a note or a folder — `dropFolderValuesAfterMove`, from App's rename door after `file.rename` and from the sidebar's Paste of a Cut after `file.paste` (`useFileClipboard`; a Copy is no move); the folders that moved are asked at their new place under their unchanged ids, and which notes hold a stale block is decided on the PRE-move snapshot, so a rename, or a move under the same folders, reads and writes nothing; (2) "Remove shortcut" (`removeShortcut`); (3) every value write — `writeFolderValues`'s `tidy` (a cell, a board drop, a panel folder row) and the panel's own-field rows. Nothing else removes a block: not reading, opening or indexing a note, not the id sweep, not a move made outside the app (the note's next value write does), not a column delete, not the panel's raw YAML Save. A note moved out and back finds the folder's columns empty.

**Asking first** (🔒 D21): a gesture that would clear values asks before it runs; one that clears nothing never asks and runs exactly as before. What would go is answered from the window's own index snapshot — no fetch — by D20's rule itself: `valuesLeftBehind({ root, moves, records, folders })` shares `staleAfterMove` with `dropFolderValuesAfterMove`, and `valuesLeftByShortcut(dir, path, records, folders)` asks `withoutLeftFolders` of the note without that shortcut (`links/shortcuts.ts`); both answer `{ notes, folders }`, the folders as directories, nearest to the note first (`foldersShowing`), across notes the union in the order first met. The sheet is advice: what a confirmed gesture clears is still decided by D20 when it runs, so a snapshot that is behind may ask for nothing, or not ask. The doors: App's rename door for a MOVE (a drag in the tree; `pendingRename` holds it, Cancel moves nothing, Move calls `renameFile` as the silent path does), the sidebar's "Remove shortcut" (`confirmingShortcut`, mounted beside the delete confirm), and the sidebar's Paste of a Cut — the menu's and ⌘V's one `pasteInto` (`useFileClipboard`; `pendingPaste`, mounted there too). The Cut's moves are the paths main's clipboard tells every window (`FileClipState.paths`), so a cut made in another window asks too: each path into the target folder, one already there being no move (main skips it), a folder being a path the Files tree holds as one; ONE sheet for all of them. A paste is judged by the vault it LANDS in (YAZ-2602 S46: its index and its folders — a paste from another vault of the same window is a paste from another window), so a path outside that vault matches no index record and never asks. Cancel pastes nothing and leaves the Cut on the clipboard; Move runs the paste as it runs unasked; a clipboard change under the sheet ends the question. A Copy never asks. The sheet (`sidebar/ConfirmMove.tsx`, on `ConfirmSheet`): Cancel / **Move** or **Remove**, the confirm button `--danger`, focus on Cancel, keys bound to the sheet as `ConfirmRename`'s are, no "Don't ask me again". The copy (🔒 verbatim, `moveConfirmMessage`, `removeShortcutConfirmMessage`, `folderList`), names through `pageLabel`, a folder by its directory's name and the vault's top level by the vault's: A pasted COPY asks the same way (YAZ-2420 D34), worded for a copy and with "Copy" on its button: once it lands, each copied note loses the blocks of folders that do not show it (`dropStaleFolderValues`, over the notes the tree lists under each pasted path), and the originals are never touched. A copy into the folder the original is in leaves nothing behind and asks nothing.

> Move 'FV-001 - Zain Shah' to 'z.ARCHIVE'? Its values for Fiverr and ENG PIPELINE will be cleared.
>
> Move 'candidates' to 'z.ARCHIVE'? 54 notes will lose their values for Fiverr and ENG PIPELINE. (one: `1 note will lose its values for …`; several items: `Move 3 items to …`)
>
> Remove the shortcut from 'ENG PIPELINE'? The values of '_PIPELINE-CONTEXT' for ENG PIPELINE will be cleared.

Folders read `A`, `A and B`, `A, B and C`; past three, `A, B, C and 2 more`.

**Delete column** (YAZ-1513): confirm-first (`ConfirmDeleteColumn`), ONE function behind both doorways (`views/deleteColumn.ts`), and its ORDER is the contract: (1) the declaration, every view reference (`order` through `withOrder`, `sort`, `groupBy`, `summaries`, `columnSize`, `cardStyle`, a cards `image`, every filter leaf naming it) and the label go in ONE settings write that is AWAITED — refused, nothing else moves; (2) only then the field is stripped, per file against fresh bytes, from THIS folder's block in every indexed note that holds it there (`notesHolding`) — nothing else in a note is touched: not its own field of that name, not another folder's. The confirm sheet states that count (`FolderHost.valueCount`), taken once, when it opens: `Delete "<label>"? This removes the column from this folder and the "<key>" value from N notes.` Strip failures are aggregated into the folder's banner; successes stay, no rollback. Never deletable, only hideable: `file.*`, `formula.*` and `reservedKeys(true)` (`undeletableReason`). Not offered at all where the vault does not use IDs (YAZ-2523 V11 — "Two kinds of vault"): the host there has no `deleteColumn`, neither menu shows the item, and a column is hidden instead.

**Templates** (🔒 E3): a folder's note template is the hidden `<folder>/.template.md` — existence is the whole declaration, read over plain `readFile`, invisible to tree, index and watcher like any dot-entry. Its frontmatter and body seed a new note under the caller's seed; an `id` in it is never copied. It replaced `<root>/.yaseendocs/templates/<name>.md`, which nothing reads now.

**The write path** (🔒 Q1): `writeFolderSettings(path, next)` writes the whole block as the one key through `writeProperty` (read → rewrite → `expectedMtime` → one retry), so the file's other keys survive; the block itself is rebuilt from the typed value, so comments and hand formatting INSIDE it do not. `columns` is omitted while empty, `problems` never reaches disk, `undefined` deletes the key. The UI reaches it through two doors on the host: `onChange` (every views edit) and the declarations' — `FolderHost.setColumns(columns, views?, labels?)`, which writes the LIVE def's views and labels when the caller names none (YAZ-1471 D4), and `setColumn` → `writeFolderColumn(path, key, next, base, ids)`, one definition against fresh bytes, other columns and unknown metadata kept, a stale `base` refused. `deleteColumn` is not a third door: its settings half awaits the same one.

Files: `shared/types.ts` (`FOLDER_SETTINGS_FILE`, `folderSettingsPath`, `isFolderSettingsPath`, `IndexResponse.folders`), `client/src/views/folderSettings.ts` (+ `folderSettings.test.ts`, `writeFolderColumn.test.ts`), `client/src/views/writeProperty.ts` (`readForWrite`), `client/src/views/deleteColumn.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/view/columnOrder.ts`, `columnLabel.ts`, `TableHeaderMenu.tsx`, `ConfirmDeleteColumn.tsx`, `client/src/views/scaffold.ts` (+ test), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts` + `live.ts` (+ `folderSettings.test.ts`), `desktop/src/main/fs/watchers.ts` (+ `watchers.test.ts`).

## Note shortcuts (YAZ-2290 — D2 as amended by YAZ-2293 D7, E4-E6, LOCKED; E6, the folder count, removed by YAZ-2631 D2)

**The model** (🔒 D2 — a vault that uses IDs; in any other no shortcut is offered or shown, "Two kinds of vault": Not offered): a note LIVES in exactly one folder and may also APPEAR in others. The shortcut is stored on the NOTE — `also_in: ["<folder id>"]`, a folder's id being the `id` in its `.folder.md` — so it is the same file wherever it shows, and nothing is rewritten when a folder or a note is renamed or moved. `client/src/links/shortcuts.ts` is the one door to the key (`foldersShowing`, `rowsByFolder`, `folderRows`, `isShortcut`, `addShortcut`, `removeShortcut`, with `folderRecord` and `foldersById` for the folder's side), over the key's name and its tolerant readers in `shared/alsoIn.ts` (`ALSO_IN_KEY`, `alsoIn`, `alsoInEntries`), and `also_in` is one of `reservedKeys(true)`. Like `id` it is no column of a folder's table: the rows hold the folder's values, not the note's own keys. Reading is tolerant: a scalar is ONE entry, anything that is not a note id is ignored, an id no folder has is ignored quietly, a folder with no `.folder.md` (or one with no id) has no shortcuts, living in a folder and naming it — or naming it twice — is one row, and of two folders still sharing an id the first in path order is the one named.

**Add**: "Add note shortcut" on a FOLDER row outside a 2+ selection (`MenuTargets.shortcutDir`, under the favorite toggle) opens `ShortcutPicker` — the search bar's own candidates, matcher and result list in the confirm sheet's shell: notes only, none the folder already shows; ↑/↓ clamp, ⏎ picks, Esc or a click away closes. The pick runs `addShortcut(dir, path)`: the folder's id is asked of the settings file's OWN bytes, never the index — a folder with no `.folder.md` gets one holding just its id, and a file with no id, or with another tool's, is given one (YAZ-2420 D30) — then one `transformFile` appends the id to the note's `also_in`, beside every entry already there. The row arrives with the index; only a refusal is said (the passive notice).

**Where it shows**: in the folder's views as a row like any other — a cell edit writes the ORIGINAL file — wearing `ShortcutIcon` beside its name (the skins learn which folder "here" is from the `ViewFolder` context). In the tree, under the folder among its own files in name order (`sidebar/folderShortcuts.ts` `folderShortcuts`, off `rowsByFolder`, the lookup the view uses), wearing the same mark: the row IS the note — it opens it, and the selected and active highlights follow its real path — but a click or right-click on it never joins the selection (what ⌘C / ⌘X / ⌘V act on, so the keyboard never cuts, copies or pastes at the real note from there), it never drags and is never the inline-rename seat. In the note's own properties panel `also_in` is a Reserved row read as folder NAMES.

**Remove** (🔒 E5): on a shortcut row "Remove shortcut" stands where Delete would (`MenuTargets.removeShortcut`) — not `danger`, and no confirm unless the removal would clear a folder's values (D21 — "Folder settings": Asking first). The row offers no Delete, Rename, Cut, Copy or Paste: the note is deleted and renamed where it lives. `removeShortcut` takes the folder's id out of `also_in`, and the key goes with its last entry; the same write drops the note's values for the folders that no longer show it (D20 — "Folder settings": Leaving a folder).

**No count** (YAZ-2631 🔒 D2, cases S21–S25, which removes the note count of 🔒 E6 on purpose): no folder row shows a number — on Files, Focus and Favorites and in the search tree, with one vault or more. `Tree` takes no `counts`, and `folderShortcuts` builds the shortcut rows alone: once per index snapshot, a vault read again only when ITS snapshot changed, and a snapshot that moved no shortcut re-renders no tree level. What still counts: the Inbox row's due number (`.tree__count`, "Review"), the Focus tab's "N in focus" line, the delete sheet's notes and folders and the rename sheet's links. Rejected: hiding the number with CSS alone (work that nothing reads); removing the Inbox due number too (it is a to-do count, not a folder count).

**Elsewhere**: a column delete strips the folder's value from a shortcut note like any other ("Folder settings": Delete column); a note's properties panel offers the folders it is a shortcut in under "Properties from" ("The properties panel"); a link column narrowed to a folder offers its shortcuts with the notes that live there ("Links": Links to folders); `yaseendocs links` lists the ids in `also_in` ("Note ids": The command line).

Files: `client/src/links/shortcuts.ts` (+ test), `client/src/links/reservedKeys.ts` (+ test), `client/src/sidebar/ShortcutPicker.tsx` (+ test), `client/src/sidebar/ConfirmMove.tsx` (+ test), `client/src/sidebar/folderShortcuts.ts` (+ test), `client/src/sidebar/Tree.tsx`, `menuSections.ts` (+ test), `Sidebar.tsx` (+ `Sidebar.test.tsx`), `client/src/views/view/GroupHeader.tsx` (`ViewFolder`), `client/src/views/view/icons.tsx` (`ShortcutIcon`).

## The page title + rename confirm (YAZ-888 — LOCKED, ⚡ amending decision E of GRO-2096; the title and the name YAZ-2420 — decisions D1-D32, LOCKED)

What every page shows above its body, and the one beat a rename now earns. The model is "Links": Rename (the pipeline, unchanged); this is the surface that names a page and the rule that guards renaming it. The `title:` line, the built name and the title edit below are a vault that uses IDs. Where it does not (YAZ-2523 V3, V12 — "Two kinds of vault") a page's title is its file name on every screen, a `title:` line is an ordinary property, and the same three surfaces rename the file or folder to the name typed, through the same door and the same confirm (`requestRetitle` → `file.rename`); `file.retitle` is refused there.

**The title is the frontmatter `title`** (🔒 YAZ-2420 D2): a note's in its own frontmatter, a FOLDER's (`PageTitle kind="dir"`, YAZ-2290 D9) in its `.folder.md` (D6), the key spelled `TITLE_KEY` (`shared/noteName.ts`). It is free text — `/`, `:`, `?` and quotes included — and what every screen shows is ONE value, `IndexRecord.title` (D14, `titleOf`): the `title`, trimmed, else the file name without its extension, for a folder its directory's name. The renderer reads it by path (`lib/pageLabel.ts`: `pathTitles` over a vault's index snapshot — `useAllPathTitles` over the snapshot of every vault of the window, YAZ-2602 — `pageLabel` falling back to the file name for a path the index does not hold — not loaded yet, a PDF, an image): the page title, sidebar rows, tabs, the right panel, the window title, search results, the `[[` picker, a rendered id link and its menu, backlinks, every view skin, "Properties from", the confirm sheets and the notices.

**The name on disk is BUILT from the title, never typed** (🔒 YAZ-2420 D3, D6, D13 — `shared/noteName.ts`): a note's is `noteFileName(title, id)` = `<kebab-title>-<id>.md`, title first; a folder's is `kebabTitle(title)`, with no id. Kebab-case: lowercase, accents dropped, every run of anything that is not a letter or a digit ONE hyphen (underscores included), none at either end — except that the underscores a title STARTS with are kept in front (`_PIPELINE-CONTEXT` → `_pipeline-context`), because they hold a note at the top of a list sorted by name and the sidebar sorts by file name (D15) — letters of any script kept, at most 60 characters cut at the end of a word — the title itself is kept whole. A note title with no letter or digit gives the id alone (`yaz-12.md`); a folder title with none is refused (D25). Two notes in one folder may share a title: their ids keep the files apart. The three moments a name is built: at birth ("Folders are the pages": Births), on a title edit IN the app (below), and for a copy made in the app (bridge table, `file.paste`). **The app never renames a file it did not name** (🔒 D5, D23): a note or a folder made outside the app keeps its name and shows it as its title, and a `title:` written by hand — an agent's edit, the properties panel's raw YAML — changes what the app shows and leaves the name where it is, until the title is next edited in the app. A PDF, an image or a text file has no title and no id: its name is its name, and renaming it is a plain rename.

**Where it sits**: the title is BLOCK ZERO of the note's own scroller (`.page-title`, above the properties panel and `.editor-mount`), so it scrolls with the note like every other block there, inside `.editor-instance`'s content column at the editor's own h1 scale. React-side chrome and NEVER a ProseMirror node: the note's markdown round-trips past it byte-identically, and an in-document `# Heading` stays a block of the note like any other — syncing the two is deliberately out of scope.

**ONE DOOR** (🔒): every rename gesture in the app — the sidebar's inline rename, its drag-move, the page title and a table's Name cell (YAZ-2420 D19) — reaches `App` as `(oldPath, to, kind)`, `to` (`RenameTo`) being a path the gesture built or a TITLE that was typed, and the rule is asked there and nowhere else (`requestRename` in front of `renameFile`, which stays the executor). THE RULE: a changed NAME asks first (`isNameChange` = the basenames differ; a title edit always asks, and is never a move), a MOVE asks only when it would clear a folder's values (D21, `ConfirmMove` — "Folder settings": Asking first) and otherwise runs silently — a confirm on every drag would be hostile, and bare links keep resolving across a move anyway. Files and FOLDERS alike: a folder's name is a name. No surface reimplements this, which is why the sidebar needed no change to inherit it.

**A title edit is ONE operation** (🔒 YAZ-2420 D16): on confirm `renameFile` calls `file.retitle({ path, title })` in place of `file.rename` — main writes the `title:` line, THEN renames the note or folder to the name built from it, and answers with the path it built (bridge table) — and the link rewrite is told the new title, so a hand-typed link that spelled the old one follows it ("Links": Rename). Three surfaces, one operation: the page title; the sidebar's Rename on a note or a folder (any other file's Rename is still a rename of its name); a table's Name cell (D19 — "View chrome": Table). The id part of a note's name never changes. A new title whose kebab-case is the one on disk — a capital letter, a comma — changes only the `title:` line: nothing is renamed, repaired or pushed. A rename that fails after the title was written leaves the title right and the name stale, and the next title edit fixes the name. A note with no id is given one first, then named like any other (D29). Refused, through the passive notice, with nothing written: a note whose properties block does not parse (D24); a folder title with no letter or digit, and one whose kebab-case a folder beside it already has — never a `-2` (D25). Renaming a folder renames nothing inside it and changes no id. Boards, cards and lists do not edit titles (D28).

**The copy** (🔒 LOCKED verbatim, `renameConfirmMessage`, pure and separately tested like `deleteConfirmMessage`):

> Rename 'Old Note' to 'New Note'? Links in 3 notes will be updated.

singular at 1, and for a page nobody links to:

> Rename 'Old Note' to 'New Note'? No other notes link to it.

A title edit with nothing to rewrite ends "No links need updating." in its place: notes may still link to the page by id, and those links are never counted (YAZ-2420 D16).

Names are PAGE titles (`pageLabel`, YAZ-2420 D14) — the old one off the index, the new one as it was typed when the gesture is a title edit — never the file's name. N is `countLinkReferences` over the window's OWN index snapshot: synchronous, no fetch, so the sheet opens in the same frame as the gesture, and it counts the very set the rewrite walks (settings-only references included, YAZ-864). Kind comes from the GESTURE — the tree row's type, or the surface the title sits on — never from the index or the extension: a folder may be named `Archive.json`, and a JSON file has no record. A folder's count also takes the links to the folder itself (YAZ-2290 D10). The rewrite re-reads a FRESH snapshot inside `renameFile`, so the copy promises what WILL be updated rather than an exact receipt. NO Obsidian-style 3-way: links ALWAYS update on confirm.

**The sheet** (`ConfirmRename`) is on `ConfirmSheet` like its siblings: our own sheet and never a native dialog, initial focus on CANCEL, Esc cancels, Enter confirms, click-away cancels, `dialog` + `aria-modal` labelled by its own text, Cancel / **Rename**, NOT `--danger` (nothing is destroyed), and no "Don't ask me again" (the gesture that silently rewrites N notes is exactly the one that earns a beat). It is rendered by App, not the sidebar, because the door is App's. ONE deliberate departure, load-bearing: the keys are bound to the OVERLAY, not to `window`. This is the first sheet a KEYSTROKE can open, and React flushes its effects inside that very keydown's dispatch — a window listener hears the Enter that opened the sheet and confirms before the sheet is read. The other half of that same hazard lives in the inputs: an Enter that commits MUST `preventDefault`, or the keystroke's default activation lands on the freshly focused Cancel button and cancels what it just asked for (both `RenameInline` and the title input do; the e2e caught this).

**Editing** copies `RenameInline`'s patterns: click swaps the heading for an input prefilled with the current title, and LEAVING the field commits (YAZ-1553: click-away, Enter, ArrowDown and Cmd-Tab all reach the one `onBlur` door → `onRetitle(title)` → App's door as a title edit; a `settled` ref makes Chromium's last blur on removal inert), Esc is the only discard, ArrowDown then hands focus to the editor below (`focusEditor`, the same call the mount ends with). The sidebar's `RenameInline` follows the same three verbs (open/leave/discard). An empty/whitespace title never commits and neither does the unchanged one; a title is free text (YAZ-2420 D2), so no name rule can reject it — `validateEntryName` holds only the sidebar's rename of a file that is not a note, and the segments of a new note's base folder — and what main refuses lands in the passive notice, exactly where `ALREADY_EXISTS` and every other failure already land — the title never grows an error slot of its own.

**Known + accepted (v1)**: committing a rename — a title edit that changes the name included — REMOUNTS the editor (`CrepeHost key={file.path}`); the dirty buffer survives through `takeRenameBuffer` (Rename, step 4), and the scroll position and caret reset. Do not "fix" this by keeping the mount alive across the path change.

Files: `shared/noteName.ts` (`TITLE_KEY`, `titleOf`, `kebabTitle`, `noteFileName`; `desktop/src/main/noteName.test.ts`), `desktop/src/main/fs/retitle.ts` (+ `retitle.test.ts`), `client/src/lib/pageLabel.ts` (`pathTitles`, `usePathTitles`, `pageLabel`), `client/src/editor/PageTitle.tsx` (+ `PageTitle.test.tsx`), `client/src/sidebar/ConfirmRename.tsx` (`renameConfirmMessage`, `isNameChange`, `RenameTo`, the sheet — its `kind` names a file or a folder through `lib/pageLabel.ts` `pageLabel`) + `ConfirmRename.test.tsx`, `client/src/App.tsx` (`requestRename` / `confirmRename` in front of `renameFile`, the sheet's one render site) + `App.test.tsx`, `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (block zero, ArrowDown), `client/src/views/FolderView.tsx` (a folder's title, `kind="dir"`), `client/src/sidebar/RenameInline.tsx` (the Enter `preventDefault`), `client/src/app.css` (`.page-title*`), `desktop/e2e/title.spec.ts`, `desktop/e2e/names.spec.ts` (titles, ids and file names walked through the real app on a vault that starts empty).

## The properties panel (YAZ-883, typed rows YAZ-884 — LOCKED)

What every page shows BETWEEN its title and its body: its own frontmatter — a note's own, and on a folder's page the folder's (`.folder.md`, **A folder's own panel** below). The model is the frontmatter primitives (`shared/frontmatter.ts`, Editor rule 1) and the write dance is `writeProperty`'s ("Cell editing"); this is the surface.

**TWO MODES, one surface** (🔒 ⚡ YAZ-884). TYPED ROWS are the DEFAULT expanded view: one row per top-level frontmatter key, each with the editor its type asks for. RAW YAML (⚡ YAZ-883) is the FALLBACK underneath them, reached by the panel's one mode control — "Edit as YAML" / "Edit as rows" — and it stays honest by staying literal. Every rule below headed VERBATIM / VALIDATE-BLOCKS-SAVE / *Editing* is the raw mode's; the typed rows' own rules follow them.

**Placement**: BLOCK ONE of the note's own scroller (`.frontmatter-panel`, between `.page-title` and `.editor-mount`), so it scrolls WITH the note like every other block there, in `.editor-instance`'s content column. React-side chrome and never a ProseMirror node, exactly like the title above it.

**VERBATIM** (🔒): a save writes the user's own bytes back through `replaceFrontmatter` — NEVER a parse→reformat. Comments, key order, quoting styles, blank lines and indentation all survive a round trip that only meant to change one value; the FRAME is the file's (opening fence, `---` vs `...` terminator, the line endings around them) and the body is untouched. One trailing newline is supplied when the text lacks it and never a second when it has one, so an unchanged interior round-trips to the identical string and the write is skipped — identity never touches disk, the same rule as the editor's autosave and `writeProperty`.

**VALIDATE-BLOCKS-SAVE** (🔒): what the panel DOES enforce is that the block still parses. `parseFrontmatter` runs over the candidate CONTENT (fences and all, never a hand-wrapped copy) and an `error` blocks the write outright, because a block that will not parse would corrupt every index that reads it (`shared/frontmatter.ts`'s header) and the user would find out through a page that silently lost its properties. Valid-yet-weird YAML saves untouched — the rule is "it parses", not "we like it".

**The write**: read the file FRESH (`api.readFile`), `replaceFrontmatter`, write with `expectedMtime`, and on `CONFLICT` re-read once and retry — the user's YAML wins over a concurrent edit, whose BODY is preserved because only the block is rewritten; a second conflict throws. The page's own open editor absorbs the resulting frontmatter-only `change` silently (`absorbFrontmatterOnly`, GRO-2186), so the panel talks to the editor not at all.

**Its own error surface** (🔒): bad YAML, a `FrontmatterWriteError`, a failed key write, a failed declaration and a failed bridge call all land on ONE inline line inside the body (`Not valid YAML: …` / `Could not save the properties: …` / `Could not delete "x": …` / `Could not set the type of "x": …`), never a dialog and never App's passive notice — that notice is for gestures with no place of their own to speak, and this one has one. A rejected save reverts nothing: the text stays there to fix. The one exception is a typed row's own optimistic failure, which `EditableCell` already shows in place as its "Save failed" chip (5B) — the panel does not double-report it.

**The header**: the backlinks / view-group collapsible idiom — one `<button aria-expanded>` with the chevron rotated −90° while collapsed — showing the chevron plus the `PropertiesIcon` glyph and the BARE top-level key count when the block parses to at least one (`4`; 0 or unparseable → no count, never a guessed one). The words live in the button's `title` and `aria-label` rather than on the chip (YAZ-1758): **Add properties** for a page with no frontmatter — the act rather than the noun, ONE control and not two — and **Properties** / **Properties (4)** otherwise. COLLAPSED by default and deliberately SUBORDINATE to the title above it (small, muted): most pages have nothing to say here. The no-frontmatter page opens on an empty row list carrying the same two acts every page's panel carries.

**Editing (raw mode)**: a monospace `<textarea>` holding the raw interior (`frontmatterInterior`, so the fences are the app's business and never the user's). Text differing from disk is DIRTY, which is the only thing that puts **Save** / **Cancel** on screen; Esc reverts to the disk text (the textarea owns the key while focused, so it never reaches the window's other listeners). Open state AND mode are session-only component state, never persisted — neither an open properties panel nor the mode it was left in is part of a note's identity (BacklinksSection's rule).

**The rows (YAZ-1417):** one row per top-level key in the block's own order, with a type icon, property-name button and shared `EditableCell` value. Clicking the name opens configuration; clicking the value opens its typed editor. Empty values have a quiet placeholder. The footer retains Add property and raw YAML access. Above the rows sits the views' own `ColumnSearch` (YAZ-1473) whenever the expanded typed view has at least one row: it filters rows by KEY — trimmed, case-insensitive substring, block order kept, Reserved/YAML-chipped rows included — while the header count stays the block's total and the footer stays put; no match shows "No properties found."; the query is session-only component state that resets when the panel collapses, when Add property starts and when the mode toggles, so a new row is never born hidden; typing never writes; raw mode, unparseable blocks and empty pages show no box.
