**Folder context** (YAZ-1417, re-pointed by YAZ-2290): the panel subscribes to the window's link feed (`wikilinks.folders`) and lists two groups in ONE list under the search bar, each under its heading (🔒 D19, D23): **Note fields** (`li.frontmatter-panel__own`) and the note's OWN fields — the top level of its frontmatter, without `in` — then a line and **Properties from <folder name>** (`li.frontmatter-property-context`), and under it the values of ONE folder at a time: that folder's block of `in` ("Folder settings": Values). "Note fields" and the line show only where a folder is chosen and an own field is listed; a note no folder shows has neither heading, only its own rows. The choices are the folders that show the note (`foldersShowing`, "Folders are the pages"): the folder it lives in, each folder above it nearest first, then each folder it is a shortcut in with the folders above that one; a top-level note's living folder is the vault root, by the vault folder's name, and the root is no choice otherwise. One choice is plain text; more are a `<select>` the words label, each choice by its folder's title (YAZ-2420 D14, as is the heading's and the popup's `<folder name>`), or by its root-relative path when another choice has that title. Chosen first: the first in that order whose `.folder.md` SAVED settings (`hasFolderSettings`), else the folder the note lives in, with its default columns. The pick is component state — never written, chosen again by the rule on a remount, and the panel is mounted per note — and picking writes nothing. A folder row is typed by that folder's declaration, then legacy vault declarations, then the value, then text, through the one `columnTyping`/`cellEditor` ladder; one of the note's own fields starts at the second rung — a folder never types it. The chosen folder's columns the note holds NO value for are listed as EMPTY rows (🔒 E1), a value in its block for a column it no longer has is a plain row there (editable, removable), and a field reaches the note only when one is filled in — a column is never stamped into a note. Every edit goes where its row lives: a folder row's edit, removal and — while a folder is chosen — Add property write that folder's block (`writeFolderValues`; a folder with no id is given one first, `folderId`), one of the note's own rows the top level. The header's count is the fields the note holds: its own plus its values for the chosen folder. Human-readable existing keys such as `Status` can carry local definitions. "Edit property" — a folder row's — saves the definition to the CHOSEN folder (`writeFolderColumn` on that folder's settings file) and its popup says `In <folder name>`; there is no shared Status vocabulary. A table cell is not this panel: it is typed by its own folder's columns ("Folder settings": Typing is per folder). With no feed, in a vault that does not use IDs (YAZ-2523 V7 — "Two kinds of vault"), or for a note outside the vault, there is no folder: the note's own rows alone, and Add property adds at the top level. A folder's own panel is unchanged — its properties are the top level of its `.folder.md`.

**A folder's own panel** (🔒 D9, YAZ-2290): on a folder's page the SAME component is mounted on `<folder>/.folder.md` itself. Its properties are facts about the FOLDER: the columns it declares for its notes neither type nor list here, `folder_settings` is no row and is left out of the count (it is edited through the views), and the other reserved keys show their chip as on a note. Its first write — a typed row, an add or a raw Save — creates the file (`readForWrite`); a raw block that will not parse creates nothing.

**A row with no editor offers nothing but its chip** (🔒): the app's RESERVED keys — `comments` (YAZ-1472), `id` (YAZ-2293), `title` (YAZ-2420 D14), `reviews` (YAZ-2322), `also_in` (YAZ-2290 D2) and `in` (D19), spelled from `COMMENTS_KEY` / `NOTE_ID_KEY` / `TITLE_KEY` / `REVIEWS_KEY` / `ALSO_IN_KEY` / `FOLDER_VALUES_KEY` (each exported by the one module that owns the string) and never a local literal, gathered since YAZ-1513 into `links/reservedKeys.ts` `reservedKeys(ids)` (the one list this panel and the views' column delete both read — "Folder settings: `.folder.md`": Delete column; all six where the vault uses IDs, and where it does not only `comments` and `reviews`, the other four being ordinary rows with their editors, `title` and `in` listed like any key — YAZ-2523 V12, "Two kinds of vault") — render read-only with a **Reserved** chip, because each already has its own door (the Comments block, the Reviews section, a folder row's "Add note shortcut") and a second one would be a second gesture to keep in step — `id` alone has no door at all: it is the note's permanent identity, and editing it would orphan every link to the note ("Note ids"). `also_in` reads as the folders' TITLES, an entry no folder has as written. `in` alone is no row at all: its blocks ARE the folder rows, and raw mode shows it as written. `title` is no row either, on a note's panel or on a folder's own, and the header's count leaves it out (YAZ-2420 D27): it is the user's value, but it is written only by a title edit, because that edit also names the file, so the page title above the panel is its one door; raw mode shows it as written, and a `title:` changed there is a hand edit — the app shows the new title and leaves the file name (D23). Add property refuses a reserved name. `folder_settings` is NOT one of them (YAZ-2318): the key is the app's only in a folder's own `.folder.md`, whose panel hides its row, leaves it out of the count and refuses the name in Add property (`FOLDER_SETTINGS_KEY`, the door being the folder's views); on an ordinary note it is an ordinary property. Values no editor can hold without LYING about them — nested maps, multi-line scalars, and lists carrying either — render read-only with a **YAML** chip pointing at raw mode. Neither kind offers the type control or the delete; a lossy editor is never the answer, and the fallback underneath is.

**Per-key writes are surgical** (🔒): a row's edit, an add and a delete of one of the note's own fields are ONE `transformFile` of `setFrontmatterProperty(content, key, value)` — `writeProperty`'s own transform — then D20's `dropStaleFolderValues`, so the rest of the block stays byte-identical, comments included (`in` alone is rewritten, and only when a stale block goes); `undefined` deletes the key. A folder row goes through `writeFolderValues(path, <folder id>, [{ key, value }], tidy)` instead, the same guarded transform over that folder's block, which rewrites `in` and no other key. A DELETE takes the deleted key's own leading comment with it: a comment sitting directly above a property describes that property, and `setFrontmatterProperty` treats it as part of the pair. The panel then applies the SAME one-key transform to its own copy of the file, so the raw fallback can never show a block a typed edit already left behind and a later raw Save cannot silently revert it. **Add** takes a name and a value: the name is checked against `PROPERTY_NAME` and reported in the registry's OWN words (`property names are snake_case (…)`), a duplicate is refused outright, and the first value is shaped by the kind the key will be read back at (a declared `number` gets a number, a declared `list` a one-item list) — the registry is the authority, not the typing.

**Editing a definition (YAZ-1417):** both note and view property settings use the shared definition editor and explicit Save/Cancel. `writeFolderColumn(path, key, next, base, ids)` reads fresh bytes and modifies only that column inside the settings map, preserving other columns, views and unknown metadata. Absent settings start from the default columns of the vault's kind (🔒 E2, YAZ-2290 — the first write states them; there are none where the vault does not use IDs) and inject no views. The captured base is compared against current known definition fields; stale same-column changes are rejected with a reopen message. The check repeats after an mtime conflict. A definition edit never bulk-renames or rewrites note values.

**Select options and ordering (YAZ-1417):** `select` stores a scalar and `multi-select` a list. `PropertyDecl.options` stores exact labels in manual order; new options start empty. Optional `optionSort` is `manual`, `ascending` or `descending`, absent meaning Manual. One shared helper computes natural, case-insensitive display order without mutating the manual array. Settings, value/filter pickers and default Board groups consume that order. Alphabetical modes hide drag grips; Manual supports keyboard and full-row dragging with a stable insertion boundary. Unknown stored values remain visible. Renaming/removing an option changes metadata only, as the UI explains.

**Value persistence (YAZ-1417):** Select uses an anchored searchable popup; Multi-select saves each choice and stays open. Per-cell writes serialize so slower earlier requests cannot overwrite the final selection. Optimistic state survives intermediate index updates while writes are pending; failed writes revert the picker to the supplied durable value and show the error. Property and value popovers are portaled outside document zoom; anchored popovers refit as content size changes.

**The dirty draft never loses** (🔒): switching modes asks NOTHING and discards nothing, because the toggle back to typed rows is DISABLED while an unsaved raw draft stands (its tooltip says why — "Save or cancel your YAML edits first"). The simplest honest rule: no dialog, no silent discard. Typed writes land immediately, so typed → raw is always free. A block that will not PARSE has no rows to show, so raw is the whole surface and the toggle is not rendered at all.

**Live truth** (⚡ amended by YAZ-1356): the panel's disk text is derived from the `file.content` prop — since YAZ-1356 CrepeHost's own `disk` state, the whole-file bytes last seen on disk, fed by the watcher path in BOTH the frontmatter-only absorb and the body reload, because `useFile`'s content is frozen at mount and a fresh `file` would remount Crepe — re-derived whenever that prop moves unless a dirty draft is standing (typed text is never thrown away by a load), and replaced by what it WROTE after its own save — compared against the prop it last saw, so its own write never reads as an external change and bounces back. KNOWN + ACCEPTED (v1): `useFile` loads a path ONCE and CrepeHost handles external changes without touching that object, so a frontmatter change made ELSEWHERE reaches the panel on the next open of the page, not the same instant. The panel is already written to follow the prop the moment it becomes live; do not "fix" this by giving the panel a watcher or a read of its own.

Files: `client/src/editor/FrontmatterPanel.tsx` (+ `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`), `shared/frontmatter.ts` (`replaceFrontmatter`, `frontmatterInterior`, `setFrontmatterProperty`) with `client/src/views/frontmatterWrite.test.ts`, `client/src/views/writeProperty.ts` (the one-key write), `client/src/views/editorType.ts` (the one ladder), `client/src/views/view/EditableCell.tsx` (the shared typed editors + the `onCommit` seam the panel writes through), `client/src/views/folderSettings.ts` (`FOLDER_SETTINGS_KEY`, the row a folder's own panel hides), `client/src/links/reservedKeys.ts` (`reservedKeys`) and `shared/comments.ts` (`COMMENTS_KEY`, exported for the RESERVED list — YAZ-1472), `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (block one, and the `root` / `properties` / `wikilinks` it threads in), `client/src/app.css` (`.frontmatter-panel*`).

## Comments (YAZ-1472 — decisions D1-D17 of its scoping, LOCKED)

What every page carries UNDER its body: its own comment stream, Linear's shape inside the note's own scroller. The model is `shared/comments.ts` (whose header doc is the spec), the write dance is `transformFile`'s ("Cell editing"), the surface is `client/src/comments/`; this section is the rules.

**Storage IS the note** (🔒 D1/D2): ONE reserved frontmatter key, `comments`, holding a YAML list of maps — so a comment travels with the note through every sync, rename, copy and Obsidian, and a vault carries no second file to keep in step. A sidecar and a body section were both REJECTED: the sidecar would be the one thing in a vault the note itself does not carry, and a body section would be text the editor could reflow, fold and re-serialise. The list is FLAT: a reply carries `reply_to: <id>` naming its TOP-LEVEL parent, a thread is exactly ONE level deep, and a reply to a reply is filed under the root (`addComment` re-roots it, and a hand-nested one is read the same way) — deeper nesting is what makes comment trees unreadable, and Linear stops at one level too. The spelling lives in ONE place, `COMMENTS_KEY` in `shared/comments.ts`: the block, the index scan and the properties panel all import it, and there is no local literal anywhere.

**The schema** (🔒 D11 — key order as written on disk): `id` (8 hex, `crypto.randomUUID().slice(0, 8)` — enough for one note; a hand-written id that is all digits must be QUOTED, or YAML reads a number and the entry is skipped — the app's writer quotes it), `n` (🔒 D15 — the comment's NUMBER, worn like an id: a top-level comment is one past the highest top-level `n` on the page, shown `#3`; a reply is one past the highest among its parent's replies, shown `#3.1`; handed out once on create and NEVER reassigned — a delete leaves a gap, the way an issue tracker does, so "see comment #3" written anywhere keeps pointing at the same comment; absent on a hand-written entry, which shows a dot), `at` (ISO UTC at SECONDS precision, `2026-09-11T18:22:31Z`, so it sorts as a plain string), `reply_to?` (the top-level parent's id, never a reply's), `by?` (WHO wrote it, declared by the WRITER and never inferred — 🔒 D3: the app writes NO `by` for the person at the keyboard, because a file carries no authorship and a guessed one would be a lie; an agent editing the file writes `by: agent` — and the `yaseendocs` command writes it for its caller, `agent` unless `--by` says otherwise, YAZ-1617 D5), `title?` (a one-line heading; blank means no key at all — a title is a key only when it says something), `edited?` (stamped by `editComment`, same format as `at`), `body` LAST (plain text, trailing whitespace trimmed on write and nothing else; a multi-line body serialises as a block scalar). `addComment` writes `id, n, at, reply_to, by, title, body` — `by` only when the writer declares one (the command does; the UI never); `editComment` strips and re-appends `title`, `edited`, `body` so they land last whether it is the first stamp or a re-stamp, and every other key — `by` included — rides through in its place. **Carry what you do not understand** (🔒): unknown keys on an entry and unknown entries in the list are spread through EVERY write byte-for-byte, never rebuilt from the typed subset — the list is the user's (and an agent's) data, and a writer that only knows its own fields must not eat theirs. Deleting the LAST comment deletes the KEY (🔒 D7 — never a `comments: []` left behind: a key with nothing to say is no key), and deleting a comment takes every reply to it in the same write. On screen the order is CHRONOLOGICAL by `at`, oldest first, stable for equal stamps — or newest-first by the ROOT's `at` when `SettingsState.commentsOrder` is `newest` (YAZ-1515: a global reading preference, one state-driven **Oldest first** / **Newest first** toggle left of fold-all, hidden below two threads; replies inside a thread always read oldest-first; the composer stays at the BOTTOM of the block under both orders — Yasin's call on the demo; under newest-first a new comment therefore lands at the top of the list, away from the box); the FILE is never reordered — a write appends or edits in place, so a hand-arranged list keeps its arrangement and a diff shows one entry.

**Placement** (🔒 D4): a block of the Markdown host's scroller — `.page-title`, `.frontmatter-panel`, `.editor-mount`, then `.comments`, then "Linked mentions" (Editor rule 25), then "Reviews" (YAZ-2322) — so it scrolls WITH the note, inside `.editor-instance`'s content column (backlinks.css's rule, byte for byte), never a panel and never a pane. The blocks stack flush: none of them carries a bottom padding, the scroller (`.editor-host`) owns the page's one 64px tail (YAZ-1680 — before that each block carried 64px, so every pair sat 64px apart). Unlike the backlinks it is ALWAYS rendered: the composer is the door to the first comment, and a block that appeared only once something was in it would have no way in. It opens EXPANDED on a page that has comments and COLLAPSED on one that has none (🔒 E, YAZ-1515 — decided once at mount, `readComments(file.content).length > 0`; a `foreign` / `invalid` block has nothing to read and starts collapsed too), so on an empty page the header is the door to the composer; nothing follows the count afterwards. It is a VIEW over the same disk truth the properties panel reads — `file.content` is CrepeHost's `disk` state (YAZ-1356), re-derived whenever that prop moves — with no watcher of its own, no read of its own and no IPC (the panel's "Live truth" rule). The header's collapse and every fold are SESSION chrome, never persisted — an open comment stream is not part of a note's identity (BacklinksSection's rule).

**A folder's comments** (🔒 D9, YAZ-2290): a folder's page carries the same stream under its views — the same component, on the bytes of `<folder>/.folder.md`, stored under that file's own `comments` key. A folder with no settings file shows the empty stream, and its first comment creates the file (`readForWrite`); from the command line, the first `comment` on a `.folder.md` path creates it too (`transformOnDisk(…, create)`), and `comments` on one not written yet prints `no comments on <path>` (`[]` with `--json`) and writes nothing — a folder that does not exist is still not found.

**Writes** (🔒 D8): every mutation — add, reply, edit, delete — is exactly ONE `transformFile(path, fresh => …)` ("Cell editing"): the change is computed on the FRESH bytes, lands with `expectedMtime`, and on `CONFLICT` re-reads and recomputes once (a second conflict throws) — so another window, an agent appending and a cell edit from a folder's view can all interleave with a comment and nobody's entry is lost. `transformFile` returns `{ mtime, content }` since YAZ-1472 (the transformed bytes, or the fresh read when the transform was a no-op) and the block ADOPTS exactly what landed — never an optimistic copy of its own — holding it against the `file.content` prop it last saw, so its own write never reads as an external change. The watcher echo is frontmatter-only, so it lands through `absorbFrontmatterOnly` → `setDisk` (GRO-2186): an open, DIRTY note never sees a conflict bar from a comment, its unsaved body edits and caret survive, and the block recognises the echo as the bytes it already holds. A failed write is ONE line at the bottom of the block (`Could not save the comment: <message>`) — never a dialog, never App's passive notice — and the draft stays in the box to retry.

**Shapes** (🔒 D10, `commentsShape`): what the key holds is one of FOUR — `absent` (no key, or a bare `comments:`), `list` (a YAML sequence, empty included), `foreign` (a value of the user's OWN under the name — an Obsidian vault may already use `comments` for a scalar or a map), `invalid` (the whole block does not parse). Only `absent` and `list` are OURS to write: `foreign` and `invalid` make EVERY mutation throw `CommentsShapeError` BEFORE any write; the block shows the header and one muted line saying which (`This note's comments property isn't a comment list, so nothing can be added here.` / `The properties block doesn't parse. Fix it in Properties to comment here.`) and no composer — nothing is ever overwritten, because a comment feature that could eat a user's own property would be worse than none. Within a list, MALFORMED entries (not a map, or missing a string `id`, `at` or `body`) are kept on write and skipped on screen; an EMPTY-string body is a comment and still renders; an ORPHAN reply (its parent gone) shows at top level in time order; a hand-made `reply_to` CYCLE never loops — its earliest member stands in as the root; an `at` that will not parse shows as written rather than as "just now".

**Bodies are Markdown, rendered read-only, sanitised** (🔒 D9, `comments/markdown.ts`): GitHub-flavoured — tables, task lists, strikethrough, fenced code, and single newlines are line breaks, the way a typed comment reads. `marked` (gfm, breaks) → DOMPurify (the `html` profile, with `style` / `form` / `input` / `button` / `select` / `textarea` forbidden on top — NO form control survives, a task box is drawn as a glyph, ☑ / ☐) → `innerHTML`, so a body — yours or an agent's — can never run script or reach outside its box. A link in a body opens through `shell.openLink` like the editor's own (one delegated click handler on the block) — never by navigating the app window. Both libraries were ALREADY in the tree through the drawing engine and are now declared DIRECT dependencies of `client/` (a transitive dependency is not a dependency). This is the ONLY place the app turns Markdown into HTML rather than into a ProseMirror document — a second renderer would be a second set of rules to keep in step. The composer is a plain `<textarea>`: what you type is what the file holds.

**The UI** (🔒 D12 — the parts that are contracts): the header is the properties panel's control (chevron, muted label, plain count) at the backlinks header's size, with NO hairline above it (YAZ-1675 — the body flows straight into the header; Linked mentions lost its line the same way in YAZ-1680 — no block under a note draws one) — chevron + **Comments (N)**, N counting every comment and reply, absent at 0, collapsing the rest — with the tools grouped at its right (`comments__tools`, one `margin-left: auto`): FIRST the order toggle (YAZ-1515 — `Oldest first` / `Newest first`, the CURRENT order as its label, the action as its tooltip, shown only with two or more threads), then ONE fold-all button, state-driven the way the views' groups toggle is: **Collapse all** while anything is open, **Expand all** once everything is folded (comments AND reply groups, together). ONE card per thread (on `--bg` — white in the light theme, so dark mode follows): the parent on top, an **N replies** fold, the replies one level in behind a guide line (the outline's, hung under the parent's dot), and a **Reply…** row at the bottom of a threaded card that is ONE line until focused (Linear's row); a comment with no replies yet offers **Reply** on hover instead. The header text NEVER moves (🔒 D16) and, since ⚡ YAZ-1516, NEVER clips — a long title or first line WRAPS and the number, stamp, writer and actions stay on its first line: a titled comment keeps its TITLE in the header row and folds its body under it (chevron, `aria-expanded`); a title-less ONE-LINER is its own header row — it wraps, has no chevron, nothing below, nothing to fold, and keeps the fold's seat so the numbers line up; a title-less multi-line comment keeps its FIRST line (Markdown marker stripped, `commentSplit`, its inline Markdown rendered by `commentInlineHtml` — same renderer, same sanitiser) in the header permanently and folds only the rest. Fold-all counts only what can fold, and is not shown when nothing can. The stamp is relative (`lib/relativeTime`, a minute's resolution) with the absolute time in its tooltip; `(edited)` follows it, and the WRITER shows as `(agent)` after the time in that same quiet voice, because the mark already says it: the avatar's seat holds the comment's NUMBER (`#3`, a reply `#3.1`, a dot when there is none), indigo-soft for you and violet on the `--agent` / `--agent-soft` tokens for a declared writer (a voice that is not yours, deliberately never the accent) — no icons. ⌘Enter submits and Esc cancels — or clears a draft that has no Cancel — with the textarea owning Esc while focused, the properties panel's way. Delete ASKS first (🔒 D17): the app's own sheet, a mirror of the sidebar's `ConfirmDelete` — focus on Cancel, Esc cancels, Enter confirms, click-away cancels, red Delete — and NO "Don't ask me again", because a comment has no Trash behind it and the sheet is the only undo; the copy names the number and, on a parent, the replies that go with it. Copy, LOCKED: **Comments**, `Leave a comment…`, `Reply…`, `Title (optional)`, **Comment** / **Reply** / **Save** / **Cancel**, `⌘ Enter`.

**Never a property** (🔒 D5/D6): `scanFile` DELETES `properties[COMMENTS_KEY]` right after `parseFrontmatter` — comments are CONTENT, not a property — so the key is never in the index, never in its cache, never a view column, never a link source (a `[[link]]` inside a comment is text, not a mention, and never a backlink). `CACHE_VERSION` 2 → **3** (the GRO-2230 rule: the extraction semantics moved, so every old snapshot degrades to one rescan, and the fingerprint pin in `cache.test.ts` moved with it). In the properties panel `comments` is one of the RESERVED keys ("The properties panel") — chipped, read-only, no type control, no delete — spelled from `COMMENTS_KEY`, because it has its own door (the block below the note) and a second one would be a second gesture to keep in step; raw mode still shows the bytes, as it does for every key.

**Testing** (🔒 D13): unit tests — `client/src/comments/*.test.ts(x)` (the model's contract lives at `client/src/comments/comments.test.ts` so vitest's client project collects it), `scan.test.ts` / `cache.test.ts` (the drop and the version pin) and `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx` (the chip) — plus typecheck and a pointed real-app pass on an isolated profile. Since YAZ-2171 one pointed Playwright spec, `desktop/e2e/comments.spec.ts`, walks add → reply → fold → delete against the note's frontmatter; the unit suites stay the default evidence.

**The door** (🔒 YAZ-1617 D1–D5): an agent leaves a comment through `yaseendocs` — the app's own Electron run as plain Node (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, VS Code's `code` pattern): a shell shim at `Contents/Resources/bin/yaseendocs` runs `app.asar/out/main/cli.js`, a second main entry that never imports `electron`. Never the app window, never the single-instance lock, no Node install, no checkout. It writes through the SAME `addComment` / `editComment` / `deleteComment`, guarded the SAME way as D8 on node fs (`transformOnDisk`: fresh read, `expectedMtime`, one retry on CONFLICT, a second conflict refuses). Eight verbs — four for comments, `comment`, `comments [--json]`, `edit`, `delete`; two for a page's id and the ids it points at, `id` and `links [--json]` (YAZ-2293 — "Note ids": The command line); `due [--json]`, when a page — or each page under a folder — is next due for review (YAZ-2322 — "Review": For agents); and `vaults [--json]`, the one verb that takes no page: the app's list of vaults, read from its state file and never written (YAZ-2556 🔒 D2 — "Multi-window": The vault list) — a comment is NAMED the way the app shows it, `#3` / `#3.1` (or bare `3`), or by its id, and a name that matches nothing is REFUSED with nothing written (🔒 YAZ-1627: the model forgives an unknown `reply_to` in a hand-written file; the command never creates one) — and `--help` IS the contract: the only documentation an agent reads, so its wording is UI copy. `comment` writes `by: agent` unless `--by` says otherwise (choosing the tool IS the declaration; D3 stands — the UI still writes no `by`); `edit` and `delete` REFUSE a comment with no `by` — a person's comment is the app's alone (D17 asks first there; a script cannot ask). Exit codes 0 done · 1 refused or failed · 2 usage. **The handshake** — TOMBSTONE (YAZ-2420 D8, D22, reversing 🔒 D2): the right-click item that put a page's path and the command's `--help` line on the clipboard is removed, and nothing replaces it. Everything that existed only for it went with it: `shared/agentInstructions.ts`, `client/src/lib/copyForAgent.ts`, main's `shell:agent-prompt` door (`desktop/src/main/ipc/agent.ts`) and their tests. A page is handed to an agent by its plain path ("Copy path", D7), and the command still ships where it always did — the shim when packaged, `node "<out/main>/cli.js"` in dev. Rejected and NOT to be added back: a PATH installer (🔒 D3 — on Apple Silicon `/usr/local/bin` means admin prompts for no gain), per-vault `AGENTS.md` (loads only when the agent STARTS inside the vault), writing into global agent files (imprinting config unasked), vault detection (the command works on the file), the `yaseendocs://comment` deep link (no receipt; a link anywhere could write).

Files: `shared/comments.ts` (the model, `COMMENTS_KEY`), `desktop/src/cli/cli.ts` + `index.ts` + `cli.test.ts` (the command), `desktop/build/bin/yaseendocs` (the shim, mode 755), `client/src/comments/CommentsSection.tsx` + `comments.css` (the block), `client/src/comments/markdown.ts` (`commentHtml` / `commentSummary`), `client/src/comments/comments.test.ts`, `client/src/views/writeProperty.ts` (`transformFile`'s `{ mtime, content }` return), `client/src/hooks/useAutosave.ts` (`absorbFrontmatterOnly`, unchanged — relied on), `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (the mount, between the Crepe host and "Linked mentions") and `client/src/views/FolderView.tsx` (a folder's stream, under its views), `client/src/editor/FrontmatterPanel.tsx` (the RESERVED entry), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts` (the drop) + `cache.ts` (`CACHE_VERSION` 3), `client/src/app.css` (`--agent` / `--agent-soft`), `client/src/lib/relativeTime.ts`, `client/package.json` (`marked`, `dompurify`).

## Review (YAZ-2322 — decisions D1-D9 and C of its scoping, LOCKED)

Upkeep review: a note that has gone unchanged and unchecked long enough comes up in an Inbox, to be confirmed as still relevant or fixed. It is NOT a memory algorithm — there is no front and back, no grading of recall (🔒 D3, D5). The model is `shared/reviews.ts` and `shared/schedule.ts` (whose header docs are the spec), the app's state is `client/src/review/`; the decision record and its scenario tables are the comments on YAZ-2322. This section is the rules.

**Storage IS the note** (🔒 D1): ONE reserved frontmatter key, `reviews`, holding a flat list with one line per review — `- {at: 2026-10-04T14:02:11Z, rating: keep, text: 9f3a1c2e}`. `at` is ISO UTC at seconds precision, `rating` is `keep` (the one answer v1 writes, 🔒 D4), `text` is the fingerprint of the body the review was written into. It travels with the file, and sync conflicts only when the same note is reviewed on two devices between syncs. A vault-wide log and a sidecar were REJECTED. Like `comments`, every write carries what it does not understand (unknown keys on an entry, unknown entries), a `reviews` value that is not a list is the user's own and is never overwritten, and an emptied list deletes the key.

**The file stores what happened; the date is computed** (🔒 D2): nothing writes a due date anywhere. `dueAt` in `shared/schedule.ts` is the ONLY place one is computed, from the record's `mtime`, `reviews` and `text` and the vault's settings — so an edit made by anything, on any device, moves the date with no extra write, and a changed setting moves every note at once. The cost, accepted: the due date is not a property or a view column, and an agent reads it through `yaseendocs due`.

**The rule** (🔒, v1): a note never reviewed, or whose body changed since its last review, is due `baseDays` after the file last changed. Otherwise it is due after its last review by `baseDays × growth^n`, capped at `maxDays`, where n is the reviews in a row that saw the body as it is now. With the defaults: 30 days untouched, then 60, 120, 240, 365. "Due now" is anything due before local midnight tonight. Smarter signals are a Future issue (YAZ-2372); they change `dueAt` and no file.

**"Changed" is the body, by fingerprint** (🔒 C): `textFingerprint` is FNV-1a over the body with line endings normalised and outer whitespace trimmed, eight hex characters, the same in the renderer and the main process. A reviewed note whose body still matches its last review is unmoved by comments, properties or the log itself. A note with NO matching review has only the file's modified time for a clock, so any write to it restarts the base wait — the one place the modified time decides.

**Who is in review** (🔒 D6): with upkeep on (🔒 D7, below), every Markdown note, until turned off. `review: false` takes one out, `review: true` puts one in whatever the vault default; any other value is ignored. `setInReview` writes the flag only when it differs from the vault default and never touches the log. Never in review: a folder's `.folder.md` (it is not among the index's `records`), a file too large to read (no `text`), a note whose frontmatter does not parse (a review could not be written into it).

**The index carries it**: `IndexRecord.reviews?` (the log as `at` / `rating` / `text`, time order; absent when never reviewed) and `IndexRecord.text?` (the body fingerprint; absent when the file was not read). `scanFile` lifts `reviews` off `properties` exactly as it drops `comments`, so the key is never a property or a column. `reviews` is one of `reservedKeys(ids)`, in both kinds of vault; `review` is an ordinary true/false property, the user's to edit.

**Settings are the vault's** (🔒 D7): `<root>/.yaseendocs/review.json` — `{ algorithm: "upkeep", enabled: false, baseDays: 30, growth: 2, maxDays: 365, reviewByDefault: true }` — through the generic `vaultConfig` door, sanitised field by field (`sanitizeReviewSettings`), so every device computes the same dates. No file means the defaults; nothing is created until a setting is changed. A file that is not valid JSON (a hand edit, a file half synced) changes nothing: the window keeps the settings it has, an open review stays open, and a save is refused with a notice, never written over it. `algorithm` has one value: it is in the file so a second algorithm is a new value, not a new shape. App owns the one `useReviewSettings`; the Settings dialog's Review section edits it.

**Upkeep is off until a vault turns it on** (🔒 D7): `enabled` is false with no file, with no `enabled` in it, and with one that is not true or false. ONE gate: `isInReview` answers false for every note, so the queue is empty, nothing is due, and `yaseendocs due` says so. Off, the app shows none of it — no Inbox row, no "Review this folder", no "Turn review on / off" (`useReview`'s `inReview` is null for every path), no Reviews section (App hands the editor no settings), and the Settings dialog's Review section is the one switch, "Enable upkeep review" (its other rows are `available` only while it is on). An open review closes when upkeep turns off. Nothing is written either way: a note's `reviews` and `review` stay as they are, `reviews` stays reserved, and the index carries the log and the fingerprint whatever the setting, so turning upkeep back on finds every note where it was.

**The Inbox and a session** (🔒 D8, D9): `useReview` (ONE PER VAULT of the window since YAZ-2602, in the vault's scope — `vault/useVaultScope.ts`) gives the due count — the Inbox row above the lens tabs, there while upkeep is on, one row per vault that has it on (R5) — and one session per WINDOW: each vault can hold a session, and App's `startReview` closes every other vault's before it starts one (S41), so a review of vault B runs with B's settings and B's notes whichever vault the tab in front is in (S42). A session takes its queue ONCE at start, most overdue first, for the vault or for one folder and its subfolders ("Review this folder"). It REPLACES the main pane and is not a tab: tabs, their history and the right panel are untouched underneath. The page shown is the real editor. A note is checked against the live index only when its turn comes (gone, turned off or no longer due, it is passed over) and never while it is showing, because editing the page it shows is what makes it no longer due. "Still relevant" (⌘⇧⏎) appends one review through `transformFile`; Skip (⌘⇧S) writes nothing and sends the note to the end; Undo removes the review just written. Leaving: Esc, the bar's ✕, the Inbox row again, or ⌘W close the review and nothing else (⌘W never closes the tab hidden under it); going to a tab — a sidebar click, ⌃Tab — ends it and shows that tab; a link clicked in the note opens in a background tab and the review stays. One editor per note: a note already open in a tab shows through that tab's own layer, and one open in the side panel is reviewed there.

**Seeing it** (with upkeep on): the Reviews section follows "Linked mentions" on a note's page — the next check, then one row per review with a delete, and "Reset review history". A note's row and tab menus turn its review off or on.

**For agents**: `yaseendocs due <page.md>` prints the day a page is next due; given a folder it lists the pages due now under it, each by its day, its title and its path (the title since YAZ-2420 D14); `--json` for the raw shape. It runs the app's own scanner and `shared/schedule.ts`, so it answers what the app shows.

Files: `shared/reviews.ts`, `shared/schedule.ts`, `client/src/review/` (`useReview`, `useReviewSettings`, the bars, the Reviews section), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts`, `desktop/src/cli/cli.ts`. Tests: `client/src/review/*.test.*`, `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.test.ts`, `desktop/src/cli/cli.test.ts`.

## The sidebar lenses (YAZ-847, YAZ-1766, YAZ-2619; the Search tab, YAZ-2638)

Three readings of the window's vault — or vaults: with two or more in the window each lens reads all of them (YAZ-2602, Several vaults below) — switched by the tabs at the top of the sidebar: **Files**, the folder tree on disk — the only hierarchy there is — **Focus**, this window's focus list (YAZ-2619), and **♥ Favorites**. All three are the same `Tree` component, the same `.tree__row` family and the same context menu — and a row of Focus and of Favorites has one item more, "Show in sidebar", first (YAZ-2638 🔒 D3, below). The tabs row holds a FOURTH tab, **Search**, second in the row (YAZ-2638 🔒 D2). It is a tab and no lens: its body is the search, and it is never stored (The Search tab, below).

**The chrome stack** (🔒 D4/D5, YAZ-847, as amended by YAZ-2638 🔒 D2): above the lens row sits the Inbox row while the vault has upkeep on ("Review") — ONE ROW PER VAULT of the window that has upkeep on, in vault order (YAZ-2602 R5, S41): a single row reads "Inbox", two or more read "Inbox · <vault>", each with its own vault's count (`aria-label` "Inbox · <vault>, N due"), and a click opens the review of THAT vault or closes it when it is the one open — one review runs per window at a time. The Inbox row stays above the tabs on each tab, the Search tab too (YAZ-2638 S25). ROW 1 is the tablist — **Files · Search · Focus · ♥ Favorites** (Search is second since YAZ-2638 🔒 D2; the last three are glyphs, a `SearchIcon` since YAZ-2638 D2, an `EyeIcon` since YAZ-2619 D4 and a `HeartIcon` since YAZ-1766 D1, each with its word only in `title` + `aria-label`; the ACTIVE glyph tab is in the accent, the heart filled too — `.sidebar__lens--glyph.sidebar__lens--active`, S18), rendered in `SIDEBAR_TABS` order (`SidebarTab` = `SidebarLens | 'search'`; `SIDEBAR_LENSES` is still the three lenses, the values that are stored) — carrying at its right end the expand/collapse-all button and nothing else: Focus Mode's eye BUTTON ("Exit focus mode", YAZ-1605) is removed (YAZ-2619 D4, R14), and the row must not clip at the sidebar's minimum width, `SIDEBAR_MIN_W` = 180 px (S22). ROW 2 is the search bar of YAZ-801, and it is on the Search tab ONLY (YAZ-2638 🔒 D2). This overturns 🔒 YAZ-797, where search was never a tab and the bar was ALWAYS visible, on every lens, loading / error / empty vault included. The expand/collapse-all button is gone on the Search tab (YAZ-2638 S17). The tabs carry `role="tab"` + `aria-selected` and nothing else — no `aria-controls`, no `tabpanel` — because the one body below is every tab's.

**The lens is App's, and it is WINDOW IDENTITY** (🔒 D4, amended by YAZ-1628): one value, `WindowEntry.sidebarLens`, beside per-window `sidebarCollapsed`, so two windows on one vault browse on different lenses. It lives in App rather than in the Sidebar because the Sidebar is mounted on ONE vault of the window — `key={sidebarKey.current}`, the vault that was first when it mounted, so a vault that joins, leaves behind it or moves does not remount the panel (YAZ-2602; YAZ-2631 R12) — and only while it is open. `changeLens` writes a LENS through `window.setIdentity` (anything but the three lenses → `BAD_REQUEST`) and then mirrors locally; another window's switch never reaches this one. The Search tab it mirrors and does NOT write (YAZ-2638 🔒 D2, S15): App's state is a `SidebarTab`, the stored value stays the last lens, and that lens is where Esc goes back to and where a window that starts again opens. A new, first or deep-link window starts on `DEFAULT_SIDEBAR_LENS` (Files, YAZ-1846), a duplicate inherits and then diverges, and a root CHANGE lands on Files (🔒 D2, YAZ-1846). On load, a window entry with no valid lens of its own takes the file's retired global `sidebarLens` when that is valid, else the default.

**The Search tab** (YAZ-2638 🔒 D2, cases S1–S25 on the issue; it overturns "Search across the lenses", 🔒 D5 of YAZ-847, where a typed query replaced the ACTIVE tab's body and the bar stood on every lens): search is a tab of its own, the second in the row. The bar is on that tab alone. Its body is the search: one line of help with no text, the search tree with text. A typed query never replaces the body of Files, Focus or Favorites. The tabs row stays visible and clickable, and a switch to a different tab keeps the text, the folds and the highlight. ⌘K and a click on the tab show it, with the caret in the bar and the text selected — also when the tab already shows. A plain show of the sidebar on the Search tab does not take the caret. Esc in a page, while the tab shows, puts the caret in the bar. Esc in the bar and the `esc` keycap go back to the lens the window last showed, and the text stays. While a different tab shows, the search does no work; on return the text, the results, the folds and the highlight are back. The tab is never stored. "Add to focus" on a search row stays on the Search tab (this overturns YAZ-2619 🔒 D3 for the Search tab). Everything else about the bar is the search contract under the `sidebar/` entry in "Repo layout".

**"Show in sidebar" on a row** (YAZ-2638 🔒 D1 and 🔒 D3 — the D3 of the "D3 changed" record on the issue, which replaces the first D3 — cases S26–S38): a right-click on a row that is not the Files tree's own shows "Show in sidebar" as the FIRST item of the menu. That is a row of the Search tab, of the Focus tab and of the Favorites tab: a file and a folder, a top row and a row inside a folder, and a vault row of the search tree (S26, S34). A row of the Files tab never has it: that row is in the sidebar's tree already. Blank space of any tab never has it (S28, S38). A right-click inside a selection of two or more rows does not show it: the item names one row (S35). The label is the label of a document tab's item. A click shows the Files tab, opens each parent folder and a closed vault row, scrolls to the row and flashes it for 3 seconds (`tree__row--revealed`, `SIDEBAR_REVEAL_MS`); a folder row opens itself too (S29, S31, S36). It opens no tab, does not change the active document, selects no row, and changes neither the focus list nor the favorites (S30, S36). If the row is gone the notice says `Can't show "<name>" in Files — it is no longer there` (S32, S37). From the Search tab the text stays (S33). WHY first: the row is the reason for the right-click, and the item writes nothing to disk, which is the rule of the Open/View group. WHY on Focus and Favorites: a favorite or a focus item can be deep in the tree, and from those tabs there was no way to see where it is. RULES IT AMENDS: YAZ-2050 🔒 D1 (the menu of a search row was the SAME as the menu of its row in Files) and the same-menu rule of YAZ-1766 and YAZ-2619 (a row of Favorites and a row of Focus carried the same menu as the row in Files). Each is now that menu plus this one item. Refused: the item lower in the menu, or in the "Open in ▸" flyout (it is harder to find there). HOW: `MenuTargets.showPath` — its OWN field, set in `openMenu` when `lens !== 'files'` and the right-click is outside a plural selection — the `showInSidebar` leaf that leads `OPEN_GROUP` in `menuSections.ts`, and `MenuHandlers.onShowInSidebar`, which is `onRevealInFiles` (App's `revealInFiles`: `changeLens('files')` and the reveal request the tab menu uses). Files: `client/src/sidebar/{Sidebar,menuSections}.ts(x)` + `hooks/{useSidebarSearch,rowGestures}.ts`, `client/src/App.tsx`, `client/src/lib/focusHandoff.ts`, `shared/types.ts` (`SidebarTab`, `SIDEBAR_TABS`), `client/src/app.css`; tests in `Sidebar.test.tsx` ("the Search tab (YAZ-2638 D2)", `"Show in sidebar" on a row of Search, Focus and Favorites (YAZ-2638 D1, D3)`, "lens tabs"), `menuSections.test.ts` (`"Show in sidebar" item (YAZ-2638 D1, D3)`), `App.test.tsx` ("S15 (YAZ-2638)", "S16 (YAZ-2638)"); end to end in `desktop/e2e/search.spec.ts` (steps 1 and 5 to 8) and `desktop/e2e/lenses.spec.ts`, each written and not run.

**Several vaults** (YAZ-2602 🔒 D3–D5, R2, R5, R9, R11, cases S10–S28 as amended by A1–A7 and A9, and by YAZ-2631 🔒 D1 and D4–D6, which overturn D5, S16, S24, S26 and the "does not drag" of S14 — the window's half is "Multi-window": Several vaults in one window): with ONE vault the sidebar is exactly as it was — no vault row, the tree at the top (S11). THE VAULT ROWS (🔒 D3): with two or more each vault is ONE top row of the Files tab, in the window's order (the order added, or the order a drag gave) — `useVaultTree`'s `forest`, a `dir` node per vault whose children are that vault's tree and whose path is the vault's root (trailing slash off), named by the vault's display name, else its folder name (S12); a vault whose tree has not landed has no row yet. `vaultRows` (path → name) tells `Tree` which rows they are: `.tree__row--vault`, the label in the header's weight, and NO page — a double click and Enter do not open one as a tab, they fold it. Rejected: always showing the row (a window with one vault pays nothing), and one panel per vault with its own header. THEY START OPEN (R9): a vault row is open unless the user closed it, and a closed one stays closed for the SESSION only — App's `closedVaults`, in neither `expanded` nor the store, and App's because the Sidebar unmounts while hidden. A tab switch does not open a closed vault row; a gesture that SHOWS a row does (`openTo`: a reveal, a create, a paste). "COLLAPSE ALL" AND THE VAULT ROWS (YAZ-2631 🔒 D6, cases S56–S62): on the Files tab with two or more vaults the button closes each folder AND each vault row, and "Expand all" opens them all — one button, one rule (`foldVaults`: the folders through the one `setAll` dispatch, the vault rows through App's `closedVaults`). It reads "Collapse all" while any folder or any vault row is open, so one vault row opened again is enough (S58), and it shows on Files also when no vault holds a folder (S59). A vault row is still in no `dirs` list and is never stored as an open folder (S62), and its closed state stays for the session and the window alone (R9). On the Focus tab and the Favorites tab the button acts on that tab's folders alone and leaves the vault rows of Files as they were (S60), and with one vault it works as it did (S61). Rejected: "Collapse all" closes the vault rows and "Expand all" opens folders only (one button with two rules). "Show in sidebar" opens the row's vault and its folders and flashes the row, asked of the tree of the vault that holds it (S17). THE VAULT ROW'S MENU (S13 as amended by A2): [Paste · Copy path] · [New note · New folder] · [New dated note · New dated folder] · [Open in ▸ — VS Code, Reveal in Finder] · [Remove from this window]; each create and the paste land in that vault's root, by that vault's kind. A vault row is not opened, cut, copied, renamed, deleted, favorited, reviewed, given a shortcut — and has NO focus item (🔒 A2: you add its folders or files; an item for a vault root would have no row in a window with one vault). A selection may hold rows of two vaults (S18), and one that holds a vault row offers no plural item, no Cut and no Copy (A3). BLANK SPACE: with one vault it is the vault's root, as before, and its "Open in ▸" group ends with "Add vault to this window ▸"; with two or more it is no one vault's — its menu is "Add vault to this window ▸" ALONE (S10), and `⌘V` with no selection pastes nothing and says `Select a vault or a folder first`. THE HEADER (S14, S15): the trigger names the vaults or the workspace ("Multi-window": Open beside), and with two or more vaults it is no drop target — a file dropped on a vault row moves to THAT vault's root, when the file is in that vault (R11: a drag to a different vault is refused, with the notice that names cut and paste). THE VAULT ROWS REORDER (YAZ-2631 🔒 D5, cases S45, S50–S52): a vault row drags over the top half or the bottom half of a different vault row, the 2px accent line shows on that edge, and the drop hands App the vaults in the new order (`onReorderVaults` → `reorderVaults` — "Multi-window": Reorder the vaults) — `Tree.reorder` on the Files tree, which it has only with two or more vaults. A file dropped on a vault row still moves to that vault's root and shows the fill of a drop folder, never the line (S50); a vault row dropped on a row below a vault row changes nothing (S51); the vault rows of the search tree do not reorder (S52). Known and accepted: on an OPEN vault row the line of an "after" drop shows under the row itself, above that vault's files, and the row still lands after that vault. THE FOCUS TAB ACROSS VAULTS (A1–A7, amending D4): the window still has ONE focus list, `focusList`, and it holds files and folders of EVERY vault of the window, in the order added or the order a drag gave (A1, A4; YAZ-2631 D3); `focusNodes` resolves it over the forest, so the tab is one list. "Add to focus" / "Remove from focus" work on a row of any vault and on a selection across vaults (A7). With two or more vaults each TOP row of the tab, file or folder, shows the name of the vault that holds it (`.tree__vault` — THE VAULT NAME, below); with one vault no row shows it. A vault that leaves the window takes its items out of the list (A5), a rename and a delete repair it as before, and the prune asks each item of the tree of the vault that holds it, once that tree has landed — so an item is never called gone by a tree still on its way, the tab shows "Loading…" and not its empty text until EVERY vault's tree is in (`loaded`), and a vault's own root in a stored list drops out. "New note" on the tab hops to Files when its folder is a vault's root: Focus never holds a vault row. FAVORITES ACROSS VAULTS (YAZ-2631 🔒 D1, rules R1–R5, R13–R18, R23 and R25, cases S1–S20, overturning YAZ-2602 D5, S24 and S26): each vault KEEPS its own `.yaseendocs/favorites.json`, so the favorites still travel with the vault through sync. With two or more vaults the tab is ONE flat list across the vaults of the window — no vault row (S2) — and each top row shows its vault's name (THE VAULT NAME, below). THE ORDER is `favoritePaths` = `favoriteOrder(roots, favoritesByRoot, storedOrder)`: the stored order across vaults, `AppState.favoritesOrder` ("App state"), says which VAULT has each place, and each vault fills its places in the order of its own file, so a synced file still wins inside its vault (R2, S8); a favorite with no place goes after the placed ones, in vault order and then in its file's order (R13 — so with no stored order the list is in vault order, S3, and a new favorite is not always the last row, S6); with one vault it is that vault's file order, whatever the store holds (S1). The rows are `favoriteRoots(forest, favoritePaths)`, the Focus tab's rule: a favorite whose file is not on this machine draws no row and stays in its vault's file (S9), and a favorite inside a favorited folder shows as a top row and inside its parent (S16). A DRAG moves a favorite to any place of the list, between the rows of any vault (S4 — Drag in the tree, below), and is `saveFavoriteOrder(next)`: each vault's file gets its own part of the new order, only when that part changed (R3, S5), and then, where the window has two or more vaults, the store gets the order across vaults once (`storage.setFavoritesOrder(read, next)`, R4 — `read` is the vaults whose list this window has read, so a vault whose `favorites.get` has not answered keeps its places, R25) — each write is whole alone, so a crash between them leaves a valid list. A file that refuses the write (malformed → `INVALID_CONFIG`) puts THAT vault's list back with the notice `Can't save favorites: …`, while the store keeps the new order across vaults, which stays valid because each vault fills its places from its own file (S15, R16). The cache holds the new order at once (R15), and a drag in another window that shows these vaults lands here through `storage.subscribe` (S12). "Add to favorites" writes to the file of the vault that holds the row — the path goes to the end of that file — a selection across two vaults writes one file per vault, and the notice counts every row (S17; YAZ-2602 S25); "Remove from favorites" takes the row and the others keep their order (S7), and takes the removed paths out of the store's order where that order holds one, so the same favorite added again goes last (R23); "Add to favorites" does not write the store (R13). A vault that leaves the window takes its rows, and the store keeps their places for when it comes back (S14). A vault row closed on the Files tab hides none of that vault's favorites (R18), "Expand all" and "Collapse all" act on the favorited folders (S18), and "New note" on a top file row hops to Files as on Focus, since neither tab holds a vault row (R17). With no favorite that exists in any vault the tab shows its empty text (S20). A `favorites:changed` for a vault of the window re-reads that vault's list (S27). Rejected: the order in the window identity, like the focus list (a new window with the same vaults loses it); a flat list with no stored order (a drag works inside one vault only). The Favorites tab has no focus of its own (A6, YAZ-2619). THE VAULT NAME (YAZ-2631 🔒 D4, cases S41–S44): with two or more vaults each top row of the Focus tab and of the Favorites tab, file or folder, shows the name of the vault that holds it — `.tree__vault`: 10px, the muted colour at 60%, at the right edge of the row, 40% of the row at most with an ellipsis, so the row's own name has the rest. A vault row of Files shows none, a deeper row shows none, and with one vault no row shows one. WHY on Favorites too: without it two files with one name look the same in a flat list. Rejected: the name only on hover; a second line below the row's name. SEARCH ACROSS VAULTS (R2, 🔒 A9 amending S38): the bar covers every vault of the window — one index feed per vault, ONE ranking over the matches of all of them (`useSearchResults(vaults, query)`) — and the tree that is cut is the forest: each match stands under its vault's row, which is a PARENT row like any folder, dim, never a match, open over its matches whatever its fold on the Files tab, and with the Files vault-row menu plus "Show in sidebar" first (YAZ-2638 S26). `SEARCH_CAP` (50) counts the matches of all the vaults together; inside one rank the first vault's rows lead. The flat row that named its vault in muted text is gone with the flat list (YAZ-2620). PER VAULT IN THE ROW RULES: a row's shortcut rows, review state, IDs (`shortcutDir`, the Rename input's mode), delete count and link rewrite all read the vault that holds the row (`vaultOf` / `rootOf`), and "Open in ▸ New window" opens that vault alone. Files: `client/src/sidebar/{Sidebar,Tree,VaultSwitcher,menuSections,vaultMenuSections}.ts(x)` + `hooks/{useVaultTree,rowGestures,useSidebarSearch}.ts`, `client/src/lib/treeState.ts` (`favoriteOrder`), `client/src/lib/storage.ts` (`getFavoritesOrder`, `setFavoritesOrder`), `shared/types.ts` (`mergeFavoritesOrder`, `MAX_FAVORITES_ORDER`), `client/src/search/useSearchResults.ts`, `client/src/app.css` (`.tree__row--vault`, `.tree__vault`); tests in `Sidebar.test.tsx` ("several vaults in one window"), `menuSections.test.ts` ("the vault items"), `treeState.test.ts` (`favoriteOrder`), `storage.test.ts` (`storage.getFavoritesOrder / setFavoritesOrder`), `store.test.ts` (`favoritesOrder`), `ipc/state.test.ts` (`state:set-favorites-order`), `search/useSearchResults.test.tsx` ("over several vaults"); end to end steps 1 to 5 and 7 of `desktop/e2e/multiVault.spec.ts` and the whole of `desktop/e2e/sidebarOrder.spec.ts`, each written and not run.

**Plain DOM, no virtualization** (🔒 D6): every row of an expanded directory is mounted. `TableView`'s windowing is deliberately not taken here — a tree's mounted set is bounded by what the USER opened.

**The Focus tab** (YAZ-2619, 🔒 D1–D5, cases S1–S40, rules R1–R20 on the issue; it REPLACES the Focus Mode of YAZ-1605 / YAZ-1628 and the Favorites tab's own focus of YAZ-1766 D5 — nothing narrows a lens now, and the Files tab always shows the full vault, S40): the second lens, the eye — the third tab, since YAZ-2638 D2 put Search second — shows a list the user builds. THE LIST is `focusList` in the WINDOW's identity (D1: `WindowEntry.focusList` / `WindowIdentity.focusList`, where `focusDirs` and `focusFavorites` were — `sidebarCollapsed`'s rule, so two windows on one vault each keep their own, S25): absolute paths, files AND folders, in the order added or the order a drag gave (YAZ-2631 D3), `MAX_FOCUS` (500) at most; restored on mount through `window.identity` (`storage.getFocusList`) and written back idempotently through `window.setIdentity` (`storage.setFocusList`) — never the per-vault bucket and never the vault, so it does not sync and another window's state broadcast cannot move it; it survives a restart, with the active tab (S24), ⌘⇧N copies it by value (S26), a root change clears it and lands on Files (S27), and `renamePath` / `removePath` repair it through the windows map like `tabs` (S28–S30); the old keys are not read (R1, S32 — no migration). THE MENU ITEM (D3) is one state-aware rule, the favorite toggle's: the `focus` leaf in `menuSections.ts` CLOSES the Open group (R6 — on a single file row it is that group's one item) on every file or dir row in every lens and on a search row, never blank space (R7); its OWN targets `MenuTargets.focusPaths` (the row, or the ordered 2+ selection holding it; a shortcut row gives the note's real path, R8) and `focusIsOn` (true only when EVERY target is in the list, read ONCE when the menu opens — a mixed selection reads "Add" and the add puts in only the missing ones, R9, S4); the label is "Add to focus" / "Remove from focus" and counts a plural ("Add 3 to focus"). `toggleFocus(paths, isOn)` in `useVaultTree.ts` removes every path, or appends the ones not yet listed in the order given and keeps the first `MAX_FOCUS` (R2); a folder just added is opened with the synthetic-child `expandTo`, a file opens nothing (R4); the notice is "Added to focus" / "Added N to focus" / "Removed from focus" / "Removed N from focus" in the plain `info` kind (R3, R15 — never the `favorite` kind, which draws a heart), or, when the add goes past the limit, "Focus limit reached: 500 items" in the `error` kind (R16, S12). THE TAB CHANGE: an ADD shows the Focus tab (D3 — `onToggleFocus` in `Sidebar.tsx` calls `onLensChange('focus')`, also when the limit cut the add to nothing, and not again when the tab is already Focus, R17 — and NOT from the Search tab, which stays: YAZ-2638 🔒 D2, S20, overturns D3 for a search row); a remove and "Clear" keep the tab (R5); the lens change clears the row selection, as every lens change does (R12). SEARCH KEEPS ITS TEXT (S11, as amended by YAZ-2638 🔒 D2): the toggle is not a `viaTree` item, so an add from a search row changes the list and the Search tab stays, with its text and its results, to add more results (YAZ-2638 S20 — before, the add changed the active tab under the results). Esc goes back to the lens the window last showed, and the text stays (before, Esc cleared the query). Search is a tab of its own and covers the full vault (S23). THE ROWS (D2): `favoriteRoots(tree, focusList)` — over the forest, where the window shows two or more vaults (YAZ-2602 A1) — the heart tab's rule: one node per stored path via `findNode`, the list's order, nesting KEPT (an item inside a focused folder draws as a top row AND inside its parent, S7–S9) — into the same `Tree`, the same `expanded` set and the same menu, plus "Show in sidebar" FIRST on each row (YAZ-2638 🔒 D3, S34 — "Show in sidebar" on a row, above; it amends the same-menu rule here). DRAG (YAZ-2631 🔒 D3, cases S26–S28 and S39, overturning S37 for a TOP row — Drag in the tree, below): a top row, file or folder, drags to a new place in the list — between the rows of any vault, where the window shows two or more — and moves nothing on disk; the writer is `reorderFocus`, the hook's `setFocusList`, and the write-back above saves it to the window identity, so the new order survives a tab change and a restart (S27). A file INSIDE a focused folder drags into a folder and moves on disk, as on Files (the tab's `move` is `fileMove`); an item that shows on both levels does each (S39). Above the rows sits THE COUNT LINE (D4, `.sidebar__focus-bar`): "N in focus" on the left — N is the number of top rows on screen, `focusNodes.length` (R11) — and "Clear" on the right (`.sidebar__focus-clear` → `clearFocus`: one click empties the list, S17); the line is absent with an empty list, and the Search tab has none (S19, S20). An empty list shows "Nothing in focus. Right-click a file or folder → Add to focus." (S15), and the tab shows the tree error and "Loading…" as the other two do (R20). The expand/collapse-all button acts on the folders this tab shows (`bodyDirs` = `focusDirs`, `allDirs(focusNodes)`, R10) and is gone when it shows none (S21). THE PRUNE (R13): an item the tree no longer holds — deleted or moved out, in the app or outside it — is dropped from the list in an effect in `useVaultTree.ts` that checks each path with `findNode`, a file as a folder; the last one leaving shows the empty message (S31). Favorites has no such prune (D14 there: its list is in the vault and may not have synced yet). THE CREATE RULE (S35, S36, R18, R19 — `startCreate` in `hooks/rowGestures.ts`, ONE rule for the two list tabs): "New note" / "New folder" on a folder the tab shows mounts the input inside that folder, on the tab; a target dir the tab does not hold, or the vault root on a tab with no rows, hops to Files first; the vault root on a tab that HAS rows mounts the input at the top of the tab — the new note opens as a tab, and its row does not stay, since it is not in the list. "Show in sidebar" goes to Files from any tab and never changes the list (S34 — the old "a reveal outside the focus exits it" went with the narrowing): a document tab's item and, since YAZ-2638 🔒 D3, the first item of each row's menu on this tab. Not included: a keyboard shortcut for the tab, a keyboard gesture to reorder the list, a list shared between windows or machines, a focus item on blank space. Focus across several vaults in one window was not included here and is built since YAZ-2602 (A1–A7: the same ONE list, over every vault of the window — Several vaults, above). Files: `shared/types.ts` (`MAX_FOCUS`, `SidebarLens`, `WindowEntry.focusList`), `desktop/src/main/{store,windows}.ts`, `desktop/src/main/ipc/window.ts`, `client/src/lib/storage.ts`, `client/src/lib/treeState.ts` (`favoriteRoots`, `findNode`), `client/src/sidebar/{Sidebar,menuSections}.ts(x)` + `hooks/{useVaultTree,rowGestures}.ts`, `client/src/app.css`; tests in `Sidebar.test.tsx` (`focus tab (YAZ-2619)`), `menuSections.test.ts`, `treeState.test.ts`, `storage.test.ts`, `store.test.ts`, `windows.test.ts`, `ipc/window.test.ts`; end to end in `desktop/e2e/focus.spec.ts`.

**The Favorites tab** (YAZ-1766, 🔒 D1–D10 on the issue; D11–D15 in 6A–6C): the third lens, the heart — the fourth tab, since YAZ-2638 D2 put Search second — shows the vault's pinned files and folders — of every vault of the window, as ONE flat list, when it shows two or more (YAZ-2631 D1 — Several vaults, above; everything below is ONE vault's list). THE LIST is `<root>/.yaseendocs/favorites.json` (D11 — it moved out of `FolderState` in 6A, D15: a clean break, no migration) — VAULT-RELATIVE POSIX paths on disk, absolute over the bridge (`favorites.get/set/onChanged`), so it TRAVELS WITH THE VAULT and GitHub sync carries it between machines; shared by every window on the vault (a second window's — or another machine's — write arrives through `favorites:changed` and a re-read), in the user's order: add appends, drag-to-reorder rewrites. The Sidebar has ONE writer of a vault's file, `saveFavorites(vault, next)`: optimistic, then `favorites.set`; a refusal (a malformed file → `INVALID_CONFIG`, D12) reverts that vault's list and toasts an error. A drag reaches it through `saveFavoriteOrder`, which writes a vault's file only when that vault's own order changed (YAZ-2631 R3). THE MENU ITEM (D3) is one state-aware rule, `toggleFavorite`, LEADING the "Open in ▸" group (… Rename · ─ · Add to favorites · Add note shortcut (a folder row, YAZ-2290) · Open in ▸ · ─ · Delete) on every file or dir row in every lens, never blank space; its OWN targets `MenuTargets.favoritePaths` (the row, or the ordered 2+ selection holding it) and `favoriteIsOn` (true only when EVERY target is pinned — a mixed selection reads "Add" and pins the missing ones); the label counts a plural ("Add 3 to favorites"), and the toast (`'favorite'` kind, the same heart) says "Added / Removed N to/from favorites". THE ROWS: `favoriteRoots(tree, list)` in `treeState.ts` — one node per stored path via `findNode` (file OR dir), STORED order, nesting and redundancy KEPT (a file pinned inside a pinned folder draws at the root AND inside it; the Focus tab draws its own list with this same function, YAZ-2619 D2); a path the tree no longer holds yields no row and NOTHING ELSE — there is no renderer prune (D14): a note that has not synced to this machine yet simply does not show until it arrives, in-app rename/delete repair the file in main (D13), and main heals dead entries on the user's next write. A pinned folder unfolds through the SAME `expanded` set as Files (D7) — open here, open there. DRAG (D4, as amended by YAZ-2631 🔒 D3 — Drag in the tree, below): top rows carry `Tree.reorder` (depth 0 only) — ALWAYS on, since the tab has no focus list of its own (YAZ-2619 S39: the tab's own focus of YAZ-1766 D5, which made reorder inert while it stood, is removed): a 2px accent line on the hovered row's edge (`.tree__row--drop-before/after`), drop rewrites the list and moves nothing on disk. Below the top rows the tab's `move` is `fileMove`: a file inside a favorited folder drags into a folder and moves on disk, as on Files, and a folder there does not drag. THE SUBSET RULE (D10, 3B1): the tab shows a subset of the vault, so a gesture that needs a row it lacks goes to Files — "Show in sidebar" hops the lens and reveals there (`App.showInSidebar`, a document tab's item; since YAZ-2638 🔒 D3 each row of this tab has the item too, FIRST in its menu — `App.revealInFiles`, "Show in sidebar" on a row, above — so a row's menu here is the menu of its row in Files plus this one item, and a click never changes the list), and "New note" / "New folder" from a row whose target dir is not on the tab hops before the inline input mounts (`startCreate`) — ONE create rule with the Focus tab, so on an EMPTY ♥ tab "New note" on the blank space hops to Files too (YAZ-2619 R19; before, the input had no place to show). Search is a tab of its own (YAZ-2638 🔒 D2): this tab has no search bar, and a typed query never replaces its body. Not in v1: reordering nested rows, a shortcut. Files: `desktop/src/main/favorites.ts` (the file, the repair) + `desktop/src/main/ipc/favorites.ts` (the bridge half, the per-root subscription and broadcast), `desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.ts` (the repair calls), `client/src/api.ts` (`api.favorites`), `client/src/lib/treeState.ts` (`findNode`, `favoriteRoots`), `client/src/sidebar/{Sidebar,Tree,menuSections}.ts(x)`, `client/src/views/view/icons.tsx` (`HeartIcon`), `client/src/components/NoticeIcon.tsx`, `client/src/lib/notice.ts`, `shared/types.ts` (`MAX_FAVORITES`, `FavoritesConfig`); tests `favorites.test.ts`, `ipc/favorites.test.ts`, `ipc/fs.test.ts`, `treeState.test.ts`, `menuSections.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx` (`favorites (YAZ-1766)`), `store.test.ts`, `ipc/window.test.ts`, `windows.test.ts`.

**Drag in the tree: one rule for the three tabs** (YAZ-2631 🔒 D3, rules R8, R9, R20 and R24, cases S26–S40; it overturns YAZ-2619 S37 for a top row, and the "nested rows do not drag" of YAZ-1766 D4): a TOP row drags to REORDER, and a FILE inside a folder drags to MOVE on disk. WHY: one rule is easy to learn, and it is the smallest code. Rejected: the rule for Focus alone, with a special case for Favorites; three drop zones on a folder row (a harder gesture). WHICH TREES REORDER: the ones that get `Tree`'s optional `reorder` (`TreeReorder`) — the Favorites tree and the Focus tree always, the Files tree only with two or more vaults, where its top rows are the vault rows. The Files tree of ONE vault gets none, so its top rows stay in name order and a file drag there moves on disk (S38); the search tree gets none and an inert `move` (`INERT_MOVE`), so no row there reorders and none moves on disk (S37). The gesture lives on depth 0 alone (`rowReorder`): the top level is the only one handed `reorder`, so a drag's line draws no level below again. A top row, FILE or FOLDER, dragged over the top half or the bottom half of a different top row puts a 2px accent line on that edge (`.tree__row--drop-before/after`, `edgeOf`) and never the fill of a drop folder (S40); the drop moves the row there — a top file over a top folder too, with nothing moved on disk (S29). BELOW THE TOP ROWS: a file drags as on Files — into a folder row, through the rename flow, with the links updated and nothing overwritten (S30), and refused between two vaults with `To move between vaults, use cut and paste` (S31) — a folder does not drag (S32), a file dropped on a top FILE row changes nothing (S33), and a reorder drag shows no line on a deeper row, where a drop changes nothing (S34). A row dropped on itself changes nothing, and a drag that ends outside the list clears the line (S35); a shortcut row never drags (S36). ONE REORDER STATE (R8 — `useTreeDrag` in `hooks/rowGestures.ts`): the dragged top row, and the hovered row with its edge, for the tab that is shown; the Sidebar hands it the one list that tab reorders and its writer (`orderRef` / `saveOrder`) — every favorite of the window and `saveFavoriteOrder`, the focus list and `reorderFocus`, or the vault roots and `onReorderVaults`. A drop that changes no order writes nothing — no vault file, no store order, no window identity, no saved workspace (R20: the guard is in `dropReorder`, so it holds on each tab). A drop does nothing when the dragged row is not in the list of the tab that is shown (R24, in `dropReorder` too): a drag that started on a different tab or tree and lost its `dragend` leaves the state set, and its drop clears the state and writes nothing — so no path of a favorite can land in the vault roots or in the focus list. A hover changes two small states and nothing else. Not included: a keyboard gesture to reorder, a drag of a folder row to move it on disk, a drag of a file between two vaults (cut and paste does that). Files: `client/src/sidebar/Tree.tsx` (`TreeReorder`, `rowReorder`, `fileDrag`), `hooks/rowGestures.ts` (`useTreeDrag`), `Sidebar.tsx` (`orderRef`, `saveOrder`); tests in `Sidebar.test.tsx` (the YAZ-2631 cases under `focus tab (YAZ-2619)`, `favorites (YAZ-1766)` and "several vaults in one window"); end to end `desktop/e2e/sidebarOrder.spec.ts`, written and not run.

## Packaging (F1 — GRO-2175)

`npm run desktop:build` = electron-vite build + `electron-builder --mac` with the config in `desktop/package.json` `"build"`: appId `com.yasinarshad.yaseendocs`, productName `Yaseen Docs`, targets `dmg` + `dir` for arm64 ONLY, `identity: null` (ad-hoc signature — no Developer-ID, no notarization, locked), category productivity, icon `desktop/build/icon.icns` (placeholder). Output: `desktop/dist-app/mac-arm64/Yaseen Docs.app` + `desktop/dist-app/Yaseen Docs-0.1.0-arm64.dmg` (`${productName}-${version}-arm64.dmg` — the version is the ROOT package.json's, injected via `-c.extraMetadata.version`). `tools/packDesktop.mjs --mac` then recompresses electron-builder's zlib dmg to LZMA (ULMO, macOS 10.15+; −32 MB) in place, mounting the new image and verifying its app's seal before it replaces the old one, and deletes the stale `.blockmap` (nothing reads it — no auto-updater) (`tools/lib/dmg.mjs`, YAZ-2181). Its test, `tools/dmg.test.mjs`, drives the real `hdiutil` and `codesign` only when `YASEEN_DOCS_DMG_TEST=1`: `npm test` skips it, since every `desktop:build` already runs that path and checks the new image's seal. Before the ad-hoc signature, the `afterPack` hook (`desktop/build/adhocSign.cjs`) deletes Chromium's non-English `locale.pak` folders from the framework (Windows: all but `locales/en-US.pak` + `en-GB.pak`), −47.6 MB installed; the app-level `Contents/Resources/*.lproj` markers stay, so Open/Save panels keep the OS language, while on a non-English macOS sorting and dates follow en-US (🔒 YAZ-2131 D1, YAZ-2182). The bundle's Info.plist claims: `protocols` registers the `yaseendocs` scheme with LaunchServices (so OS-level link opens route to the app), and `fileAssociations` lists `.md`/`.markdown` with role Editor, rank **Alternate** — Yaseen Docs appears in Finder's Open With but never steals the default handler. Packaged files are `out/**` only, and `app.asar` carries NO `node_modules`: every dependency is bundled into `out/main` — electron-vite 5 externalizes `desktop/package.json`'s `dependencies` by default, so chokidar is a devDependency and main/preload set `externalizeDeps: false`; `tools/mainBundle.test.mjs` fails if the bundle requires anything but Node built-ins and electron (YAZ-2185). The renderer is esbuild-minified with `sourcemap: 'hidden'` maps (no `sourceMappingURL`), and `renderSourcemapsAside()` moves every `.map` to the gitignored `desktop/.maps/<version>/` as the bundle is written, so none ships and a minified stack can still be symbolicated locally; main, preload and the cli stay unminified (YAZ-2183). No auto-update, no Intel/universal, no Windows/Linux (locked — Future issues).


**Size and integrity gate (YAZ-2131 1B).** `npm run perf:budget` measures the build against `tools/perf/budget.json` and exits 1 when a row crosses its ceiling or an integrity check fails. Rows: packaged `appBytes`, `frameworksBytes`, `asarBytes`, `dmgBytes`, `chromiumLocaleCount`/`chromiumLocaleBytes`, `lprojCount` (exactly 55: the app-level markers that keep Open/Save panels in the OS language), `asarNodeModulesFiles`; build `rendererEagerJsBytes`/`rendererEagerCssBytes` (the entry's static-import closure + its stylesheet), `rendererTotalBytes`, `rendererMapBytes`, `mainBundleBytes`. Integrity: `out/main/{index,cli}.js`, `out/preload/index.js`, `out/renderer/index.html` exist (and are in the asar); every `./x.js|css` a renderer chunk names is shipped (a lazy Mermaid or CodeMirror chunk that went missing would only fail on first use); KaTeX and the drawing fonts are present; Info.plist carries `yaseendocs` and `.md` as Editor/Alternate; the bundle is ad-hoc sealed and `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passes; `Resources/bin/yaseendocs` is executable. `drawingAssetBytes` (`out/renderer/excalidraw-assets`) is reported on its own row and never gated: the drawing component is out of scope. **The ratchet:** a change that shrinks a row lowers its ceiling in `budget.json` in the same PR; raising a ceiling needs Yasin's OK in the PR description. `tools/perf/baseline.json` is the frozen v0.9.27 @ 63aeeea measurement every before/after quotes, and is never edited.
## Editor rules (client)

1. **Frontmatter**: on load, `splitFrontmatter(content)` → `{ frontmatter, body }`; only `body` goes into Crepe. On save, write `frontmatter + getMarkdownForSave(crepe)`. Frontmatter is re-prepended byte-identically (Crepe would otherwise turn `---` YAML into `***` + paragraph + setext heading underline).
2. **Crepe construction**: always via `createCrepe()` — features come ONLY from `src/editor/featureConfig.ts` (`ENABLED_FEATURES` / `DISABLED_FEATURES`, every `CrepeFeature` classified; ImageBlock, TopBar, AI off; `featureConfig.test.ts` asserts the running editor loads exactly the allowlist); the `Crepe` class itself is `src/editor/crepe.ts` (YAZ-2184) — Crepe rebuilt from its own `CrepeBuilder` and per-feature entry points with ONLY the enabled features bundled, because the package root imports every feature (KaTeX with Latex) and one VALUE import from it puts them all back in the main chunk; production code imports only types from `@milkdown/crepe`, `crepe.test.ts` guards that and holds the class to the package's own `Crepe` (same loaded features, schema, plugins, rendered DOM), and `main.tsx` imports Crepe's common theme file by file without `latex`/`top-bar`/`ai`/`diff` CSS, list_item content `block+` (extended from GFM task item schema), `markdownUpdated` listener wired. The allowlist says which features load; **`featureConfigs` configures one of them**. Its ONE entry is `[CrepeFeature.BlockEdit]`: `listGroup.orderedList = null` removes Crepe's stock Ordered List row (YAZ-1329), while optional `buildMenu` appends Drawing (YAZ-877); neither changes the feature SET. **The Drawing slash item** (`src/editor/drawingMenu.ts`, second build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852): Crepe builds its stock groups and then hands the `GroupBuilder` to `buildMenu`, so Drawing is APPENDED to the existing `advanced` group (Image / Code / Table / Math) of Crepe's OWN menu — never a parallel slash plugin, so filtering, keyboard navigation and hiding all come for free. HOST-SUPPLIED CREATOR: the row exists only when `createCrepe({ drawing })` provides `create()` (Editor wires `() => createDrawing(root)` plus the window's passive `onNotice`) — absent → NO Drawing row is added, while Ordered List remains absent in both cases. `onRun` matches the stock items' mechanism as far as it can: `clearTextInCurrentBlockCommand` first (the same opening move every stock item makes — it clears the block the `/query` was typed into), then, where a stock item would call a schema command, a `tr.insertText` of the embed — because a drawing embed is PLAIN MARKDOWN TEXT (rule 23's `![[…]]`, no schema change, no serializer change, round-trip byte-identical outside the one inserted line). Crepe's `onRun` is synchronous and the write is not, so the view is re-read after the await and the text goes at the LIVE selection; a failed create inserts nothing and goes to the passive notice, never a dialog.
