The file itself: `createDrawing()` (`src/drawings/`) writes a valid EMPTY Excalidraw scene (`{ type: 'excalidraw', version: 2, source: 'yaseen-docs', elements: [], appState: {}, files: {} }`, pretty-printed + trailing newline) to `assets/drawings/Drawing YYYY-MM-DD HH.mm.ss.excalidraw` through `writeAsset` with `create: true` — the drawings home is made by `writeAsset`'s own `mkdir -p`, never scaffolded, and NEVER OVERWRITES: a same-second collision comes back `ALREADY_EXISTS` and the bounded loop retries ` 2`, ` 3`, … (any other failure propagates to the notice). It returns the BASENAME, which is all the embed needs — `readAsset`'s shortest-path rule finds it anywhere under the root. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` step 1 (the spec the preview YAZ-878 extended in steps 2-4, rule 27, and the modal YAZ-879 extended in step 5, rule 28).
3. **Save post-processing**: `postProcessMarkdown()` un-escapes `\[\[` → `[[` (wikilinks/embeds) and restores the visible same-line numeric bullet spelling armored by rule 8. It is ONE walk through `mapOutsideFences` (rule 8's 🔒 one-walk rule, YAZ-2660 D2): each line outside fenced code gets `restoreWikilinks` (`\[\[` → `[[`, and rule 31's `\=` → `=` inside a `[[…]]`) and then rule 8's `restoreItemLine`; a line inside a fence keeps its bytes. 🔒 The wikilink rule also skips a code span on its line (D3): remark escapes nothing inside code, so a backslash there is the user's own text — `` `\[\[page]]` `` saves as written, and a link beside it on the same line is still restored. A line with no backslash does no work. Not covered: a code span that runs over two or more lines. After the walk the text ends in a single `\n` (rule 6). Contract tests: `listItemRoundTrip.test.ts` and `roundtrip.test.ts` ("the text rules never read code" / "… never change code").
4. **Replacing content in a live instance** (external change / Reload): `setMarkdown(crepe, md)` → `replaceAll(md, true)` from `@milkdown/kit/utils`, keeping focus and caret. TAB SWITCHES never remount (GRO-2234): every VISITED tab keeps its editor mounted in its own layer, inactive layers hidden via `visibility: hidden` plus `content-visibility: hidden` (YAZ-2195: a hidden layer skips style, layout and paint, so switches, resizes and theme changes stop scaling with the number of mounted tabs; the right panel's editor layers do the same) — never `display: none`, which would reset the scroller's scrollTop — so scroll position, caret, undo history and the unsaved buffer all survive a switch. Opening a DIFFERENT file in the same tab still remounts the Crepe host (`key={path}`): the old file's editor unmounts (→ autosave flush) and the previous file stays on screen until the next one has loaded.
5. **Outline folding** (GRO-2011): `createOutlineFolding()` (`src/editor/outline/`) is registered in `createCrepe()`. Parent `list_item`s (those owning a nested `bullet_list`/`ordered_list`) — and since YAZ-1709 any `list_item` holding an `image` in its own blocks, leaf or not — get a widget `<button class="outline-toggle" aria-expanded>` at the start of their content (one stroked SVG chevron sized by `--fold-chevron-size`, default 14px, rotated −90° via CSS when collapsed — GRO-2093; the 18px box is unchanged so strip/gate geometry holds); collapsing adds `data-outline-folded="true"` to EVERY nested list of the item (CSS `display:none`) — mixed markers at one indent (`* a` then `- b`) are unified on load (rule 8, GRO-2112); should sibling lists still arise (e.g. bullet + ordered), all of them fold (GRO-2031). An image bullet's fold additionally stamps every image in the item's OWN blocks (nested lists excluded) with `data-outline-folded-image="true"` — a node decoration on the `image` node, so the image node view (rule 30) knows nothing about folding — and `imageView.css` turns that `.image-view` into a one-line chip (a fixed text-height thumbnail, `object-fit: cover`, NO label — a pasted image's alt is its file name, and images are named by typing beside them on the bullet; no handles); the plugin's `handleClickOn` on the chip's THUMBNAIL (not the wrapper) unfolds its bullet — the chevron's toggle, from the chip. Every image of a foldable item, folded or not, also carries `data-outline-foldable-image="true"`, which is what shows the node view's corner `button.image-view__fold` on an expanded image; the plugin's `handleDOMEvents.mousedown` folds the bullet from that button (taken on mousedown so PM never turns the press into a node selection). Fold-all and the guide-line click read the same `entries`, so image bullets take part like any parent. Toggles are metadata-only transactions (`tr.docChanged === false`) — the listener never fires `markdownUpdated`, `getMarkdownForSave()` is unchanged, and the file's mtime is untouched. Fold keys = FNV-1a hash of the item's first-block text (an image counts as its alt text, width stripped, src as the fallback — `itemLabelText`, YAZ-1709) + occurrence index (`outlineFoldKeys.ts`), persisted via `storage.getFolds/setFolds`. Fold transactions carry one of: a position (toggle), `fold-all` / `unfold-all`, `{ set, collapsed }` (guide-line click: direct parents on collapse, their full parent-item subtree on expansion), `undo-fold`; the multi-item ones remember the previous collapsed set so ⌘Z reverts them as one step.
6. **Accepted lossy normalisation** (standard remark-stringify behaviour; content preserved, formatting normalised): `-`/`+` bullets → `*` (alternating `-` for adjacent sibling lists), tabs → 2-space indent, structural `1)` ↔ `1.` ordered markers swap/renumber (but rule 8's same-line numeric bullet text keeps its visible delimiter), setext → ATX headings, two-space hard breaks → `\`, trailing whitespace stripped, `___` → `***`, indented code → fenced, tables re-padded, lazy blockquote continuation gets `> `, `_`/`*`/`[`/`=`/`&` escaped in text where ambiguous, bare URLs/emails → `<autolink>`, file always ends with a single `\n`. First save of an untouched file WILL rewrite the file in this normalised form.
7. **Outliner keymap** (GRO-2012, `src/editor/outline/listCommands.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` with keymap priority 100): Tab = indent (no-op on a first sibling — never inserts spaces), Shift-Tab = outdent (level 1 → paragraph; following siblings nest under the lifted item), Enter at the end of a parent item = new FIRST child (after the subtree when the parent is folded), Enter on an empty item = outdent / leave the list, Backspace at the start of an item = join into the previous paragraph (empty parent: children take its place; non-empty parent: no-op). Multi-item selection + Tab/Shift-Tab moves all selected items.
8. **List-item round trip** (`src/editor/listItemRoundTrip.ts`): an empty bullet/ordered item is written as a bare marker (`*` / `1.`), never `* <br />` — `<br />` opens a CommonMark HTML block that swallows the item's nested children on reload. Parsing gives an item whose nested list starts on a later line (`* ` + children, Obsidian's empty parent) an empty leading paragraph. **Same-line numeric bullet text** (YAZ-1329): a numeric delimiter immediately after a bullet marker (`* 6) text` / `* 6. text`, any bullet spelling or indent) is temporarily escaped before CommonMark parses it, so it remains the bullet's paragraph text rather than becoming a nested ordered list; save removes that boundary escape and preserves the visible digits/delimiter. Real ordered Markdown on its own line remains structural. Fenced code, decimals, prose and non-immediate numeric text are untouched. Before parsing, `normalizeEmptyItems()` also rewrites legacy `* <br />` lines to bare markers and an empty task `* [ ] ` / `* [x]` to `* [ ] <br />` (remark alone reads `[ ]` as text and would write `* \[ ]`); on save `restoreItemLine()` writes it back as `* [ ]` — `<br />` never reaches the disk. **Mixed markers** (GRO-2112): the same pass runs `unifySiblingMarkers()` — `-`, `*` and `+` are one bullet: bullets at one indent parse as ONE list (the first sibling's marker wins on load; a blank line starts a new list, as in Obsidian). CommonMark would otherwise start a new list on every marker change. Line rules live in the function's docblock. Disk is untouched by loading; the first save writes `*` throughout (rule 6) as before. **Empty nested item** (YAZ-1357): the same pass runs `separateEmptyNestedItems()` LAST — a text item followed on the very next line by a DEEPER bare marker gets one blank line between them, because CommonMark forbids an empty item from interrupting a paragraph (without it `* a` + `  *` reads back as the text `a *`, and the outline seed's `-` spelling as a setext heading the bullets-only schema drops whole, which is what tripped the YAZ-974 guard). The blank line is what Milkdown writes back, so the file converges on it; fenced code is untouched. 🔒 **ONE WALK FOR EVERY TEXT RULE** (YAZ-2660 D2): EVERY text rule of the editor, on load (`normalizeEmptyItems()`: the same-line escape, the two empty-item rewrites, `unifySiblingMarkers()`, `separateEmptyNestedItems()`) and on save (`postProcessMarkdown()`, rule 3), reads the text through the ONE walk `mapOutsideFences(markdown, (line, next) => …)` — a rule that reads the text by itself reads code as Markdown, and changes the user's code. A new text rule MUST use the same walk. **What the walk knows**: the fence character (`` ` `` or `~`), the fence length, and that a closing fence has no text after it — so a line of 3 backticks inside a fence of 4, a backtick line inside a `~~~` fence, and a line such as `` ```js `` inside a fence do not end the code; a fence in a bullet is found at any indent, and a fence that never closes runs to the end of the text. The callback gets the line below as `next`, for a rule that looks ahead (`separateEmptyNestedItems()`), and a rule adds a line by returning the line plus `\n`. **Two known limits**: indented code (4 spaces, no fence) is not seen as code — one rare shape still gets a blank line there, a text bullet and a deeper bare marker (a guess at indented code can read a nested list as code, which is worse in an outline note; the editor writes each code block as a fence at the first save, rule 6) — and a code span that runs over two or more lines (rule 3). Contract tests: `listItemRoundTrip.test.ts` (the rules as pure functions) and `roundtrip.test.ts` (real editors; one note with every trigger inside code keeps each code line).

9. **Depth bullet glyphs** (GRO-2013, `src/editor/outline/bullets.css`): CSS only — Crepe's bullet svg is hidden and `.label.bullet::after` draws ● / ○ / ■ by nesting depth of `.milkdown-list-item-block` (repeating every three levels; ordered levels count), `--list-indent: 2.15em`, marker colour 78% of the text colour, all from Crepe theme tokens. Heading-first items (`* # Part 1`) offset the label row by the heading's margin and size it to the heading's line box (`--list-label-offset/height`, shared with the fold chevron). **Done tasks read as done** (YAZ-1514, 🔒 D1–D4): the same file strikes and dims (`--list-done-color`, 55% of the text colour) the FIRST line (a textblock — a first-child code block or table is left alone) of any `li.list-item` whose label carries Milkdown's own `checked` class — so `Mod-Enter`, the ☑ click and a `[x]` written by Obsidian all look identical; nested children keep their own state, a second paragraph in the item stays normal, and `~~` is NEVER written to the file. Markdown untouched.
10. **Typography** (GRO-2009, `src/app.css`): Obsidian defaults — system font stack, 16px / 1.5 body, heading scale 1.802 / 1.602 / 1.424 / 1.266 / 1.125 / 1em at weight 600 (h1 700), line-height 1.3 — set through Crepe's `--crepe-font-*` / `--crepe-base-font-size` custom properties on `.editor-instance .milkdown`.

11. **Underline** (GRO-2028, `src/editor/marks/underline.ts`): ProseMirror mark `underline` (`<u>` in the DOM). On disk it is obsidian-underline's inline HTML `<u>text</u>`: a `$remark` plugin wraps each mdast `html("<u>")` … `html("</u>")` pair (nearest match, any depth, inside any inline parent such as `**…**`) into an `underline` mdast node after parsing, and registers the remark-stringify handler that writes it back as `<u>` + children + `</u>` — so `a <u>b</u> c` round-trips byte-identically. Unmatched tags and every other inline HTML keep going through Milkdown's `html` atom node unchanged. Not a Crepe feature (allowlist untouched, no toolbar button).

12. **Zoom into a bullet** (GRO-2029, `src/editor/outline/zoom.ts` + `zoom.css`): view state ONLY, like folding — the plugin holds the zoomed `list_item` position (mapped through every transaction; cleared if the item is deleted), and decorations hide every block off the path root → zoomed item (`outline-zoom-hidden`), strip the ancestors' glyph/chevron/first block (`outline-zoom-ancestor`, a folded ancestor renders expanded without touching fold state) and render a breadcrumb widget (`File name › Ancestor › … › Item`, labels = first-block text truncated at 40 chars; crumbs zoom to that ancestor, the file crumb zooms out fully). Triggers: click the bullet glyph (`.label-wrapper`; task checkboxes keep toggling), `Mod-.`, `Mod-Shift-.` (see Keyboard). Zooming dispatches a metadata-only transaction: never `markdownUpdated`, disk untouched, fold state unaffected. Not persisted — switching files remounts the editor and clears it, and an external file change (watcher → `replaceAll`) replaces the doc and clears it too. `createCrepe({ zoom: { fileName } })`; allowlist untouched. **History** (GRO-2091): every zoom change (glyph, crumb, `Mod-.`, `Mod-Shift-.`, zoom-out included) pushes one in-memory entry `history.state = { mdappZoom: { file, key } }` with the URL untouched (2-arg `pushState`; `key` = fold-key scheme over ALL list items, `null` = zoomed out), and `popstate` restores that level without pushing — so Back / Forward walk zoom levels. Entries of another file are ignored (left behind by a file switch → a Back press can be silent), an unresolvable key zooms out, the original page entry (no zoom state) = zoomed out.

13. **List guide lines** (GRO-2030, `src/editor/outline/guideLines.ts` + `.css`): every nested list (`li.list-item > .children > .content-dom > ul/ol`) gets a CSS `::before` strip — 10px wide, absolutely positioned (`-1 * (--list-indent / 2 + 10px)`) so its centred 1px line runs under the parent's glyph centre; zero layout shift, folded lists take their line with them, colours from `currentColor` (14%, hover 35%). Pseudo boxes hit-test as the list element, so a pointer left of the list's border box = the strip: the plugin's `handleDOMEvents` turns mousedown into `toggleOutlineFoldChildren(listPos)` (GRO-2107/YAZ-1317: any direct parent expanded collapses the direct parents; when all are collapsed, the click unfolds every parent item in their full subtree; the line's owner and leaves remain untouched; an all-leaf line is a swallowed no-op; markdown/mtime untouched, caret unmoved) and mousemove into the `outline-guide-hover` highlight (element `:hover` would light up while editing text inside).

### Clipboard input and paste modes (YAZ-1394)

Normal Paste keeps Milkdown’s supported rich HTML preference, document typography, existing fake-bullet outline conversion, and Markdown interpretation when only plain text is available. `editor/clipboardPaste.ts`, registered after `outlinePaste`, converts external root-level `p / br… / p` separators into one empty paragraph per standalone break. A break-containing paragraph otherwise acquires an extra visual line. This preserves intentional blank spacing rather than deleting it; inline breaks, nested blocks, explicit empty paragraphs, and unmarked internal `data-pm-slice` HTML are untouched. Our marked copy-out separators are restored to empty paragraphs first, including within internal slices and nested lists, preserving attributes and slice metadata. The upstream Google Docs wrapper cleanup runs first. This rule is based on a reproduced clipboard shape; it is not a promise to normalize arbitrary source HTML.

Native Edit and right-click menus expose **Paste as → Plain text / Markdown**; Plain text uses **⌘⇧V**. Main captures text once per gesture and sends `ClipboardPasteRequest` to that window. Plain text inserts characters literally, preserves spaces and line breaks (CRLF/CR become LF), and does not parse Markdown. Markdown explicitly parses that text with the existing schema, even when the clipboard also has HTML; open slice boundaries let inline content remain in its surrounding sentence. Both modes apply existing paste rules (including table repair and the bullets-only lock of a folder's outline), replace the active selection in one undoable transaction, and leave empty clipboard text as a no-op. In code, text remains literal.

**Normal pasted numbering (YAZ-1429):** external numbered lists become ordinary text with visible labels, so Enter cannot continue or renumber them. `editor/clipboardNumbers.ts` converts HTML ordered-list markers before DOM parsing, honoring numeric start, reverse order and explicit item values while retaining rich inline content, nested blocks and empty parent labels. Foreign `data-pm-slice` context is removed so it cannot recreate automatic numbering. Plain-text-only paste uses the existing remark AST and each item's source position to preserve original labels/punctuation, repeated numbers and task markers; code nodes remain literal. Mixed fake-bullet outlines use the same conversion, paste constraints and undo boundary. Saved ordered Markdown, Number children and explicit Paste as Markdown remain unchanged. Newly copied app HTML carries `data-yaseendocs-clipboard` alongside ProseMirror's slice metadata so intentional internal lists round-trip. This identifier exists only in clipboard HTML, never in note files. A text-only copy has no source identity and follows normal external-paste rules; explicit Markdown is the deliberate override. Existing notes are not migrated. Source-specific CSS counters are outside this normalization contract.

Each Crepe view subscribes through `menu.onPasteAs`, claims only its own focused surface, and unsubscribes on destroy. CodeMirror and ordinary inputs use the private validated native `insertText` fallback, retaining their own caret and edit handling. Read-only Crepe views cannot be changed by either path. No clipboard writes, mode flags, async clipboard reads, new dependency, or changed save serializer are involved in paste handling. `clipboardPaste.test.ts`, native menu, preload bridge, and window IPC tests cover these boundaries; native input undo and the actual source-app clipboard still require a pointed manual check when validating a release.

### Clipboard output (YAZ-938, YAZ-1389, YAZ-1411, YAZ-1443)

Normal Copy/Cut provides readable `text/plain` and rich `text/html` (YAZ-1443). `editor/clipboardPlainText.ts` reads the selected ProseMirror structure rather than Markdown source. Paragraph boundaries contribute one newline and each explicit empty paragraph contributes one additional blank line; repeated/leading/trailing selected empty rows, inline breaks, code whitespace and literal punctuation remain intact. Headings/marks/links copy their visible text without Markdown syntax; links retain their target in HTML and explicit Markdown. Lists retain readable labels, nesting and task state, and table cells use tabs. A single text block selected inside open list wrappers copies its selected words without inventing a bullet; selections spanning parent and nested children keep their hierarchy. No global whitespace collapse or note migration is involved.

Edit and right-click menus expose **Copy as → Plain text / Markdown**. Both write text only, removing rich formats so destinations cannot select HTML instead. Plain text matches normal Copy’s readable text. Markdown uses the existing source serializer, including intentional syntax and empty-paragraph markers. Each Crepe subscribes through `menu.onCopyAs`, claims only its focused selection (including read-only selections), and unsubscribes on destroy. Focused CodeMirror uses its public editor-state selection API, preserving all nonempty ranges and line breaks as literal code in both explicit modes. Preload falls back to selected input/textarea or focused DOM text; password fields and empty selections leave the clipboard unchanged. Main validates the registered live sender, main frame, renderer URL, current target and bounded string before the private `clipboard.writeText` call. No arbitrary clipboard writer is exposed through the renderer API.

For rich HTML (YAZ-1411), the existing clipboard-only DOM serializer reuses paragraph rendering/attributes and marks, sets paragraph margins to zero, and exports each empty paragraph as a standalone BR carrying `data-mdapp-empty-paragraph`. Actual Google Docs testing showed that it drops `p > br` empty paragraphs but preserves a standalone BR as an empty paragraph; our paste adapter restores the marked BR before ProseMirror parsing. The app clipboard identity and ProseMirror slice metadata remain intact for intentional internal lists. No placeholder spaces or invisible characters are introduced. Default HTML, the live editor and the save serializer are unchanged by the plain-text fix. Destination apps can apply their own formatting; real-app review remains useful. Clipboard copy/paste integration tests and native menu, preload and private IPC tests cover these contracts.

## Keyboard (client)

All bindings are `$shortcut` keymaps registered in `createCrepe()` with priority 100 (Crepe's own keymaps are 50), so they run first and fall through (`return false`) when they do not apply. `Mod` = ⌘ on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere (ProseMirror decides by `navigator.platform`). App-level window & tab shortcuts (⌘⇧N / ⌘⇧O / ⌘W / ⌘⇧W / ⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab) live in the application menu — see "Menus & shortcuts".

| Keys | Where | Does | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Tab` | caret/selection in list items | indent (sink) the item(s); no-op on a first sibling — never inserts spaces | GRO-2012 `listCommands.ts` |
| `Shift-Tab` | list items | outdent (lift); level 1 → paragraph; following siblings nest under the lifted item | GRO-2012 (Crepe default, kept) |
| `Enter` | end of a parent item | new FIRST child (after the folded subtree when the parent is collapsed) | GRO-2012 |
| `Enter` | empty item (no children) | outdent; at level 1 leaves the list as a paragraph | GRO-2012 |
| `Backspace` | start of an item's first block | join into the previous paragraph / parent item; empty parent: children take its place; non-empty parent: no-op | GRO-2012 |
| `Mod-Enter` | list item(s) whose first block touches the selection | cycle bullet → `[ ]` → `[x]` → bullet, each item from its own state; a `[x]` item's first line renders struck + dimmed (rule 9, CSS only — the file stays `[x]`); outside lists falls through (table exit / CodeMirror exit keep theirs) | GRO-2027 `hotkeys.ts` · YAZ-1514 `bullets.css` |
| `Mod-ArrowUp` / `Mod-ArrowDown` | caret in a list item, or in a heading section outside lists | fold / unfold that item (`setOutlineFoldAtSelection`, same meta-only transaction as the chevron — persisted, ⌘Z-revertible; text-only leaf or already in that state = consumed no-op; an image bullet folds to its chip, YAZ-1709), or the innermost heading section at the caret (rule 29, `headingHotkeys.ts`); outside both falls through to the native document-start/end jump | GRO-2092 `hotkeys.ts`, YAZ-1140 |
| `Mod-Shift-u` | anywhere | fold every foldable bullet (parents and image bullets, YAZ-1709) and foldable H1-H3 heading section in one metadata-only transaction; both plugins persist through the app state's `folders[root].folds` | `foldAllHotkeys.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-i` | anywhere | unfold every foldable bullet and foldable H1-H3 heading section in one metadata-only transaction | `foldAllHotkeys.ts` |
| `Mod-z` | a fold, a zoom or a heading fold was the latest VIEW action (no doc change since) | revert exactly that one step — an individual fold, a combined bullet+heading fold-all/unfold-all (both exact prior sets in one transaction), or a zoom. All transaction kinds carry `VIEW_ACTION_META` (`viewActions.ts`); each plugin drops its pending undo when a foreign kind fires, while `document-fold` deliberately keeps both halves of the combined action eligible. Otherwise falls through to history's undo. Plugin-appended transactions (e.g. Crepe's trailing paragraph) don't break eligibility | GRO-2075 `outlineFolding.ts`, GRO-2091 `zoom.ts`, YAZ-1140 `headingFolding.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-x` | selection | toggle strikethrough (Obsidian binding; Crepe's `Mod-Alt-x` still works) | GRO-2027 |
| `Mod-u` | selection | toggle underline (`<u>…</u>` on disk) | GRO-2028 `marks/underline.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-h` | selection or caret | toggle the YELLOW highlight (`==…==` on disk): lit if any of the selection carries yellow → remove it, else apply it (edge whitespace excluded); at a caret flips the stored mark for what is typed next; the toolbar's yellow dot is the same command | YAZ-1480 `marks/highlight.ts` |
| `Mod-.` | caret in a list item | zoom into that item (view-only; breadcrumbs appear); outside lists falls through | GRO-2029 `outline/zoom.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-.` | while zoomed | zoom out one level (parent item, or fully at top level); not zoomed falls through | GRO-2029 |
| `Shift-Tab` / `Mod-[` | while zoomed, on the zoomed item or a direct child | no-op (lifting would escape the zoomed subtree); everywhere else outdents as usual | GRO-2029 |
| `↑` / `↓` / `Enter` / `Esc` | while the `[[` link picker is open | move the highlight (wraparound) / insert the highlighted suggestion as plain `[[name]]` text / dismiss leaving the typed text; with the picker CLOSED all four fall through — the picker's keymap is registered before the outliner's so its priority-100 Enter wins the tie only while open (rule 24) | GRO-2191 `wikilink/wikilinkPicker.ts` |
| `Shift-ArrowUp` / `Shift-ArrowDown` | any text selection | **Whole-line selection (YAZ-1734, D1–D7; `lineSelection.ts`, one file, registered before the outliner keymap).** A "line" is the textblock — a paragraph, a heading, a list item's own text — never the visual row (D1). Only the HEAD moves. On a block edge it moves to the same edge of the previous / next VISIBLE textblock, so `⌘←` `⇧↓` and `⌘→` `⇧↑` select exactly one whole line and each repeat adds exactly one more; mid-line it first moves to its own line's near edge (`⇧↓` the end, `⇧↑` the start); an empty block: `⇧↓` takes the start edge, `⇧↑` the end (D2). A press never crosses the anchor and always reaches it, so `⇧↑` undoes exactly one `⇧↓`; collapsed on the anchor, the next press flips past it (D5). Hidden lines — inside a folded bullet's subtree, inside a folded heading section, or outside the zoom (`isHiddenTextblock` asks the three plugins' STATE: `collapsedItemsHiding`, `collapsedHeadingsHiding`, `getZoomedItemPos`; never the DOM) — are skipped, so the range may span hidden text. Hidden text is NEVER deleted: `⌫`, `Delete`, typing and `Enter` over such a range (`deleteVisible`, `visibleTypeOver`, `enterVisible`) remove only the visible pieces by explicit construction (the last block and every whole visible block between go as nodes together with the wrappers they were the only child of; the first block keeps its head and gains the last block's tail — selected from its start it goes as a node and the last keeps its tail), and `Enter` then re-presses itself on the view so the ordinary Enter splits at the caret it left (the keymap hands every handler of one key the same pre-delete state, so merely declining is not enough). `⌘X` stays the ordinary cut on purpose: it MOVES the whole range, hidden kids included, and paste restores them (D6, D7). A `list_item` left without its own paragraph never survives a transaction: `liftHeadlessItems` (`appendTransaction`, every doc change, whatever path) lifts its kids one level up, subtrees intact — a deleted parent's kids move up, folded or not (D3); to delete a folded parent WITH its kids, unfold it first. No visible neighbour (document edge, zoom edge) is a consumed no-op. Falls through to the native pixel-column move only for a node selection or a leaf block (`hr`) in the gap; an image is an inline atom inside a paragraph (ImageBlock off), so an image line is a line. Pinned neighbours (`lineSelection.scenarios.test.ts`): a fenced code block is a line (the head lands at the start of its text); above a table the head never enters it — `prosemirror-tables` normalises the cross-cell selection back to the paragraph above, selected whole; the zoomed item's own text is the visible line above its children; in a folder's outline (bullets-only) a deleted parent row leaves an empty bullet keeping its kids, because that schema cannot drop the row. A selection with no hidden line inside falls through to the ordinary path untouched. | YAZ-1734 `lineSelection.ts` |

14. **Multi-block drag** (GRO-2019, `src/editor/multiBlockDrag.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()`): dragging the block handle while a multi-block selection contains the grabbed block moves the WHOLE selection. A document-capture mousedown expands the selection to whole sibling blocks (`expandedBlockRange`: deepest level containing both ends — sibling `list_item`s inside a nested list, not the outer list) and suppresses Crepe's single-block selection replacement; a capture dragstart hands ProseMirror a fully CLOSED slice (an open slice would splice into the target paragraph) via `dataTransfer` + `view.dragging`; after the drop an `appendTransaction` (on `uiEvent: 'drop'`) deletes the merged empty shell the move-deletion leaves at the origin, walking up wrappers it was the only child of. Drop X-position controls nesting depth (ProseMirror `dropPoint`). A grab OUTSIDE the selection never arms the plugin — Crepe's single-block drag is untouched. Folded parents drag with their hidden children.
15. **Block handle gate** (GRO-2081, `src/editor/blockHandleGate.ts`): while the pointer is inside a guide-line strip band or a fold chevron's box, `.milkdown-block-handle` gets `mdapp-handle-muted` (pointer-events none + invisible) so those clicks land; muting pauses during drags. Band geometry is imported from `guideLines.ts` — one source of truth. Crepe's `blockHandle.shouldShow` config is typed upstream but never consumed; do not reach for it.
16. **Hotkey reference** (GRO-2067, `src/settings/hotkeys.ts` — the Settings › Hotkeys page since YAZ-1679 — a `standalone` section of the dialog, its own page under the nav divider rather than a stretch of the scrolling settings page — which retired the keyboard button beside the settings cog): the page lists every binding, one group per table, from the exported `HOTKEYS`/`VIEW_HOTKEYS`/`WINDOW_HOTKEYS`/`MOUSE_TIPS` lists, and the settings search indexes every key and label so "close tab" finds the table (`WINDOW_HOTKEYS` = the app-menu window & tab shortcuts — ⌘⇧N, ⌘⇧O, ⌘O Switch vault (YAZ-1767) and right below it ⌘1 … ⌘9 Go to the vault with that number (YAZ-2555 D3 — the line says a right-click on a vault gives it one), ⌘, Settings (YAZ-1679), ⌘W Close Tab, ⌘⇧W Close Window, ⌃Tab/⌃⇧Tab and ⌘⇧]/⌘⇧[ Next/Previous Tab (the ⌥-click Open Recent tip is gone since YAZ-1914 D2 — ⌥ no longer differs); `MOUSE_TIPS` carries the editor wiki-link click conventions — click = current tab with create-on-missing, Links C GRO-2192 — the SHARED "⌘-click file or link" background-tab line (sidebar I3 + editor links, one convention) and the right-click-for-Open-in-new-window tip, plus the vault right-click tip, YAZ-1798, with Set display name since YAZ-1974 and Set shortcut since YAZ-2555 D2; the ⌘O line names ⇧⏎) — update them together with any keymap or menu-accelerator change (`hotkeys.test.ts` pins the set).
17. **URL** (GRO-2069, `src/lib/urlHash.ts`): the open file is mirrored as `#/abs/path.md` via `history.replaceState` (never pushState); on boot a hash file wins over the folder's remembered `lastFile` and may live OUTSIDE the current root (the sidebar's stale-file validation skips out-of-root paths). No file → no hash. The shareable `yaseendocs://` link is separate — see "Links".

18. **Bullet threading** (GRO-2094, `src/editor/outline/bulletThreading.ts` + `.css`): view-only decorations derived from `selection.$from` on every update — `outline-thread-node` on each list_item on the root → caret path (glyph takes the app accent `--accent` via `--list-marker-color` on its `.label-wrapper`; Crepe's frame `--crepe-color-primary` is #333 and would read as black), and inside each NESTED list on the path `outline-thread-seg` on items 0…pathIndex plus `outline-thread-stop` on the path child (segment cut at its glyph centre, elbow into the bullet). Top-level lists have no guide line and get no segments (Logseq-identical). 2px `--accent` `li::before` at the guide-line x with `pointer-events: none` (a pseudo hit-tests as its element; guide-line click-to-fold needs the UL as target). No state, no persistence, never `markdownUpdated`. Drawn only under `.app[data-threading='on']` — the dialog's Bullet threading › "Show" row (default On, `settings.bulletThreading` in the app state); the dialog's Bullet threading › "Line width" (1/2/3px) and "Line colour" (picker + Default = accent) rows feed `--thread-width` / `--thread-color` (GRO-2109). Caret semantics: the path is that of `selection.$from` — a NodeSelection on an item threads to its parent, a multi-item range threads the first item.

19. **Base files** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2125, deleted in YAZ-844): `.base` is no longer a file the app opens. `BaseHost` (the main-pane host with its view/raw-YAML modes, its own autosave baseline and the "opening a base and doing nothing never writes" invariant) is gone, `Editor` has no `.base` branch: it dispatches supported files to their Markdown/text/PDF/image capability owners, while a `.base` on disk remains unsupported: invisible to the index, not openable in-app, not creatable — it lists in the tree with `kind: null` (YAZ-1577 D4) and clicking it hands it to the OS. What survives is the ENGINE, the views, `ViewsPane` and the schema types (`viewSchema.ts`'s `parseViews` / `serializeViews` / `updateViews`), all of them a folder's internals now: the views live in the folder's `.folder.md`, under `folder_settings`, and render on the folder's page, where the behaviour lives on ("Folders are the pages"). The 1B normalisation note travels with them — `yaml`'s `indentSeq` is document-global, so a views block mixing sequence-indent styles is re-indented to one style on its first REAL save. What the `.base` file's byte-identical round trip becomes here is narrower and stated honestly: every other frontmatter key of `.folder.md` survives, because `writeFolderSettings` goes through the one-key writer — but the settings block itself is rebuilt from the parsed value, so comments and hand-formatting INSIDE `folder_settings` do not survive an edit to it.

20. **Base embeds** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2145, deleted in YAZ-844): `![[X.base]]` / `![[X.base#View]]` no longer renders anything. The whole `src/editor/baseEmbed/` folder — the `$prose` decoration plugin, the slot store, the `BaseEmbed` widget and `resolveBase`'s shortest-path resolution over `api.tree` — is gone, and with it the read-only embed chrome's only production caller — `ViewsPane`'s `readOnly` and `initialView` props outlived it by one unit and were amputated in YAZ-846 (see "View chrome"). An `![[X.base]]` left in a note is now just an ordinary embed whose target does not exist: the text stays byte-identical, nothing renders under it. A folder's page is where views are read now, and it is not embeddable anywhere else — one folder, one page, always its own notes ("Folders are the pages").

21. **`base` code blocks** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2146, deleted in YAZ-844): a fenced block with `language === 'base'` is a plain CodeMirror code block again. `src/editor/baseCodeBlock/` — the `$view` replacement for `code_block`, its slot store, the `BaseCodeBlock` widget and the raw-YAML toggle — is gone, `createCrepe` no longer takes `baseCodeBlocks`, and every language now delegates to Crepe's stock block. The text on disk was always the exact code-block text, so nothing about any existing note changed except that the block stops rendering as a view. Views live in a folder's settings now, edited through the toolbar rather than through YAML by hand (rule 19's tombstone, "Folders are the pages").

22. **Typed-page scaffolds & the new-entity flow** — TOMBSTONE (Bible B GRO-2202, deleted in YAZ-836): the registry scaffold, `page_type`, per-type templates, the starter `All <pluralName>.base`, `createType` / `NewTypeDialog` and the sidebar's "New ▸ <type>" submenu are all gone; a new note is born in its FOLDER from that folder's `.template.md` and the caller's seed, and nothing else ("Folders are the pages": Births — `createNote`), and the still-valid vault-wide property declarations moved to `.yaseendocs/properties.json` ("Properties"). The number is kept because rules 23-26 and the `// rule N` citations in the code index into this list; `ensureFolder` is the one helper of `src/views/scaffold.ts` that outlived the type half.

23. **Wikilinks in the editor** (Links A, GRO-2190, `src/editor/wikilink/`): `[[target]]` renders Obsidian-live-preview style via DECORATIONS only (`wikilinkPlugin.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` — never a Crepe feature, the allowlist is untouched; never a schema/serializer change, so the text round-trips byte-identically — `roundtrip.test.ts` pins alias / heading / `#^block` / adjacent / inside-bold / unicode-and-spaces / unclosed-`[[` forms). Caret OUTSIDE the match: the `[[` `]]` brackets (plus `target|` before an alias and each `#`) get `wikilink__syntax` (CSS `display: none`); visible text carries `wikilink` (accent colour); `[[t|alias]]` shows only the alias; `[[t#h]]` shows `t > h` (each post-`#` segment gets `wikilink__sub`, whose `::before` draws the separator). An ID link (YAZ-2293 — the target is a note's `id` and the source resolves it) hides the id as well and shows the note's CURRENT TITLE — `IndexRecord.title`: its frontmatter `title`, or with none its file name without the extension (YAZ-2420 D14) — in its place: the ONE widget decoration here (`idLinkTitle` gives the title; the widget is keyed by it and wears the classes the id text would have worn), where every other decoration only hides or styles characters the document holds. The title is read off the index at each build, so a rename — by anything — changes what the link shows and not one byte of the document. `[[<id>#h]]` shows `Title > h`; a hand-typed `[[<id>|label]]` shows its label, never the title; an id NO note has stays the raw id, dimmed exactly like an unresolved name; and before the index has loaded an id shows raw and undimmed. `linkDisplayText(inner, titleOf?)` is that one mapping, shared with the backlinks snippets (rule 25). Resolved vs unresolved comes from the ONE resolver (`resolverFor`, see "Link resolution" — and, for a target no note answers, the FOLDER of that name: `linkResolver`, YAZ-2290 D10, "Links": Links to folders; such a link is resolved, opens the folder's tab on click and is never created): an unresolvable target dims via `wikilink--unresolved`; an empty target (`[[#h]]`) is a same-file link, never dimmed. A match whose DISPLAY would be empty (`[[Note|]]`, `[[|]]`, `[[#]]`) gets NO decorations at all and stays raw and editable (GRO-2197): hiding every part of it would erase the text from the page and leave no `.wikilink` span for the click handler either. Caret INSIDE or immediately adjacent (selection overlapping the match, boundaries INCLUSIVE): that match's decorations drop — raw editable syntax; this is also why `display: none` hiding is arrow-safe: the caret never crosses hidden text because at a boundary the match is already raw. An id link's title widget sits INSIDE the match (after the `[[`), so the same rule covers it: it is gone before the caret reaches the id, and the raw `[[<id>]]` is what is edited. Excluded: `![[…]]` embeds (they stay plain text — the base-embed widget that used to claim some of them died in YAZ-844, rule 20's tombstone) and code (`code_block` nodes and inline-`code` marks — mirrors the index's `stripCode`). Resolution reaches the plugin through a per-window `WikilinkResolveSource` (`createCrepe({ wikilinks })`): App owns one stable source and `WikilinkIndexBridge` feeds it from `useIndex`; an index update swaps the resolve function and pokes each subscribed editor with a META transaction — decorations recompute live, the editor never remounts, the doc never changes, `markdownUpdated` never fires. Interaction (Links C, GRO-2192, `wikilinkClick.ts` + `createFromLink.ts`, wired as `createCrepe({ wikilinkNav })` — App threads this window's tabs API + its notice setter through `Editor`): collapsed `.wikilink` spans get `cursor: pointer` and navigate on MOUSEDOWN with the default prevented — BEFORE the caret could land in the match and flash the raw syntax, so the reveal stays caret-adjacency/keyboard-only (exactly Obsidian's live-preview model: click navigates; to edit, place the caret adjacent and arrow in). Plain click → the CURRENT tab (`nav.openCurrent`, which App feeds the workspace's `navigate` since YAZ-2648 D3: the target takes the tab the link was clicked in, kept tab or preview tab, and Back returns — an already-open path activates its tab); ⌘-click → a BACKGROUND tab (`openBackground` — appended, never activated, focus untouched; already-open → no-op); clicks on REVEALED raw text never navigate (a revealed match has no decoration span — that IS editing); Alt/Shift/Ctrl-modified clicks keep their defaults (future gestures — note ⌥ is now the right-panel key on a folder's Table/Board rows, YAZ-1557, so a future ⌥-click on a link should mean the same). An UNRESOLVED link CREATES its page first (`createFromLink`), then opens by the same gesture — except an unresolved ID link (YAZ-2293), which is NEVER created: an id names a note that existed, so a page called `<id>.md` would be a different note wearing its name, and the click says `That note no longer exists` through the passive link-notice — `This vault does not use IDs, so this link cannot be opened` where it does not (YAZ-2523) — and does nothing else. What an ID link IS, is asked of the vault (`vaultNoteId(page, source.letters)`, YAZ-2677 R5): an old ID, or a number ID with the vault's letters, now or before (`[[YAZ-99]]`, S25); any other `LETTERS-NUMBER` text — `[[GPT-4]]`, `[[BUS-12]]` from another vault — is a NAME and is created like any name (R6, S21, S22), and a vault that does not use IDs has no letters, so `[[COVID-19]]` is a name there (a RESOLVING id link opens from its title widget, which carries `.wikilink` and sits inside the match, so the hit-test finds it like any segment). For every other unresolved link: `|alias`/`#…` stripped (`linkPageName` — the ONE strip with the decorations); `[[#h]]` (empty target, same-file) is a no-op — the heading jump is GRO-2239; a bare target creates under the "Default location for new notes" setting (C2-, GRO-2240): `current` (default, YAZ-1643) → the SOURCE page's folder — the page the link was clicked or typed in, so a right-panel editor creates beside ITSELF, and a folder's outline as a note in that folder would (its `.folder.md` stands in as the source), never beside the main tab (a source outside the vault → the root), `root` → the vault root, `folder` → `<root>/<newNoteFolder>` — App resolves it (`newNoteBase`) behind a stable ref-backed `newNoteFolderFor(sourcePath)` getter threaded through `Editor` into `wikilinkNav` as `createFolder` (each editor binds its OWN path) and read at CLICK time, so settings changes land live without remounting any editor; a pathed `[[Sub/Page]]` (or `[[/Page]]`) is an EXPLICIT AIM: always root-relative whatever the setting (Obsidian's behavior), with missing parents — the base FOLDER of the setting included — created level by level (scaffold's `ensureFolder`); what was typed is the new note's TITLE (YAZ-2420 D20) — for a pathed target its last segment, the earlier ones being the TITLES of its folders (D6), each a folder that is already there, by title and then by directory name, before it is one to make (YAZ-2478) — and the file is the name `createNote` builds from it; two notes of one title are two files, so the disk refuses no second one, and a click on a dead link whose note is still being made does nothing (YAZ-2478); an invalid segment of the setting's base folder (`validateEntryName`) and create failures surface via the passive link-notice — never a dialog. Two more notices, both GRO-2197: a click landing in the PRE-INDEX window (no resolver yet, so every link still renders resolved and creating anything could shadow an existing note) is still swallowed, but now SAYS so instead of doing nothing at all; and a ⌘-click that CREATES a page names it in a notice, because a background tab is by definition not shown and the gesture otherwise looks like nothing happened. The watcher-driven index refetch then flips unresolved→resolved styling everywhere on its own (the live restyle above). **Right-click on a rendered id link** (YAZ-2293, `wikilinkMenu.ts`, registered with the click plugin on the same source and nav — so only when `wikilinkNav` is given, and only where the vault uses IDs, `source.ids`, YAZ-2523): an id link hides its id, so this menu is where the id can be SEEN and copied — rule 26's `.ctx-menu.ctx-menu--editor` popup (`blockHandleMenu.ts` `renderMenu`), holding the note's title (a folder's reads `<title> (folder)`) and its id as two disabled label rows, then **Copy ID** — the one place it is offered (YAZ-2420 D31; "Note ids": Copy ID). An id no note has, and any id before the index loads, has no name row; the id and Copy ID are still offered. The right-button MOUSEDOWN — and a Ctrl-click's left-button one, macOS's other right-click — is swallowed on an id link for the click handler's own reason: left to its default the caret lands in the match, the reveal rule drops the rendered span, and `contextmenu` arrives on raw text. The popup closes on a row click, an outside mousedown, a scroll, Escape, window blur and any doc change, one at a time. A NAME link, a view-only link, revealed raw text and plain text keep the native menu — nothing is prevented or drawn for them.

24. **The `[[` link picker** (Links B, GRO-2191, `src/editor/wikilink/wikilinkPicker.ts` + `.css`): typing `[[` in any non-code textblock opens a suggestion popup over every markdown note in the vault — and, since YAZ-2290 D10, every folder, its rows after the notes', reading `<title> (folder)` and inserted by id — by title or path when it has none ("Links": Links to folders) —; typing filters, ↑/↓ navigate (wraparound), Enter or click INSERTS PLAIN TEXT — `[[<id>]]` when the note has an `id` (YAZ-2293, **By id** below), else `[[name]]` — (replacing `[[fragment` up to the caret — never a schema/doc-structure change; the rule-23 decorations restyle it on their next pass, raw while the caret still touches it), Esc dismisses leaving the typed text — and that same `[[` stays closed while typing continues (a fresh `[[` reopens). Candidates come from the index through the shared matcher (`links/completion.ts` — the ONE `[[…]]` matcher with EditableCell's link editors). Matching is case-insensitive over the candidate NAME with the typed fragment trimmed at its ENDS (internal spaces stay significant), RANKED exact → prefix → substring and stable within each bucket, capped at 8 AFTER ranking (GRO-2197 — so `[[cac` puts the note actually named `CAC` first instead of whatever the path order happened to reach first). Ranking means the full candidate list is scanned every keystroke, which the per-candidate lowercase precomputed at construction pays for; the 5,000-record smoke in `completion.perf.test.ts` (the `perf` project) is the budget guard: `linkCandidates(records)` lists each note under its TITLE (YAZ-2420 D17 — the basename of a note that has no `title:`) — duplicate titles follow the resolver's shallowest-depth rule, so the bare title belongs to the shallowest match and every other duplicate shows as `folder/title` and inserts its root-relative `folder/basename`, the path no title joins. **Aliases** (E2, GRO-2214): a note with frontmatter `aliases` also gets ONE row per alias — typed as the alias, shown as `CAC — Customer Acquisition Cost` (the note half is that note's own unambiguous name, so duplicate aliases are folder-disambiguated by construction) and INSERTING the piped `[[Customer Acquisition Cost|CAC]]`, which targets the note and displays the alias. The piped form is exact whoever else claims the alias, so an alias row is unambiguous exactly like a folder-disambiguated duplicate; a row matches on its own name only (never on the note half of its label). Two alias rows are skipped outright (GRO-2197): one whose alias IS the note's own chosen name (it would read `CAC — CAC` and insert the pointless `[[CAC|CAC]]`), and one whose alias contains `[` or `]` (its piped insert would re-parse as the inner link and point somewhere else entirely). **By id** (YAZ-2293, 🔒): a note that has an `id` is offered under exactly the same rows — typed and read by its title, its `folder/title`, or `Alias — Note` — but EVERY one of them inserts the plain `[[<id>]]`: no name, no pipe, so the link survives any rename or move, and rule 23 shows the note's current title in its place. The names above are then only what a row is TYPED and READ as; a note with no id keeps inserting them. A non-empty fragment nothing matches offers ONE "Create" row: it creates the page right there AND links it by the id it is born with (YAZ-2293), in a fixed order (🔒 YAZ-2677 S35): the NUMBER first, then the link, then the note. At the pick the picker replaces `[[fragment` with the name link `[[typed text]]`, the caret after it — the brackets are closed at once, so what the user types during the wait lands after the link and no character is lost (S36) — and asks the door for the vault's next ID (`api.mintNoteId(nav.root)`, bridge table). The plugin state follows the place of the typed page name through every edit made meanwhile (`PickerState.waiting`, each place mapped per transaction). When the number comes, `[[<id>]]` takes the place of the typed page name where it now stands (a `#heading` typed after it rides along) — only while that text still reads as it was typed: a name the user changed or deleted during the wait is theirs and stays — and the same id goes to `createFromLink`, which passes it to `createNote` and so to `createFile({ path, content, id })`, the typed text being the note's title (YAZ-2420 D20). No number (the door fails): nothing is made, the link stays the typed name, and the failure is said. `null` from the door (the vault stopped using IDs after the pick): the page is made the plain way (YAZ-1357, 🔒 D3 revised — Yasin's ruling that a picked Create shows up in the sidebar at once: `createFromLink` under `nav.createFolder()`, the same placement as create-on-click — beside the page being typed in (YAZ-1643), no navigation, a passive `Created "…"` notice). When NO page is born with that id — a create failure, said through the same notice — the id in the document goes back to the typed page name, found by its text wherever typing has since pushed it. Without a nav there is no vault to create in, so the row only inserts `[[typed text]]` as-is. Where the vault does not use IDs (`source.ids` false, YAZ-2523 V3 — "Two kinds of vault") the door is not asked: the row inserts `[[typed text]]` and the page is made the plain way, as `<typed text>.md`. A hand-typed dangling link is still created on click (rule 23's Links C interaction); typing `|` is alias entry: the suggestions close and typing continues as plain text. Anatomy: a `$prose` plugin owns the state (session recomputed per transaction from doc + selection; candidate updates poke a meta transaction — same live-update pattern as rule 23) and the row DOM (`role="listbox"`/`option`, mousedown-preventDefault, theme tokens); `SlashProvider` (`@milkdown/kit/plugin/slash`) positions the popup at the caret and toggles `data-show` (its one-char `trigger` cannot express `[[`, so it runs a custom `shouldShow` over the plugin state; closing hides directly — the provider skips meta-only transactions). Keys ride `wikilinkPickerKeymap` (`$shortcut`, priority 100 — see "Keyboard"): every command declines (`return false`) while the picker is closed, so ↑/↓/Enter/Esc are NEVER swallowed then; the Enter tie with the outliner's priority-100 Enter breaks by addition order — `createCrepe()` MUST `use` this keymap before `outlinerKeymap` (`wikilinkPicker.test.ts` pins both directions). Per-window plumbing mirrors rule 23: `createCrepe({ wikilinkCandidates })`, App owns one `WikilinkCandidateSource`, `WikilinkIndexBridge` feeds it `linkCandidates(records)` per ready snapshot. No new GLOBAL hotkeys — the Hotkeys page (rule 16) is untouched.

25. **Backlinks — "Linked mentions"** (Links D, GRO-2193, `src/links/backlinks.ts` + `BacklinksSection.tsx` + `backlinks.css`; decision D of GRO-2096 and the GRO-2193 placement thread, both LOCKED). **Storage**: none — the referencing set is computed CLIENT-SIDE in ONE pass over the Markdown index snapshot the window already holds (`backlinksFor`), through THE shared semantic resolver (`resolverFor`, see "Link resolution"). View-only files never enter that snapshot or resolver and therefore never receive or contribute backlinks. No reverse map in the main process, no new IPC, no new `IndexRecord` field, and no watcher of its own: auto-refresh rides the existing 300 ms-debounced index refetch. A mention is a Markdown record's `links` **or** `embeds` entry resolving to the open Markdown path — an `![[embed]]` mentions its target exactly like a `[[link]]` — with the open note itself excluded, ONE entry per referencing note however many mentions it holds, path-sorted. A FOLDER's page mentions what its settings record links by the rename engine's rule (`referenceTargets`, never a second reading of `folder_settings`): its own link properties (`owner: "[[Alice]]"` in `.folder.md`), a view's `order` entry, a link column's `target`; and any link in a line of its outlines, prose included, as a note's body does (`folderMentionSnippets`). The folder is ONE entry — its `.folder.md` record, sorted by that file's path — and never lists itself. Alias-awareness is free (E2, GRO-2214): a note linking `[[CAC]]` IS a linked mention of the page whose frontmatter aliases it. So is id-awareness (YAZ-2293): a note linking `[[<id>]]` IS a linked mention of the note that id names. Results are memoized per (records array identity, path) in a WeakMap (`resolverFor`'s idiom), so one snapshot costs one pass per open path however many Markdown tab layers are mounted. **Placement**: a collapsible block near the BOTTOM of a Markdown note, INSIDE the editor's scroller — `.editor-host` holds `.page-title` (block ZERO, ⚡ YAZ-888), `.frontmatter-panel` (block ONE, ⚡ YAZ-883), `.editor-mount` (where the Crepe instance lands), the comment stream ("Comments", 🔒 D4), then the section, then "Reviews" (YAZ-2322) — so it scrolls WITH the note, Obsidian's in-document mode. No right panel, no sidebar section; one per Markdown tab. A FOLDER's page carries the same section as its last block, listing the notes and the folders that link to the folder (YAZ-2290 D10, `FolderView`); text/PDF/image dispatch before these hosts and mount none of it. **Header**: "Linked mentions (N)" with the view-group chevron (rotated −90° while collapsed), no hairline above it (YAZ-1680), COLLAPSED by default, N always visible — and N === 0 renders NOTHING at all (quiet over complete: Obsidian's in-document mode keeps an empty block, we do not). **Snippets, on demand**: an entry only mounts while the section is EXPANDED and reads its Markdown file from that mount (`fs:read`) — collapsed costs zero reads, and the index stores no positions. A snippet is the mention's own LINE — one snippet per line however many mentions it holds (GRO-2197) — whitespace-trimmed and windowed to ~120 chars around the FIRST mention with `…` on the clipped sides, at most 5 lines per note. The line reads exactly as the editor renders it: every wikilink on it is shown as its DISPLAY text through the shared `linkDisplayText` (`[[Note|CAC]]` → `CAC`, `[[Note#H]]` → `Note > H`, an id link → its note's current title through `idLinkTitle` and an id no note has → the raw id, exactly as rule 23 draws them (YAZ-2293), embeds and the empty-display forms of rule 23 stay raw), never the raw `[[…]]` source, and EVERY mention of the open note on that line is highlighted (`<mark class="backlinks__match">` per range, so a line mentioning it twice is one row with two marks; a mention pushed outside the window by the ~120-char clip drops, the centred one never does). Code is skipped through the rename engine's length-preserving `maskCode` (the index's `stripCode` mirror), and frontmatter lines scan like any other — the index reads links there too, though only as whole-value exact `[[…]]` strings, so a frontmatter line CAN highlight a mention the index did not count (GRO-2197 audit, deferred). A skeleton bar shows while a read is in flight; a FAILED read leaves the entry standing WITHOUT snippets — never a dialog, never a notice. A FOLDER entry reads nothing: its snippets are the bullets of its outlines that hold the link (`folderMentionSnippets` — the same `mentionSnippets`, over the outline text the index already holds), and none when it links only by `order` or `target`. **Live**: a fresh snapshot recomputes N (and drops the whole section when the last mention goes); an EXPANDED entry re-reads its snippets only when its own record's `mtime` moved — changed files only, so unrelated vault activity costs no reads. **Clicks**: plain → the CURRENT tab (the section's `openCurrent` prop, fed `navigate` like a wiki link's), ⌘ → a BACKGROUND tab (`openBackground`) — the same model as wiki-link clicks (rule 23); a sidebar row's plain click is no longer the same (YAZ-2648 D1: it goes to the preview tab); entry title and snippet rows behave identically (title = the page's title, YAZ-2420 D14, tooltip = the full path; a folder entry is titled by the folder's title and opens the folder's page). **Collapse state** is per-component and in-memory: since every visited Markdown tab keeps its editor layer mounted (rule 4) it is effectively per tab for the session, and it is never persisted — an open backlinks block is not part of a note's identity. **Feed**: the per-window `WikilinkResolveSource` carries the semantic resolver AND the Markdown snapshot it was built from (`update(resolve, records)`, fed by `WikilinkIndexBridge` — one semantic snapshot feed for decorations, the Markdown picker rows and backlinks alike), while the view-only catalog remains a separate navigation-only source; the section subscribes only to the semantic source, so index updates never re-render App or remount an editor.

26. **Block handle menu + manual numbering** (YAZ-726/728/729/793/1329, `src/editor/blockHandleMenu.ts`, `outline/numberChildren.ts`, `outline/numberChildrenRow.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()`): right-click on the 6-dot handle opens a `.ctx-menu.ctx-menu--editor` popup mounted in the editor's own parent (no overlay; closes on outside mousedown, Escape, scroll, window blur and any doc change). Rows are DATA from a `RowProvider` called at open time with the probe target (`blockHandleTarget.handleTargetPos`, shared with multi-block drag) — the plugin never knows what a row does. First row: "Number children" ↔ "Bullet children" flips EVERY direct child list of the hovered `list_item` between `bullet_list` and `ordered_list` in one transaction (one undo step); it REPLACES the child list's marker type, never stacks a bullet and number on one row; grandchildren untouched; disabled (still shown) when the item has no direct child list. List commands NEVER read or write paragraph text — hand-typed same-line `* 1) Setup` is literal text under rule 8. **This is the only editor conversion command that creates numbering**: `createCrepe()` removes upstream's `1. ` `wrapInOrderedListInputRule` and `Mod-Alt-7` `orderedListKeymap`, and configures BlockEdit without the slash-menu Ordered List row; `- ` / `* ` bullet rules stay (E1). Explicit ordered Markdown still loads/persists because it is the command's disk format and valid imported/pasted content. Right-button mousedown/mouseup on the handle are stopped at document capture so Crepe's NodeSelection + focus never fire for a right-click.

27. **Drawing previews in the page** (YAZ-878, third build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852, `src/editor/drawing/drawingPreview.ts` + `.css`, registered in `createCrepe()` only when `opts.drawingPreview` supplies the vault root): an `![[Sketch.excalidraw]]` embed RENDERS AS THE SCENE. **DECORATIONS ONLY** (🔒 the same rule as 23): the embed stays plain markdown TEXT — no schema node, no serializer, no node view — so round trip is byte-identical (`roundtrip.test.ts` plus a guard in the plugin's own test). ProseMirror has no replace-a-range-with-a-widget decoration, so the mechanism is the honest pair the codebase already uses: a `Decoration.inline` over the whole match carrying `drawing-embed__syntax` (CSS `display: none`, `wikilink__syntax`'s exact mechanism) plus a `Decoration.widget` at the match START (`side: -1`, `contenteditable="false"`, `key` = target + state so PM reuses the DOM node and a settled preview never flickers). **REVEAL** is rule 23's, with boundaries **EXCLUSIVE** instead of inclusive: a selection strictly INSIDE the match drops both decorations (raw, editable syntax), while the caret sitting immediately before or after it keeps the preview — because YAZ-877's insert leaves the caret exactly there and an inclusive rule would hide the preview the insert exists to show. Boundary behaviour stays sane either way: an arrow key off an edge steps inside and reveals, and Backspace at the right edge eats the final `]`, un-matching the embed and leaving what a plain-text delete leaves. **ONLY `.excalidraw` TARGETS** (`isDrawingTarget`, case-insensitive suffix): every other embed (`![[img.png]]`), every link, and anything inside a code block or an inline-`code` run is untouched. The target goes to `readAsset` **RAW** — path or bare basename — because that is where the resolution rule lives (YAZ-876); the renderer never joins paths. **STATES**: *loading* is a quiet empty frame (no spinner — nothing to jitter); *ready* is the SVG inside that same frame (`max-width: 100%`, `max-height: 24em`, `cursor: pointer` only when a click handler exists) — one shared frame so the wait never jumps into the render AND an empty drawing, which exports to a near-nothing SVG, is still a visible clickable line; *broken* — missing file, unreadable bytes, not a scene, a render that threw — is a small INERT chip (“Broken drawing”) and the embed text **stays visible and editable**, because a drawing whose file is gone must remain a line the user can fix by hand. Nothing is ever repaired or rewritten. **THE DEPENDENCY** (`@excalidraw/excalidraw`, PINNED 0.18.1, the one addition of this unit): loaded ONLY through a dynamic `import()` on the first preview render (`drawings/renderScene.ts`), so the editor's main chunk never carries it and a note with no drawing embed loads none of it. 🔒 **OFFLINE**: the renderer must NEVER reach a CDN, and Excalidraw falls back to esm.sh whenever `window.EXCALIDRAW_ASSET_PATH` fails — so that path is set to the app's OWN copy of the package's asset folder (`app://yaseen/excalidraw-assets/`) BEFORE the module is imported. The copy is made at build time by `excalidrawAssets()` in `desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` (from node_modules into `out/renderer/`, and served from node_modules by a dev-server middleware) — never committed, and carried into the packaged app by electron-builder's existing `out/**`. Fonts are fetched at all only for scenes containing TEXT; a text-free scene asks for nothing. **REFRESH**: scenes are cached per target for the life of ONE editor mount; a `DrawingFeed` poke (`drawings/drawingFeed.ts` — the `WikilinkResolveSource` subscribe/poke idiom with no state, threaded as `drawingPreview.feed`) drops that target's entry and rebuilds the decorations, which re-read and re-render it. Since YAZ-879 `CrepeHost` creates exactly ONE feed per editor mount and gives it to both the preview plugin and the modal, so a save redraws every preview of that target in place — no remount, no reload, no document change. Without a feed previews still render, they just never refresh short of a remount. **CLICK**: a rendered preview calls `drawingPreview.onOpenDrawing(target)` and swallows the mousedown so the caret never lands in the match; Editor threads it to the modal (rule 28), and a mount that supplies neither wire keeps inert previews. CSS note: every rule in `drawingPreview.css` is scoped under `.milkdown`, because Crepe's own reset carries `.milkdown * { margin: 0; padding: 0 }` at equal specificity and loads later — an unscoped class silently loses all spacing. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` steps 2-4 (previews render and the raw text is not duplicated; a drawn scene WITH TEXT renders with its font inlined from a `data:` URL and **zero** http(s) requests leave the app; a corrupted sidecar reopens as the broken chip with the note still byte-identical).

28. **The drawing modal** (YAZ-879, fourth build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852, `src/drawings/DrawingModal.tsx` + `drawingModal.css` + `ExcalidrawSurface.tsx` + `saveDrawing.ts`): clicking a rendered preview opens the scene FULL SIZE, edits it, and Save writes the sidecar back. 🔒 **A MODAL, NEVER AN INLINE NODE VIEW**: the embed is plain text under decorations (rule 27), so there is no node to host an editor in, and a drawing canvas inside the text column would fight the caret for every key it owns. It is React BESIDE the editor — the `PageTitle` precedent: `CrepeHost` holds `openDrawing: string | null`, the preview's `drawingPreview.onOpenDrawing` sets it, the modal is keyed by target and unmounted (never hidden) on close. 🔒 **ONE ENGINE SEAM**: `ExcalidrawSurface` is the ONLY component that mounts `<Excalidraw>`, the only place a scene is serialized, and the only place "changed" is defined; the chrome — overlay, Save, dirty, discard strip, error line — imports nothing but that component and its types, so YAZ-868's swap to yaseendraw rewrites one file. The boundary is pinned as SOURCE in `DrawingModal.test.tsx` (a runtime assertion would pass with a second hidden import). Everything is LAZY: the module through `renderScene.ts`'s one `loadExcalidraw()`, the stylesheet through the surface's own dynamic CSS import — the entry chunk gains only the chrome's own few KB, and a note with no drawing loads neither. ⚡ Every prop handed to the engine is STABLE (memoized `initialData`, ref-backed `onChange`, module-level `UIOptions`): `<Excalidraw>` is memoized and calls `onChange` whenever it renders, so a prop rebuilt per render is an infinite loop (React #185, caught in e2e). The modal keeps the latest snapshot in a REF and only its VERSION in state, so a drag costs one render rather than one per pointer move. **LOAD / SAVE**: `openDrawing` (`drawingScene.ts`) reads through `api.readAsset` with the embed's RAW target and keeps the resolved ABSOLUTE path and the read `mtime`; the save goes to `api.writeAsset` at that explicit path (writes are never fuzzy, 🔒 YAZ-876) with that mtime as `expectedMtime`. 🔒 **THE CANVAS WINS**: `writeProperty`'s CONFLICT dance with the one difference a scene forces — the re-read fetches nothing but a fresh guard (two scenes cannot be merged and the user is looking at theirs), then writes once; a second CONFLICT throws. **SHAPE ON DISK**: the library's own `serializeAsJSON(…, 'local')` — so element cleanup and the `files` filter (an image paste survives) are its rules — with two of ours: `source` stays `yaseen-docs` (pinned as `window.EXCALIDRAW_EXPORT_SOURCE` before the import, beside the font path) and a trailing newline, so a file YAZ-877 created and this saved back differ only in what was drawn. After a successful write the modal pokes the `DrawingFeed` (rule 27) and closes. ⚡ **KEYS ARE THE OVERLAY'S, NEVER `window`'s** — the YAZ-888 lesson `ConfirmRename` learned and paid for in e2e: React flushes mount effects INSIDE the dispatch of the event that opened the component, so a `window` listener hears the very click or keystroke that opened it, and this modal is opened by a CLICK. The overlay takes focus on mount so Esc lands before the canvas exists. **LEAVING** is one gesture with three triggers (Esc, click-away, ✕): clean → close; dirty → an INLINE confirm strip under the title bar ("Discard drawing changes?" / Cancel / Discard), never a second sheet stacked over this one, and Esc while it is up answers the strip. **SAVE IS DISABLED WHEN CLEAN** — the first snapshot the surface emits (at mount, from the loaded scene, before the engine volunteers one) is the baseline, and `getSceneVersion` is the comparison. **FAILURES ARE INLINE, NEVER A DIALOG**: a sidecar that is missing, unreadable or not a scene opens as the error state (message + close, nothing to write — reachable only by a race, since rule 27's broken chip is inert), and a failed write leaves the modal open with its error line and the canvas untouched. **THEME** is App's already-resolved appearance read back off `<html data-theme>` (`lib/theme.ts` `appliedTheme`), once, at open: the setting lives in App and threading a prop down to one transient modal is plumbing the feature has not earned. Known gap for the polish pass: a theme change while the modal is open does not follow. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` step 5 (a preview click opens the modal on a real canvas with Save disabled; the rectangle tool is picked on the engine's own toolbar and DRAWN with real mouse drags; Save writes an element into the sidecar — still `yaseen-docs`, still pretty-printed with a trailing newline — the in-page preview redraws from the feed's poke, and reopening the saved drawing is clean and Esc-closes, with the note byte-identical throughout).

29. **Heading folding** (YAZ-1140, `src/editor/outline/headingFolding.ts` + `.css`, keymap `headingHotkeys.ts`): the heading-shaped twin of rule 5, registered in `createCrepe()` right after the bullet plugin. Foldable = an H1–H3 outside every list whose SECTION — its following sibling blocks up to the next heading of the same or a shallower level, or the end of the parent (blockquotes are walked; lists are skipped whole: the bullet chevron owns that gutter) — is non-empty. Each gets a widget `<button class="heading-toggle" aria-expanded>` at the start of the h element's inline content — INSIDE the h element, so it inherits the heading font-size and one `height: 1.3em` rule centres the chevron on the first line at every level — and collapsing adds `data-heading-folded="true"` to every section block (CSS `display:none`; an inner collapsed heading's widget sits inside a hidden block, so nesting compounds for free). Toggles are metadata-only transactions stamped `'heading-fold'` (`viewActions.ts`): never `markdownUpdated`, disk and mtime untouched. Fold keys = `h:` + the rule-5 scheme (`getOutlineFoldKey`) — a third key space beside fold and zoom keys — persisted in the SAME `folders[root].folds` bucket: `Editor.tsx` splits by prefix on load and writes the union of both plugins' latest reports, so neither kind can wipe the other's keys and rename/delete remapping rides the existing store path unchanged. ⌘↑/⌘↓ fold/unfold the innermost section at the caret (declining inside lists — rule 7's bullet handler owns those; ⌘⇧U/⌘⇧I stay bullets-only in v1); ⌘Z joins the view-action protocol, which YAZ-1140 made SYMMETRIC: every view-action plugin (fold, zoom, heading fold) drops its pending panic-undo when any OTHER kind fires, so ⌘Z reverts the single latest view action and a second ⌘Z can never resurrect an older one. CMD+F reveals matches hidden by collapsed sections silently and re-collapses them on its own `revealedHeadings` books (`findInPage.ts`); the block handle mutes over the chevron (rule 15); the bullets-only outline editor computes zero entries and stays inert. End-to-end: `desktop/e2e/headingFold.spec.ts` — chevron → section hidden → `h:` key in `yaseendocs.json` → quit → relaunch → restored, with a bullet fold coexisting in the same bucket.

30. **Inline `<br>` ↔ hardbreak** (YAZ-1452, `src/editor/inlineBreaks.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` right after underline): a `<br>` (any spelling, any case) is the hardbreak node, not an `html` atom — inside table cells it is the only line break GFM has, and Milkdown's `remarkPreserveEmptyLinePlugin` used to delete every one of them on parse (data loss on the next autosave). 🔒 **ORDER IS THE MECHANISM**: our `$remark` plugin runs BEFORE Milkdown's, which is removed and re-registered AFTER ours — never dropped, because `shouldPreserveEmptyLine` resolves its id to keep writing `<br />` for empty paragraphs. **LOAD**: a `<br>` becomes `break`; in a prose paragraph a `<br>` with nothing after it is dropped (CommonMark has no trailing hard break; remark would write a stray `\`), which is also what keeps the lone `<br />` blank-line marker round-tripping. 🔒 **A `<br>` BESIDE A LINE ENDING IS ONE BREAK** (YAZ-2659 D1): Milkdown has already made the line ending inside a paragraph a break of its own (a soft break, `isInline`), so a `<br>` at the end of a line, or on a line of its own, takes the soft break before it and the soft break after it — `a<br>` + `b` on the next line is ONE paragraph with ONE hardbreak, in prose, in a bullet and in a quote, and `a<br><br>` + `b` has two. Two breaks in a row would save as `\` + a blank line, which ends the paragraph, and the next save would write `\\`. Files that an earlier build already split are not repaired (D4). **SAVE**: a hardbreak inside `tableCell` is written as `<br>` (remark's default writes a space there); elsewhere remark's default `\` + newline — so `a<br>b` in prose, and `a<br>` + `b` on the next line, normalise to the backslash form (rule 6), and a second save writes the same text. **CELLS SERIALISE THEMSELVES**: `table_cell` / `table_header` `toMarkdown` feed the cell's inline content directly instead of going through the paragraph runner, because that runner always drops a paragraph's LAST hardbreak (a trailing `<br>` in a cell decayed one per save) and wrote `<br />` for an empty cell; several paragraphs in one cell are joined by a break, an empty cell is written empty. **ENTER AND SHIFT-ENTER IN A CELL** are our shortcut (priority 90 — above Milkdown's 50, below the wikilink picker's and outliner's 100 so an open `[[` picker still takes Enter): both always insert a hardbreak, a new line in the cell as in Google Docs (🔒 YAZ-1462). Milkdown's own Shift-Enter refuses to run inside tables, and its "second Shift-Enter after a break makes a new paragraph" rule split the table. Mod-Enter is still Milkdown's `exitTable`. Contract test: `inlineBreaks.test.ts` (19 cases, one row copied verbatim from the reporting doc; and the cases B1–B12 of YAZ-2659, each saved, opened again and saved again).

31. **Highlight** (YAZ-1480, `src/editor/marks/highlight.ts` + the shared `marks/htmlPairs.ts`): ProseMirror mark `highlight` with ONE optional attribute `color`. **On disk**: `color: null` (yellow) is Obsidian's `==text==` — a vendored micromark tokenizer (gfm-strikethrough's attention run with `=`, exactly two: a lone `=` is text, `===` cancels, `\==` escapes) plus stringify `unsafe` rules: any `=` touching another `=` is written `\=` (`a == b` → `a \=\= b`, reads back as the same text; a lone `=` never; URLs never; an odd run escapes one short, `a === b` → `a \=\== b`, still stable). `postProcessMarkdown()` restores `=` inside `[[…]]` so a wikilink target keeps its bytes (outside code only, rule 3). `==` on its own line under a paragraph is still a setext heading. Two tight runs on one line pair up (`x==5 and y==6` on disk highlights `5 and y`, as in Obsidian); the typing rule is stricter than the parser (no `=` inside, no word character before), so the same string TYPED stays text and saves escaped. `green`/`blue`/`pink` have no Markdown syntax and store inline HTML `<mark class="highlight-<name>">text</mark>` — rule 11's `<u>` mechanism through the shared pair walk. A bare `<mark>` is read as yellow and written back as `==…==` (rule 6 normalisation); any other `<mark class/style>` is not this mark and passes through as inline HTML, bytes untouched — on PASTE every `<mark>` becomes the mark (unknown class → yellow): a pasted `<mark>` is a highlight. 🔒 A highlight nested inside a highlight ENDS the outer one (Milkdown's `ParserState` keeps one mark set) — lossy on the first save, stable after; a highlight inside a DIFFERENT mark is unaffected. **Toolbar** (`buildToolbar` in createCrepe.ts): four swatches (yellow, green, blue, pink), dots filled with the wash token, added into Crepe's own `formatting` group after Strikethrough — `getGroup('formatting')` THROWS if Crepe ever renames that group; `headingToolbar.test.ts` pins it. 🔒 D5, Bold's toggle rule: a dot is LIT when ANY selected text carries its colour (at a caret: the marks it would type with); pressing a lit dot removes exactly that colour, an unlit one applies it over any other colour; edge whitespace stays outside the mark; where marks cannot apply (code) the command declines and the key falls through. A lit dot is painted as a pressed button (`--crepe-color-selected`). `setHighlightCommand(color)` is the one command behind the swatches, `Mod-Shift-h` (yellow; a `$useKeymap`, so Crepe's `keymapRef` labels the yellow dot and its `aria-keyshortcuts` from it) and the `==x==` typing rule (yellow). Copy-out emits `<mark>`. **Styling**: translucent washes `--mark-bg` + `--mark-bg-<name>` in `:root` (one value per colour, both themes), text colour inherited, selector `.milkdown .ProseMirror mark` so every Crepe surface paints it. Not a Crepe feature (allowlist untouched).

**YAZ-1289 editor-link amendment to rules 23–25:** the editor still mounts these plugins only for Markdown source notes. Rule 23 consults the separate view-only source only when the raw target has a recognized explicit text/PDF/image extension; missing/loading/removed targets and heading/block suffixes remain unresolved and never run create-on-click, while `|display` is presentation only. Rule 24 composes explicit-extension catalog candidates with the Markdown candidates before the same matcher/ranker; recognized view-only target spellings suppress unroutable semantic rows, but semantic aliases whose display text matches remain. `WikilinkResolveSource.resolve` and `.records` stay the original Markdown feed by identity, so rule 25 and every other semantic consumer remain Markdown-only.
