30. **Images, first-class** (YAZ-1656 — decisions D1–D8 and the demo learnings are comments on that issue; `src/editor/image/`): `![alt](src)` RENDERS from the vault, a pasted or dropped image is SAVED to the vault, and the picture can be resized, opened full-size and copied. 🔒 **THE ONE NODE VIEW** (`imageView.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` only when `opts.image` supplies the vault root + the note's absolute path): rules 23/27 forbid node views for wikilinks and drawing embeds because those are plain TEXT with no node to view; the commonmark `image` node ALREADY EXISTS, so a node view here changes no schema and no serializer and `![alt|300](src "t")` stays byte-identical (`roundtrip.test.ts`). The rule it establishes: a node view is allowed only on a node the schema already has and never touches the serializer. **SRC** (`imageSrc.ts`): anything with a scheme passes through untouched (`https:`, `data:`; a foreign `app://fs/…` simply 404s); a leading `/` is root-relative; everything else becomes `app://vault/<encoded root>/<ref, each segment decoded ONCE then encoded>?from=<note's root-relative dir>` — decode-once because a markdown src is already percent-encoded (Obsidian writes `%20`), so `case%20a.png` reaches main as `case a.png`, never `case%2520a.png`. Main (`vaultProtocol.ts`) resolves note-relative → root-relative → shortest-path basename, images only. **STATES**: loading is quiet; broken (missing, foreign, escaped, non-image) is a small INERT chip "Broken image: <src as written>" with the markdown still editable — nothing is repaired or rewritten. **WIDTH** is Obsidian's `![alt|400](src)` — alt is already preserved byte-for-byte, so no serializer work — parsed for display (`parseAlt`; `a|b` is not a width) and written back by the resize. On the `<img>` the width is the `--image-width` custom property (`width: var(--image-width, auto)` in `imageView.css`; no property when the alt carries none — the stylesheet's `auto` fallback), NEVER `style.width` — an inline width beats every stylesheet rule, and a stylesheet state must be able to override it without `!important`. **FOLDED CHIP** (YAZ-1709): a bullet holding an image folds like a parent (rule 5); the fold plugin's `data-outline-folded-image` node decoration turns the view into a one-line chip — one fixed text-height box for every image (1.75em wide × (line box − 4px) tall, so it follows the line-spacing setting; `object-fit: cover`; short enough not to grow the row), no label, handles hidden — and a click on the thumbnail unfolds the bullet. An expanded image inside a foldable item shows a corner fold button (`.image-view__fold`, top-left, on hover/selection; absent while broken — the broken chip has no button) rendered dumb by the node view and driven by the fold plugin. The node view itself knows nothing about folding: it sets the width property and renders the button, the stylesheet reads the decorations. **RESIZE**: eight handles (four corners, four edge midpoints), every one WIDTH-ONLY so the aspect ratio can never break (only a width is stored): west handles grow leftward, north/south map through the aspect ratio, a corner follows the axis that moved more; clamp `MIN_WIDTH` 40 px … the editor column; the width is live on the `<img>` during the drag and ONE `setNodeMarkup` on mouseup writes `alt|<w>` (⌘Z is one step); Escape mid-drag cancels (capture-phase, so the editor's Escape→sidebar hand-off never hears it), `window` blur ends the gesture where it stands, a plain click commits nothing, read-only editors have no gesture. `update()` patches width/alt/title in place and rebuilds the `<img>` only on a src change; `ignoreMutation` answers false to PM's `selection` pseudo-mutation. **LIGHTBOX** (`ImageModal.tsx`, rule 28's shape): double-click → a full-window overlay of the FULL-resolution file (`95vw/95vh`, `object-fit: contain`, alt as caption); Esc / backdrop / ✕ close; keys are the overlay's (YAZ-888); focus returns to what had it. It is a GALLERY of every image on the page in document order (folded ones included — they are still in the doc): the node view walks the document at double-click time and hands over `{ images, index }` (no registry of node views, so the list is always current and stateless), the modal owns only the cursor; ← / → and the bottom bar's arrows page, an "n / m" counter sits between them, NO wrap-around (← at the first and → at the last are no-ops, the edge arrow disabled), and a single image shows no bar at all — the old lightbox exactly. **PASTE / DROP** (`insertImage.ts`, wired in `clipboardPaste.ts` through the `imageOptionsCtx` slice, D6): image BYTES on the clipboard or in a drop beat whatever HTML rode along — a "Copy Image" from another app carries the PNG and an `<img src>` only that app can read, and the old behaviour kept the private src and lost the bytes (the reported bug). The file is written FULL-RESOLUTION, never re-encoded, to `assets/images/<note, whitespace → '-'>-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.<ext from mime>` with `create: true` — never overwritten, a same-second collision retries `-2`, `-3`… — the sibling of `assets/drawings/` and root-relative so moving a note never breaks the link; the inserted node is `![<name>|400](assets/images/…)`, the `|400` (`PASTED_IMAGE_WIDTH`) only when the bitmap is wider than 400 px, so a screenshot lands at a manageable size and a 16 px icon is not blown up. Never in code (a fenced block is a CodeMirror node view that owns its own paste anyway); a drop over code or outside the document is REFUSED rather than passed on, because the next handler is Milkdown's upload plugin, which would inline the file as base64; a selected image becomes a caret AFTER itself before the insert (paste adds, never replaces); the first image file wins when several are dropped; any failure is the host's passive notice and nothing is inserted. HTML with a foreign `<img src>` and NO bytes is left alone (renders the broken chip; foreign URLs are never fetched, nothing is silently deleted). **WHERE IT IS NOT**: a folder's outline editor (its bullets-only lock has no `image` node — wiring options there would write the file and then filter the insert away) and the hover preview card (no vault root in reach) get no `image` options and keep Crepe's stock `<img>`, both commented where they mount. **DEFERRED** (YAZ-1673): `![[img.png]]` embeds (zero in the vault, paste never produces them, would need a second decoration renderer) and a per-vault attachment-folder setting (free later: links are root-relative). End-to-end: `desktop/e2e/imagePaste.spec.ts` and `imageRender.spec.ts` (fixture PNGs encoded at test time by `imageFixtures.ts`).

32. **Code blocks wrap and follow Appearance** (YAZ-2270, decisions D1–D4 are comments on that issue; `src/editor/codeTheme.ts`, wired in `createCrepe()`'s `featureConfigs[CodeMirror]`): every fenced block is Crepe's stock CodeMirror editor with two app settings. **WRAP**: `EditorView.lineWrapping` — a long line wraps at the column edge and breaks mid-word only when it has no space; never a CSS `white-space` override, which bypasses CodeMirror's line measuring. Display only: the markdown is untouched. A diagram wider than the column wraps too (accepted). The `overflow-x: auto` on `.milkdown-code-block` stays as the guard for tables and the rare unbreakable element. **COLOURS**: the app's own theme replaces Crepe's One Dark (built for a dark block, ~2:1 on the light one). Every colour is an `app.css` variable — the `--code-*` syntax tokens plus `--fg`, `--fg-muted`, `--accent`, `--bg-hover` — defined under `:root` and `[data-theme='dark']`, so Appearance re-colours code live with no remount (the `crepeTheme.ts` mechanism). ONE look for every language: no per-language styling (D2, dropped — untagged fences mix code, diagrams and prose). `crepe.ts` no longer defaults a theme, so `@codemirror/theme-one-dark` is not bundled. `codeBlock.test.ts` pins wrapping, the theme, and that every `--code-*` is defined for both appearances and used.

33. **Marks on line breaks** (YAZ-2280, decisions D1–D3 are comments on that issue; `src/editor/breakMarks.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` in place of Milkdown's `hardbreakClearMarkPlugin`, which is removed): a SOFT break is a newline inside a paragraph (`hardbreak` with `isInline`, shown as a space); a HARD break is `\` + newline. 🔒 **ONE RULE**, enforced by one `appendTransaction` on the span a transaction changed: a soft break keeps a mark only while the text on BOTH sides carries that same mark; a hard break never carries one. So a mark added across soft-wrapped lines runs through them as one span (`==two⏎three==`, `**two⏎three**`, `<mark class="highlight-green">two⏎three</mark>`) and the lines keep flowing, and a mark never starts or ends on a newline — Markdown cannot open or close one there, and the mark would be lost on reload with its markers left as text. The rule runs on every document change, not only when a mark is added: un-marking or deleting the text beside a marked break unmarks that break too. Adding a mark never changes a break's kind (the bug this replaced: Milkdown rebuilt each break with default attrs, turning soft into hard). Unchanged: hard breaks split a mark per line (`==a==\⏎==b==`), Shift-Enter inserts an unmarked hard break, and a file that already holds a multi-line mark saves byte-identical. Tests: `breakMarks.test.ts`.

34. **Line numbers and the page settings menu** (YAZ-2643, decisions D1–D9 and cases S1–S82 are comments on that issue; `src/editor/PageSettings.tsx`, `src/editor/lineNumbers/`, the host effects in `Editor.tsx`): each Markdown note has a cog as the FIRST chip of its status row — cog, zoom pill, sync chip, save chip; a folder page, a text file, a PDF and an image have none. A click opens a small menu under the cog (`role="group"`, on the zoom menu's own CSS rules): ONE switch, "Line numbers" (`aria-pressed`, a check mark while it is on, and the menu stays open), a divider, and two grey lines, "N words" and "N characters". A second click on the cog, a click outside, or Esc closes it; Esc stops at the menu and puts the focus on the cog. 🔒 **NOTHING IS STORED**: the switch is React state of `CrepeHost`, beside the document zoom. Each mounted editor has its own, a tab switch keeps it, and a closed tab, a rename (a new editor) or a restart starts with the numbers off. No hotkey, no menu-bar item, no setting. **THE COUNTS** (D5): the menu reads `getPlainText(crepe)` ONE time, when it opens. The text it read IS its open state, so the counts cannot change while the menu shows, and nothing is counted while it is closed. The text is the note body only — not the title, the properties, the comments, the linked mentions or the reviews — with one line break between two blocks and none at the end: the empty paragraph Crepe keeps under a last bullet, heading, code block or table counts as nothing (S18). A word is a run of characters with no white space in it; both numbers use the locale's separators. 🔒 **WHAT A NUMBER IS** (D2): the line of the file ON DISK where the block starts. Line 1 is the first line of the file, so the frontmatter lines count, and a file the editor did not write (an AI's `-` bullets and blank lines) shows the lines of its own bytes. A paragraph, a heading, a code block (its opening fence), a rule and a table (its header row) each show the line where they START; a bullet shows the number of its first block; no line inside a code block gets a number in the lane, because the block's own gutter counts them (D8, below). 🔒 **THE RANGE** (D7): a block that is two or more lines of the file shows where it ENDS too, `8–10`, so that a line inside it can be found. A paragraph ends on its last line (soft-wrapped lines, in a bullet and in a quote too, and never its nested bullets), a fenced code block on its closing fence (one that is never closed: on the last line of the block), an indented code block on its last code line, a table on its last row, a setext heading on its underline, a raw HTML block on its last line. A block of one line shows ONE number. `blockLines` returns `ends` beside `lines`, in the same order and of the same length — the end of the block's token, and for the empty paragraph of a bare marker or of an empty quote the start — the worker posts it, and the host maps both through `toFileLine`. The plugin puts the start in `data-line` (an integer, as before) and the end in a SECOND attribute, `data-line-end`, only when the end is the greater: never one merged string, so CSS alone decides how a range looks. **WHEN IT IS BUILT**: only when the page and the disk agree — the switch turns on, a save settles, an outside change lands (a reload, a frontmatter-only absorb, "Reload" or "Keep mine") — never on a keystroke, and never while the save state is `unsaved` or `saving`. `useAutosave().diskParts()` gives the frontmatter block and the body as they are on disk, and the counter `diskLandings` says an outside change landed, because `disk` does not follow this editor's own saves. `fileLines(frontmatter, body)` runs on the main thread: it gives the text the editor parses (`normalizeEmptyItems`, rule 8) and the map from a line of that text to a line of the file — it adds the frontmatter's lines and takes out the blank lines the load added; a lone CR is a line ending, as it is in the parse. An answer that comes back after a newer request, after the numbers went off, or for an editor that is gone is dropped. **THE WORKER** (D1): `blockLines(text)` reads the block structure from `micromark` events with the GFM extension — no syntax tree — and returns the start line and the kind of each leaf block. It runs ONLY in `lineNumbers.worker.ts`, its own renderer chunk, never on the main thread: `tools/lineNumbersWorker.test.mjs` fails when any other source module imports it as a value. ONE worker per window, started by the first request (`requestBlockLines`); a window that never shows line numbers never loads it. The worker imports `workerDocument.ts` FIRST: micromark's browser build decodes a character reference through a DOM element it makes at module load, a worker has no `document`, and the stand-in decodes nothing — a reference never makes block structure. A worker that fails (`onerror`, `onmessageerror`) rejects every request that waits on it, is terminated and is dropped, so the next request starts a new one; the host shows the passive notice "Line numbers could not load." and does not retry (S65). 🔒 **THE PAIRING AND ITS TWO STOPS** (`build` in `lineNumbers.ts`): the plugin pairs the page's leaf blocks with the file's, in order, and compares their kinds, because no number is better than a wrong one (the file has two kinds of code block, `c` fenced and `i` indented, and both pair with the page's `code_block`). D7 and D8 change neither stop. At the first pair whose kinds disagree — a footnote definition is such a block — the numbers stop, and the blocks before it keep theirs (S45). When every pair agreed and one side still has blocks — the file has an entry left, or the page has a block left that is not an empty paragraph — the page shows NO number at all (S64). The empty paragraph Crepe keeps at the end of the page is not such a block, and it has no number (S44). Neither stop shows an error. **VIEW STATE ONLY**, like folding (rule 5): `showLineNumbers(crepe, blocks | null)` sends the lines in a metadata-only transaction with `addToHistory: false` — never `markdownUpdated`, never the autosave, never undo — and each numbered block gets ONE node decoration: `data-line`, and on the same decoration `data-line-end` and `data-line-code` where they apply. Between two sends the set only moves through the position mapping, so typing builds nothing, and with the numbers off the plugin holds `DecorationSet.empty`. A new block, and a block that Enter split or Backspace joined, has no number until the next save settles (accepted: a node decoration does not survive a change of its node's bounds). 🔒 **A CODE BLOCK'S OWN NUMBERS** (D8, `lineNumbers/codeLines.ts`): while the numbers show, the gutter INSIDE a code block counts in lines of the file — a fenced block whose opening fence is line 27 reads 28, 29, … — so the page has ONE number system; with the switch off, and for a block that has no number, it reads 1, 2, 3 as before. A fenced block's first code line is the line under its fence; an indented block has no fence, so its first code line is the block's own start line. The plugin puts that line in a third attribute, `data-line-code`, ONLY when the page's block holds as many code lines as the file has inside the block: an empty block and a fence that is never closed do not, and keep 1, 2, 3. `createCrepe` adds `codeLines` to the CodeMirror extensions of every code block: ONE shared `Compartment`, empty, and a `ViewPlugin` with a constructor only. `syncCodeLines(cm)` reads `data-line-code` on the CodeMirror's block and reconfigures the compartment to `lineNumbers({ formatNumber })`, or back to empty; Crepe's own `lineNumbers()` sets no formatter, so the two configs merge with no conflict, and the changed config makes CodeMirror draw the gutter again. It dispatches ONLY when the line changed. `showLineNumbers` runs it, after its ProseMirror dispatch, for each code block that has a CodeMirror (`EditorView.findFromDOM`). Crepe makes a block's CodeMirror only when the block comes near the window, and drops it 5 s after the block left, so a NEW CodeMirror runs it for itself, one microtask after it is made. The transaction carries effects only. Crepe's `forwardUpdate` forwards a change of the text or of the selection and nothing else, so nothing reaches ProseMirror, `markdownUpdated` or the autosave, and the focus and the caret stay where they are. The staleness is that of the outer numbers: the lines are set at a build and never on a keystroke, a code block typed while the numbers are on reads 1, 2, 3 until the save settles, and inside a numbered block CodeMirror counts on from the block's first line. With the switch off nothing of this runs on a keystroke, and no CodeMirror transaction is dispatched unless the switch is or was on. **THE LOOK** (D4, `app.css`): pure CSS, no layout in JavaScript. While `.editor-host[data-line-numbers]` is set, `.ProseMirror` is a query container and a numbered block's `::after` draws `attr(data-line)` to the left of the column: a block's right edge is the column's right edge at every bullet depth, and `100cqw` is the column's width, so one rule puts every number in ONE column. A block with `data-line-end` draws its range on the SAME pseudo-element, on one line: `content: attr(data-line) '–' attr(data-line-end)` (a table on its `::before`). 🔒 **THE LANE** (D9, the new S51): the 48px margin left of the column belongs to the drag handle (on hover its box is 49px to 17px left of the column) and to the fold arrows (the last 18px), so a number in that margin was covered by the handle and a range did not fit. The numbers have a lane of their own, left of the margin, and the scroller makes it: `.editor-host[data-line-numbers] { padding-left: 64px; }`. It is the scroller's OWN padding — nothing else sets its left padding — so the title row, the body, the comments, the linked mentions and the reviews slide right together and stay one aligned column. So the page SLIDES RIGHT while the numbers show (64px in a window narrower than the column, half of that in a wide one, where the column is centred), text can wrap again, and the page slides back when they go off. A number ends 52px left of the column, 3px clear of the handle's box: the drag handle and the fold arrows are at their usual places, and neither covers a number. `--line-box: 1lh` puts the number on the block's first line. The number is inside the zoomed body, so its size and its gap are divided by `--document-zoom` (`calc(11px / var(--document-zoom, 1))`, `calc(100cqw + 52px / var(--document-zoom, 1))`): it is 11px on screen at every zoom, 52px left of the column, and it stays in the lane (S53). A rule and a code block give up their clip (`overflow: visible`) while the numbers show; a table keeps its clip, stays `position: static`, and draws its number as `::before`. The number is grey and monospace, with `user-select: none` and `pointer-events: none`. The lane is 60px: about 9 characters of the 11px code font (0.6em each, not measured in the app), `1207–1210`. In a window narrower than the column the left part of a range of two 5-digit lines can be cut off (accepted, S57). **NOT INCLUDED**: line numbers in the text, PDF and image viewers and on a folder page; "Go to line"; a raw text view; a live count chip; any change in the main process. Tests: `lineNumbers/lineNumbers.test.ts` (real editors: the hand-counted note shapes as loaded and after a save, the ranges, the first code line of each code block, the two stops, folding, zoom, undo), `lineNumbers/codeLines.test.ts` (real editors and the real CodeMirror of each code block: its own gutter on, off, built again, in an indented block, in a CodeMirror made after the build, and never a document change), `lineNumbers/blockLines.test.ts` (`blockLines` with its ends, `fileLines`), `lineNumbers/requestBlockLines.test.ts` (the one worker and its failure), `lineNumbers/lineNumbersCss.test.ts` (the CSS contract), `PageSettings.test.tsx`, `Editor.test.tsx` ("the page settings cog", "line numbers follow the file on disk"), `createCrepe.test.ts` (`getPlainText`), `hooks/useAutosave.test.tsx` (`diskParts`), `tools/lineNumbersWorker.test.mjs` (the worker bundle with no DOM). End to end: `desktop/e2e/lineNumbers.spec.ts`, written and not run.

## View modules

TOMBSTONE: this heading was "Bases modules" until YAZ-858.

Everything under this heading serves ONE surface — a folder's page (YAZ-2290; YAZ-844 retired `.base`, Editor rule 19's tombstone). The name no longer stays: YAZ-858 renamed the machinery to match what it serves — `client/src/bases/` → `client/src/views/`, `baseFile.ts` → `viewSchema.ts`, `BaseView` → `ViewsPane`, `.base-*` → `.view-*` — a mechanical rename with zero behaviour change. The property index, the query engine, the view chrome and the cell editors were still BUILT for Obsidian Bases and were not rewritten — they were re-pointed, then re-named. Where an entry below still says "base", it is a tombstone naming something the format took with it (or a stored key kept for compatibility, see `baseGroups` under App state); the surface that mounts them is "Folders are the pages", and the config they read is "Folder settings: `.folder.md`". Nothing here reads or writes a `.base` file any more, and no entry may describe one as current.

### Properties (YAZ-835, under the LOCKED rulings of YAZ-816 — `<root>/.yaseendocs/properties.json`, succeeding the Bible A registry)

**What it is**: vault-wide property DECLARATIONS — the ONE registry concept that survived the type system's deletion (YAZ-836). `{ version: 1, properties: { <name>: { kind, target?, required? } } }`, keyed by BARE frontmatter key — never a canonicalised `note.x`. `kind` ∈ `PROPERTY_KINDS` = text / number / date / checkbox / list / link / multi-link (`shared/types.ts`), `target` is a link/multi-link column's picker constraint (an opaque string here; its meaning is `belongsToBasenames`' — a wikilink to a FOLDER, see "Links": Links to folders), `required` is report-only metadata that gates nothing, exactly as it did in the registry. Read and written in main through the vaultConfig plumbing (`desktop/src/main/properties/`, Electron-free like the vault index); the IPC half is `desktop/src/main/ipc/properties.ts`.

**The version rule, unchanged from the registry** (LOCKED, R2.5): `version: 1` is readable AND mutable; a numeric `version > 1` reads best-effort (known fields consumed, no `error`) but refuses every mutation — writing a v1 shape over a newer file would destroy fields this version does not know. An ABSENT file is empty declarations with NO error, and reading NEVER creates anything: the file is LAZY, the first successful mutation writes the `{ version: 1, properties: {} }` skeleton along with the dotfolder. A MALFORMED file (unparsable JSON, non-object root, missing/non-numeric `version`) reads as empty + an `error` string, and every mutation rejects `INVALID_CONFIG` — the file is never overwritten and never moved aside, deliberately unlike `store.ts`'s `.corrupt-<epoch>` policy: app state is disposable, the user's schema is not.

**Surface**: `getProperties(root)` / `setProperty(root, name, decl)` / `removeProperty(root, name)` / `subscribeProperties(root, listener)`, over channels `properties:get` / `properties:set-property` / `properties:remove-property` / `properties:changed` (`CONTRACT.properties` in `shared/ipc.ts`), reaching the renderer as `window.yaseenDocs.properties.get/setProperty/removeProperty/onChange` (bridge table above). Targeted mutators, never a whole-file PUT (the state door's anti-clobber shape); every mutation is a serialised per-root read-modify-write (the store's write-chain idiom, so two can never interleave reads) that preserves unknown fields at every level for forward compat, and removing an unknown name is a no-op that never creates the file. `subscribeProperties` re-reads through the SAME chain, so notifications deliver in order; main holds ONE subscription per open-vault root (`rootsOf(state)` — every vault of every window, YAZ-2602 S77 — dropped with the last window that shows it) and broadcasts the fresh declarations to EVERY window, which filter by their own root.

**Validation** (strict at the IPC boundary — a write is config, not content): names must match `PROPERTY_NAME` `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` (snake_case, ONE definition in `shared/types.ts` so no mirror can drift), a declaration must be an object whose `kind` is in `PROPERTY_KINDS`, `target` a string and `required` a boolean — everything else `BAD_REQUEST`. Reading is deliberately laxer than writing: an unknown on-disk `kind` string stays on disk and reads back as `text`. `page_type` is now an ORDINARY name that declares like any other (`properties/index.test.ts`) — the identity rule that reserved it died with the type system.

**The clean break** (LOCKED, YAZ-835): `.yaseendocs/types.json` is NEVER read. No migration, no converter, no read-old-write-new — a vault carrying one simply starts with no declarations, and the file is left untouched on disk. Pinned by a test: *"an old types.json is simply not read — properties.json is the only file this module knows"* (`desktop/src/main/properties/index.test.ts`).

**Not ours** (TOMBSTONE, ⚡ YAZ-815 — ruled by Yasin): `.obsidian/types.json` is another app's file — a different folder, a different owner, a different shape — and we no longer touch it AT ALL. It used to be read fresh per `index(root)` call (never cached), ride `IndexResponse.types` and hold a rung in the typing ladder one BELOW our own declarations; nothing ever consumed that rung, because the one surface that mounts cell editors reads the window's link snapshot instead of fetching an index. A stale foreign artifact feeding a rung with no feed is worse than no rung, so the whole chain — the read, the field, the parameter and the rung — is deleted. `.yaseendocs/properties.json` is the vault's schema; we never wrote `.obsidian/types.json` and now we never read it either.

Files: `desktop/src/main/properties/index.ts`, `desktop/src/main/properties/index.test.ts`, `desktop/src/main/ipc/properties.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/useProperties.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/propertiesStub.ts` (+ test — the in-memory `YaseenDocsApi['properties']` the client tests install as `window.yaseenDocs.properties`), `shared/types.ts` (`PROPERTY_KINDS`, `PROPERTY_NAME`, `FOLDER_NAME`, `PropertyDecl`, `PropertiesResponse`).

### Property index (GRO-2127 contract, GRO-2128 index — now in the Electron main process)

The query engine behind a folder's views reads notes through `IndexRecord`, never the filesystem. Transport: bridge method `index(root)` (Desktop D10), no HTTP anywhere — the index (`desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/`) is transport-agnostic (tests call `getIndex` directly); it moved, not rewrote, from the retired server process (GRO-2157).

Files: `shared/types.ts` (`IndexRecord`, `IndexResponse`), `shared/frontmatter.ts` (`splitFrontmatter` — moved from the client, which re-exports it — and `parseFrontmatter`), `shared/noteId.ts` (`isNoteId` — what `id` holds), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts` (`walk`, `scanFile` + pure `extractAliases` / `extractTags` / `extractLinks` / `extractEmbeds`), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/live.ts` (`getIndex`, cache + watcher), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/idSweep.ts` (`sweepIds` — "Note ids": The sweep), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/index.ts` (barrel), `client/src/views/testRecords.ts` (`TEST_RECORDS`).

`IndexResponse { root, records: IndexRecord[], folders: IndexRecord[], generatedAt, ids: boolean, ask?: { notes, folders, foreign } }` — `ids` says whether the vault gives its notes IDs, and `ask`, present only while the vault has not answered, counts what a yes would write (YAZ-2523 — "Two kinds of vault": What the index hands out); `records` is every `.md`/`.markdown` file under `root` (dot-entries and `node_modules` skipped, exactly like `tree(root)`), sorted by path; `folders` (🔒 D8, YAZ-2290) is every folder's settings file `.folder.md` — the ONE dot-entry the walk and the watcher let through — as the same `IndexRecord` shape, sorted by path, its `folder` naming the folder it configures, and NEVER among `records` (`getIndex` splits one record map by file name; pinned by `vaultIndex/folderSettings.test.ts`). Images and everything else are never records — nor were `.base` files while the format existed (YAZ-844), which is why retiring it cost the index nothing: no field, no `CACHE_VERSION` bump, no rescan.

| `IndexRecord` field | Value |
|---|---|
| `path` | absolute |
| `id` | OPTIONAL (YAZ-2293, YAZ-2677): the frontmatter `id` when it is an ID of this vault (`vaultNoteId`, `shared/noteId.ts`), as the app writes it — a number ID with the vault's CURRENT letters in capitals (`YAZ-12`; one the file holds in another case, or with letters the vault had before, goes out so too), or an old 12-character ID — the note's permanent identity, see "Note ids". ABSENT when the note has none or its `id` is anything else (another tool's, another vault's letters — which the sweep replaces, YAZ-2420 D30), and on EVERY record a vault that does not use IDs hands out (`plain`, YAZ-2523 V12). The key is not removed from `properties`: it stays there as written, like any other. |
| `name` / `basename` / `ext` | `VSL-v1.md` / `VSL-v1` / `md` (`md` or `markdown`, lower-case, no dot) |
| `title` | what the app shows (YAZ-2420 D14 — `titleOf`, `shared/noteName.ts`): the frontmatter `title`, trimmed (a number is read as the text it was written as), else `basename` — for a folder's settings file, the folder's own name. Every screen reads this one value. In a vault that does not use IDs it is ALWAYS the `basename` (a settings file's: its folder's name), whatever `title:` line the file holds (`fileTitle`, YAZ-2523 V12). The key is not removed from `properties`. |
| `folder` | root-relative, `/` separators, `''` at the root (`Content Pillars/1. Agentic Agency`) |
| `size` / `mtime` / `ctime` | bytes / mtime ms / birthtime ms (ctime ms where the platform has no birthtime) |
| `properties` | frontmatter parsed with yaml's default **core** schema: `null`/booleans/numbers typed, dates stay strings (`date: 2026-08-01` → `'2026-08-01'`), `pillar: null` is present with value `null`. `{}` when there is no frontmatter, the block is not a map, or YAML fails — the latter two also set `frontmatterError` (one-line yaml message or `frontmatter is not a map`). Files over `MAX_FILE_BYTES` are stat-only (`{}`, empty lists, no error). The reserved `comments` key is DELETED right after the parse (YAZ-1472, 🔒 D5 — a note's comment stream is content, not a property), so it never reaches a record, the cache, a view column or a link source; see "Comments". |
| `aliases` | frontmatter `aliases` — extra NAMES the note answers to (Links E2, GRO-2214): a list → each string item; a scalar string → ONE alias (never comma-split, the deliberate contrast with `tags`); trimmed, empties and non-strings dropped, de-duplicated (first appearance). Absent / non-string → `[]`. Only the `aliases` key (Obsidian's), never `alias`; nothing is stripped from a value, so `aliases: ["[[X]]"]` is an alias literally named `[[X]]`. |
| `tags` | frontmatter `tags`/`tag` (list → each string item; string → split on commas/whitespace; non-strings ignored; leading `#` stripped) followed by inline `#tags` in order of appearance. Inline rule: `#` preceded by start of text, whitespace or one of `( [ , ;`, then `[A-Za-z0-9_/-]+` with at least one non-digit (`#123` is not a tag), case preserved, nested `a/b` kept whole. Never inside fenced code (``` or ~~~), inline code spans or `http(s)://…` runs. De-duplicated, first appearance wins. |
| `links` | `[[target]]` targets with `\|alias`, `#heading` and `#^block` stripped and trimmed — frontmatter string values (top-level and inside lists) that are exactly `[[…]]` first, then the same in each folder's block of `in` (D19 — a link column's value; `folderBlocks`), then the body. Embeds are excluded, and so is everything under the `aliases` key whatever it looks like (GRO-2214: an alias is one of this note's own NAMES, never an outgoing link). De-duplicated. |
| `embeds` | `![[target]]` targets from the body, same stripping, de-duplicated. |
| `reviews` | Optional. The note's review log (frontmatter `reviews`, YAZ-2322) as `{ at, rating, text }` in time order; absent when never reviewed. Lifted OFF `properties`, like `comments` is dropped. |
| `text` | Optional. `textFingerprint` of the body (YAZ-2322): eight hex characters; a review records the one it saw. Absent when the file was not read (over `MAX_FILE_BYTES`). |

Tags, links and embeds share one code-stripping pre-pass (`stripCode` in `scan.ts`): nothing inside a fenced code block (``` or ~~~) or an inline code span counts, matching Obsidian's metadata cache; `http(s)://…` runs are additionally ignored for tags only. Frontmatter values are never code-stripped.

`TEST_RECORDS` (`client/src/views/testRecords.ts`) is the 8-note `bases-fixture` as records rooted at `/vault` with `size`/`ctime`/`mtime` zeroed; `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/live.test.ts` deep-equals the live scan to it, so client query tests and the main-process index are pinned to one truth.

Cache lifecycle (`getIndex(root)`):
1. First call: walk the root (`isSkipped` pruning — `.folder.md` excepted, YAZ-2290 D8 — markdown only, 32 reads in flight), `scanFile` each file, keep a `Map<path, IndexRecord>`; concurrent first calls for one root share the single scan. A missing / non-directory root rejects with the same `BridgeFailure` codes as the fs bridge (`NOT_FOUND`, `NOT_A_DIRECTORY`, `FORBIDDEN`).
2. The root's shared watcher (`watchers.subscribe`) keeps the map live: `add`/`change` on a markdown path → re-`scanFile` and replace (a file that vanishes before the scan is dropped), `unlink` → delete, `unlinkDir` → delete every record under it; `addDir` changes no record (the sweep gives that folder its `.folder.md`, where the vault uses IDs), `ready`/`error` are no-ops. No rescan is ever needed. The id sweep (YAZ-2293 — "Note ids": The sweep) rides both ends: once after the first build's reconcile, over every record and every folder walked, and NOT awaited, and after each `add`/`change` scan lands, over that one record; a write it makes comes back as an ordinary `add`/`change` and rescans the file.
3. Every call FIRST drains the watcher scans still in flight (YAZ-986: the answer is the truth as of the ask — a just-created file can never miss the snapshot its own `add` event provoked, which was YAZ-958's invisible-newborn bug; `liveDrain.test.ts` pins it by holding a scan open across a call), then returns the map sorted by path — each record as the vault's kind has it, the answer read fresh from `ids.json` on every call, while the map and the persisted cache hold the files as scanned (YAZ-2523, "Two kinds of vault") — with a fresh `generatedAt` and resets a 10-minute idle timer; on expiry the watcher subscription is released and the map dropped (the next call rescans). Test hooks: `_evictAll()`, `_setIdleMs(ms?)`.

Persistent cache (GRO-2223, decisions D1–D4 — write GRO-2228, load GRO-2229): the record map additionally persists as one JSON per vault in `app.getPath('userData')/index-cache/<first 16 hex of sha256(root)>.json` (`desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/cache.ts` — Electron-free like `store.ts`: `main/index.ts` injects the dir via `initIndexCache`; the vault itself is never written, and cross-machine sync of the cache is a non-goal — the reconcile makes a foreign cache harmless anyway). Payload `{ version: CACHE_VERSION, root, records: IndexRecord[] }` (the constant is **7** today — see the load rules below); records whose frontmatter cannot round-trip through JSON (non-finite numbers — YAML `.inf`/`.nan` — or self-referential alias cycles) are left out of the write and simply rescan on the next start; the rest of the vault still caches. Persists are debounced ~5 s per root (`schedulePersist` — called from the watcher-incremental mutations, build success, and idle eviction as a natural last flush point) and land through the same `atomicWrite` as everything else, chained per root so writes never reorder; `flushIndexCache()` writes every pending root at once and joins `store.flush()` in the `before-quit` chain. The payload has ALWAYS been exactly those three keys and nothing else — pinned by `liveCache.test.ts` ("the persisted payload is version/root/records and nothing else"). `.obsidian/types.json` never entered it even while it was read per call, which is why ⚡ YAZ-815 deleting that read needed no invalidation and no CACHE_VERSION bump. GC (GRO-2230): `initIndexCache` sweeps the dir once per launch — ANY file (cache JSONs and crashed `.tmp-` leftovers alike) whose mtime is older than 90 days (`GC_MAX_AGE_MS`) is deleted, so vaults never reopened stop accumulating dead files. Every vault open refreshes its file's mtime (build success schedules a persist), so only truly abandoned entries age out, and a wrongly deleted cache is self-healing — exactly one full rescan. Deliberately minimal: no size caps, no LRU, no eviction policy of its own.

THE INVARIANT: the cache is only an accelerator — a wrong, missing, stale or corrupt cache can never produce wrong data. `loadIndexCache(root)` never throws; it answers `{ records, status }` with status `hit`, or degrades: `miss` (no file, or the payload names another root — a hash-prefix collision or a copied cache dir is simply not this vault's cache), `corrupt` (unparsable JSON, wrong shape, or any structurally malformed record — one bad element poisons the whole file), `version-mismatch` (numeric `version` ≠ `CACHE_VERSION`, now **7** — E2 added `aliases` to the record shape, GRO-2214; YAZ-1472 dropped `comments` from `properties`, see "Comments"; YAZ-2293 added `id`, see "Note ids"; YAZ-2322 added `reviews` and `text`; YAZ-2454 made the links in a folder's values part of `links`; YAZ-2420 D14 added `title`). On build, `reconcile(root, files, load)` (`reconcile.ts`) turns the load into the live map: a non-hit is exactly today's full `scanAll`; on a hit it stat-sweeps every walked-and-cached file and reuses a cached record only when path + mtime + size all match (D2 — the record is its own validation key), `scanFile`-ing anything new or differing and dropping per-file stat/scan errors silently (same semantics as `scanAll`). The sweep is a sync `statSync` loop (~27 ms at 10k notes) and the cache load happens before `subscribe`, because the fresh watcher's initial scan floods the 4-thread libuv pool right at build time — async stats queue FIFO behind it and drain at the watcher's pace (~10× slower measured); warm 10k start lands ~99 ms vs ~1040 ms cold (GRO-2229 bench). A corrupt cache file is ignored IN PLACE (one `console.warn` at build) and overwritten by the next persist — deliberately unlike `store.ts`, which moves corrupt user state aside as `.corrupt-<epoch>` for forensics: the cache is derived data whose source of truth is the vault itself, so a corrupt copy holds nothing worth keeping, and move-aside would only accumulate junk for the GC to learn about (GRO-2230). RULE: any change to `IndexRecord`'s shape or the scanner's extraction semantics MUST bump `CACHE_VERSION` (`cache.ts`) — an old snapshot must never be read as if it had the new semantics; discard IS the migration (a mismatch degrades to one full rescan, there is no converter), and the fingerprint pin in `cache.test.ts` fails on such a change until the bump lands.

The reconcile also emits the cold-start diff for E1c rename detection (GRO-2242): `getColdStartDiff(root)` → `ColdStartDiff { root, scannedAt, cacheStatus, added, removed, changed }` (= the shared `ColdStartDiffResponse`, shipped to the renderer verbatim as the bridge's `coldDiff(root)`), held on the live entry and dropped with it on idle eviction. `added` carries ON-DISK stats and `removed` the CACHED stats — their (size, mtime) join is the rename signal; `changed` lists paths whose mtime or size moved. Miss semantics are honest: on any `cacheStatus` other than `hit` there is no before-snapshot, so all three lists are EMPTY (never "everything added") and `cacheStatus` says why — consumers must check `cacheStatus === 'hit'` before trusting them. Test hooks: `_resetIndexCache()`, `_setPersistDebounceMs(ms?)`, `_gcDone()`.

### Engine (GRO-2132 function library, GRO-2133 filters / sort / group / limit, GRO-2134 summaries)

`client/src/views/engine.ts` — `runView(def, view, records, { root?, resolve?, declared? }) → ViewResult { rows, groups, summaries, errors, total }`, pure and DOM-free, never throws. `RunOptions.resolve` (🔒 D2, YAZ-819) is the resolver every link in the run resolves through: absent, the engine builds one from `records` — which is the vault whenever the rows ARE the vault — while a folder's view passes only the folder's ROWS and injects the FULL-VAULT resolver, so a link cell pointing outside the folder still resolves ("Folders are the pages"). `RunOptions.declared` is the folder's declared column names (YAZ-1549): `propertyKeys` shows them before any note carries a value, which is what lets a column exist without being stamped into notes (🔒 E1, YAZ-2290). Pipeline: filters → values → sort → limit → group → summaries. A record passes when the base-level `def.filters` AND the view's `filters` both pass; `and` / `or` / `not` nest (`not` = none of the children true); a leaf is truthy per `isTruthy` (an ErrorValue fails). Every filter string is compiled once (`compile()` cache); a compile error or a malformed node makes that whole filter reject every row and adds one `EngineError { where, message }` — `where` is `filters` / `views[i].filters`, with `[j]` per child (`views[0].filters[1][0]`). `Row { record, file: FileValue, values }` — `values` is keyed exactly as the view writes the key (`view.order`, else `propertyKeys`): `file.x` is the file field, `formula.x` evaluates the formula (a compile error → ErrorValue cell + one `EngineError` at `formula.x`; an unknown formula is the evaluator's `unknown formula x` ErrorValue with no report), anything else is the note property (`note.` prefix optional) through `fromYaml`. Sort: `view.sort` in priority order, type-aware (numbers, dates/durations by ms, strings `localeCompare` base-insensitive + numeric, booleans false < true, lists by first element, links by what they READ as (`linkText` — a label, else the title of the note an id link names, else the target; YAZ-2293 D8), mixed types by rank), null / missing / ErrorValue always last whatever the direction, path as the final tiebreak. `limit` applies after sort and before grouping; `total` is the pre-limit count. `groupBy` → one `Group { key, label: render(key, resolve), rows, summaries, fannedOut }` per distinct key (`equals`; the label reads an id link as its note's title while `key` stays the stored value — YAZ-2293 D8), ordered by `groupBy.direction` with the same comparator; null / `''` / empty list / ErrorValue rows go to a trailing `{ key: null, label: 'No value' }` group that is always last; no `groupBy` → `groups: null`. **Two levels** (YAZ-745, YAZ-1097/1098): `groupBy` is an ordered LIST of levels, OUTER FIRST — `groupByLevels` reads the single-object form as a one-element list, so the old spelling stays valid and byte-identical in behaviour, levels past the second are ignored in v1, and a level with no `property` string is not one. With two, the outer level buckets exactly as above and each outer group is then bucketed AGAIN by the inner one: `Group.children` (its inner groups, ordered by the inner level's own direction; a child never has children of its own) and `Group.direct` (the rows sitting straight under the outer) appear on EVERY group of a two-level run — possibly empty, and absent entirely in single-level mode, which is how a consumer tells the two apart — while the outer's own `rows` stay the WHOLE branch, `direct` plus every child's rows, so counts and summaries above it are unchanged. **The merge rule**: an inner key that `equals` its outer's is no child at all, its rows are `direct` — grouping problems by top-function then function, a function that IS its own top shows its rows straight under the section instead of under a header repeating the name — and a "No value" OUTER puts its value-less rows in `direct` too, so it never carries a "No value" child. Everything else applies PER LEVEL and last within its own branch: each outer gets its own trailing "No value" child, and fan-out stays one flag per level (YAZ-671's semantics unchanged — one list anywhere inside marks every inner group). **Fan-out** (YAZ-671 D1): a LIST value joins the entry to one group per DISTINCT non-empty element (`equals`, no resolver — so link identity is the exact target, and two spellings of one note stay two groups), never a combination group; empty elements drop and a list left with none falls to "No value" like `[]` already does. `Group.fannedOut` is true on EVERY group of such a grouping (the "No value" group included) and false otherwise — it is what the group-derived writes read. A fanned-out row is counted in each group it joins, so per-group summaries can sum ABOVE `total` while the footer stays de-duplicated over `kept` (D2, deliberate). `groupKeyOf` keys shift from the rendered list to the rendered element, so collapse state persisted against an old combination group simply never matches again — orphaned, harmless, no migration. `this` is ALWAYS null: the views are a FOLDER's and a folder is no record (YAZ-2290), so the engine takes no "this file" option any more. `propertyKeys(def, view, records, declared?)` is `view.order` if set, else `file.name` + every note key seen or declared (sorted, as `note.<key>`), none left out (YAZ-2523 V12): where the vault uses IDs the rows hold a folder's values, so the note's own `id`, `also_in` and `title` are not among them, and where it does not they are ordinary properties and columns like the rest; `propertyLabel(def, key)` is `def.properties[key].displayName` (looked up as written, bare and `note.`-prefixed) else the key without `note.`. **Group by Folder** (YAZ-2541): `RunOptions.page = { folder, folders, vault }` — the folder whose page the views are on (as the index names it), the vault's folder records and the vault's name; `ViewsPane` always gives it, and without it `file.folder` groups by the whole path as before. Given, a `file.folder` LEVEL groups by the folder one step below the page that holds the note (`folderAt`), deeper notes rolled up into it; a SECOND Folder level is the next folder down (depth 2 — the merge rule puts a subfolder's own notes straight under it), and a Folder level under another property is depth 1. A note not under the page (a shortcut's home) keeps its own folder, the vault root included (`''` is a group there, not "No value"), and those homes are ordered AFTER the page's own folders whichever the direction. The group's `key` stays the folder's root-relative path — collapse state, the group "+" (`seed.folder`) and filters read it — while its `label` is the folder record's `title`, else its directory name, the root's the vault's name. `file.folder` in a filter or a formula is untouched: still the note's own folder. Budget: 1000 records × 3-clause filter + 2 formulas + sort + groupBy under 50 ms (`engine.perf.test.ts`, the `perf` project) — a SECOND grouping level fits inside it (two levels measured at 4.1 ms), which is why the scalar path stayed a single lookup rather than wrapping every value in a throwaway list.

Link resolution (`makeResolver(files, root?, opts?)`, also `Scope.resolve`): a target matches FIRST by a note's own `id` (YAZ-2293 D5 — `IndexRecord.id`; first, so a note merely NAMED like an id never captures it, and of two notes still sharing an id the first in path order answers), then by absolute path, then root-relative path (with or without `.md` / a leading slash), then TITLE (YAZ-2420 D17 — `IndexRecord.title`, what a hand-typed `[[Name]]` means first), then bare basename — duplicates of either resolve to the SHALLOWEST folder (Obsidian's shortest-path rule, GRO-2190), equal depth to the first in path order — a pathed target no file path answers as a path of TITLES (YAZ-2478, `ResolverOptions.folders`, the snapshot's folder records: `Sub/Page` is the note titled Page in the folder `Sub` names, each folder segment by its title and then by its directory name, `typedFolders`) — and finally, LAST, a frontmatter ALIAS (Links E2, GRO-2214): a real name always beats an alias, whatever their depths, and two notes claiming one alias follow the same shallowest-then-first-in-path-order rule. An alias is a bare NAME, so it never joins a folder path (`[[Costs/CAC]]` does not resolve through the alias `CAC`). Case-insensitive throughout, `[[…]]` / `|alias` / `#heading` stripped. `opts.aliases: false` builds a NAME-ONLY resolver and `opts.ids: false` one that resolves no ids (YAZ-2293 D5) — the rename engine's referencing-set probe passes both (see "Links": Rename), the one consumer that must NOT see aliases or ids; it gives no `folders` either, so a path of titles is not one of its links. `resolverFor(records, root?, opts?)` is the memoized accessor (WeakMap keyed on the records array identity, then on the `folders` array's, then per root, alias mode and id mode): everything reading the SAME snapshot gets ONE resolver — a refetched index is a new array and gets a fresh one; the per-resolver target cache is unchanged. Precisely (GRO-2197 audit — the earlier wording overclaimed; amended by YAZ-819/844): the resolver is one IMPLEMENTATION everywhere and the wikilink surfaces genuinely do share one INSTANCE, because `WikilinkIndexBridge` is the window's single index feed for decorations, the picker and backlinks alike (rules 23/24/25). The three surfaces that used to duplicate that feed — `BaseHost`, `BaseEmbed` and `BaseCodeBlock`, each with its own `useIndex` and therefore its own snapshot array and memo entry — died with the format (YAZ-844), and the ONE surface that runs views today reads the window's `WikilinkResolveSource` instead, so `useIndex` has exactly ONE caller left (`WikilinkIndexBridge`) and one window makes one fetch: the duplicated FETCHES are closed. The duplicated INSTANCE is not, and the reason is worth knowing — the wikilink feed built `resolverFor(records, root)` while the views built `resolverFor(records)` with no root, and the memo is keyed per records identity THEN per root and alias mode — so the same snapshot handed out two resolvers, and a link target written as an absolute `<root>/…` path resolved for the wikilink surfaces but not inside the views. CLOSED in YAZ-846: the root is threaded into every views resolver (`ViewsPane`'s injected `RunOptions.resolve` and the link pickers' in `TableView` / `CardsView` / `ListView`), so one window on one snapshot now holds ONE note resolver instance and one set of semantics. Since YAZ-2290 D10 the wikilink feed's own `ResolveLink` is `linkResolver` (`links/folderLinks.ts`), which wraps that same memoized instance and falls back to a FOLDER when no note answers; the engine's resolver answers notes only. A null root — a mount that does not know one — still degrades to name-and-relative-path resolution, which is the same resolver the engine would have built for itself. It backs the global `file(path)`, `link.asFile()`, and tightens `file.hasLink()`, `link.linksTo()` and link `==` to compare resolved paths — an unresolved side (e.g. `levels.png`, not a note) falls back to the textual `linkTargetsMatch`. Without a resolver `file()` / `asFile()` stay ErrorValues and matching is textual. List helpers `sum() mean() median() min() max()` ignore non-numbers and give null on none.

`client/src/views/summaries.ts` — `summarize(kind, values, custom?)`: built-ins (`BUILTIN_SUMMARIES`, case-insensitive) `Count` / `Empty` / `Filled` / `Unique` on any type (`isEmpty`, `equals`), `Average` / `Max` / `Median` / `Min` / `Range` / `Stddev` (population) / `Sum` on numbers (non-numbers ignored, none → null), `Earliest` / `Latest` / `Range` (→ duration) on dates, `Checked` / `Unchecked` on booleans; a non-built-in kind is looked up in `custom` (= `def.summaries`) and evaluated with `values` bound to the column (`Scope.extra`), else `ErrorValue('unknown summary kind: …')`. `runView` fills `result.summaries[prop]` over the post-limit rows and `group.summaries[prop]` per group from `view.summaries { prop: kind }`.

### View chrome (GRO-2135)

`client/src/views/ViewsPane.tsx` props: `{ parsed, onChange, root, folderPath, records, properties?, onOpenFile, folder }` — `root` keys view state persisted outside the page AND roots the resolver (see "Engine"), `folderPath` is the FOLDER's directory path (it keys the collapsed-group store, YAZ-2290), `records` is the folder's ROWS — the notes in it and its shortcuts — `properties` is the vault-wide declarations for 5B editor inference (typing rung 2, wired in YAZ-846; a `properties.error` renders its own passive "Could not load the vault's property declarations" line, never a fetch failure), and `folder` is the `FolderHost` bundle `FolderView` passes (settings + whole-vault snapshot, records and folders + create + the column doors + background/right-panel open, 🔒 D3 of YAZ-818 — see "Folders are the pages"), REQUIRED since YAZ-846. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846, the amputation): `readOnly` (the retired embed chrome, which also gated cell editing, column resize, summary picking and group drag in `TableView` / `BoardView` / `CardsView` / `ListView` — gone from all four), `initialView` (which picked the starting tab for `![[X.base#View]]`), `types` (typing rung 3, `.obsidian/types.json` — nothing on this surface ever fed it, and ⚡ YAZ-815 then deleted the rung, the parameter and the read), `indexStatus` / `indexError` (the host's snapshot is always in hand) and the plain 5D `createFromSeed` path all had NO production caller once YAZ-844 retired `.base`, and were deleted rather than carried. `newNote.ts`'s `targetFolder` went with that last one. **Write-through**: every config change is `onChange(updateViews(parsed, mutate))` (re-parsed via `parseViews(serializeViews(…))` so sequential edits inside collections a previous edit created keep working — `doc.setIn` stores plain JS values), exactly one `onChange` per interaction, no Save button; the host turns it into exactly ONE `folder_settings` write through the one door. `onOpenFile` is threaded App (`openFile`) → `Editor` → `FolderView` → `ViewsPane`, and the host feeds `records` (the folder's rows) plus the whole-vault snapshot off the window's `WikilinkResolveSource` rather than a `useIndex` of its own. Component state is transient view state only — the active tab, the search text, the collapsed group keys (mirrored into the main-owned `baseGroups` store, see 4C), the pending optimistic moves (5C) and the last move/create error — everything else lives in the file. The body switches on `view.type`: `table` → `TableView`, `board` (OUR schema extension, D1 — not Obsidian's, and its keys round-trip untouched) → `BoardView`, `cards` → `CardsView`, `list` → `ListView`, `outline` → `OutlineView` (the folder's free-form document, always the FIRST outline view's), anything else (unknown types) → the 4A placeholder row list. One narrowing rides here: while an outline is showing the toolbar offers no Properties, Filter, Preview or Search (`documentView`) — see "Folders are the pages": The outline is a document.

Toolbar (`view/Toolbar.tsx`, `.view-toolbar`): left `view/ViewTabs.tsx` — one `role="tab"` per view (type glyph + name, active tab accent underline). The tabs are EDITABLE (🔒 D0, YAZ-1471, re-ruling 🔒 rule 4 of YAZ-819 and the "view CRUD is not this block's gesture" clause of 🔒 D3 of YAZ-818 — the whole editable half was behind a `readOnly` prop `ViewsPane` set UNCONDITIONALLY from YAZ-846 until YAZ-1471, kept whole and pinned but unreachable; the prop is gone from `ViewTabsProps` and the "…" button never returned, right-click being the one menu gesture). EVERY tab gesture is ONE `ViewsPane.update` — the same `onChange(parseViews(serializeViews(updateViews(…))))` door sort and columns use — and therefore ONE `folder_settings` write; which view is ACTIVE stays session state and switching still writes nothing. **Reorder** (D1): HTML5 drag, the insertion-slot idiom spelled ONCE for both horizontal strips in `client/src/lib/dragSlot.ts` — `insertionSlot(e, i)` (the target's own horizontal midpoint: before it, or after it) and `dropIndex(from, slot)` (past the grab point the slot shifts one left) — shared with the window's tab bar so the two strips cannot drift apart; the strip's empty tail is the END slot (the `e.target !== e.currentTarget` guard), a drop back on the item's own slot writes nothing, the ACTIVE view FOLLOWS its tab (the same move replayed over the indices, never a remembered name), and the in-flight indicators are `view-tab--dragging` on the grabbed tab plus `view-tab--insert-before` / `view-tab--insert-after` on the slot's neighbour. The drag payload is the PRIVATE mime `application/x-yaseen-view-tab` (TabBar's `WORKSPACE_PAGE_MIME` idiom) and never `text/plain`, which a tab mis-dropped on an editor would have ProseMirror insert as text; `dataTransfer` is guarded throughout for jsdom. **The view menu** (D2): right-click on any tab opens the shared `ContextMenuSurface` — Rename (the tab becomes an inline `TextField`, `normalize` committing only a non-empty name NO OTHER view answers to, so a duplicate or an empty draft snaps back and writes nothing, and a tab in rename is not draggable) · Duplicate (`structuredClone` of the whole config inserted right after, named by `freeName` off `<name> copy`, and selected — greyed for an `outline` view, because a folder owns ONE document) · Delete (greyed on the last view — `views` can never be empty — and never immediate: `view/ConfirmDeleteView.tsx` is the in-app sheet, on `ConfirmSheet` like its siblings, with the confirm `--danger` because a view's configuration has no way back, focus starting on Cancel, Esc and click-away cancelling; its copy is the pure `deleteViewMessage(view)`, tested apart). The open menu and sheet hold INDICES, so they are guarded against a list shrunk under them by another window or a hand edit (YAZ-1488): `menuView` / `confirmView` are derived in render and a stale index renders nothing rather than throwing through a block with no error boundary above it. **"+"** (D5): the button at the end of the strip opens an anchored `Popover` type picker over `VIEW_TYPES` (`table` · `board` · `cards` · `list` · `outline`, each labelled by `viewTypeLabel` — the type capitalised; `outline` is offered only while the folder has NONE, for the same one-document reason) — picking a type APPENDS a view named `freeName(<label>)` and mounts it in rename, so the two writes are the append and (only if you type) the name. **Overflow** (D6): `.view-tabs` IS the scroller and wears the shared `.scroll-strip` utility (`app.css`) — no scrollbar, a scroll-driven edge fade — with the active tab `scrollIntoView`n on every activation and labels ellipsized at 180px; the "+" sits OUTSIDE the scroller in `.view-tabs-wrap`, always visible and never masked. Right (YAZ-1397): **Sort** (badge = sort count + grouping-level count), **Properties**, **Filter** (badge = `countRules(view.filters)`, button and badge red for filter errors), the **groups toggle** when grouped, **Preview on hover** for Table/Board, **New**, **Search** (inline input; filters rendered row values case-insensitively without writing), then `N items` or `shown / total items`. While the outline — a document, not rows — is showing, Properties, Filter, Preview and Search are not offered (`documentView`). The groups toggle (YAZ-744) is ONE button with no badge: any group expanded → "Collapse all groups", all collapsed → "Expand all groups"; a click replaces the whole collapsed set for the view — every group the view HAS, the ones an active search hides included — through the same `baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]` entry the per-group chevrons write, never the folder's settings file. Popovers (`view/Popover.tsx`, `.view-popover`) anchor under their trigger, close on Esc / click-away, move focus in on open. `view/TextField.tsx` commits on Enter/blur only (one change per edit), Esc reverts.

The Filter menu (deleted in YAZ-846, RESTORED in YAZ-1218 / YAZ-1226-1229). `view/FilterMenu.tsx` is the builder behind the **Filter** toolbar button: conjunction All / Any / None → `and` / `or` / `not`, property · operator · value rule rows, an Advanced raw-expression input, and the engine's `*.filters` `EngineError`s listed at the top of the popover (`.view-menu__errors`) with the button turned red — the same errors still ride the muted footer note. Its rule ↔ expression model is the pure `view/filterRows.ts` (`ruleToExpr` / `exprToRule` / `inferType` / `operatorsFor` / `toGroup` / `fromGroup` / `countRules`), back beside `view/keys.ts`'s `canonicalKey` (`status` → `note.status`; `file.x` / `formula.x` / `note.x` untouched; non-identifier names as `note["…"]`). What did NOT come back, deliberately (D1; 🔒 Q3, "Folders are the pages": What a folder shows): the This view / All views scope segment — the menu edits `view.filters` ONLY, because a folder's set IS what lives in it and `def.filters` has no persistence door on this surface (a write would round-trip in memory and vanish on the next index rebuild). `inferType` now defers to the typing ladder (D4): the column's assigned kind, else its dominant kind, else the value walk — so the operator list always agrees with the cell editor beside it, `link` / `multi-link` columns included. Five operators take a value CHECKLIST instead of a typed box (🔒 D5, YAZ-1467): single-value columns (text, link, select, `file.folder`) offer **is any of** / **is none of**, written `["a", "b"].contains(<prop>)` and its `!` form — the list literal, never the string `containsAny`, because only the list method compares by equality; list columns (list, tags, multi-select, multi-link, `file.tags`) offer **has any of** / **has none of** / **has all of**, written `<prop>.containsAny("a", "b")`, its `!` form, and `<prop>.containsAll("a", "b")`. `file.links` stays out (link-vs-string equality unverified) and the engine needed no change. A `Rule`'s `value` is therefore `string | string[]`, and moving to or from one of the five resets it to `[]` or `''`. A stored node the builder cannot represent renders read-only as `.view-rule__code` and is never destroyed.

Sort menu (`view/SortMenu.tsx`): `view.sort` rows (searchable property chooser, ASC/DESC toggle, drag grip, remove — an empty list deletes the key) + "Add sort" (`file.name ASC`); beneath, **Group by** edits BOTH levels (YAZ-745): the first chooser is the outer level, a **Then group by** row beneath it (offered only once an outer is set, its options omitting the outer's own key) sets the inner — one level keeps today's single-object form, two write the ordered list, clearing the inner collapses back to the object, "None" on the outer clears both, and an outer moved onto the inner's property drops the inner (the same property twice is not a grouping — except **Folder**, YAZ-2541: `file.folder` joins THIS menu's key list only, never `allPropertyKeys`, so it is a sort and a grouping but not a column, and it may be picked at both levels, the second being the next folder down); rendering of groups is 4C — `GroupHeader` shows the engine's `Group.label`, with the full name as a tooltip when the key is text. Properties menu (`view/PropertiesMenu.tsx`): checklist over the shown keys (as written, so `view.order` round-trips) plus every other known key (`view/properties.ts` `allPropertyKeys`: shown + `file.name` + note keys + the folder's DECLARED columns — YAZ-895, so a declared column is offerable before any note carries a value — + formulas, deduped canonically; never `note.id` or another of the note's own keys — the rows hold a folder's values, D19, so such a column could hold nothing); toggling/reordering writes `view.order` (shown keys only, in order; Table and Board views may hide `file.name`, while Cards and List keep their existing filename/title behavior); reordering is the 6-dot grip at the head of every SHOWN row — `file.name` included, hidden rows gripless — dragged with TabBar's midpoint insertion rule or nudged one step via ArrowUp/ArrowDown on the focused grip, always exactly one `writeOrder` per move so `frozenColumns` keeps following positionally (YAZ-1207; the ↑↓ arrow buttons are gone); for `type: board` views every shown note row additionally carries four card-style toggles (YAZ-1206/YAZ-1217) — bold, underline, hide label, and ⤴ join-to-the-row-above — while the shown `file.name` row carries ONLY ⤴ (the title takes part in layout, never text styling); each click is ONE write to `view.cardStyle[<canonical key>]`, with false flags, emptied entries and an emptied map all deleting themselves; the pencil sets `def.properties[key].displayName` (looked up as written / bare / `note.`-prefixed, written bare for note keys; empty deletes the displayName and an emptied entry/map). A Table-only **Frozen columns** select writes optional `view.frozenColumns`: None deletes it; every other option names one visible column and freezes the contiguous leading prefix through it. The number is positional, never a remembered key set — reorder changes which columns occupy the prefix, restore/new columns append outside it, and hiding clamps and persists it. `frozenColumnCount` is the one tolerant reader: absent, malformed, negative or non-finite → zero; fractions truncate; the effective count never exceeds visible columns. A Board-only **Board → Column width** row is the ONE Board-wide numeric editor, with `px` visible beside it: 280 by default, `min=180`, step 1 and no arbitrary maximum. Only a CHANGED finite draft commits, on Enter or blur, rounded to the nearest integer and then lower-clamped to 180; Enter followed by blur still commits once. Normalized 280 deletes `cardSize`, while every other valid width persists as a NUMBER through ONE `folder_settings` write. Escape, empty or non-finite input, and an unchanged or normalized-unchanged value write nothing. The field displays existing numeric and legacy small/medium/large values through the unchanged `cardWidth` resolver, and open/focus/blur of an untouched value never rewrites it — including a legacy numeric width below 180. Columns are DECLARED here too (YAZ-896/897): every `note.*` row carries a kind select — `auto` (a disabled placeholder, never a choice) while undeclared, meaning the ladder's lower rungs decide — writing `folder_settings.columns[<bare>].kind` through `FolderHost.setColumns`, the declarations' one door beside `Mutate` (ONE settings write, `views` untouched; C1, LOCKED: note VALUES are never migrated or rewritten by a retype — the declaration alone moves, so retyping is always reversible); a declared link/multi-link column additionally shows its per-folder `target` inline (empty deletes the key), and a trailing **+ Add column** (name per `PROPERTY_NAME`, kind, target for link kinds only, duplicates and bad names refused inline with nothing written) declares the column AND appends `note.<name>` to the view's `order` in that SAME single settings write — `setColumns`' optional `views` argument exists precisely so this is never two. Declaring a column writes NOTHING into any note (🔒 E1, YAZ-2290 — "Folder settings: `.folder.md`": No empty keys in notes). For `type: list` views only, a trailing **List** section edits how those properties display — `markerStyle` (Bullet / Number / None select), `indentProperties` (checkbox) and `propertySeparator` (TextField, placeholder `, `) — one write per change, the default value DELETES the key (bullet / unchecked / empty-or-`, `), like SortMenu clearing `sort` / `groupBy`.

Searchable controls (YAZ-1395/1396): `ColumnSearch` matches trimmed, case-insensitive substrings across display labels, written keys and canonical keys. The Properties header stays visible while scrolling, reports match count and hides reorder grips during filtering (clear search to reorder). `ColumnPicker` reuses the search field for Sort, Group by and Then group by; its bounded list expands inside the existing menu, shows labels/keys and the selected option, and supports arrows/Enter, Escape, click-away and normal focus traversal. Typing, canceling or choosing the current canonical value does not write. Existing candidate sources remain: Properties includes declared folder columns; Sort keeps its existing shown/record/formula discovery and missing-current-key fallback. The Filter menu's Property is the same `ColumnPicker` (YAZ-1466), on top-level and nested-group rows alike, built from the shared `view/properties.ts` `propertyOptions` helper Sort also uses. The same component has a `multiple` mode (🔒 D6, YAZ-1467) — the Filter menu's value checklist (trigger labelled `Value`, like the typed box it replaces; `noun="options"` rewords the search box, its label and the no-match line — "Search options…", "No options found."): a click or Enter TOGGLES one value and writes once, the panel stays open until Escape or click-away, and a stored value no record carries still lists itself, ticked. `multiple` defaults off, so Sort's picker is untouched. Toolbar popovers constrain themselves to the viewport and clipping ancestors, resize with their content and allow internal option scrolling without dismissal.

Sort priority (YAZ-1396): drag the same six-dot grip used for columns, or press ArrowUp/ArrowDown while it is focused. The complete rule moves with its direction in one write; cancellation, same-position and boundary moves write nothing. Duplicate-property rules have distinct local UI identities that survive reparse after our edits and are never serialized. A changed external sort invalidates an in-flight drag. Single-rule grips are disabled. Grouping levels are not draggable. The compact sort grid uses a flexible property field, fixed direction width, equal control heights and a separate grouping section; only the new picker removes the native select width cap.

Bulk property visibility (YAZ-1384): Table and Board offer **Select all** and **Unselect all** above the Properties checklist when search is empty. During search, the labels become **Select results / Unselect results** and affect only matching canonical keys, preserving unmatched visibility and shown order. No matches disables both actions. Select all writes the menu’s canonical-deduplicated keys, retaining shown order and appending hidden properties, including declared columns and formulas. Unselect all writes explicit `order: []`, including hiding `file.name`; the existing `writeOrder` path removes frozen columns. Each effective click makes one active-view settings write; satisfied actions are disabled and dimmed. The menu remains available with no visible columns, and Board retains its blank card shells. Selecting all after clearing uses the default menu order and does not restore the previous custom order or frozen columns. Cards and List do not offer these actions.

Body (4A placeholder, view types with no real body yet): one row per result — `file.name` as a button calling `onOpenFile(record.path)`, then the other `order` values `render()`ed and ` · `-joined. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846, completed in YAZ-861): the two index states that used to replace the body whatever the view type — `.view-view__pending` "Loading the vault index…" and `.view-view__error` "Could not load the vault index: …" — went with `indexStatus` / `indexError`; the one host there is always has its snapshot in hand. The `__pending` class outlived them by one wave, holding a "no views — [Add view]" branch that could not be reached — `readViews` and `folderViewSet` BOTH fall back to `DEFAULT_VIEWS`, so `def.views` is never empty — and YAZ-861 deleted branch, rule and class together, on the FilterMenu's precedent (below): unreachable UI with a live code path behind it is worse than no UI. `.views-pane__error` (renamed from `.view-view__error` with the pane) survives and still carries the create / properties / settings-write failures. A folder with no notes renders an empty body, which is the truth. Tests: `view/keys.test.ts`, `view/Toolbar.test.tsx` (mounts ViewsPane as a folder's views over `TEST_RECORDS` + a views fixture, `testFolderHost()` supplying the required bundle; asserts YAML via `serializeViews` next to the DOM). The tabs are pinned there in four describes (YAZ-1471): **drag to reorder** — a drop on a tab's LEFT half inserts before it in ONE write, the strip's empty tail lands it LAST with the last tab marked `--insert-after`, a drop back on its own slot writes nothing, the ACTIVE view follows its tab both when passed and when dragged, a tab in rename is not draggable, and the payload is the PRIVATE view-tab mime (a `text/plain` name would paste into an editor); **the right-click menu** — opening writes nothing, Rename refuses a duplicate or empty name (the field snaps back, nothing written) and commits a free one in ONE update on Enter OR blur (once, never twice), Duplicate clones after and opens the copy and is disabled for an outline, Delete is disabled on the last view and ASKS first (Escape and click-away both cancel and write nothing), confirming is ONE update, and the selection hands over to the right neighbour (the left one for the last tab); **"+"** — the picker lists every type in menu order and opening writes nothing, Outline is offered only while the folder has none, picking appends in ONE update and mounts the tab in rename (Escape keeps the given name, typing over it is the SECOND update), and the name is the first FREE one off the type label; **overflow** — the ACTIVE tab is scrolled into view on mount and on every activation, and the tablist IS the scroller with the "+" outside it. `view/ConfirmDeleteView.test.ts` pins the sheet's pure copy apart from the sheet (`deleteViewMessage`: an outline names the document that goes with it, every other view its configuration). `FolderView.test.tsx` drives the same arc through the real host: a folder with no settings file shows Table then Board and opening writes NOTHING, its first change writes the whole block into `.folder.md` with the default columns stated, a toolbar change → ONE whole-key `folder_settings` write through the one door, switching a view writes NOTHING, the tabs edit, an open menu or sheet survives the views list shrinking under it (YAZ-1488), an own-write echo arriving after a newer optimistic edit does not take it back (YAZ-1241), and the default view is pinned end to end (a saved START opens there and opening writes nothing, a stale name falls back to the first view silently, `Page → Default view` is ONE whole-key write carrying the name, `First view` clears the key, a rename CARRIES the saved START and a delete CLEARS it in the SAME write, and an external edit that changes ONLY `defaultView` rebuilds the def — D4's proof that it rides in the stamp). `folderSettings.test.ts` pins 🔒 D3's retirement from the read side: a persisted list WITHOUT a board reads back without one (what the card says is what you get, YAZ-1471), a persisted list WITH a board — whatever its name — passes through untouched, and `DEFAULT_VIEWS` is two — Table, then Board, no Outline (🔒 D5a, YAZ-2290) — each read handing back its own copy.

Table (4B, GRO-2136, YAZ-742, YAZ-1151, `view/TableView.tsx`, `.view-table`): `type: table` renders a `<table>` over the post-search rows — sticky header (labels via `propertyLabel`), columns = `propertyKeys` in `order`, widths from `view.columnSize` (px per key, default 150; drag the header's right-edge handle, min 60, live preview, ONE write on mouseup), `view.rowHeight` presets short/medium/tall → 28/44/68px via `--view-table-row-h`. The folder's `.editor-host` remains the ONLY vertical scroller and `.view-table-wrap` remains the table's horizontal scroller. Because native sticky otherwise chooses that horizontal wrapper for both axes, `TableView` listens to the nearest vertical ancestor and translates the existing `<thead>` by `--view-table-header-y`: zero before the table reaches the viewport, the outer scroll delta while rows remain, and clamped at `table height − header height` so it exits with the table bottom. Scroll events coalesce into one animation frame; resize observation refreshes layout changes; unmount removes every listener, observer, pending frame and style. The first effective `view.frozenColumns` real cells in every header/body/summary row are CSS-sticky horizontally, with cumulative `left` offsets derived from those same live widths; resizing a leading column therefore moves every later frozen offset during preview without another state model. Header intersections sit above body cells, frozen cells have an opaque background, and the two axes compose because vertical motion belongs to `<thead>` while horizontal `left` offsets remain on its `<th>` cells; the header remains vertically sticky even when the frozen count is zero. Group headers and window spacers stay ordinary full-width `colSpan` rows — only real property cells freeze — so grouping, keyboard coordinates and vertical windowing keep one semantic table and their existing structure. Inside each group row, the complete compact controls cluster is horizontally sticky at the Table's left padding edge independently of `frozenColumns`; the spanning cell itself remains ordinary, never becomes a data/frozen cell, and is not vertically sticky. A TWO-LEVEL grouping (YAZ-745, YAZ-1100) is still ONE flat display list — an outer header, then its merge-rule `direct` rows, then one inner section per child, whose header cell carries `.view-table__group-cell--nested` — so windowing, `data-cell` coordinates and keyboard navigation are untouched, and collapse-all covers both levels in document order against the store's key-count cap. The inner indent is one 28px step applied to that cluster's sticky `left` (36px against the outer's 8px), NOT to cell padding, precisely because the cluster pins at `left` while the table scrolls sideways: padding would slide the inner label back onto the outer's offset at the right edge, which is the YAZ-1043-46 mechanism honoured once per level.
