Collapsing an outer folds its whole BRANCH, inner headers included, while the inner section's own collapse survives underneath it — its key being outer-scoped, see "Folders are the pages": Collapsed groups. The `file.name` cell (`.view-table__name`) is PLAIN TEXT, the note's title, and not a link (🔒 YAZ-2420 D26): a click SELECTS it, as on any cell, and opens nothing; a double-click edits the title in place — a `TextField` labelled "Edit title", Enter or leaving it commits through `FolderHost.retitle` to App's one rename door as a title edit (D19, "The page title + rename confirm": the same `file.retitle`, the same confirm; where the vault does not use IDs, a rename of the file to the name typed — "Two kinds of vault"), an emptied or unchanged field commits nothing and Esc cancels; ⌘-click opens the note in a background tab and ⌥-click in the right panel (`openByGesture`); there is no hover "Open" button; other cells by type — numbers right-aligned (`.view-table__cell--num`), booleans as checkboxes, lists and links/files as chips (`.view-table__chip`, `--link` — an id link's chip reads as its note's title, YAZ-2293 D8, see "Note ids": What a link reads as), ErrorValues as a red `#ERROR` chip with the message in `title`, everything else `render()` (editing: see "Cell editing" below). A secondary click on any non-editing data cell first focuses that exact cell, closes an open hover preview, then opens `PageContextMenu` for the row's exact absolute `record.path` (and its `record.title`, which is what a notice calls it), a Table's leading with **Open** — the current tab, the door a click on the Name cell no longer is (YAZ-2420 D26) — then Open in new tab → Copy path → Reveal in Finder; typed editors keep the native menu. With `view.preview: true` (YAZ-1244), resting on a data row opens that page's read-only body after the shared hover-intent delay — the preview is handed the window's link source, so an id link in it reads as a title (YAZ-2293); leaving uses the shared grace window, while drag start and secondary click close it immediately. Summary row pinned in `<tfoot>`: each cell is a click-to-pick kind (None + `BUILTIN_SUMMARIES` + `def.summaries` names) writing `view.summaries[columnKey]` (existing keys matched canonically; removing the last kind deletes the map), value shown via `summarize` over the shown rows. Arrow keys move focus between body cells (`data-cell="row:col"`), Enter on a name cell opens the note — ⌘⏎ a background tab, ⌥⏎ the right panel, the rule its ⌘- and ⌥-click follow (YAZ-1557, `lib/openGesture.ts`); a BOARD card is the same kind of cell (🔒 D2): every card is `tabIndex=0`, a plain click selects it (the table cell's focus ring), arrows walk ↑↓ through the column's cards in DOM order (nested sections included) and ←→ to the same row of the neighbour column, clamped — DOM-driven off `:scope > .view-board__col`, not a parallel index, because a column is a tree and the DOM already is its reading order — and the title, Enter, or a modifier opens; a title-less card keeps `aria-label={record.title}` and has no role; one click selects an editable property cell and a double-click opens its editor. Above 500 rows only a scroll-positioned slice of `<tr>`s is mounted between spacer rows (no library); rows render from one flat slice so 4C can interleave group header rows. Tests: `view/frozenColumns.test.ts`, `view/TableView.test.tsx`, `view/TableNestedGroups.test.tsx` (the two-level render), and the dedicated `desktop/e2e/freezeColumns.spec.ts` (kept out of YAZ-999's shared folder-column spec).

**The Table since YAZ-1513** (the column overhaul, YAZ-1537–1551; header drag YAZ-1548; polish YAZ-1549): the `#` GUTTER is the first, always-sticky column (`.view-table__gutter`, fixed width, the frozen offsets starting after it) — 1-based and RESTARTING at every group header, nested sections included, a DISPLAY POSITION and never an id: no `data-cell` and no tabIndex (outside the arrow-key grid), not a drag source or target, nothing to summarise. `ViewDef.rowNumbers?: boolean` decides it, ABSENT = shown, so a shown gutter leaves no key behind — Properties → Table → **Row numbers**, or the `#` header's own right-click, whose only item is "Hide row numbers". The NAME cell renders the page TITLE — `record.title` (YAZ-2420 D14) — in every skin through ONE shared renderer (`GroupHeader.tsx` `rowTitle`: the table's name cell, the board's card title, the card's title, the list's primary), and `file.name`'s VALUE is the title too (D18), so the column sorts and filters by what it shows; its label is `defaultLabel` = **Name**. The file name is its own column, `file.basename`, labelled **File name**: a view can add it, and it is not shown by default. A HEADER RIGHT-CLICK (`view/TableHeaderMenu.tsx`, on the row menu's own surface) offers **Rename column…** (an inline field holding the stored display name with the default label as its placeholder — Enter commits through `setDisplayName`, an emptied field goes back to the default, Esc cancels), **Hide column** (a table may hide `file.name`, YAZ-1007), **Add column to the right…** (the SAME "+ Add column" form, the new key landing after this one) and **Delete column…** (disabled with a tooltip for the undeletable tiers, otherwise the confirm sheet — "Folder settings: `.folder.md`": Delete column). HEADER DRAG (YAZ-1548) reorders a property header by dropping it on another header's left or right half — the Properties list's slot rule — and writes ONE order; the gutter is neither source nor target. `view/columnOrder.ts` `withOrder(view, order)` is THE one order writer, carrying the frozen clamp (YAZ-1007: the prefix is a count over the shown columns, so a shorter order clamps it and an empty order deletes the key): the Properties checklist, reorder and bulk buttons, "+ Add column", the header menu's Hide and Add-right, the header drag and a delete's prune all spell an order through it, so no view can hold an order its `frozenColumns` disagrees with. The PROPERTIES panel is TWO-LEVEL: the list row is grip · checkbox · type icon · label, the key shown only when two labels collide; opening a row is the DETAIL — an editable title (the label, the same `setDisplayName`), the key, the type, options (select / multi-select), the relation, card styling (Board only), "Back to columns", Hide in this view and Delete column…. Every declaration edit reaches disk through the host's doors ("Folder settings: `.folder.md`": The write path), and `FolderView` keeps an optimistic AHEAD copy of `columns` — exposed as `FolderHost.settings.columns`, exactly as `parsed` runs ahead of the index for the views — so a declaration written and not yet echoed back is what the next gesture (a delete's prune, a second add) reads.

Board (4D, GRO-2138, YAZ-1177, `view/BoardView.tsx`, `.view-board`): `type: board` is OUR schema extension (D1) — the view keys are `type: board`, `groupBy` (required for columns), `order` (the card fields) and `cardSize`. The tolerant reader remains the shared `view/cardWidth.ts`: a finite positive number is px, legacy small/medium/large presets are 220/280/340, and anything else is 280. The Properties editor above is intentionally Board-only and writes numeric `cardSize`; it does not alter Cards' `cardSize`, Table's per-column widths, or the global content-width setting. Every outer column gets that one width through `--view-board-col-w`, so columns stay equal and nested child sections inherit their outer column's geometry; there is deliberately no per-column resize drag or preset picker. Every key round-trips byte-for-byte through the parser (`viewSchema.test.ts` board fixture) — a property that mattered for Obsidian interop while `.base` files existed and now simply means a hand-written board survives an edit to any other view. With ONE group level, columns remain the engine's post-search groups on one horizontally scrolling row (a column with no shown cards disappears, "No value" last). With TWO levels, there is still exactly ONE column per outer group; the outer's merge-rule `direct` cards render first, then its child groups stack vertically inside that column. Every outer and child header uses the shared `GroupHeader` content (chevron, typed value, count, summaries over that level's SHOWN cards). Outer collapse folds the whole branch; child collapse hides only that child's cards and persists under the outer-scoped `nestedGroupKeyOf` ("Folders are the pages": Collapsed groups). Cards: when `file.name` is present in `order`, it renders as a `.view-board__title` button → `onOpenFile`; when omitted, the title/open button is omitted and an otherwise-empty order leaves blank draggable card shells; the other `order` properties render as small label/value rows (`propertyLabel` + the table's typed `cellContent` — shared from `GroupHeader.tsx`; a missing value keeps the label with an empty value). A secondary click anywhere on a rendered `.view-board__card` closes an open hover preview, then opens the shared `PageContextMenu` actions in the fixed order Open in new tab → Copy path → Reveal in Finder (a board card, a card and a list row keep their click-to-open title and are not renamed from, YAZ-2420 D28, so only a Table's menu leads with Open), always targeting that rendered row's exact absolute `record.path` — including hidden titles, direct/inner nested cards, and repeated fanned-out cards — while headers, add controls, placeholders, and the no-group hint retain the native menu; background open leaves the folder's tab active, and failed clipboard/reveal actions report through the passive notice. With `view.preview: true` (YAZ-1244), resting on any rendered card opens that page's read-only body after the shared hover-intent delay — handed the window's link source like the table's (YAZ-2293); leaving uses the shared grace window, while drag start and secondary click close it immediately. Per-property `view.cardStyle` (YAZ-1206, reshaped by YAZ-1217's JOIN model) lays those rows out: a card is `.view-board__line` rows following `view.order`, where each key starts a new line unless its `join: true` continues the line being built — appended right after a muted `.view-board__dash` element (never a schema flag); consecutive joins chain onto one line, join on the first rendered key is a no-op, and the title is UN-PINNED — `file.name` renders at its order position, can be joined onto and can itself join, while ignoring `bold`/`underline`/`hideLabel` (those class the note rows; `hideLabel` drops the label span). Because the flags ride the shared `cardList`, cards inside subgroup sections inherit them unchanged. A writable group header's `+` creates in that exact scope; a nested child seeds BOTH raw note properties, while `file.*` and `formula.*` levels remain read-only. Without `groupBy` the body is a centered `.view-board__hint` whose "Group by…" button writes the first non-file property as `view.groupBy { property, direction: ASC }` through the file (one write; the Sort menu edits it after). Cards drag at their visible level — direct cards between outer columns, child cards between child sections, including across outer columns. An inner target is never a descendant of the outer target, so one drop cannot bubble into a second move. A cross-outer child move updates both raw properties through ONE `writeProperties` whole-file transaction; the ordinary one-key path still uses `writeProperty`. Out of scope: a third grouping level, swimlanes and board card images. Tests: `view/BoardView.test.tsx`, `view/NestedGroupActions.test.tsx` and `writeProperty.test.ts`; card styles and the grip additionally in `view/Toolbar.test.tsx` and `desktop/e2e/propertiesReorder.spec.ts`.

Cards (4E, GRO-2139, `view/CardsView.tsx`, `.view-cards`): `type: cards` (Obsidian's schema; view keys `image`, `cardSize`, `imageFit`, `imageAspectRatio`) renders a responsive grid — `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(var(--view-card-w), 1fr))`, width from the shared `cardWidth` (Obsidian's numeric px, or the legacy small/medium/large presets). Each card: an optional cover, then `file.name` as a `.view-card__title` button → `onOpenFile`, then the view's other `order` properties as label/value rows (`propertyLabel` + the shared typed `cellContent`). The view's `image` names a per-note property; the note's VALUE decides the cover — `#rgb`/`#rrggbb` → a colour block, `http(s)://` → the URL straight into `src`, a wikilink `[[pic.png]]` or plain path → a local vault asset through `api.readAsset` (bridge table above) turned into a `data:` URL, cached per root+ref so a grid never fetches one cover twice. While loading, on any failure, or with no/an unresolvable value the cover is the neutral `.view-card__cover--empty` placeholder (never a broken image); only note properties resolve (`file.`/`formula.` image keys → placeholder); a view without `image` renders no cover element at all. `imageFit` cover|contain and `imageAspectRatio` (number, default 1:1) land as `--view-card-fit` / `--view-card-ratio` on the grid. Grouped results render 4C sections (the shared `GroupHeader` over each group's own grid) with the SAME persisted collapse state as the table/board (`baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]`, never the folder's settings file); search narrows cards and drops empty groups. A lightbox is out of scope; property editing: see "Cell editing". Tests: `view/CardsView.test.tsx`, `desktop/src/main/fs/assets.test.ts`.

List (4F, GRO-2140, `view/ListView.tsx`, `.view-list`): `type: list` (Obsidian's schema; view keys `markerStyle`, `indentProperties`, `propertySeparator`) renders one item per record. The FIRST property in `order` is the primary line (Obsidian: the primary list item is whatever sits on top of the Properties menu): `file.name` — the default when `order` is empty or absent — renders as a `.view-list__title` button → `onOpenFile`; any other first property renders its typed value (`cellContent`) as `.view-list__primary` and file.name is NOT implicitly added. The remaining `order` properties render inline after the primary — `render()`ed, empty values skipped, joined by `propertySeparator` (default `, `) — or, with `indentProperties: true`, as indented label/value rows beneath it (`propertyLabel` + the shared typed `cellContent`). `markerStyle` bullet | number | none draws the item marker (default bullet; number = the ordinal within its list, restarting per group; an unknown value falls back to bullet). Grouped results render 4C sections (the shared `GroupHeader` over each group's own list) with the SAME persisted collapse state as the table/board/cards (`baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]`, never the folder's settings file); search narrows items and drops empty groups. The three keys are edited in the Properties menu's List section (above). Property editing: see "Cell editing". Tests: `view/ListView.test.tsx`.

### Cell editing (5B — GRO-2141 write path, GRO-2142 editors)

`transformFile(path, transform)` (`client/src/views/writeProperty.ts`) is the shared application-level whole-file write boundary: read the current bytes, apply a pure transform, write with `expectedMtime`; on `CONFLICT` re-read and recompute once, then surface a second conflict. A no-op never touches disk. `writeProperties(path, writes)` is its ordered multi-key specialization, applying every `setFrontmatterProperty` transform before the single guarded disk write; `writeProperty(path, key, value)` delegates to it for the ordinary one-key case. This matters when one gesture changes coupled keys — YAZ-1177's cross-outer child drag — because parallel same-file writes can each succeed while the last one silently erases the other. Broken frontmatter (`FrontmatterWriteError`) propagates and nothing is written. `writeFolderValues(path, folderId, writes, tidy)` is the same boundary over ONE folder's block of `in` (D19) — every value a folder's views write, and the panel's folder rows, where the vault uses IDs; where it does not the views write through `writeProperties` itself (YAZ-2523 V6, "Two kinds of vault": A folder's page); `tidy` is D20's `dropStaleFolderValues` for that note, applied in the same write. The properties panel's TYPED ROWS (⚡ YAZ-884) write the note's own fields through `transformFile` with `writeProperty`'s one-key transform and the same `tidy`, one key per row edit / add / delete, then apply that transform to the panel's own copy so its raw fallback cannot fall behind. Its RAW mode (⚡ YAZ-883, "The properties panel") keeps its purpose-built `replaceFrontmatter` flow. ONE file is created by a write (🔒 D1, YAZ-2290): `readForWrite`, the read every `transformFile` starts with, creates a folder's `.folder.md` when it is not there yet ("Folder settings: `.folder.md`"); for every other path a missing file is still `NOT_FOUND`.

Editor inference (`client/src/views/editorType.ts`, locked precedence — `columnTyping` computes the per-column halves once per render, `cellEditor` adds the per-note value on top). The LADDER, top rung first: (1) the FOLDER's own `folder_settings.columns` declaration (its `.folder.md`, or the default columns while it has none), view-scoped — WIRED in YAZ-819 (`columnTyping(key, records, properties, settings)` reading `settings.columns[<bare>]`, threaded `ViewsPane` → `TableView` / `CardsView` / `ListView`), see "Folder settings: `.folder.md`": Typing is per folder; (2) a vault-wide declaration in `.yaseendocs/properties.json` (`PropertiesResponse`, via `useProperties` — see "Properties"), which also carries the link/multi-link `target` that narrows the picker — WIRED in YAZ-846, App-owned (ONE `useProperties(root)` per window) and threaded App → `Editor` → `FolderView` → `ViewsPane`; a corrupt `properties.json` arrives as a `properties.error`, renders its own passive line and the column simply falls through; (3) the note's own YAML value; (4) the dominant value type across the view's records for a note without the key; (5) text, the final fallback. `file.*` and `formula.*` never get an editor (read-only). TOMBSTONE (⚡ YAZ-815, ruled by Yasin): between (2) and (3) sat an explicit `.obsidian/types.json` assignment, delivered as `IndexResponse.types` — an imported artifact ranking BELOW the vault's own schema. It was UNWIRED from the day rung 2 landed, because its data rode `IndexResponse`, which the one surface that mounts these editors deliberately never fetches (🔒 D2 — it reads the window's link snapshot instead), so `columnTyping`'s `types` parameter was passed `undefined` by every caller. A rung with no feed is not a rung: the rung, the parameter, the `IndexResponse` field and the per-call read of another app's file are all deleted. Kinds: text / number / checkbox / date / list / link / multi-link. TOMBSTONE: the type-scoped rung that used to sit above all of these — a `page_type`-keyed per-type schema — died with the type system in YAZ-836.

`view/EditableCell.tsx` is one editable property cell, shared by table cells and card/list property rows: its display (typed like a read-only cell) opens the editor directly in cards/lists, while `TableView` selects the editable `<td>` on a single click and delegates a double-click — including on an empty cell — or Enter on its focused cell to that same `[data-edit]` control. In table edit mode the real `<td>` is the cell-sized surface and sole geometry owner: the shared editor fills its normal containing block with no measured/copied geometry, while cards and lists keep their compact shared-editor layout unchanged. A table checkbox follows the same deliberate gesture and toggles exactly once. Clicks inside an open editor stay with that editor; nested activation is never replayed. Enter/blur commit through the host's `onCommit` — the cell has no writer of its own: a folder's views pass `FolderHost.writeValues` (D19), the properties panel its own (⚡ YAZ-884, "The properties panel") — and Esc cancels; the link editor completes `[[…]]` over the index's titles (YAZ-2420 D17) through the shared matcher (`links/completion.ts` — one matcher with the editor's `[[` picker, rule 24; GRO-2191), each suggestion READING as the title and WRITING the note's id — `[[<id>]]` — when it has one, `[[title]]` when it has none (`basenameCandidates`, YAZ-2293; the multi-link chips editor completes the same way). A stored id link RESTS as its note's title — in the cell, and on the chips editor's chips — while the editor's text and every commit stay the stored form, so an unchanged Enter writes nothing and an edited list writes the stored ids back (YAZ-2293 D8). The `list` editor remains free-form chips: Enter adds the pending text as one item, a comma is literal text, and the committed value is a YAML array. Commits are optimistic: the committed value renders immediately and stays until the index refetch delivers it; a failed write reverts the cell and shows an inline error. Checkboxes are live and commit on every toggle, no edit mode. An untouched chips editor (no chip added/removed, no pending text) never commits — its seed stringifies the list, so a no-op commit would rewrite `[1, 2]` as `['1', '2']` (7B, GRO-2148). The note's own open editor window absorbs the resulting frontmatter-only `change` silently (GRO-2186, see the autosave note under "Bridge API").

### Group drag (5C — GRO-2143)

`view/groupDrag.ts`, shared by the board's columns and the grouped table's sections. `dragKey(view)` is the bare frontmatter key a drop writes — null (drag disabled) unless `groupBy` is a note property. (It was also off under the views' `readOnly`, the read-only embed chrome's prop; that lost its last caller with the `.base` format in YAZ-844 and was deleted in YAZ-846 — see the View chrome tombstone.) `useGroupDrag(key, onMove)` owns the transient HTML5 drag state: `source(path, gk)` spreads on each card/row, `target(group)` on each column/section (rows included — events bubble), the hovered target highlights, a drop on the source group is a no-op, and Esc cancels (real drags fire `dragend`; a document keydown fallback covers jsdom). A drop on ANOTHER group fires `onMove(path, value)` with the target's raw YAML value read off that group's FIRST row — so the written type matches what the group's notes already carry — or `undefined` for "No value", which DELETES the key. **Fanned-out groups SWAP instead** (YAZ-671 D3): because the row sits in several groups, overwriting with a neighbour's whole list would destroy its other values, so `onMove` carries a third `GroupSwap { remove, add }` argument — `remove` is the source group's value (`drag.key`, which is why the drag state carries the group VALUE and not just its `groupKeyOf` string) and `add` is the target's; either is null at "No value". ViewsPane edits the page's list rather than replacing it: elements are matched with the engine's own `equals` over `fromYaml` and NO resolver — the exact comparison that decided the grouping — so a swap can only remove the element that put the row in that group, and every other value survives. Dropping a fanned-out row on "No value" removes just that element and never deletes the key. `dragKey` is now `groupByKey` (one name; the group "+" needs the same key whether or not a drag is in flight). ViewsPane owns the optimistic side: `PendingMove { key, value, prevRaw }` per path, patched into the records BEFORE the engine runs (`applyMoves`) so the card lands in its target group with sort/values/summaries consistent, committed through `FolderHost.writeValues` (D19). Clearing follows 5B's discipline — an entry clears only when the index refetch moves that key's raw off `prevRaw` (our write landing, or a concurrent writer winning), never before. A failed write drops the move (the card snaps back) and flags the card/row inline (`moveError`). Tests: `view/GroupDrag.test.tsx`.

### New note (5D — GRO-2144)

`newNote.ts`, behind the toolbar's **New** button and each group header's "+". `deriveSeed(def, view)` is pure over the filter ASTs (def + view): equality leaves `note.x == <literal>` seed `x` with the literal's YAML type and `file.hasTag("t")` adds to the seed's own `tags` (the note's tags, never a folder's value) — only **and-reachable** leaves count (`or`/`not` subtrees are skipped entirely; seeding those would not, or would anti-, satisfy the view), and non-equality rules are ignored by design (locked kickoff decision). A group "+" additionally seeds the group's property with the raw value of that group's first row ("No value" seeds nothing). Under FAN-OUT (YAZ-671 D4) it seeds `[render(group.key)]` instead — that group's own element as a one-item list, because the first row's raw value is a neighbour's WHOLE list there; `render()` returns a link to its `[[…]]` form, the same one the picker inserts. "No value" still seeds nothing. PLACEMENT is the folder's, always (🔒 D4/E1/E3, YAZ-2290): `FolderHost.create` (`FolderView`) births the note IN the folder being viewed, from the folder's `.template.md` and the seed and nothing else — no column is stamped empty — so a group "+" still seeds its group ("Folders are the pages": Births). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846): the plain path — `targetFolder` (the `inFolder` seed under the root, else the page's own folder, else the root) plus `ViewsPane`'s `createFromSeed` — is deleted, function and all, and the folder half of the seed followed it (YAZ-2318): `deriveSeed` no longer reads `file.inFolder`, and `NewNoteSeed` is `{ properties, tags? }`: the values go into the opened folder's block, the tags to the note's top level ("Folder settings": Values). The note's TITLE is `Untitled`, every time (YAZ-2420 D20: the ids keep the files apart, so nothing is numbered and no create is retried) — or the typed one, the board's inline add (YAZ-943), free text. `createNote(dir, title, ids, seed)` (`scaffold.ts`) is ONE atomic content-at-create call — `createFile({ path, content, id })`, the path being the name built from the title and that id (`noteFileName`), the content being what the seed makes of the template's frontmatter with `title` set last, plus the template's body — no create-then-write second step and no race; an empty seed in a folder with no template still creates the file, holding only its `title` and the `id` the create door writes (YAZ-2293, bridge table). Where the vault does not use IDs (YAZ-2523 V3 — "Two kinds of vault") the seeded values are the note's own top-level properties, the file is `<title>.md` with no `title` and no `id` written (no seed and no template: zero bytes), and an untyped one is `Untitled`, then `Untitled 2`, …, the create tried again on `ALREADY_EXISTS`. Success opens the note; failure shows `.views-pane__error` "Could not create note: …" and opens nothing. Tests: `newNote.test.ts`, `view/NewNote.test.tsx`.


## Document magnification (YAZ-1410)

Each Markdown `CrepeHost` owns a temporary percentage, default **100%**. The control is ONE
segmented pill immediately left of Sync — `− | 100% | +` (YAZ-1710, which retired YAZ-1430's
dropdown arrow: the percentage alone opens the dropdown). The chip on the pill's left is the page
settings cog (editor rule 34), which is outside the scroller and never zooms. `−` / `+` step to the
next preset in that direction, a custom value snapping to the nearest preset that way (117 → 125 or 100); the
end button is disabled at 50 / 400. A step applies at once through the same commit path as a
preset: it closes an open menu, discards its draft (valid or invalid) and does not move focus,
so repeated clicks keep stepping (`stepZoom` in `editor/DocumentZoom.tsx`). The KEYS take a finer
ladder above 200 (`stepZoomByKey`, D16): presets to 200, then 25 at a time to 400, a custom value
snapping to that grid in the step direction (210 → 225 up, 200 down) — 200 → 300 is one click in
the menu but too big a jump under a held key. A labeled Custom input above the presets accepts
whole numbers **50–400**, with an optional `%` suffix, and presets are **50, 75, 90, 100, 125,
150, 200, 300, 400** (D12). Enter or leaving the control applies a valid draft;
Escape cancels it. Invalid input preserves the applied value and keeps the panel open with a
short range message, including after an outside dismissal attempt; focus is not pulled back.
Choosing a preset replaces the draft and closes the list. Typing a partial number never resizes
the document. Explicit completion or Escape returns focus to the trigger; outside dismissal
does not steal focus from the destination.

Note and outline editors use the native text caret through Crepe's `Cursor.virtual: false`
setting. The virtual overlay and its caret-hiding class are absent: its viewport measurements
would scale twice inside CSS zoom. Click/selection coordinates are not remapped. The Cursor
feature stays enabled for gap/drop support. The virtual directional affinity tail and its
extra formatting-boundary arrow step are intentionally removed; native/editor mark behavior
applies. Tests cover the shared factory, preserved selection/typing/undo and boundary keys.

The zoom lives on the CONTENT, never on a shell (YAZ-1710 D14). The scroller `.editor-host` carries
only `--document-zoom`; `app.css` applies `zoom: var(--document-zoom)` to the children of every
block (`.editor-host > :not(.editor-mount) > *`) and to the body's `.ProseMirror` node itself. The
`display: contents` properties panel is one of those children and passes the zoom on to its pieces
through computed style — listing the pieces again zoomed the chip twice (6B1). Each block's column shell — `max-width`,
`margin: 0 auto`, the 48px gutters — stays in unzoomed px, so the five blocks keep one aligned
column and the gutters never grow; bullet indent (`--list-indent: 2.15em`) scales with the text
on purpose (damping was tried and rejected, D13). Crepe appends its floating chrome — toolbar,
slash menu, link preview/edit, block handle — beside `.ProseMirror` inside `.milkdown`, so it is
unzoomed and floating-ui positions it in unzoomed space, the only space it gets right (a
counter-`zoom` rescales the popup's own left/top; a counter-`transform` leaves a z× layout box
that `flip()`/`shift()` cannot keep on screen at 400%). The block handle's probe
(`blockHandleTarget.ts`) therefore scales with `cssZoom(view.dom)`, not with the handle.
The control/status row, tabs, sidebar and dialogs outside the scroller keep their usual size.
Retained editor mounts preserve zoom across tab switches; closing/reopening or another remount
(including path changes) resets it. Separate mounted editors, including right panes, are
independent. Zoom does not change Markdown, autosave, IPC, settings, or persisted app state.
Non-Markdown viewers, Electron's existing application zoom and outline bullet-focus zoom keep
their separate behavior. No Fit mode or persistent preference. `⌘+` / `⌘−` / `⌘0` step this ladder
while focus is inside the note's section and zoom the whole app otherwise (YAZ-1710, see the menu
table). **Caret anchor (D11):** every zoom change — keys or pill — goes through one door,
`changeZoom` in `Editor.tsx`, which snapshots the caret's offset inside the scroller BEFORE the zoom
(when the selection is inside this note's section and visible); a `useLayoutEffect` then scrolls
by exactly what the zoom moved it, so the line being edited does not budge on screen, like a design
tool zooming around the cursor. A caret that was off screen, or none in this note, is brought to
`scrollIntoView({ block: 'center' })`. **Sideways slack (D15):** the scroller is `overflow-x: auto`
with a zero-height `::after` runner `calc(100% + var(--zoom-slack))`; `Editor.tsx` MEASURES
`--zoom-slack` = the deepest bullet's indent at this zoom minus the same at 100% (over
`.list-item > .children`, on every zoom change and on a `ResizeObserver` of the body), so a full
drag parks the deepest bullet where it sits unzoomed and never further — the page still reflows on
screen (panning to a fixed column was tried and rejected); 0 at 100% and for a note with no bullets.

DOM geometry used for pointer hit areas, drag distances and pinned folder-table headers must
convert viewport measurements to local layout units when inside a scaled block (`cssZoom` on the
element being measured — the scroller itself is unscaled since D14).

Milkdown table drag previews and insertion indicators use the version-pinned
`@milkdown/components@7.22.1` archive in `client/vendor`, enforced for all transitive consumers
by the root npm override. The narrow patch converts viewport sizes/offsets to local CSS pixels;
it preserves row/column drop targeting and the existing 100% behavior. Rebuild/provenance
instructions live in `client/vendor/milkdown-components-7.22.1-yaz1410.md`. The shipped runtime
has geometry regression coverage at 50%, 100%, 125% and 200%; upgrades must preserve those tests.
The same archive carries YAZ-2131 4B: Crepe's list-item node view restores the caret it saw at
mount once per editor view per frame (the latest capture of a still-mounted item wins, as the last
of the old per-item dispatches did), never once per list item — N items were N whole-plugin
transactions in one frame. `listItemCaretRestore.test.ts` pins it on the installed runtime.
The same pipeline (`tools/buildMilkdownPatch.mjs`, one entry per package) carries a pinned
`@milkdown/plugin-tooltip@7.22.1` for YAZ-2238: a throttled `TooltipProvider` update is skipped only
when the state matches both the call's `prevState` AND the state its last evaluated update saw, so a
no-op transaction can no longer swallow the update that hides Crepe's floating toolbar (a stale
toolbar after ⌘Z covered the row above and kept the block handle off it). Provenance in
`client/vendor/milkdown-plugin-tooltip-7.22.1-yaz2238.md`; `tooltipThrottle.test.ts` pins it on the
installed runtime.

### Configured empty Board columns (YAZ-1417)

`view.showEmptyColumns` defaults off and is saved per Board view. `boardOptionGroups` inserts configured Select/Multi-select options after engine evaluation, retaining observed unlisted/typed values. `ASC` means property option order and `DESC` reverses it; the Sort menu labels this override explicitly. Within-column card sorting stays independent. Search retains configured destinations when the toggle is on.

Empty destinations carry an exact `optionValue`; drag and New card can therefore write/seed a value without borrowing the first card. Multi-select targets remain fanned out and cross-outer nested moves retain the existing atomic two-property write. Nested groups obey the engine merge rule: an inner value equal to its outer belongs in `outer.direct`, not a duplicate empty child.

Verification lives in `boardOptionGroups.test.ts`, `PropertyDefinitionEditor.test.tsx`, `SelectValueEditor.test.tsx`, `writeFolderColumn.test.ts`, `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`, `Toolbar.test.tsx`, and the existing Board/drag/typing/filter/scaffold suites. No new colors, bulk record migrations, duplication/layout customization or AI property features are included.
